# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 99 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 501

...the one who causes growth; the one who takes the oblations that the sacrificers offer in the fire to the gods and gives them; or the one who himself performs the work of the priest called Neṣṭā: this is the meaning.

तिसृभ्य आ वरं (*tisṛbhya ā varaṃ*)—it means the fire in the three forms (fire-pits): Gārhapatya, Āhavanīya and Dakṣiṇāgni.

Here the word धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*) is used as an adjective of the word स्वसारः (*svasāraḥ*) in the sense "pleasing like cows". The fingers in the hand of the priest called Neṣṭṛ serve the three forms of fire abundantly in the sacrificial rites. As cows please by giving milk, so the services and the like performed by the fingers of the Neṣṭṛ please Agni: this is the chief intention.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यदी॑ मा॒तुरुप॒ स्वसा॑ घृ॒तं भरं॒त्यस्थि॑त ।
> तासा॑मध्व॒र्युराग॑त्॒ यवो॑ वृ॒ष्टीव॑ मोदते ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यदि । मातुः । उप । स्वसा । धृतं । भरंती । अस्थित ।
> तासां । अध्वर्युः । आगतौ । यवः । वृष्टिऽइव । मोदते ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यदि यदा मातुः सर्वस्य निर्मातुर्भूम्या वेदिलक्षणायाः स्वसा स्वसृस्था-
> नीया जुहूर्घृतं भरंती सत्युपासित । उपतिष्ठेदग्निं । एकवचनमविवक्षितं ।
> जुहूपभृद्धुवा घृतं भरंत्यस्तिष्ठंतीत्यर्थः । तदा तासां जुह्वादीनामागतौ
> यागाय प्राप्तौ सत्यामध्वर्युरेतन्नामक ऋत्विग्रूपोऽध्वरं कामयमानो
> वायमग्निर्मोदते । हृष्यति । यवो वृष्टीव वृष्ट्या यथा मोदते तद्वत् ॥

*Gist:* When the sister vessels (ladles) of the altar-earth, filled with ghee, stand near, Agni the Adhvaryu rejoices at their arrival as barley rejoices at rain.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यदि (*yadi*)—when. मातु: (*mātu:*)—of the earth, which is the maker of the world and has the form of the altar. स्वसा (*svasā*)—the ladle vessels (Juhū) that are sisters to her. धृतं (*dhṛtaṃ*)—clarified butter (ājya). भरंति (*bharaṃti*)—filling themselves with. उप अस्थित (*upa asthita*)—are placed near the fire (then),

### Printed p. 502

तासां (*tāsāṃ*)—of those ladle vessels. आगतौ (*āgatau*)—at the time of arrival. अध्वर्युः (*adhvaryuḥ*)—Agni, who desires the sacrifice or who is named Adhvaryu. यवः (*yavaḥ*)—the barley grain. वृष्टीव (*vṛṣṭīva*)—as if indicating joy at the time of the coming of rain. मोदते (*modate*)—rejoices.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

When the ladle vessels, which are sisters to the earth in the form of the altar, filled with ghee, come near Agni, then Agni, who desires the sacrifice, rejoices greatly, as barley shows its joy at the time of the coming of rain.

**Printed English translation**

When the sister,(vessel), bearing the clarified butter, is placed near the maternal(altar), the Adhwaryu(Agni) rejoices at their approach, like barley(at the fall of) rain.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the words मातु: स्वसा (*mātu: svasā*) have no clearly stated meaning, but the commentator has fitted in the meanings "altar" and "Juhū" according to the context and explained the sense. For the word *mātṛ* he gives the various meanings mother, maker, altar, world, earth, according to context. Here he gives the meaning "sacrificial altar", and for the word स्वसा (*svasā*) he explains "Juhū, the wooden ladle for offering ājya, which is like a sister to the altar". Although the sense fits well with this kind of explanation, there remains doubt whether these words are in fact used in these senses.

अध्वर्युः धृतं भरंती आगतौ यवः वृष्टीव मोदते (*adhvaryuḥ dhṛtaṃ bharaṃtī āgatau yavaḥ vṛṣṭīva modate*)—the comparison stated in this illustration fits well. When the priest called Adhvaryu fills the offering ladles with ājya (ghee) in order to offer it in the fire and comes near the fire, the joy that arises in Agni is described: as the barley plants welcome with great joy the rain that they need, so Agni too becomes joyful.

### Printed p. 503

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स्वः स्वाय॒ धाय॑से कृणु॒तामृ॒त्विगृ॒त्विजं॑ ।
> स्तोमं॑ य॒ज्ञं चादरं॑ व॒नेमा॑ ररि॒मा व॒यं ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> स्वः । स्वाय । धायसे । कृणुतां । ऋत्विक् । ऋत्विजं ।
> स्तोमं । यज्ञं । च । आत् । अरं । वनेम । ररिम । वयं ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अयमग्निः स्वः स्वयमेव स्वाय स्वकीयाय धायसे कर्मणे हविषां
> धारणाय वा ऋत्विक् होताजनिष्टेत्यादिनोक्तलक्षणो होतृपोतृप्रशास्त्रादृत्ति-
> ग्रूपः सन् ऋत्विजमार्त्विज्यं ॥ भावप्रधानोऽयं निर्देशः ॥ देवर्त्विग्रूपः सन्
> ऋत्विजं मानुषमार्त्विज्यं कृणुतां । करोतु । वयं च तदनुग्रहात् आदनंतर-
> मेव स्तोमं स्तोत्रं यज्ञं च तदाश्रयं यागमप्यरमलमत्यर्थं वनेम ।
> संभजेमहि । ररिम ददेमहि च हविः । यद्वा स्तोमं वनेम यज्ञं यज्ञ-
> योग्यं हवी ररिम ॥

*Gist:* Agni himself, as priest, performs the priestly office for his own purpose; we then fully offer praise, sacrifice and oblation.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(अयमग्निः (*ayamagniḥ*)—this Agni) स्वः (*svaḥ*)—himself. स्वाय (*svāya*)—his own. धायसे (*dhāyase*)—for the work, or for holding the oblations. ऋत्विक् (*ṛtvik*)—in the form of the priests, the Hotṛ and others, described before. ऋत्विजं (*ṛtvijaṃ*)—the work to be performed by the Hotṛ and others. कृणुतां (*kṛṇutāṃ*)—let him perform. वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we also. आत् (*āt*)—immediately after that. स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—the praise. यज्ञं च (*yajñaṃ ca*)—and the sacrifice that depends on it. अरं (*araṃ*)—fully. वनेम (*vanema*)—we worship. ररिम (*rarima*)—we also offer the oblation.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Let this Agni himself, in the form of the priest, perform for his own work the work to be performed by the Hotṛ and others. Immediately after that we too fully worship the praise and the sacrifice that depends on it (we perform them), and we also offer the oblation.

### Printed p. 504

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 5, p. 504.*

**Printed English translation**

May he, the ministering priest, discharge the office of priest at his own ceremony, and may we worthily repeat(his) praise, and offer(him) sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सः ऋत्विक् ऋत्विजं कृणुतां (*saḥ ṛtvik ṛtvijaṃ kṛṇutāṃ*)—in this sacrifice which Agni himself performs, the wish is expressed that, as said before, he himself discharge the duties of the many priests who take part in the sacrifice, the Hotṛ, Potṛ, Praśāstṛ and others, and bring the sacrifice to completion. The use of the words ऋत्विक् ऋत्विजं (*ṛtvik ṛtvijaṃ*) here indicates all the priests, the Hotṛ and the rest. That is, Agni himself (in the form of each respective priest) performs the rites that all of them perform; the meaning is that Agni, who has the form of a divine priest, performs the rites that the human priests perform.

स्तोमं यज्ञं चादरं वनेमा ररिमा वयं (*stomaṃ yajñaṃ cādaraṃ vanemā rarimā vayaṃ*)—if, as said above, Agni himself performs the sacrificial rites of all the priests, what is left for us to do? The answer: we too recite hymns and the like in the sacrifice and praise the gods, and offer oblations and the like; thus the acts to be performed by us (the sacrificers and others) are also explained.

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यथा॑ वि॒द्वा अरं॒ कर॒द्विश्वे॑भ्यो यज॒तेभ्यः॑ ।
> अ॒यम॑ग्ने॒ त्वे अपि॒ यं य॒ज्ञं च॑कृ॒मा व॒यं ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यथा । विद्वान् । अरं । करत् । विश्वेभ्यः । यजतेभ्यः ।
> अयं । अग्ने । त्वे इति । अपि । यं । यज्ञं । चकृम । वयं ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने विद्वान् त्वन्माहात्माभिज्ञः प्रयोगज्ञो वा यजमानो यथा
> विश्वेभ्यो यजतेभ्यः सर्वॆभ्यो यजनीयेभ्यो देवेभ्योऽरं पर्याप्तं करत्

> *he agne vidvān tvanmāhātmābhijñaḥ prayogajño vā yajamāno yathā*
> *viśvebhyo yajatebhyaḥ sarvèbhyo yajanīyebhyo devebhyo 'raṃ paryāptaṃ karat*

### Printed p. 505

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 26, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 505.*

> कुर्यात् तथा त्वमनुगृहाणेत्यर्थः । वयमपि यं यज्ञं यागविशेषं चक्रम
> कुर्महे स यागस्वे अपि ॥ अपिरेवार्थे ॥ तवैव त्वत्प्रीत्यर्थः । त्वमेव तस्मात्तं
> सर्वदेवार्थयागं तत्तदृश्निग्रूपः सन् निर्वहेत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* O Agni, grant that the knowing sacrificer may fully satisfy all the gods, and that our sacrifice, offered for your pleasure, you yourself carry through in the form of each priest.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni. विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—the sacrificer who knows your greatness (or who knows the procedure). यथा (*yathā*)—in whatever manner. विश्वेभ्यः (*viśvebhyaḥ*)—to all. यजतेभ्यः (*yajatebhyaḥ*)—to the gods who are worthy of sacrifice. आरं (*āraṃ*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has अरं (*araṃ*)]—so as to be complete. करत् (*karat*)—offers worship. (Favour him thus.) वयमपि (*vayamapi*)—we also. यं यज्ञं (*yaṃ yajñaṃ*)—which sacrifice. चक्रम (*cakrama*) [as supplied]—we perform (may that sacrifice). त्वे अपि (*tve api*)—in you also (attain fulfilment).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, grant that the worship offered by the sacrificer, who knows your greatness and knows the nature of the procedure, may reach all the gods worthy of sacrifice and bring them satisfaction. May the sacrifice that we perform also be offered to you and reach fulfilment in you.

**Printed English translation**

And do you, Agni,(grant), that this wise(worshipper) may worthily offer(worship) to all the adorable(divinities), and that the sacrifice which we offer may also be perfected in you.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

This Ṛk is the conclusion of the sūkta. The prayer is expressed that the sacrifice we perform may bring satisfaction both to the gods and to you, Agni.

विद्वान्—त्वन्माहात्माभिज्ञः प्रयोगज्ञो वा यजमानः (*vidvān—tvanmāhātmābhijñaḥ prayogajño vā yajamānaḥ*)—the meaning is the sacrificer performing the sacrifice who knows your greatness and extraordinary personality, Agni, or who knows the various procedures to be carried out in the sacrifice and the methods of performing them.

विश्वेभ्यः यजतेभ्यः (*viśvebhyaḥ yajatebhyaḥ*)—Agni is prayed to grant that all those gods whom we have intended to invoke in this sacrifice and to satisfy with hymns and offerings of oblations may be satisfied.

अयं अग्ने त्वे अपि यज्ञं चकृमा वयं (*ayaṃ agne tve api yajñaṃ cakṛmā vayaṃ*)—besides granting that the gods concerned be satisfied, Agni is asked to grant that in this sacrifice we perform you (Agni) too may be gladdened, and

### Printed p. 506

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 506.*

that we may perform the sacrifice; and, since we perform this sacrifice for the pleasure of Agni, Agni is prayed that he himself, addressing all the gods, should carry the sacrificial rites through completely in the form of each respective priest.

## Sūkta 6 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The sixth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> इमां म इत्यष्टर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं सोमाहुत्यार्षमाग्नेयं गायत्रं ।
> इमां मे गायत्रं हीत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोगायत्रे छंदस्यस्य
> विनियोगः । सूत्रितं च । इमां मे अग्ने समिधमिमामिति त्रयाणामुत्तमामुद्ध-
> रेत् । आ. ४-१३ । इति ॥ अपराह्नि क्यामुपसदीमां मे अग्ने समिधमिति तिस्र ऋच-
> स्त्रिरभ्यस्ता नव सामिधेन्यः कृत्वा विनियोक्तव्याः । अथोपसदिति खंडे सूत्रितं । इमां
> मे अग्ने समिधमिमामिति तु सामिधेन्यः । आ- ४-८ । इति ॥

*Gist:* *imāṃ me* is the sixth sūkta, of eight ṛks, Gāyatrī metre, deity Agni, seer Somāhuti Bhārgava; used in the Prātaranuvāka and Āśvina-śastra, and as Sāmidhenī verses.

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

This sūkta *imāṃ me* is the sixth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has eight ṛks. The seer is the same Somāhuti Bhārgava who was the seer of the preceding sūkta; the deity is Agni; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇī it is stated "*imāṃ me gāyatraṃ hi*". It is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra rule इमां मे समिधमिमामिति त्रयाणामुत्तमामुद्धरेत् (*imāṃ me samidhamimāmiti trayāṇāmuttamāmuddharet*) (Āś. 4-13) that this sūkta is applied to the Gāyatrī ṛks at the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka and the mantras of the Āśvina-śastra. Also, in the Upasad sacrifice performed in the afternoon, the three ṛks of this sūkta beginning इमां मे अग्ने समिधं (*imāṃ me agne samidhaṃ*), each recited three times, are to be used as nine Sāmidhenī ṛks; this is explained by the rule इमां मे अग्ने समिधमिमामिति तु सामिधेन्यः (*imāṃ me agne samidhamimāmiti tu sāmidhenyaḥ*) (Āś. 4-8) in the section called Athopasad of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra.

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 6. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 6. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 27. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 8. Seer: सोमहृतिर्भार्गवः (*somahṛtirbhārgavaḥ*) (as supplied; the introduction above has Somāhuti). Deity: Agni (printed देवका (*devakā*)). Metre: Gāyatrī (printed छंद‘ (*chaṃda‘*) गायत्री (*gāyatrī*)).

### Printed p. 507

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 27, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 507.*

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इ॒मां मे॑ अग्ने स॒मिध॑मि॒मामु॑प॒सदं॑ वनेः ।
> इ॒मा उ॒ शु श्रु॑धी॒ गिरः॑ ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> इमां । मे । अग्ने । संइधं । इमां । उपसदं । वनेरिति वनेः ।
> इमाः । ऊं इति । सु । श्रुधि । गिरः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने मे मदीयामिमामाधीयमानां समिधं इमामुपसदमुप-
> सदनसाधनामाहुतिमुपसद्यागे क्रियमाणां वा वनेः । संभजेथाः । तथेमा उ
> इमा अपि गिरः स्तुतीः सु सुष्टु श्रुधि । शृणु ॥

*Gist:* O Agni, accept this fuel and this oblation of the Upasad rite, and listen well to these praises.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni. मे (*me*)—my (offered by me). इमां समिधं (*imāṃ samidhaṃ*)—this fuel-stick too. इमां उपसदं (*imāṃ upasadaṃ*)—this oblation, a part of the Upasad sacrifice. वनेः (*vaneḥ*)—accept and partake. इमा उ गिरः (*imā u giraḥ*)—these praises also. सु श्रुधि (*su śrudhi*)—listen with favour.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, accept this fuel-stick offered by me and this oblation that belongs to the Upasad sacrifice. Listen with favour to these praises also.

**Printed English**

Agni, accept this my fuel, this my oblation; listen, well pleased, to my praises.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

उपसदं (*upasadaṃ*)—Upasad is said to be an important part of the Jyotiṣṭoma sacrifice.

समिधं (*samidhaṃ*)—इंध् दीप्तौ (*iṃdh dīptau*): from the root meaning "to shine well, to burn", this word is the name for the ends of certain dry trees that burn well. When offering in the fire

### Printed p. 508

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 508.*

as a homa, these are the small dry twigs fallen beneath sacrificially suitable trees such as the aśvattha (peepal). They are thinner than a little finger and four to six inches long. The tip (end) is called the agra-bhāga (front part); sticks having such a tip are used for offering in the fire. When the sticks are offered, certain prescribed Ṛks are recited; these are called Sāmidhenī mantras, and there are seventeen of them.

गिरः (*giraḥ*)—praises in the form of speech or of a Ṛk. Yāska has read the word गीः (*gīḥ*) among the words for speech (Nir. 2-23); also गिरः—स्तुतयो गिरो गृणातेः (*giraḥ—stutayo giro gṛṇāteḥ*) (Nir. 1 10) [as supplied]. The word गिरः (*giraḥ*) is derived from the root गृ शब्दे (*gṛ śabde*); गिरः (*giraḥ*) means praises. There is also the explanation गृणातिः स्तुतिकर्मा (*gṛṇātiḥ stutikarmā*) (Nir. 3-5). Moreover there is the explanation गिरा—गीत्या स्तुत्या (*girā—gītyā stutyā*) (Nir. 6-24 and Nir. 10-5); hence गिरः (*giraḥ*) means hymns of praise.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒या ते॑ अग्ने विधे॒नोर्जो॑ नपा॒दश्व॑मिष्टे ।
> ए॒ना सू॒क्तेन॑ सुजात ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अया । ते । अग्ने । विधेम । ऊर्जः । नपात् । अश्वं इष्टे ।
> एना । सुउक्तेन । सुजात ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने ते त्वामयानयाहुत्या विधेम । परिचरेम । हे ऊर्जो नपात्
> बलस्य नप्तः तस्य नपातयितर्वा हे अश्वमिष्टे व्यापकयज्ञ । यद्वा आशु क्रियमाणं
> कर्माश्वं । तदिच्छतीत्यश्वमिष्टिः । संश्वरमाणा ह्युपसदमाचरंति । ते कर्मभिः सम-
> त्वरंत ते तिस्रः सामिधेनीरनूच्य तिस्रो देवता आयजन्निति ब्राह्मणं । ऐ। ब्रा ३-४५।
> तादृशाग्ने ते त्वामयानयाहुत्या विधेम । परिचरेम । तथा हे सुजात शोभन-
> जनन एना एतेन सूक्तेन स्तोत्रेण प्रीणयेमेति शेषः । यद्वा स्तुत्युपवृहितेन
> विधेम ॥

> *he agne te tvāmayānayāhutyā vidhema | paricarema | he ūrjo napāt*
> *balasya naptaḥ tasya napātayitarvā he aśvamiṣṭe vyāpakayajña | yadvā āśu kriyamāṇaṃ*
> *karmāśvaṃ | tadicchatītyaśvamiṣṭiḥ | saṃśvaramāṇā hyupasadamācaraṃti | te karmabhiḥ sama-*
> *tvaraṃta te tisraḥ sāmidhenīranūcya tisro devatā āyajanniti brāhmaṇaṃ | ai| brā 3-45|*
> *tādṛśāgne te tvāmayānayāhutyā vidhema | paricarema | tathā he sujāta śobhana-*
> *janana enā etena sūktena stotreṇa prīṇayemeti śeṣaḥ | yadvā stutyupavṛhitena*
> *vidhema ‖*

*Gist:* We serve you, Agni, with this oblation and this hymn; the bhāṣya explains अश्वमिष्टे (*aśvamiṣṭe*) as one who desires swift, wide-spread sacrifice, citing a Brāhmaṇa on the Upasad.

### Printed p. 509

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 27, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 509.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऊर्जो नपात् (*ūrjo napāt*)—son of strength. अश्वमिष्टे (*aśvamiṣṭe*)—one who desires the Upasad sacrifice performed promptly and with faith. अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni. ते (*te*)—you. आया (*āyā*) [as supplied]—by this oblation. विधेम (*vidhema*)—we shall serve. सुजात (*sujāta*)—O Agni of excellent birth. एना सूक्तेन (*enā sūktena*)—also with this hymn in the form of praise (we shall honour you).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, son of strength, who desire the Upasad sacrifice performed promptly and with faith, we shall serve you with this oblation. O Agni of excellent birth, we shall honour you also with this mantra in the form of praise. [The supplied text reads परिचयिसुवॆवु (*paricayisuvèvu*) "we shall make acquainted" for the first verb; the Pratipadārtha has परिचरिसुवॆवु (*paricarisuvèvu*) "we shall serve".]

**Printed English**

May we propitiate you, Agni, by this(oblation); graudson [sic] of strength, you are gratified by prompt sacrifice;(my [sic] we please you), well-horn [sic], by this hymn.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सूक्तेन (*sūktena*)—सुष्टु उक्तेन (*suṣṭu uktena*): well recited, that is, a hymn or Ṛk that praises the qualities of the gods well. In the Ṛgveda a group of many such Ṛks is called a sūkta. In sum, sūkta means a hymn of praise.

ऊर्जो नपात् (*ūrjo napāt*)—बलस्य नप्तः (*balasya naptaḥ*): when fire is churned out of the araṇi sticks, Agni arises only by churning with great force; therefore Agni is addressed with the words सहसस्पुत्र (*sahasasputra*) [as supplied] and ऊर्जोनपात् (*ūrjonapāt*).

अश्वमिष्टे (*aśvamiṣṭe*)—व्यापकयज्ञ (*vyāpakayajña*): one who pervades the sacrifice. If the word अश्व (*aśva*) is understood as derived from the root अशू व्याप्तौ (*aśū vyāptau*), the above meaning results. Or आशु क्रियामणं कर्म अश्वं (*āśu kriyāmaṇaṃ karma aśvaṃ*) [as supplied]: the word अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*), formed from अशु (*aśu*) meaning "quickly", also means a sacrifice performed quickly. Here both meanings are possible.

अया (*ayā*)—आनया (*ānayā*) [as supplied]: for the convenience of the metre the na of अनया (*anayā*) is dropped, giving आया (*āyā*). Likewise the a of आत्मना (*ātmanā*) is dropped and the form त्मना (*tmanā*) is used in many places. Such usages are numerous in the Ṛgveda.

### Printed p. 510

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 510.*

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तं त्वा॑ गी॒र्भिर्गिर्व॑णसं द्रविण॒स्युं द्र॑विणोदः ।
> स॒प॒र्येम॑ सप॒र्यवः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तं । त्वा । गीःभिः । गिर्वणसं । द्रविणस्युं । द्रविणःदः ।
> सपर्येम । सपर्यवः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे द्रविणोदो धनस्य दातरग्ने तं तादृशं गिर्वणसं गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिर्वन-
> नीयं द्रविणस्युं हविर्लक्षणधनेच्छुं त्वा त्वां सपर्यवः परिचरणकर्तारो वयं
> गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिः सपर्येम । परिचरेम ॥

*Gist:* Agni, giver of wealth, praised by hymns and desiring the wealth of oblations, is to be served by us, his worshippers, with praises.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

द्रविणोदः (*draviṇodaḥ*)—O Agni, giver of wealth. गिर्वणसं (*girvaṇasaṃ*)—one who is worshipped with praises. द्रविणस्युं (*draviṇasyuṃ*)—one who desires wealth in the form of oblation. तं त्वा (*taṃ tvā*)—you, the well-known one. सपर्यवः (*saparyavaḥ*)—we, your worshippers. गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises. सपर्येम (*saparyema*)—we shall worship.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, giver of wealth, worshipped with praises, desiring wealth in the form of oblation, the well-known one: we, your worshippers, shall worship you with praises.

**Printed English**

Granter of riches, may we, your worshippers, propitiate you, who are desirous of(sacrificial) wealth, and deserving of laudation, by our praises.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

द्रविणोदः (*draviṇodaḥ*)—a detailed explanation of this word has been given on pages four hundred and seven to four hundred and nine of this same volume.

गिर्वणसं (*girvaṇasaṃ*)—स्तुतिलक्षणैर्वचोभिः संभजनीयं (*stutilakṣaṇairvacobhiḥ saṃbhajanīyaṃ*): one to be resorted to by words in the form of praise; that is, one worthy of hymns, one fit to be praised. There is Yāska's explanation: गिर्वणा देवो भवति गीर्भिरेनं वनयंति (*girvaṇā devo bhavati gīrbhirenaṃ vanayaṃti*) (Nirukta 6-14).

### Printed p. 511

*Running head: A. 2, A. 5, V. 27, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 511.*

सपर्यवः (*saparyavaḥ*)—परिचरणकर्तारो वयं (*paricaraṇakartāro vayaṃ*): we who attend on you; that is, we, the sacrificers and priests who serve you.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स बो॑धि सू॒रिर्म॒घवा॒ वसु॑पते॒ वसु॑दावन् ।
> यु॒यो॒ध्य१॒॑ १ स्मद्वेषां॑सि ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । बोधि । सूरिः । मघवा । वसुपते । वसुदावन् ।
> युयोधि । अस्मत् । द्वेषांसि ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> मघवान्नवान् सूरिर्विद्वान् प्रेरको ना स उक्तलक्षणकस्त्वं बोधि । बुध्यस्वास्मदीयं स्तोत्रं । किंच हे अग्ने द्वेषांसि द्वेष्टानस्मदस्मत्तो युयोधि ।
> पृथक्कुरु ॥

*Gist:* Agni, wise and wealthy, is asked to attend to our hymn and to keep our hateful enemies away from us.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

वसुपते (*vasupate*)—lord of wealth. वसुदावन् (*vasudāvan*)—giver of wealth, O Agni. मघवा (*maghavā*)—possessor of wealth. सूरिः (*sūriḥ*)—wise, or inspirer of the rite. सः (*saḥ*)—that you. बोधि (*bodhi*)—understand (our rite and praise). द्वेषांसि (*dveṣāṃsi*)—our enemies who hate us. अस्मत् (*asmat*)—from us. युयोधि (*yuyodhi*)—separate (drive far away).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, lord and giver of wealth, possessor of wealth, wise or inspirer of the rite: understand our rite and our praise. Separate from us the enemies who hate us.

**Printed English**

Lord of wealth, giver of wealth, possessor of riches, Agni, you are wise, understand(our prayers), and scatter our enemies.

### Printed p. 512

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 512.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

मघवा (*maghavā*)—the word मघ (*magha*) is read among the names of wealth (Nirukta 3-9). There is also the explanation मघमिति धननामधेयं महंतेर्दानकर्मणः (*maghamiti dhananāmadheyaṃ mahaṃterdānakarmaṇaḥ*) (Nirukta 1-7), which indicates the act of giving. The word मघ (*magha*), derived from the root मंहति (*maṃhati*) [as supplied], means wealth; मघवान् (*maghavān*) means one who has wealth. This word is used also of other deities such as Agni and Indra; because its use is most frequent in connection with Indra, in modern Sanskrit the word मघवत् (*maghavat*) has no meaning other than Indra.

सूरिः (*sūriḥ*)—the word सूरि (*sūri*) is read among the names of praisers (Nirukta 2-19). This word has the meanings intelligent, wise, clever, inspirer, and so on.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स नो॑ वृ॒ष्टिं दि॒वस्परि॒ स नो॒ वाज॑मन॒र्वाणं॑ ।
> स नः॑ सह॒स्रिणी॒रिषः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । नः । वृष्टिं । दिवः । परि । सः । नः । वाजं । अनर्वाणं ।
> सः । नः । सहस्रिणीः । इषः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सः स एवाग्निर्नोऽस्मदर्थं दिवस्परि द्युलोकादंतरिक्षात् ॥ परीति पंच-
> म्यर्थानुवादी ॥ वृष्टिं करोति च शेषः । स एव नोस्मदर्थमनर्वाणमविचल-
> मनल्पं वाजं बलं ददातु । तथा सहस्रिणीरपरिमितप्रकाराणीषोऽन्नानि स एवाग्नि-
> र्ददातु ॥

*Gist:* May that same Agni send rain from the sky, and give us abundant unfailing strength and countless foods.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सः (*saḥ*)—that same Agni described above. नः (*naḥ*)—to us. दिवस्परि (*divaspari*)—from the atmosphere. वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—rain (he pours). सः (*saḥ*)—he. नः (*naḥ*)—to us. अनर्वाणं (*anarvāṇaṃ*)—(everlasting) abundant. वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—strength (may he give; likewise). नः (*naḥ*)—to us. सहस्रिणीः (*sahasriṇīḥ*)—countless. इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—foods. सः (*saḥ*)—may that same Agni give.

### Printed p. 513

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 27, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 513.*

**Bhāvārtha** (continued from p. 512)

That same Agni described above pours rain on us from the atmosphere. May he give us abundant strength and unlimited food.

**Printed English**

(For you are) he(who gives) us rain from heaven; he(who gives) us undecaying strength, he(who gives) us infinite(abundance of) food.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ईळा॑नायाव॒स्यवे॒ यवि॑ष्ठ दूत नो गि॒रा ।
> यजि॑ष्ठ होत॒रा ग॑हि ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ईळानाय । अवस्यवे । यविष्ठ । दूत । नः । गिरा ।
> यजिष्ठ । होतः । आ । गहि ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे यविष्ठ युवत्तम दूत देवानां यज्ञादिसूचक यजिष्ठातिशयेन
> यष्टव्य होतर्देवानामाह्वातरग्ने नो गिरास्मदीयया स्तुत्येळानाय पूजयि-
> त्रेवस्यवे स्वरक्षणमिच्छते मदर्थमागहि । आगच्छ ॥

*Gist:* Agni, youthful messenger and invoker of the gods, is asked to come at our praise to the worshipper who seeks his protection.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)—most youthful. दूत (*dūta*)—messenger of the gods. यजिष्ठ (*yajiṣṭha*)—possessed of exceeding worthiness of sacrifice. होतः (*hotaḥ*)—O Agni, who call the gods to the sacrifice. नः (*naḥ*)—our. गिरा (*girā*)—by the voice that takes the form of praise. ईळानाय (*īḷānāya*)—to me who worship. अनस्यवे (*anasyave*) [sic, as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada have अवस्यवे (*avasyave*)]—who desire protection. आ गहि (*ā gahi*)—be gracious, come.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, most youthful, messenger of the gods, exceedingly worthy of sacrifice, who call the gods to the sacrifice: be gracious to me, who worship you with the voice of praise and desire your protection.

### Printed p. 514

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 514.*

**Printed English**

Youngest(of the gods), their messenger, their invoker, most deserving of worship, come, upon our praise, to him who offers(you) adoration, and is desirous of(your) protection.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अं॒त्यर्ह्य॑ग्न॒ ईय॑से वि॒द्वाञ्जनो॒भया॑ कवे ।
> दूते जन्मेव मित्रः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अंशः । हि । अग्ने । ईयसे । विद्वान् । जन्म । उभया । कवे ।
> दूतः । जन्माऽइव । मित्रैः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे कवे मेधाविन्नग्ने अंतर्जनानाका हृदय ईयसे हि । गच्छसि जानासि वा ।
> हिः स होता सेदु दूत्यं चिकित्त्वा अंतरीयते । ४-८-४ । इत्यादिमंत्रांतरप्रसिद्धि-
> द्योतनार्थः । कीदृशस्त्वं । उभया उभयविधानि जन्म जन्मानि यष्ट्यणां यज-
> मानानां यष्टव्यानां देवानां च संबंधीनि विद्वान् जानन् सन् । ईयस इति
> संबंधः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । जन्यो ज र्भ्यो हितो मित्रो विशेषेण मित्रेभ्यो
> हितो दूत इव । स यथा प्रजानां चित्तवृत्तिज्ञानाय राज्ञा प्रेरितस्तासां मनो
> जानाति तद्वत् त्वमप्यस्माकमंतरीयस इत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* Agni, knowing both kinds of births, those of worshippers and of gods, goes within men like a messenger friendly to men and especially to friends.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

कवे (*kave*)—wise. अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni. उभया जन्म (*ubhayā janma*)—the births of both kinds, the sacrificers and the gods worthy of the sacrifice. विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing well. जन्यः (*janyaḥ*)—beneficial to people. मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—and especially one who does good to friends. दूत इव (*dūta iva*)—as a messenger knows the mind of his master and acts for his good (so do you also). अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—in the hearts of men. ईयसे हि (*īyase hi*)—you surely enter and understand it.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O wise Agni, knowing well the birth of the sacrificers and the birth of the gods worthy of the sacrifice, beneficial to people and especially doing good to friends, like a messenger who knows the mind of his master and acts for his good, you also

### Printed p. 515

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 27, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 515.*

enter the hearts of men, know their wishes and fulfil their desires.

**Printed English**

Sage Agni, you penetrate the innermost(man) knowing the birth of both(the worshipper and the worshipped); like a messenger favourable to men, especially to his friends.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अंतः हि अग्ने ईयसे (*aṃtaḥ hi agne īyase*)—O Agni, you enter the hearts of men; that is, you know all the matters in the hearts or minds of men: this is the meaning intended. The commentator has given as an example, indicating this same sense, the Ṛk स होता सेदु दूत्यं चिकित्त्वा अंतरीयते (*sa hotā sedu dūtyaṃ cikittvā aṃtarīyate*) (Ma. Saṃ. 4-8-4) [reference as supplied]. But in the commentary on that Ṛk the idea that he enters the hearts of men and knows what is in their minds is not indicated. Instead, for the words अंतः ईयते (*aṃtaḥ īyate*) he explains the sense अंतर्द्यावापृथिव्योर्मध्य ईयते गच्छति (*aṃtardyāvāpṛthivyormadhya īyate gacchati*), that is, he goes (moves about) between heaven and earth (earth and atmosphere). Therefore the Ṛk given here as an example does not establish the meaning of the present Ṛk.

जन्म उभया विद्वान् (*janma ubhayā vidvān*)—उभयविधानि जन्मानि यष्टणां यजमानानां यष्टव्यानां देवानां च संबंधीनि च जानन् (*ubhayavidhāni janmāni yaṣṭaṇāṃ yajamānānāṃ yaṣṭavyānāṃ devānāṃ ca saṃbaṃdhīni ca jānan*)—here Agni is described as knowing the two kinds of births. The commentator explains this as knowing the birth, or order of origin, of the sacrificing worshippers and of the gods who are worshipped in the sacrifice.

दूतो जन्मेव मित्रः (*dūto janmeva mitraḥ*)—here an illustration is given. जनेभ्यो हितो विशेषेण मित्रेभ्यो हितो दूत इव । स यथा प्रजानां चित्तवृत्तिज्ञानाय राज्ञा प्रेरितस्तासां मनो जानाति तद्वत् त्वमप्यस्माकमंतरीयस इत्यर्थः (*janebhyo hito viśeṣeṇa mitrebhyo hito dūta iva | sa yathā prajānāṃ cittavṛttijñānāya rājñā preritastāsāṃ mano jānāti tadvat tvamapyasmākamaṃtarīyasa ityarthaḥ*). Agni is praised as being like a messenger who is a well-wisher of people, and especially of those who are friends. How? In the world a messenger sent by the king, moving among the people to learn their minds, learns their wishes and reports them to the king; in the same way Agni too enters the minds of men and, like a well-wisher, makes their heart's desire known to the gods to whom it is directed: this is the intention.

### Printed p. 516

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 6, p. 516.*

(Continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 7:) Although an explanation has been given of this Ṛk in a way that makes some sense, it cannot be said that the correct meaning is just this. There remains room for doubt about the main intention.

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स वि॒द्वा आ च॑ पिप्रयो॒ यक्षि॑ चिकित्सॆ आनु॒षक् ।
> आ चा॒स्मिन्त्स॑त्सि ब॒र्हिषि॑ ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । विद्वान् । आ । च । पिप्रयः । यक्षि । चिकित्वः । आनुषक् ।
> आ । च । अस्मिन् । सत्सि । बर्हिषि ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने स त्वं विद्वान् विशेषाभिज्ञः सन् आ च पिप्रयः । सर्वतोऽस्मान्सू-
> रय च कामैः । हे चिकित्सश्चेतनावन् आनुषक् अनुक्रमेण यक्षि । यज देवान् ।
> आसत्सि । आसीद चास्मिन् स्त्रीणे बर्हिषि ॥

*Gist:* Agni, knowing, satisfy us on every side, worship the gods in order, and sit on this spread sacred grass.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सः (*saḥ*)—(O Agni) you. विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing everything. आ च (*ā ca*)—in every way. पिप्रयः (*piprayaḥ*)—fulfil our desired aims. चिकित्स्यः (*cikitsyaḥ*)—you, who are wise. आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*)—in order. यक्षि (*yakṣi*)—worship (the gods). अस्मिन् (*asmin*)—spread here. बर्हिषि च (*barhiṣi ca*)—and on the seat of darbha grass. आ सत्सि (*ā satsi*)—sit down.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, you who know all, fulfil our desired aims in every way, and worship the gods in order. Sit down also on the darbha-grass seat spread here.

**Printed English**

Agni, you are all-wise; fulfil(our desires): you, who are intelligent, worship( the gods) in order; and sit down on this sacred grass.

### Printed p. 517

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 517.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*)—the meaning is "in order". For this word Yāska has said: आनुषगिति नामानुपूर्वस्यानुषक्तं भवति । स्तृणंति बर्हिरानुषक् इत्यपि निगमो भवति (*ānuṣagiti nāmānupūrvasyānuṣaktaṃ bhavati | stṛṇaṃti barhirānuṣak ityapi nigamo bhavati*). That is, he says that आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*) is a synonym of the words अनुपूर्व (*anupūrva*) and अनुक्रम (*anukrama*), which give the sense "one after another", and he has given the example स्तृणंति बर्हिरानुषक् (*stṛṇaṃti barhirānuṣak*):

आ घा ये अग्निमिंधते स्त्रणंति बर्हिरानुषक् । येषामिंद्रो युवा सखा ॥ (*ā ghā ye agnimiṃdhate straṇaṃti barhirānuṣak | yeṣāmiṃdro yuvā sakhā ‖*) (Mi. Saṃ. 8-45-1) [abbreviation as supplied]

In this example it is said: first they kindle the fire; afterwards they spread the darbha grass called barhis as a seat. Here kindling the fire is one act; spreading the darbha grass is another act. After the first act, kindling the fire, the second act, spreading the darbha grass, is performed. When acts are done in this way, one after another in order, the word आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*) is used to indicate that order. In the present Ṛk also there are three acts of Agni: fulfilling our desired aims, worshipping the gods, and sitting on the darbha grass. The word आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*) is used here so that they be done knowing the order.

*The seventh sūkta.*

## Sūkta 7 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> श्रेष्ठं यविष्टेति षडृचं सप्तमं सूक्तं सोमाहुत्यार्षमाग्नेयं
> गायत्रं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोर्गायत्रे छंदस्यस्य विनियोगः पूर्वसूकु
> एवोक्तः ॥ श्रवणाकर्मण्याद्या विनियुक्ता ॥

*Gist:* *śreṣṭhaṃ yaviṣṭha* is the seventh sūkta, six ṛks, Gāyatrī, deity Agni, seer Somāhuti Bhārgava; used in the Prātaranuvāka and Āśvina-śastra; Ṛk 1 in the Śravaṇākarman.

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

This sūkta *śreṣṭhaṃ yaviṣṭha* is the seventh sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Somāhuti Bhārgava; the deity is Agni; the metre is Gāyatrī. It was said earlier (in the introduction of the preceding sūkta itself) that this sūkta is applied to the Gāyatrī ṛks at the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra mantras. The first Ṛk of this sūkta is applied in the Śravaṇākarman.

### Printed p. 518

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 7, p. 518.*

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 7. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 7. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Somāhuti Bhārgava. Deity: Agni. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> श्रेष्ठं॑ यविष्ठ भार॒ताग्ने॑ द्यु॒मंत॒मा भ॑र ।
> वसो॑ पुरु॒स्पृहं॑ र॒यिं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> श्रेष्ठं । यविष्ठ । भारत । अग्ने । द्युमंतं । आ । भर ।
> वसो इति । पुरुस्पृहं । रयिं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने यविष्ठ युवत्तम हे भारत । भरता ऋत्विजः । तेषां संबंधी
> भारतः । तेऽध्वर्याद्वादिभिर्मंथनहविःस्तोत्रादिना व्याप्रियमाणत्वात् । तादृश हे
> वसो वासक व्याप्त वा एवंरूपाग्ने श्रेष्ठमतिप्रशस्यं द्युमंतं दीप्तिमंतं पुरु-
> स्पृहं बहुभिरर्थिभिः स्पृहणीयं रयिमाभर । आहर ॥

*Gist:* O Agni, youngest, Bhārata (of the priests), bring the best, shining, much-desired wealth.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)—most youthful. भारत (*bhārata*)—kinsman of the priests. वसो (*vaso*)—giver of dwelling. अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni. श्रेष्ठं (*śreṣṭhaṃ*)—most praiseworthy. द्युमंतं (*dyumaṃtaṃ*)—brilliant. पुरुस्पृहं (*puruspṛhaṃ*)—desired by many. रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth. आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring.

### Printed p. 519

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 519.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, most youthful, kinsman of the priests, giver of a dwelling: bring for us wealth that is most praiseworthy, brilliant and desired by many.

**Printed English translation**

Youngest(of the gods), Agni, descendant of Bharata, granter of dwellings, bring(to us) excellent, splendid, and enviable riches.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

भारत (भारत (*bhārata*)) — The word भारत (*bhārata*) is read among the names of priests (Ni. 3-18). भारत (*bhārata*) means a priest (ṛtvij). The word is derived from the root भृञ् भरणे (*bhṛñ bharaṇe*) and, according to Skandasvāmin's explanation, यज्ञद्वारेण नृन् संभरतीति (*yajñadvāreṇa nṛn saṃbharatīti*) — he has the sacrifice performed and by that means lifts up men (the sacrificers); therefore this priest is called Bhārata. Durgācārya, the commentator on the Nirukta, explains भ्रियंते दक्षिणाभिस्ते इति भारताः (*bhriyaṃte dakṣiṇābhiste iti bhāratāḥ*): since they cause the sacrifice to be performed and obtain satisfaction through the fees (dakṣiṇā) and the like, these priests are called Bhāratas. Thus the word *bharata* has two kinds of etymological sense.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> मा नो॒ अरा॑तिरीशत दे॒वस्य॒ मर्त्य॑स्य च ।
> पर्षि॒ तस्या॑ उ॒त द्वि॒षः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> मा । नः । अरातिः । ईशत । बेवस्य । मर्त्यस्य । च ।
> पर्षि । तस्याः । उत । द्विषः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने त्वदनुग्रहान्नोऽस्मानरातिरदानं शत्रुत्वं । यद्वा छंदस्यरातिशब्दः
> शत्रुवाची स्त्रीलिंगो भवति । अदाता शत्रुर्मेशत । पराभवितुं शक्तो मा भूत् ।

> *he agne tvadanugrahānno 'smānarātiradānaṃ śatrutvaṃ | yadvā chaṃdasyarātiśabdaḥ*
> *śatruvācī strīliṃgo bhavati | adātā śatrurmeśata | parābhavituṃ śakto mā bhūt |*

### Printed p. 520

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 7, p. 520.*

> मा विषयाकुर्यात् । आरातिर्विशेष्यते । देवस्य च मर्त्यस्य च संबंधिनी तैः
> कारिता । उत अपि च तस्या उभयविधाया द्विषोऽस्मदरातेः पर्षि । पूरय ॥

*Gist:* Let no enemy, of gods or of men, prevail over us; "pūraya" — protect us from that twofold hatred.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O Agni) देवस्य (*devasya*) — one belonging to the company of the gods, or मर्त्यस्य च (*martyasya ca*) — belonging to the company of men; आरातिः (*ārātiḥ*) — the enemy; नः (*naḥ*) — us; मा ईशत (*mā īśata*) — let him not be able to withstand and defeat; उत (*uta*) — and; तस्याः (*tasyāḥ*) — of those two kinds; द्विषः (*dviṣaḥ*) — from enemies; पर्षि (*parṣi*) — protect us.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, let the enemy, whether belonging to the company of the gods or to the company of men, not be able to withstand and defeat us; and protect us from enemies of both these kinds.

**Printed English translation**

Let no enemy prevail against us, whether of god or man; protect us from both such foes.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अरातिः (*arātiḥ*) — The bhāṣya says: अरातिरदानं । यद्वा छंदस्यरातिशब्दः शत्रुवाची स्त्रीलिंगो भवति । (*arātiradānaṃ | yadvā chaṃdasyarātiśabdaḥ śatruvācī strīliṃgo bhavati |*) That is, a man who gives nothing to anyone, or who does not give oblations and the like to the gods, is called *arāti*. Or, in the Veda the word *arāti* is used especially in the sense of "enemy"; then the word *arāti* is feminine, as the commentator explains. Yāska in the Nirukta gives two kinds of meaning: अरातयोऽदानकर्माणः (*arātayo 'dānakarmāṇaḥ*) — those who do not give are *arātis* (Ni. 3-11); or अरातीः—अमित्रानदानानिति वा (*arātīḥ—amitrānadānāniti vā*) — that is, enemies who are not friends, or those who do not give (Ni. 11-1).

देवस्य (*devasya*) — For the word *deva* Yāska has given the explanation देवो दानाद्वा, दीपनाद्वा, द्योतनाद्वा, द्युस्थानो भवतीति वा (*devo dānādvā, dīpanādvā, dyotanādvā, dyusthāno bhavatīti vā*): he who gives, who shines, who is radiant with brilliance, or who dwells in heaven; therefore he is called *deva* (Ni. 7-15).

मर्त्यस्य (*martyasya*) — This word is derived from the root मृज् प्राणत्यागे (*mṛj prāṇatyāge*); म्रियंत इति मर्त्याः (*mriyaṃta iti martyāḥ*) — those who die are *martya*, so men are called *martya*. The gods are said to be *amartya*. In the Ṛgveda the use of the words *martya* (*marta*) and *amartya* is frequent.

### Printed p. 521

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 521.*

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> विश्वा॑ उ॒त त्वया॑ व॒यं धारा॑ उद॒न्या॑ इव ।
> अति॑ गाहेमहि॒ द्विषः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> विश्वाः । उत । त्वया । वयं । धाराः । उदन्याः इव ।
> अति । गाहेमहि । द्विषः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उत अपि च हे अग्ने वयं त्वदनुग्रहाद्विश्वा द्विषः सर्वानपि द्वेष्टान्
> उदन्या धारा इव उदकसंबंधिनीर्धारा अल्पस्तुतीरिव त्मनात्मनैवान्यनिरपेक्षेणाति-
> गाहेमहि । अतिक्रम्य गच्छेम ॥

*Gist:* Through your favour, Agni, we shall pass over all hatreds easily, as one crosses small streams of water.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उत (*uta*) — and; वयं (*vayaṃ*) — we; उदन्याः धाराः इव (*udanyāḥ dhārāḥ iva*) — as one crosses without effort streams in which only a little water flows; विश्वाः द्विषः (*viśvāḥ dviṣaḥ*) — all our enemies; त्वया (*tvayā*) — by your favour; अति गाहेमहि (*ati gāhemahi*) — may we become able to defeat and cross over.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, as (people) cross without effort small brooks or streams flowing with a little water, so may we also, by your favour, obtain the power to defeat all our enemies.

**Printed English translation**

Let us, through your favour, overwhelm all our foes, like torrents of water.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

धारा उदन्या इव (*dhārā udanyā iva*) — Rivers flowing deep and swift with abundant water cannot easily be crossed by people. But a small stream with only a little water, flowing gently, people pass over without effort. Likewise, the prayer is expressed here that we may defeat our enemies without effort.

### Printed p. 522

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 7, p. 522.*

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> शुचिः॑ पावक॒ वंद्योऽग्ने॑ बृ॒हद्वि रो॑चसे ।
> त्वं घृ॒तेभि॒राहु॑तः ॥ ४ ॥

> *śuciḥ pāvaka vaṃdyo 'gne bṛhadvi rocase |*
> *tvaṃ ghṛtebhirāhutaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> शुचिः । पावक । वंद्यः । अग्ने । बृहत् । वि । रोचसे ।
> त्वं । धृतेभिः । आहुतः ॥ ४ ॥

> *śuciḥ | pāvaka | vaṃdyaḥ | agne | bṛhat | vi | rocase |*
> *tvaṃ | dhṛtebhiḥ | āhutaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे पावक शोधकाग्ने शुचिः शुद्धो वंद्यो नमस्कार्यः सन् बृहत् महत्
> अत्यधिकं विरोचसे । विविधं दीप्यसे । यतस्त्वं घृतेभिर्घृतैराहुतः । समंता-
> सिक्तः । अतो विरोचसे ॥

*Gist:* O purifying Agni, being pure and worthy of homage you shine widely, since you are sprinkled all round with ghee.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पावक (*pāvaka*) — purifier; अग्ने (*agne*) — O Agni; शुचिः (*śuciḥ*) — pure; वंद्यः (*vaṃdyaḥ*) — worthy of homage; त्वं (*tvaṃ*) — you; धृतेभिः (*dhṛtebhiḥ*) — with offerings of ghee; आहुतः (*āhutaḥ*) — sprinkled; बृहत् (*bṛhat*) — exceedingly; वि रोचसे (*vi rocase*) — you shine in many ways.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, purifier, pure and worthy of homage, you are sprinkled with offerings of ghee and shine exceedingly and in many ways.

**Printed English translation**

Purifier, Agni, holy, adorable, you shine exceedingly when(you are) worshipped with(oblations of) butter.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पावक (*pāvaka*), शुचिः (*śuciḥ*), वंद्य (*vaṃdya*) — Many substances are purified by being burnt in fire. By heating water, all the impurities in it are destroyed and it becomes fit for drinking. Likewise things or places infested with insects, germs and the like can be purified by fire. Because many such purifying works are accomplished by fire, Agni is called पावक (*pāvaka*) — (the passage breaks off at the page end with "पावक (*pāvaka*)-"; it continues on p. 523)

### Printed p. 523

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 523.*

(Continuation of the Special Topics of Ṛk 4:) ... the purifier; शुचिः (*śuciḥ*) — one who is pure by himself; वंद्यः (*vaṃdyaḥ*) — therefore worthy of homage: Agni is praised with these epithets.

अग्ने बृहत् विरोचसे त्वं धृतेभिः आहुतः (*agne bṛhat virocase tvaṃ dhṛtebhiḥ āhutaḥ*) — The meaning of this sentence is plain. When ghee, samidh, havis, firewood and the like are put into the fire and offered, the flames of Agni grow by the ghee-oblation (offering clarified butter) and shine with great brilliance. Therefore, O Agni, you are praised as blazing greatly through the ghee-oblation.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वं नो॑ असि भार॒ताग्ने॑ व॒शाभि॑रु॒क्षभिः॑ ।
> अ॒ष्टाप॑दीभि॒राहु॑तः ॥ ५ ॥

> *tvaṃ no asi bhāratāgne vaśābhirukṣabhiḥ |*
> *aṣṭāpadībhirāhutaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> त्वं । नः । असि । भारत । अग्ने । वशाभिः । उक्ष्षभिः ।
> अष्टापदीभिः । आहुतः ॥ ५ ॥

> *tvaṃ | naḥ | asi | bhārata | agne | vaśābhiḥ | ukṣṣabhiḥ |*
> *aṣṭāpadībhiḥ | āhutaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे भारत ऋत्विजां पुत्रस्थानीयाग्ने नोऽस्मदीयस्त्वं वशाभिर्वंध्याभि-
> र्गोभिः उक्षभिः सेक्तृभिर्बलीवर्दैः अष्टापदीभिर्गर्भिणीभिश्चाहुत आराधितोऽसि ॥

*Gist:* O Agni, son-like to the priests, you are worshipped by us with barren cows, bulls and pregnant cows.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

भारत (*bhārata*) — one who is like a son to the priests; अग्ने (*agne*) — O Agni; नः (*naḥ*) — related to us; त्वं (*tvaṃ*) — you; वशाभिः (*vaśābhiḥ*) — with barren cows; उक्षभिः (*ukṣabhiḥ*) — with bulls; अष्टापदीभिः (*aṣṭāpadībhiḥ*) — with pregnant animals; आहुतः असि (*āhutaḥ asi*) — you are worshipped (by offering these).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, who are like a son to the priests, you who are related to us are worshipped with barren cows, bulls and pregnant animals, that is, by offering these as oblations.

### Printed p. 524

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 7, p. 524.*

**Printed English translation**

Agni, descendant of Bharata, you are entirely ours. when sacrificed to with pregnant cows, with barren cows, or bulls.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

भारत (*bhārata*) — The word भारत (*bhārata*) is read among the names of priests (Ni. 3-19). Here the commentator explains the word भारत (*bhārata*) as ऋत्विजां पुत्रस्थानीय (*ṛtvijāṃ putrasthānīya*), "O Agni, who are like a son to the priests". At the beginning of the sacrifice the priests produce Agni by churning in the fire-sticks (araṇi), so it is proper to say that Agni stands in the place of a son to the priests.

वशाभिः उक्षभिः अष्टापदीभिः आहुतः (*vaśābhiḥ ukṣabhiḥ aṣṭāpadībhiḥ āhutaḥ*) — वशाभिर्वंध्याभिर्गोभिः उक्षभिः सेक्तृभि-र्बलिवर्द्यैः अष्टापदीभिर्गर्भिणीभिश्चाहुतः (*vaśābhirvaṃdhyābhirgobhiḥ ukṣabhiḥ sektṛbhi-rbalivardyaiḥ aṣṭāpadībhirgarbhiṇībhiścāhutaḥ*). Sāyaṇa explains: O Agni, you are worshipped by the barren cows we offer you, by bulls capable of impregnating, and by cows with young. Although animals are of these three kinds, the offering of cows as oblations into the fire is nowhere found. Or the words वशाभिः (*vaśābhiḥ*) and the rest need not mean cows; other animals fit for sacrifice, such as goats, must be what is meant here. The word अष्टापदीभिः (*aṣṭāpadībhiḥ*) means an animal with eight feet: the four natural feet of a pregnant animal and the four feet of the calf in the womb together make eight. Therefore the word अष्टापडीभिः (*aṣṭāpaḍībhiḥ*) (as printed; the earlier form has अष्टापदीभिः (*aṣṭāpadībhiḥ*)) is used to designate a female animal with young.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> द्र्व॑न्नः स॒र्पिरा॑सुतिः प्र॒त्नो होता॒ वरे॑ण्यः ।
> सह॑सस्पु॒त्रो अद्भु॑तः ॥ ६ ॥

> *drvannaḥ sarpirāsutiḥ pratno hotā vareṇyaḥ |*
> *sahasasputro adbhutaḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> द्रुऽन्नः । सर्पिऽआसुतिः । प्रत्नः । होता । वरेण्यः ।
> सहसः । पुत्रः । अद्भुतः ॥ ६ ॥

> *dru 'nnaḥ | sarpi 'āsutiḥ | pratnaḥ | hotā | vareṇyaḥ |*
> *sahasaḥ | putraḥ | adbhutaḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

### Printed p. 525

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 28, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 525.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> द्र्वन्नः समिद्रूपान्नः सर्पिरासुतिः सर्पिरासूयत आसिच्यते यस्मिन् तादृशः
> प्रत्नः पुरातनो होता होमनिष्पादको वरेण्यो वरणीयः सहसस्पुत्रः सहसो
> बलस्य पुत्रस्थानीयः । बलेनोत्पन्नत्वात् । अद्भुत आश्चर्यभूतः । रमणीय
> इत्यर्थः । एवं महानुभावोऽग्निर्मामनुजानात्वित्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* Agni, whose food is fuel, sprinkled with ghee, ancient, the invoker, the choice one, the son of strength, wonderful; may such an Agni favour me.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

द्वन्नः (*dvannaḥ*)—one whose food is fuel. सर्पिरासुतिः (*sarpirāsutiḥ*)—one who is sprinkled with ghee. प्रत्नः (*pratnaḥ*)—the ancient one. होता (*hotā*)—the accomplisher of the oblation. वरेण्यः (*vareṇyaḥ*)—the desired one. सहसस्पुत्रः (*sahasasputraḥ*)—the son of strength [the supplied text reads "ಶಕ್ತಿ ಪುತ್ರನೂ", "son of power"]. अद्भुतः (*adbhutaḥ*)—the wondrous one [printed आश्चरभूतनू (*āścarabhūtanū*), as supplied]. (May such Agni favour us.)

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May Agni, whose food is fuel, who is sprinkled with ghee, the ancient one, the accomplisher of the oblation, desired by all, the son of strength, the wondrous one, favour us.

**Printed English translation**

Feeder upon fuel, to whom butter is offered,(you are) the ancient, the invoker of the gods, the excellent; the son of strength, the wonderful.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

द्र्वन्नः (*drvannaḥ*)—द्रुऽन्नः (*dru 'nnaḥ*). समिद्रूपान्नः (*samidrūpānnaḥ*). Fuel sticks are the food (nourishment) of Agni. The fuel sticks are connected with द्रु (*dru*), a tree; so द्र्वन्नः (*drvannaḥ*) means Agni who has as food the fuel sticks connected with trees.

सर्पिरासुतिः (*sarpirāsutiḥ*)—Although the word सर्पिः (*sarpiḥ*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), the word सर्पिः (*sarpiḥ*) also means ghee. The commentator in the Ṛks

त्वं हि मानुषे जनेऽग्ने सुप्रीत इध्यसे । सुचस्त्वा यंत्यानुषक्सुजात सर्पिरासुते ॥ (*tvaṃ hi mānuṣe jane 'gne suprīta idhyase | sucastvā yaṃtyānuṣaksujāta sarpirāsute ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-21-2)

आ यस्ते सर्पिरासुतेऽग्ने शमस्ति धायसे । (*ā yaste sarpirāsute 'gne śamasti dhāyase |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-7-9)

has explained the word सर्पिरासुते (*sarpirāsute*) as "food mixed with ghee". Since ghee is offered into Agni in sacrifices, that ghee is said to be Agni's food. Therefore Agni is addressed in many places by the word सर्पिरासुति (*sarpirāsuti*).

### Printed p. 526

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 8, p. 526.*

**Special Topics (continued)**

प्रश्नॆ (*praśnè*) [as supplied; the word commented is प्रत्न (*pratna*) of the Ṛk]—the word is read among the names of the ancient (Ni. 3-20); it means one who is old, who has existed from long ago. Not only this sage who praises Agni, but those more ancient than this sage also praised him for a long time. Therefore Agni is said to be of very ancient time.

सहसः पुत्रः (*sahasaḥ putraḥ*)—When fire is produced by churning in the fire-sticks (araṇi), the priests have to use the strength of their arms greatly; hence it is very common in the Ṛgveda to say that Agni is born of strength, the son of strength.

*The eighth sūkta.*

## Sūkta 8 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> वाजयन्निवेति षडृचमष्टमं सूक्तं । सोमाहुतेर्हशब्दानुवृत्तस्याभावा-
> न्मंडलद्रष्टा गृत्समद ऋषिः । अग्निर्देवता । हिशब्दाद्गायत्री छंदः । अंत्यानुष्टुप् ।
> वाजयन्निवांत्यानुष्टुबित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोस्तृतीयसूक्तत्वे-
> नास्योत्तमावर्जितस्य विनियोग उक्तः ॥

*Gist:* *vājayanniva* is the eighth sūkta, six ṛks; seer Gṛtsamada, deity Agni, Gāyatrī with the last Ṛk Anuṣṭubh; used in the Prātaranuvāka and Āśvina-śastra.

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

This sūkta वाजयन्निव (*vājayanniva*) is the eighth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada, the seer of the maṇḍala; the deity is Agni; the metre is Gāyatrī, only the last ṛk being in Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: वाजयन्निवांत्यानुष्टुप् (*vājayannivāṃtyānuṣṭup*). It was said before that the Ṛks of this sūkta (except the last) are employed for the Gāyatrī-metre ṛks in the recitation of the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra mantras.

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 8. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 8. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Gṛtsamada. Deity: Agni. Metre: 1–5 Gāyatrī, 6 Anuṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 527

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 527.*

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वा॒ज॒यन्नि॑व॒ नू रथा॒न्योगा॑ अ॒ग्नेरुप॑ स्तुहि ।
> य॒शस्त॑मस्य मा॒हुषः॑ ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> वाजयन् इव । नु । रथान् । योगान् । अग्नेः । उप । स्तुहि ।
> यशःतमस्य । माहुषः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अंतरात्मन् नु क्षिप्रमग्नेयोगानश्वनुपस्तुहि । कीदृशस्याग्नेः । यशस्त-
> मस्य प्रवृद्धयशसः ॥ मत्त्वर्थो लुप्यते ॥ मीळ्हुषः सेक्तुः फलस्य । ईदृशस्य
> योगान् स्तुहि । होतरिति वा संबोध्यं । स्तुतौ दृष्टांतः । वाजयन्निव । अन्न-
> मिच्छन् पुरुष इव । स यथाश्वान् गमनाय स्तौति तद्वत् । यद्योक्तलक्षणस्याग्ने-
> र्गमनसाधनान्रथान् स्तुहि ॥

*Gist:* O inner self, quickly praise the horses and chariots of Agni, the most renowned and munificent, like one wishing for food who urges his horses.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यशस्तमस्य (*yaśastamasya*)—of him whose fame is spread most widely; मीळ्हुषः (*mīḷhuṣaḥ*)—who gives fruits generously; अग्नेः (*agneḥ*)—of Agni; योगान् (*yogān*)—the horses; रथान् (*rathān*)—the chariots; वाजयन्निव (*vājayanniva*)—as one who desires food urges the horses of his chariot; नु (*nu*)—quickly; उप स्तुहि (*upa stuhi*)—praise.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O inner self, as one who desires food urges the horses of his chariot, quickly praise the horses and chariots of Agni, whose fame is spread most widely and who gives fruits generously.

**Printed English translation**

Praise quickly the yoked chariot of the glorious and munificent Agni, like one who is desirous of food.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk, Sāyaṇa has explained the meaning of the Ṛk by addressing the inner self (antarātman) and saying "praise the horses and chariots of Agni". Here it is not necessary to address the inner self alone. Any of the priests taking part in the sacrifice, or the sacrificer, may be addressed.

### Printed p. 528

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 8, p. 528.*

(Continuation of the Special Topics of Ṛk 1.)

योगान् रथान् (*yogān rathān*)—this can be understood either as "praise the horses and the chariots" or as "praise the chariots to which horses are yoked".

वाजयन्निव (*vājayanniva*)—the word वाजः (*vājaḥ*) is read among the names for food (Ni. 3-9) and among the names for battle (वाजे (*vāje*)) (Ni. 3-9). Here the meaning "food" is taken, and the illustration is given of a man who desires food.

नु (*nu*)—नु इत्येषोऽनेककर्मा । इदं करिष्यतीति हेत्वपदेशे । कथं नु करिष्यतीत्यनुपृष्टे (*nu ityeṣo 'nekakarmā | idaṃ kariṣyatīti hetvapadeśe | kathaṃ nu kariṣyatītyanupṛṣṭe*) (Ni. 1-4). The particle नु (*nu*) has many meanings. In इदं नु करिष्यति (*idaṃ nu kariṣyati*) the word नु (*nu*) has the sense of a reason ("therefore"); in कथं नु करिष्यति (*kathaṃ nu kariṣyati*) it is interrogative. Likewise अथाप्युपमार्थे भवति (*athāpyupamārthe bhavati*)—the word नु (*nu*) is also used when a comparison is indicated. For example:

अक्षो न चक्र्योः शूर बृहन्प्रते मह्ना रिरिचे रोदस्योः । वृक्षस्य नु ते पुरुहूत वया व्यू ३ तयो रुरुहुरिंद्र पूर्विः ॥ (*akṣo na cakryoḥ śūra bṛhanprate mahnā ririce rodasyoḥ | vṛkṣasya nu te puruhūta vayā vyū 3 tayo ruruhuriṃdra pūrviḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-24-3, as printed: "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

The Nirukta-writer has cited this Ṛk and explained नु (*nu*) in the sense of comparison: "like a tree, or like the branches of a tree" (वृक्षस्येव ते पुरुहूत-शाखाः (*vṛkṣasyeva te puruhūta-śākhāḥ*)). Likewise,

नू मर्तो दयते सनिष्यन्यो विष्णव उरुगायाय दाशत् । प्र यः सत्राचा मनस्सा यजात एतावंतं नर्यमाविवासात् ॥ (*nū marto dayate saniṣyanyo viṣṇava urugāyāya dāśat | pra yaḥ satrācā manassā yajāta etāvaṃtaṃ naryamāvivāsāt ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-100-1, as printed: "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

in Ṛks of this kind नु (*nu*) has the sense of क्षिप्रं (*kṣipraṃ*), "quickly"; and in

नू इंद्र शूर स्तवमान ऊती ब्रह्मजूतस्तन्वा नावृधस्व । उप नो वाजान्मिमाह्युप स्तीन्यूयं पात स्वस्तिभिः सदा नः ॥ (*nū iṃdra śūra stavamāna ūtī brahmajūtastanvā nāvṛdhasva | upa no vājānmimāhyupa stīnyūyaṃ pāta svastibhiḥ sadā naḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-11)

and similar Ṛks the sense "now, at once" (अद्य (*adya*)). And, as the Nirukta-writer says, नु इत्येषोऽनेककर्मा (*nu ityeṣo 'nekakarmā*) (Ni. 1-4), the word नु (*nu*) is used in still other senses. In the present Ṛk the meaning is "quickly, soon".

### Printed p. 529

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 529.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः सु॑नी॒थो द॑दा॒शुषे॑ ऽ जु॒र्यो ज॒रय॑न्न॒रिं ।
> चारु॑प्रतीक॒ आहु॑तः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । सुनीथः । ददाशुषे । अजुर्यः । जरयन् । अरिं ।
> चारुप्रतीकः । आहुतः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सुनीथः शोभननयनोऽजुर्योऽजरणीयोऽनभिभाव्यः चारुप्रतीकः शोभ-
> नोपक्रमो योऽस्ति सोऽग्निर्ददाशुषे हविर्दत्तवते यजमानाय तदर्थमरिं
> तस्य शत्रुं जरयन् नाशयन् आहुतः । समंतादाराधितो भवति ॥

*Gist:* Agni, a good leader, unconquerable and of beautiful form, destroys the foe of the sacrificer who offers oblations, being worshipped.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सुनीथः (*sunīthaḥ*) (*sunīthaḥ*)—the exceeding guide; अजुर्यः (*ajuryaḥ*) (*ajuryaḥ*)—the unopposed; चारुप्रतीकः (*cārupratīkaḥ*) (*cārupratīkaḥ*)—of lovely form; यः (*yaḥ*)—the Agni who (is such); ददाशुषे (*dadāśuṣe*)—for the sacrificer who offers oblations; अरिं (*ariṃ*)—his enemy; जरयन् (*jarayan*)—destroying; आहुतः (*āhutaḥ*)—becomes worshipped.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Agni who is the exceeding guide, unopposed and of lovely form becomes worshipped, destroying the enemy of the sacrificer who offers oblations.

**Printed English translation**

Who, sagacious, invincible, and of graceful gait, destroys, when propitiated, his foe, for him who presents(offerings).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सुनीथः—शोभननयनः (*sunīthaḥ—śobhananayanaḥ*). Sāyaṇa has explained it as "a good guide". Since Yāska reads the word सुनीथ (*sunītha*) among the ten names of the praiseworthy (आस्रेमाः, अनेमाः (*āsremāḥ, anemāḥ*) etc.) (Ni. 3-13), सुनीथः (*sunīthaḥ*) may also mean "praiseworthy, worthy of praise". Besides,

### Printed p. 530

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 8, p. 530.*

in the commentary on the mantra त्वामिंद्र मतिभिः सुते सुनिथासो वसूयवः । गोभिः क्राणा अनूषत (*tvāmiṃdra matibhiḥ sute sunithāso vasūyavaḥ | gobhiḥ krāṇā anūṣata*) Durgācārya explains: सुनीथासः सुष्टु ये स्तोतुं शक्नुवंति ते सुनीथाः (*sunīthāsaḥ suṣṭu ye stotuṃ śaknuvaṃti te sunīthāḥ*), "those who are able to praise well" (Ni. 4-19).

अजुर्यः (*ajuryaḥ*)—अजरणीयोऽनभिभाव्यः (*ajaraṇīyo 'nabhibhāvyaḥ*). This word is formed from the root ज्ञ वयोहानौ (*jña vayohānau*) [as printed]; since it has the negative prefix आ (*ā*) (sic, as printed: "ಉಗಸರ್ಗ"), it means "one who is not old", that is, one who, like a youth, is unopposed.

चारुप्रतीकः (*cārupratīkaḥ*)—for the word प्रतीक (*pratīka*) Yāska gives the etymology प्रतीकं-प्रत्यक्तं भवति प्रत्यंचितं प्रतिगतं प्रकाशस्य (*pratīkaṃ-pratyaktaṃ bhavati pratyaṃcitaṃ pratigataṃ prakāśasya*) (Ni. 7-31); hence चारुप्रतीकः (*cārupratīkaḥ*) means one who has a charming form or shape.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> य उ॑ श्रि॒या दमे॒ष्वा दो॒षोषसि॑ प्रश॒स्यते॑ ।
> यस्य॑ व्र॒तं न मीय॑ते ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । ऊं इति । श्रिया । दमेषु । आ । दोषा । उषसि । प्रशस्यते ।
> यस्य । व्रतं । न । मीयते ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> योऽग्निरु श्रिया ज्वालया युक्तः सन् दमेषु आ अगत्य दोषा रात्रिषु
> उषस्यहःसु च प्रशस्यते । स्तूयते । यस्याग्नेव्रतं कर्म न मीयते । न हिंस्यते
> न क्षीयते वा ॥

*Gist:* Agni, endowed with splendour of flame, is praised in the sacrificial houses by night and by day; his rite is never diminished.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

य उ (*ya u*)—that same Agni; श्रिया (*śriyā*)—with the flame full of radiance; दमेषु (*dameṣu*)—in the sacrificial houses; आ (*ā*)—coming; दोषा (*doṣā*)—in the nights; उषसि (*uṣasi*)—in the days; प्रशस्यते (*praśasyate*)—is praised (and); यस्य (*yasya*)—of that same Agni; व्रतं (*vrataṃ*)—the rite; न मीयते (*na mīyate*)—is not diminished.

### Printed p. 531

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 531.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The same Agni described above, filled with radiance and endowed with flame, comes to the sacrificial houses and is praised in the night-times, in the dawn-times and in the days. And the rite of worshipping that Agni is never impaired.

**Printed English translation**

Who, invested with radiance, is glorified, morning and evening, in (our) dwelling: whose worship is never neglected.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> आ यः स्व१॒॑र्ण भा॒नुना॑ चि॒त्रो वि॒भात्य॒र्चिषा॑ ।
> अं॒जा॒नो अ॒जरै॑र॒भि ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आ । यः । स्वः । न । भानुना । चित्रः । विभाति । अर्चिषा ।
> अंजानः । अजरैः । अभि ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> योऽग्निश्चित्रिश्चायनीयो नानावर्णो वार्चिषा ज्वालया रश्मिसमूहेना-
> विभाति । सर्वतो भासते । तत्र दृष्टांतः । स्वर्भानुना । किरणेन सूर्य इव । स
> यथा विभाति तद्वत् । किं कुर्वन् । अजरैर्नित्यैर्ज्वालासमूहैरभि सर्वतोऽंजानो
> व्यंजयन् प्रकाशयन् ॥

*Gist:* Agni of manifold hues shines all round with his rays and unageing flames, like the sun with its beams.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

स्वः (*svaḥ*)—the sun; भानुना न (*bhānunā na*)—as he shines with his rays; यः (*yaḥ*)—that same Agni who; चित्रः (*citraḥ*)—being of manifold hues; अर्चिषा (*arciṣā*)—and endowed with masses of rays; अजरैः (*ajaraiḥ*)—with his everlasting flames; अभि (*abhi*)—all round; अंजानः (*aṃjānaḥ*)—spreading splendour; आ विभाति (*ā vibhāti*)—shines fully.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

As the sun shines with his rays, Agni, of manifold hues and endowed with masses of rays, shines fully, spreading splendour all round with his everlasting flames.

### Printed p. 532

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 8, p. 532.*

**Printed English translation**

Who, many-tinted, shines with radiance, like the sun with(his) lustre, spreading light over(all) by his imperishable(flames),

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

स्वः न भानुना (*svaḥ na bhānunā*)—the meaning is "as the sun shines with his rays". The word स्वः (*svaḥ*) is read among the common names (sādhāraṇa-nāmāni), स्वः पृश्निः नाकः (*svaḥ pṛśniḥ nākaḥ*) and so on. साधारणान्युत्तराणि षट् दिवश्चादित्यस्य च (*sādhāraṇānyuttarāṇi ṣaṭ divaścādityasya ca*) (Ni. 2-13): that is, the six words स्वः पृश्निः नाकः गौः विष्टपं नभः (*svaḥ pṛśniḥ nākaḥ gauḥ viṣṭapaṃ nabhaḥ*) are common designations, both as synonyms of the heavenly world and as referring to the sun; so the Nirukta-kāra states. Then, explaining what the root of the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) is in the sense of the sun, and how the word is derived, he says:

स्वरादित्यो भवति सु अरणः सु ईरणः स्वृतो रसान् स्वृतो भासं ज्योतिषां । स्वृतो भासेति वा (*svarādityo bhavati su araṇaḥ su īraṇaḥ svṛto rasān svṛto bhāsaṃ jyotiṣāṃ | svṛto bhāseti vā*) (Ni. 2-14).

The Nirukta-kāra states that the sun has this name because he is very far-travelling, or because he thoroughly scatters and destroys darkness, or because he enters into the juice (rasa) and becomes inward with it, or because he spreads his light in the orb of radiance and becomes one with it, or because he pervades the universe in the form of light; and for this very reason (एतेन द्यौर्वाख्याता (*etena dyaurvākhyātā*)) the heavenly world also has the name स्वः (*svaḥ*).

स्वरादित्यो भवति स एनानि सारयति (*svarādityo bhavati sa enāni sārayati*) (Ni. 5-4).

Because he makes the days move, too, the sun is called स्वः (*svaḥ*). Where the word is used in the Ṛgveda:

प्रत्यज् देवानां विशः प्रत्यज्जुदेषि मानुषान् । प्रत्यज् विश्वं स्वर्दृशे (*pratyaj devānāṃ viśaḥ pratyajjudeṣi mānuṣān | pratyaj viśvaṃ svardṛśe*) (Ma. Saṃ. 1-50-5) [as printed]

in such Ṛks the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) means the heavenly world (svarga).

त्मना वहंतो दुरो व्यृण्वन्नवंत विश्वे स्व १ र्दृशीके (*tmanā vahaṃto duro vyṛṇvannavaṃta viśve sva 1 rdṛśīke*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-69-10)

in such Ṛks the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) means "in the sky, in the atmosphere" (नभसि (*nabhasi*)).

दानो अग्ने बृहतो दाः सहस्रिणो दुरो न वाजं श्रुत्या अपा वृधि । प्राची द्यावापृथिवी ब्रह्मणा कृधि स्व १ र्ण शुक्रमुषसो वि दिम्युतुः (*dāno agne bṛhato dāḥ sahasriṇo duro na vājaṃ śrutyā apā vṛdhi | prācī dyāvāpṛthivī brahmaṇā kṛdhi sva 1 rṇa śukramuṣaso vi dimyutuḥ*) (Pu. Saṃ. 2-2-7) [as printed]

### Printed p. 533

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 533.*

in such Ṛks the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) has been explained as the sun. In the same way the words स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) and the like, formed with suffixes such as वत् (*vat*), are also to be explained according to the context. For example:

या इंद्र भुज आभरः स्वर्ना असुरेभ्यः । स्तोतारमिन्मघवन्नस्य वर्धय ये च त्वे व्यक्तबर्हिषः (*yā iṃdra bhuja ābharaḥ svarnā asurebhyaḥ | stotāraminmaghavannasya vardhaya ye ca tve vyaktabarhiṣaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-97-1)

In this Ṛk—सुखवान्, स्वर्गवान् ना अथवा स्वशब्दः सर्वपर्यायः । सर्वं भूतजातं आत्मन एवोत्पन्नत्वात्तद्वान् (*sukhavān, svargavān nā athavā svaśabdaḥ sarvaparyāyaḥ | sarvaṃ bhūtajātaṃ ātmana evotpannatvāttadvān*). Since the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) has the meanings happiness, heaven or "all", स्वर्वान् (*svarvān*) means one who has happiness, one who dwells in heaven, or one who has obtained everything. When it is an epithet of Indra, all these meanings fit; and again:

तं शिशीता स्वध्वरं सत्यं सत्वानमृत्वियं । उतो नु चिद्य ओहत आंडा शुष्णस्य भेदत्यजैः स्वर्वतीरपो नभंतामन्यके समे (*taṃ śiśītā svadhvaraṃ satyaṃ satvānamṛtviyaṃ | uto nu cidya ohata āṃḍā śuṣṇasya bhedatyajaiḥ svarvatīrapo nabhaṃtāmanyake same*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-40-11)

In such passages, when सर्वतीः अपः (*sarvatīḥ apaḥ*) [sic, as printed] is an epithet of water, दिव्यानि (*divyāni*) means water produced in the heavenly world. This epithet also describes the qualities of other deities such as Agni.

ज्योतिष्मतीमदितिं धारयत् क्षितिं स्वर्वतीमा सचेते दिवे दिवे जागृवांसा दिवेदिवे (*jyotiṣmatīmaditiṃ dhārayat kṣitiṃ svarvatīmā sacete dive dive jāgṛvāṃsā divedive*) (Ma. Saṃ. 1-136-3) [as printed]

Here स्वर्णती (*svarṇatī*) [as printed] is an epithet of Aditi. Here it is explained as यज्ञनिष्पत्तिद्दारा स्वर्गवतीं (*yajñaniṣpattiddārā svargavatīṃ*) [as printed], as if so interpreted.

आ ते शुष्मो वृषभ एतु पश्चादोत्तरादधराद पुरस्तात् । आ विश्वतो अभि समेत्वर्वाजिंद्र द्युम्नं स्वर्वद्धेह्यस्मे (*ā te śuṣmo vṛṣabha etu paścādottarādadharāda purastāt | ā viśvato abhi sametvarvājiṃdra dyumnaṃ svarvaddhehyasme*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-19-9)

In such Ṛks the meaning सुखयुक्तं धनं (*sukhayuktaṃ dhanaṃ*) ("wealth accompanied by happiness") is also indicated. In the present Ṛk the meaning is the sun.

भानुना (*bhānunā*)—since the word भानुः (*bhānuḥ*) is read among the twelve names of day (ahar-nāmāni), वस्तोः द्यौः (*vastoḥ dyauḥ*) and so on, it means the shining day. भानुः भासते हैतत् ज्योतिषा (*bhānuḥ bhāsate haitat jyotiṣā*): because it shines by its own light, it is explained here as the rays of the sun.

### Printed p. 534

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 8, p. 534.*

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अत्रि॒मनु॑ स्व॒राज्य॑म॒ग्निमु॒क्थानि॑ वावृधुः ।
> विश्वा॒ अधि॒ श्रियो॑ दधे ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अत्रिं । अनु । स्वराज्यं । अग्निं । उक्तानि । ववृधुः ।
> विश्वाः । अधि । श्रियः । दधे ॥ ५ ॥

> *atriṃ | anu | svarājyaṃ | agniṃ | uktāni | vavṛdhuḥ |*
> *viśvāḥ | adhi | śriyaḥ | dadhe ‖ 5 ‖*

(Note: the Saṃhitā as supplied reads वावृधुः (*vāvṛdhuḥ*) and उक्थानि (*ukthāni*), while the Pada reads ववृधुः (*vavṛdhuḥ*) and उक्तानि (*uktāni*); the two readings differ in the supplied text and are left as supplied.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अत्रिं शत्रूणामन्नानां ना भक्षकं स्वराज्यं स्वयमेव राजमानं स्वराज्य-
> मनु अभिलक्ष्य वाग्निमुक्थानि शस्त्राणि ववृधुः । वर्धयंति । स चाग्निर्विश्वाः सर्वाः
> श्रियोऽधि दधे धारयति ॥

*Gist:* The hymns of praise (uktha-śastras) magnify Agni, the devourer of enemies, self-shining; he holds all glories.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अत्रिं (*atriṃ*)—the devourer of enemies, or one who eats food; स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*)—who shines forth by himself as a perfect lord; अग्निं अनु (*agniṃ anu*)—recited with Agni as the object; उक्तानि (*uktāni*)—our śastra-mantras; ववृधुः (*vavṛdhuḥ*)—(they) make (him) grow strong; विश्वाः श्रियः (*viśvāḥ śriyaḥ*)—all wealths; अधि दधे (*adhi dadhe*)—he possesses.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Our śastra-mantras, recited in praise of Agni, the devourer of enemies, who shines forth by himself as a perfect lord, make him grow strong. He possesses all wealths.

**Printed English translation**

Our praises have increased the self-irradiating devouring Agni: he is possessed of all glory.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अत्रिं (*atriṃ*)—For this word, formed from the root अद भक्षणे (*ada bhakṣaṇe*) ("ad", to eat), the commentator has given two kinds of meaning. अत्रिं (*atriṃ*)—one who eats (burns) and destroys enemies is अत्रिः (*atriḥ*): because he devours, or burns up and destroys, enemies he is called Atri; or

### Printed p. 535

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5, Varga 29, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 535.*

according to the Śruti sentence अद्यत्तेऽत्ति च भूतानि । तस्मादन्नं तदुच्यत (*adyatte 'tti ca bhūtāni | tasmādannaṃ taducyata*) ("it is eaten and it eats beings; therefore it is called food"), a thing fit to be eaten is called food. Because he eats (consumes) such food, or the food in the form of the oblation, he is called Atri: thus two kinds of meaning are stated. Hence the sense is that Agni is the destroyer of enemies, or the eater of food in the form of the oblation.

स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*)—स्वयमेव राजमानं स्वराज्यमनु अभिलक्ष्य वा (*svayameva rājamānaṃ svarājyamanu abhilakṣya vā*). Two meanings are explained: Agni shines by his own power, without the help of another shining thing; or Agni is the lord who gives wealth and the like to people and to creatures.

उक्तानि (*uktāni*)—The word उक्त्य (*uktya*) is read among the names of praiseworthy things (Ni. 3-13). The meaning is hymns and the like recited in description of the qualities of the deities.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒ग्नेरिंद्र॑स्य॒ सोम॑स्य दे॒वाना॑मू॒तिभि॑र्व॒यं ।
> अरि॑ष्यंतः सचेमह्य॒भि ष्या॑म पृतन्य॒तः ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अग्नेः । इंद्रस्य । सोमस्य । देवानां । ऊतिभिः । वयं ।
> अरिष्यंतः । सचेमहि । अभि । स्याम । पृतन्यतः ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अरिष्यंतः केनाप्यहिंस्यमाना वयमग्नेरिंद्रस्य सोमस्य देवानामन्येषा-
> मप्यूतिभी रक्षणाभिः सचेमहि । युक्ता भवेम । तथा रक्षायुक्ता वयं पृतन्यतः
> पृतनामात्मन इच्छतः शत्रूनभिष्याम । अभिभवेम ॥ पृतनाशब्दात्कचि कव्यध्वरपृतन-
> स्यर्चि लोप इत्यवर्णलोपः ॥

> *ariṣyaṃtaḥ kenāpyahiṃsyamānā vayamagneriṃdrasya somasya devānāmanyeṣā-*
> *mapyūtibhī rakṣaṇābhiḥ sacemahi | yuktā bhavema | tathā rakṣāyuktā vayaṃ pṛtanyataḥ*
> *pṛtanāmātmana icchataḥ śatrūnabhiṣyāma | abhibhavema ‖ pṛtanāśabdātkaci kavyadhvarapṛtana-*
> *syarci lopa ityavarṇalopaḥ ‖*

*Gist:* Unharmed, may we be joined with the protections of Agni, Indra, Soma and the gods, and overcome enemies who seek battle; a grammatical note on the elision of the vowel in पृतन (*pṛtana*) follows.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अरिष्यंतः (*ariṣyaṃtaḥ*)—not harmed by anyone; वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we; अग्नेः (*agneḥ*)—of Agni; इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra; सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of Soma (and); देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of all the other gods; ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*)—with the protections

### Printed p. 536

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 7 [as printed], p. 536.*

; सचेमहि (*sacemahi*)—may we be united (and likewise); पृतन्यतः (*pṛtanyataḥ*)—the enemies who have armies; अभिष्याम (*abhiṣyāma*)—may we obtain the power to defeat (them).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May we, unharmed by anyone, be united with the protections of Agni, Indra and Soma, and likewise with the protections of all the other gods; and may we obtain the power to defeat the enemies who have obtained exceedingly great military strength.

**Printed English translation**

May we, uninjured, be secure in the protection of Agni, Indra, Soma, and the gods, and, confident in battle, overcome(our enemies).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk, which forms the conclusion of the sūkta, protection is sought and prayed for from Agni, Indra, Soma and all the other gods.

पृतन्यतः (*pṛtanyataḥ*)—Since the word प्रतना (*pratanā*) [sic, as printed; the Ṛk has पृतना (*pṛtanā*)] is read among the forty-six names of battle beginning with रणः (*raṇaḥ*) (Ni. 3-9), पृतना (*pṛtanā*) means war. पृतन्यतः (*pṛtanyataḥ*) means enemies who, accompanied by armies, intend to make war.

The eighth sūkta is ended.

**Adhyāya colophon (Sanskrit; Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 5):**

> वेदार्थस्य प्रकाशेन तमो हार्दं निवारयन् ।
> पुमर्थांश्च तुरो देयाद्विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरः ॥
> इति श्रीमद्राजाधिराजपरमेश्वरवैदिकमार्गप्रवर्तक श्रीवीरबुक्कभूपालसाम्राज-
> धुरंधरेण सायणाचार्येण विरचिते माधवीये वेदार्थप्रकाशे
> ऋक्संहिताभाष्ये द्वितीयाष्टके पंचमोऽध्यायः
> ॥ समाप्तः ॥ ॥ समाप्तः ॥

> *vedārthasya prakāśena tamo hārdaṃ nivārayan |*
> *pumarthāṃśca turo deyādvidyātīrthamaheśvaraḥ ‖*
> *iti śrīmadrājādhirājaparameśvaravaidikamārgapravartaka śrīvīrabukkabhūpālasāmrāja-*
> *dhuraṃdhareṇa sāyaṇācāryeṇa viracite mādhavīye vedārthaprakāśe*
> *ṛksaṃhitābhāṣye dvitīyāṣṭake paṃcamo 'dhyāyaḥ*
> *‖ samāptaḥ ‖ ‖ samāptaḥ ‖*

Closing line (Kannada): "Here ends the fifth adhyāya, in the second aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's commentary and a Kannada translation." The Sanskrit verses give: dispelling the darkness of the heart by the light of the meaning of the Veda, may Vidyātīrtha-Maheśvara grant the four human aims; the colophon names Sāyaṇācārya as author of the Mādhavīya Vedārthaprakāśa on the Ṛk-saṃhitā.

Then a verse of forgiveness (Sanskrit):

> यदक्षरपदभ्रष्टं मात्राहीनं तु यद्भवेत् ।
> तत्सर्वं क्षम्यतां देव वागीश्वर ननोऽस्तु ते ॥
> ॥ शुभं भूयात् ॥ ॥ मंगळं ॥

> *yadakṣarapadabhraṣṭaṃ mātrāhīnaṃ tu yadbhavet |*
> *tatsarvaṃ kṣamyatāṃ deva vāgīśvara nano 'stu te ‖*
> *‖ śubhaṃ bhūyāt ‖ ‖ maṃgaḷaṃ ‖*

The next printed leaf opens the sixth adhyāya. Invocation verses (to Gaṇapati, to Vidyātīrtha-Maheśvara, a verse stating that the fifth adhyāya of the middle of the second aṣṭaka has been explained by Sāyaṇa and the sixth will be explained, and a verse in praise of the Veda):

> ॥ श्री ॥ ॥ श्री महागणाधिपतये नमः ॥
> वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे
> यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥
> यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योखिलं जगत्
> निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥
> द्वितीयाष्टकमध्यस्थः पंचमोध्याय आदरत् ।
> व्याख्यातः सायणार्येण षष्ठश्च व्याकरिष्यते ॥
> वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्याया सदाशिवः ।
> तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥

> *‖ śrī ‖ ‖ śrī mahāgaṇādhipataye namaḥ ‖*
> *vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame*
> *yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*
> *yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyokhilaṃ jagat*
> *nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*
> *dvitīyāṣṭakamadhyasthaḥ paṃcamodhyāya ādarat |*
> *vyākhyātaḥ sāyaṇāryeṇa ṣaṣṭhaśca vyākariṣyate ‖*
> *vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyā sadāśivaḥ |*
> *tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*

Title: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's commentary; Second Aṣṭaka, Sixth Adhyāya ("In the second aṣṭaka, the sixth adhyāya"); Maṇḍala 2, Sūktas 9–23.

> ॥ द्वितीयाष्टके षष्ठोऽध्यायः ॥

> *‖ dvitīyāṣṭake ṣaṣṭho 'dhyāyaḥ ‖*

## Sūkta 9 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> नि होतेति षडृचं नवमं सूक्तं गृत्समदस्यार्षं नि होतेत्यनुक्रांत-
> त्वात् । अनुक्तत्वात् त्रिष्टुप् । मंडलादिपरिभाषयाग्निर्देवता ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाशिन-
> शस्त्रयोराग्नेये क्रत् त्रैष्टुभे छंदस्यस्य विनियोगः । अथैतस्या इति खंडे
> सूत्रितं । नि होता होतृषदन इति सूक्ते । आ. ४-१३ । इति ॥ अग्निप्रणयॆन आद्ये
> ऋचौ विनियुक्ते । पंचम्यां पूर्णमास्यामिति खंडे सूत्रितं । नि होता होतृ-
> षदन इति द्वे । आ. २-१७ ॥

*Gist:* *ni hotā* is the ninth sūkta, six ṛks, by Gṛtsamada, deity Agni, Triṣṭubh; used in the Prātaranuvāka and the Āgneya-kratu; the first two ṛks are used in the agni-praṇayana, and the Pūrṇamāsa sūtra uses two ṛks.

### Printed p. 538

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 538.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

This sūkta नि होता (*ni hotā*) is the ninth sūkta in the first anuvāka of the second maṇḍala. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada, the seer of the maṇḍala; the deity is Agni; the metre is Triṣṭubh. It is used at the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka and Aśvina-śastra mantras, as set out by the sūtra नि होता होतृषदन इति सूक्ते (*ni hotā hotṛṣadana iti sūkte*) in the section beginning अथैतस्या (*athaitasyā*) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 4-13); and the first two ṛks of this sūkta are used in the rite called agni-praṇayana, an auxiliary of the sacrifice, as set out by the sūtra नि होता होतृषदन इति द्वे (*ni hotā hotṛṣadana iti dve*) in the section beginning पंचम्यां पौण्यमास्यां (*paṃcamyāṃ pauṇyamāsyāṃ*) of the same Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 2-17).

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 9. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Gṛtsamada (printed गृत्समदॆः (*gṛtsamadèḥ*) in the supplied text). Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh. A stray word कंठीरव (*kaṃṭhīrava*) follows the heading in the supplied text, apparently not part of the sūkta.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> नि होता॑ होतृ॒षद॑ने॒ विदा॑नस्त्वे॒षो दी॑दि॒वा अ॑सदत्सु॒दक्षः॑ ।
> अद॑ब्धव्रतप्रमति॒र्वसि॑ष्ठः सहस्रंभ॒रः शुचि॑जिह्वो अ॒ग्निः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> नि । होता । होतृसदने । विदानः । त्वेषः । दीदिवान् । असदत् । सुदक्षः ।
> अदब्धव्रतप्रमतिः । वसिष्ठः । सहस्रंभरः । शुचिजिह्वः । अग्निः ॥ १ ॥

> *ni | hotā | hotṛsadane | vidānaḥ | tveṣaḥ | dīdivān | asadat | sudakṣaḥ |*
> *adabdhavratapramatiḥ | vasiṣṭhaḥ | sahasraṃbharaḥ | śucijihvaḥ | agniḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

(Note: the Pada as supplied reads होतृसदने (*hotṛsadane*) where the Saṃhitā has होतृषदने (*hotṛṣadane*); left as supplied.)

### Printed p. 539

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 539.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> गृत्समदः स्तौति । होता देवानामाह्वाता विदानो विद्वान् त्वेषः
> प्रवृद्दो दीदिवान् दीप्यमानः सुदक्षः शोभनबलोऽदब्बव्रतप्रमतिः । अदब्बान्य-
> हिंसितानि व्रतानि कर्माणि यस्याः सादब्बव्रता प्रमतिः प्रकृष्टा बुद्धिर्यस्य स
> तथोक्तः । वसिष्ठः सर्वस्य वासयितृतमः ॥ वासयतेस्तृजंतस्यॆष्ठनि रूपं ॥
> सहस्रंभरः सहस्रस्यानेकविधस्य भर्ता । विहरणेन नानाविधरूपस्य धारक इत्यर्थः ।
> यद्वा सहस्रस्य सर्वस्य भर्ता । तथा च वाजसनेयं । सर्वं वै सहस्रं सर्वं-
> भर इत्येव तदाहेति । शुचिजिह्वः । शुचिर्दिप्ता जिह्वा ज्वाला यस्य स तथोक्तः ।
> एवंभूतोऽग्निर्होतृषदने । होतात्र सीदतीति होतृषदनमुत्तरा वेदी ॥
> अधिकरणे ल्युट् ॥ तत्र न्यसदत् । नितरां सीदतु ॥ उक्तार्थे ब्राह्मणं । नि
> होता होतृषदने विदाद इत्यग्निर्वै देवानां होता तस्यैतद्दोतृषदनं
> यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिस्वेषो दीदिवा असदत्सुदक्ष इत्यासन्नो हि स तर्हि भवत्य-
> दब्बव्रतप्रमतिर्वसिष्ठ! इत्यग्निर्वै देवानां वसिष्ठः सहस्रंभरः शुचिजिह्वो अग्निरित्येषा
> ह वा अस्य सहस्रंभरता यदेनमेकं संतं बहुधा विहरंति । ऐ. ब्रा. १. २८. । इति ॥

*Gist:* Gṛtsamada praises Agni, the invoker of the gods, wise, resplendent, of fair strength and unharmed rites, the best dweller, bearer of a thousand, bright-tongued; may he sit on the northern altar. A Brāhmaṇa passage (Ai. Brā. 1. 28) is cited.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

होता (*hotā*)—he who calls the gods to the sacrifice; विदानः (*vidānaḥ*)—the wise one; त्वेषः (*tveṣaḥ*)—the grown, the mighty; दीदिवान् (*dīdivān*)—the shining one; सुदक्षः (*sudakṣaḥ*)—he who has excellent strength; अदब्धव्रतप्रमतिः (*adabdhavratapramatiḥ*)—he who has the excellent knowledge that his rite cannot be harmed; वसिष्ठः (*vasiṣṭhaḥ*)—he who has the best dwelling-place; सहस्रंभरः (*sahasraṃbharaḥ*)—he who bears thousands of forms; शुचिजिह्वः (*śucijihvaḥ*)—he who has a tongue that is shining and of the form of flame; अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni; होतृषदने (*hotṛṣadane*)—in the northern altar, the seat of the hotṛ; नि असदत् (*ni asadat*)—may he sit down.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May Agni, who calls the gods to the sacrifice, the wise, the grown, the shining, the one of excellent strength, the one who knows that his rites cannot be harmed, the one of the best dwelling-place, the bearer of thousands of forms, the one whose tongue is bright and flame-like, sit down on the northern altar, the seat of the hotṛ.

**Printed English translation**
