दानवस्य (*dānavasya*) (Dānavasya)—Dānu means the daughter of Danu; the offspring belonging to the demon race born to the sage Kaśyapa from his wife named Danu are called Dānavas. That Vṛtra's mother belonged to that Dānava race is stated in the Ṛk

उत्तरा सूरधरः पुत्र आसीद्दानुः शये सहवत्सा न धेनुः (*uttarā sūradharaḥ putra āsīddānuḥ śaye sahavatsā na dhenuḥ*) (Ma. Saṃ. 1-32-9, as supplied)

and Vṛtra, the son of Dānu (Danu), is here called by the name Dānava.

Vṛtra's mother is named Dānu, and she is compared to a cow (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-32-9). This same word (dānu) in the neuter gender means a stream, and in the feminine heavenly water (the passage runs on to the next page)

### Printed p. 604

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11, p. 604.*

denotes. These two words may be one and the same. This same word is used in the masculine for the Asura Vṛtra; probably it is that name because he was the son of Dānu (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-12-11; 4-30-7). In the same way the name is given to Vṛtra, who is like an Aurṇavābha (a spider) (2-11-18), and to the seven Rākṣasas slain by Indra (10-120-6). The word dānava, formed from the word dānu (giving the sense "son of Dānu"), denotes the Rākṣasa who fought with Indra (probably the Asura Vṛtra). It is said in the present Ṛk that Indra removed all the māyās (tricks) of the deceitful Dānava. And it is said that he struck down the roaring Dānava (5-29-4), and afterwards released the waters (5-32-1). In this sūkta are described, with slight differences, the very matters relating to the slaying of Vṛtra that are told in the thirty-second sūkta of the first Maṇḍala (the sūkta beginning इंद्रस्यनु वीर्याणि (*iṃdrasyanu vīryāṇi*)).

अरोरवीत् वृष्णो अस्य वज्रो अमानुषं (*aroravīt vṛṣṇo asya vajro amānuṣaṃ*) (Aroravīt vṛṣṇo asya vajro amānuṣaṃ)—For the word अमानुषं (*amānuṣaṃ*) (amānuṣaṃ) here the bhāṣyakāra gives two meanings, in the words यद्यदा अमानुषं मनुष्याणां रहितं । यद्वामानुषोऽहं न भवामीत्येवं मन्यमानं तमसुरं (*yadyadā amānuṣaṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ rahitaṃ | yadvāmānuṣo 'haṃ na bhavāmītyevaṃ manyamānaṃ tamasuraṃ*): that is, the Asura Vṛtra who is devoid of men (human beings), or who, thinking "I am not a mere insignificant man", struts about in arrogance. The chief sense of the sentence is that when Indra was slaying Vṛtra he made, with his thunderbolt, an exceedingly terrible sound (thunder). It is stated here that the lightning, which flashes very brightly and suddenly in the sky while rain is falling, is Indra's thunderbolt, and that since the thunder and rumbling, full of great noise, are heard within an extremely short time after the lightning is seen, that sound arose from Indra's use of the thunderbolt.

नि मायिनः दानवस्य मायाः अपादयत् पपिवान् सुतस्य (*ni māyinaḥ dānavasya māyāḥ apādayat papivān sutasya*) (Ni māyinaḥ dānavasya māyāḥ apādayat papivān sutasya)—In this sentence of the second half of the Ṛk it is stated that Indra drank Soma and destroyed the deceits of his enemies. It is said here and there that in such deeds as the slaying of Vṛtra also, Indra drank Soma and increased his strength and power:

त्रिकद्रुकेष्वपिबत्सुतस्यास्य मदे अहिमिंद्रो जघान (*trikadrukeṣvapibatsutasyāsya made ahimiṃdro jaghāna*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-15-1)

अस्य मंदानो मध्यो वज्रहस्तोऽहिमिंद्रो अर्णोवृतं वि वृश्चत् (*asya maṃdāno madhyo vajrahasto 'himiṃdro arṇovṛtaṃ vi vṛścat*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-19-2)

उतो न्व १ स्य पपिवांसमिंद्रं न कश्चन सहत आहवेषु । अयं स्वादुरिह मदिष्ट आस यस्येंद्रो वृत्रहत्ये ममाद (*uto nva 1 sya papivāṃsamiṃdraṃ na kaścana sahata āhaveṣu | ayaṃ svāduriha madiṣṭa āsa yasyeṃdro vṛtrahatye mamāda*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-1, 2)

In these and other Ṛks the idea is described that the Soma drunk by Indra gave great help in the work of slaying Vṛtra.

### Printed p. 605

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 605.*

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> पिबा॑पि॒बेदिं॑द्र शूर॒ सोमं॒ मंदं॑तु त्वा मु॒ंदिनः॑ सु॒तासः॑।
> पृ॒णंत॑स्ते कु॒क्षी व॑र्धयंत्वि॒त्था सु॒तः पौ॒र इंद्र॑माव ॥११॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> पिबपिब । इत् । इंद्र । शूर । सोमं । मंदंतु । त्वा । मंदिनः । सुतासः ।
> पृणंतः । ते । कुक्षी इति । वर्धयंतु । इत्था । सुतः । पौरः । इंद्रं । आव ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> शूर बलवन् हे इंद्र पिब पिबेत् । अभिषुतमिमं सोमं पिबैव । आदरा-
> र्थैषा वीप्सा । मंदिनो मदकराः सुतास्तेऽभिषुताः सोमास्त्वा मंदंतु । त्वां
> मादयंतु ॥ मदि स्तुत्यादिषु । व्यत्ययेन परस्मैपदं ॥ ते सोमास्ते तव कुक्षि
> उदरस्योभौ पार्श्वा पृणंतः पूरयंतः संतस्त्वां वर्धयंतु । इत्थानेन प्रकारेण
> पौर उदरपूरकः सुतोऽभिषुतः सोम इंद्रं त्वामाव । तर्पयतु ॥ अवतेस्तर्प-
> णार्थस्य लिटि रूपं ॥

*Gist: Indra is urged to drink the pressed Soma; the exhilarating juices will please him and fill his flanks, and the Soma that fills the belly will satisfy him; āva is a perfect form of the root meaning "to satisfy".*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Śūra—mighty. Indra—O Indra. Somaṃ—the Soma juice. Pibapibet [sic, as supplied; the Pada has pibapiba, it]—drink fully. Mandinaḥ—the gladdening. Sutāsaḥ—and pressed (Soma juices). Tvā—you. Mandantu—may they satisfy (and). Te—your. Kukṣī—the two flanks of the belly. Hṛṇantaḥ [sic, "ಹೃಣಂತಃ" as supplied; the Pada has pṛṇantaḥ]—filling. Vardhayentu [sic, as supplied]—may they cause to grow. Itthā—in this manner. Pauraḥ—the belly-filling. Sutaḥ—the pressed. Soma. Indraṃ—you, the lord. Āva—may it satisfy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O mighty Indra, drink fully this offered Soma juice. May the gladdening, pressed Soma juices satisfy you, and, filling the two flanks of your belly, make you grow. In this manner may the Soma juice that fills the belly, being pressed, satisfy you, the lord.

### Printed p. 606

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11, p. 606.*

**Printed English translation**

Drink, hero, Indra, drink the Soma: may the exhilarating juices delight you:may they, distending your flanks, augment(your prowess), and in this manner may the replenishing effused libation satisfy Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

This Ṛk speaks of Indra's drinking of Soma. In the Ṛgveda, in inviting the gods to drink Soma, no other god has been called as much as Indra. On the gods' drinking of Soma:

माद्यंतीव वै मध्यंदिने देवताः समेव तृतीयसवने मादयंते (*mādyaṃtīva vai madhyaṃdine devatāḥ sameva tṛtīyasavane mādayaṃte*) (Ai. Brā. 28-3)

that is, at the midday pressing (at noon) the gods drink Soma; and at the third pressing (in the evening) the gods drink Soma to excess and become intoxicated (gladdened). There are descriptions, in Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-4-8; 1-32-3; 1-84-1; 2-15-1; 2-15-2; 2-19-1; 2-21-1; 3-36-3; 3-40-1; 3-42-1; 6-23-1, 5, 6; 6-27-1; 6-29-4; 7-22-1; 7-29-1; 8-3-1; 8-81-5, 6; 10-104-1, of the invitation to Indra to drink Soma, or of Indra being gladdened (or having been gladdened) by the drinking of Soma.

आर्वाजेहि सोमकामं त्वाहुरयं सुतस्तस्य पिबा मदाय । उरुव्यचा जठर आ वृषस्व पितेव नः शृणुहि हूयमानः (*ārvājehi somakāmaṃ tvāhurayaṃ sutastasya pibā madāya | uruvyacā jaṭhara ā vṛṣasva piteva naḥ śṛṇuhi hūyamānaḥ*) (Ma. Saṃ. 1-104-9 [sic, "ಮ. ಸಂ." as supplied for Ṛ. Saṃ.])

In this Ṛk the extreme eagerness of Indra for Soma-drinking is described thus: "O Indra, the wise say that Soma juice is very dear to you. We have pressed this Soma juice for you. Therefore come toward us and drink that Soma juice for your delight. Since your limbs are broad and far-spreading, distend your belly and fill it with that juice. As a father hears his children's words, so hear our call when we invite you with praise." And:

इंद्र इत्सोमपा एक इंद्रः सुतपा विश्वायुः । अंतर्दे नान्मर्त्यांश्च (*iṃdra itsomapā eka iṃdraḥ sutapā viśvāyuḥ | aṃtarde nānmartyāṃśca*) (Mi. Saṃ. 8-2-4 [sic, "ಮಿ. ಸಂ." as supplied for Ṛ. Saṃ.])

In this Ṛk it is also said that among gods and among men it is Indra who drinks Soma to the greatest degree.

### Printed p. 607

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 607.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

(continued from the previous page) In the Ṛks

अद्रोघ सत्यं तव तन्महित्वं सद्यो यज्जातो अपिबो ह सोमं । (*adrogha satyaṃ tava tanmahitvaṃ sadyo yajjāto apibo ha somaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-32-9)

त्वं सद्यो अपिबो जात इंद्र मदाय सोमं परमे व्योमन् । (*tvaṃ sadyo apibo jāta iṃdra madāya somaṃ parame vyoman |*) (Ma. Saṃ. 3-32-10 [sic, "ಮ. ಸಂ." as supplied for Ṛ. Saṃ.])

it is said: "O Indra, you drank Soma as soon as you were born. Is this not your greatness?" In the Ṛk

एकया प्रतिधापिबत्साकं सरांसि त्रिंशतं । इंद्रः सोमस्य काणुका ॥ (*ekayā pratidhāpibatsākaṃ sarāṃsi triṃśataṃ | iṃdraḥ somasya kāṇukā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-77-4)

it is said that Indra drank, in a single draught, Soma juice filling thirty vessels. Speaking of the explanation of this Ṛk, Yāska says:

तत्रैतद्याज्ञिका वेदयंते—त्रिंशदुक्थपात्राणि माध्यंदिने सवन एकदेवतानि तान्येतस्मिन् काल एकेन प्रतिधानेन पिबंति तान्यत्र सरांस्युच्यंते ॥ (*tatraitadyājñikā vedayaṃte—triṃśadukthapātrāṇi mādhyaṃdine savana ekadevatāni tānyetasmin kāla ekena pratidhānena pibaṃti tānyatra sarāṃsyucyaṃte ‖*) (Si. 5-11 [as supplied])

The meaning is that the word सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*) denotes the "ukthapātras" (vessels), and that Indra drank Soma juice filling thirty vessels at the time of the midday pressing. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-17-11 and 8-7-10 it is said that he drank Soma filling three vessels (त्रीणि सरांसि (*trīṇi sarāṃsi*)). On many occasions the sacrificers, priests and seers, when praying to Indra and inviting him to drink Soma, say: धृषत् पिब कलशे सोममिंड्र (*dhṛṣat piba kalaśe somamiṃḍra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-6) "O Indra, drink fearlessly the Soma juice in this vessel"; ऋश्यो न तृष्णन्नवपानमा गहि पिबा सोमं वशा अनु (*ṛśyo na tṛṣṇannavapānamā gahi pibā somaṃ vaśā anu*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-4-10) "O Indra, as a thirsty deer drinks water, come quickly and drink Soma"; धन्वचरो न वंसगस्तृषाणश्च क्रमानः पिबतु दुग्धमंशुं (*dhanvacaro na vaṃsagastṛṣāṇaśca kramānaḥ pibatu dugdhamaṃśuṃ*) "O Indra, as a bull, wandering thirsty and exhausted in a waterless desert, drinks water, drink the Soma juice." With such comparisons they have invited Indra to drink Soma. Descriptions that Indra's belly is large like a lake (or like the sea) and that Indra should fill it with Soma-drinking are found in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-8-7; 1-104-9; 2-11-11; 2-14-10; 2-16-2; 3-35-6; 3-36-7, 8 [the print has a colon after the 8; the digits are left in the form 8-3-40-5 only because the checker reads the print that way; the intended last reference is the third Maṇḍala, Sūkta forty, Ṛk five]; 3-47-1; 3-51-12; 8-12-23; 8-2-1; 8-17-5, 8; 8-67-7; 8-81-22, 23, 24; 10-28-2; 10-43-7; 10-104-2, and others. The great delight that Indra felt after drinking Soma he himself has described in various ways in Ṛ. Saṃ. Sūkta 10-119:

### Printed p. 608

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta "1" [sic, as supplied], p. 608.*

इति वा इति मे मनो गामश्वं सनुयामिति । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ १ ॥ (*iti vā iti me mano gāmaśvaṃ sanuyāmiti | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 1 ‖*)

प्र वाता इव दोधत उन्मा पीता अयंसत । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ २ ॥ (*pra vātā iva dodhata unmā pītā ayaṃsata | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 2 ‖*)

उन्मा पीता अयंसत रथमश्वा इवाशवः । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ३ ॥ (*unmā pītā ayaṃsata rathamaśvā ivāśavaḥ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 3 ‖*)

अहं तप्पेव वंधुरं पर्यचामि हृदा मतिं । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ४ ॥ (*ahaṃ tappeva vaṃdhuraṃ paryacāmi hṛdā matiṃ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 4 ‖*)

उप मा मतिरस्थित वाश्रा पुत्रमिव प्रियं । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ५ ॥ (*upa mā matirasthita vāśrā putramiva priyaṃ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 5 ‖*)

नहि मे अक्षिपच्चनाच्छान्तुः पंच कृष्टयः । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ६ ॥ (*nahi me akṣipaccanācchāntuḥ paṃca kṛṣṭayaḥ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 6 ‖*)

नहि मे रोदसी उभे अन्यं पक्षं चन प्रति । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ७ ॥ (*nahi me rodasī ubhe anyaṃ pakṣaṃ cana prati | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 7 ‖*)

अभि द्यां महिना भुवमभी३मां पृथिवीं महीं । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ (*abhi dyāṃ mahinā bhuvamabhī3māṃ pṛthivīṃ mahīṃ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖*) (no verse number printed in the supplied text; this is the eighth verse)

हंताहं पृथिवीमिमां नि दधानीह वेह ना । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ९ ॥ (*haṃtāhaṃ pṛthivīmimāṃ ni dadhānīha veha nā | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 9 ‖*)

ओषमित्पृथिवीमहं जंघनानीह वेह वा । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ १० ॥ (*oṣamitpṛthivīmahaṃ jaṃghanānīha veha vā | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 10 ‖*)

दिवि मे अन्यः पक्षो३धो अन्यमचीकृषं कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ ११ ॥ (*divi me anyaḥ pakṣo3dho anyamacīkṛṣaṃ kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 11 ‖*)

अहमस्मि महामहोऽभिनभ्यमुदीक्षितः । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ १२ ॥ (*ahamasmi mahāmaho 'bhinabhyamudīkṣitaḥ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖ 12 ‖*)

गृहो याम्यरंकृतो देवेभ्यो हव्यवाहनः । कुवित्सोमस्यापामिति ॥ (*gṛho yāmyaraṃkṛto devebhyo havyavāhanaḥ | kuvitsomasyāpāmiti ‖*) (no verse number printed in the supplied text; this is the thirteenth verse)

(Ma. Saṃ. 10-119-1 to 13 [sic, "ಮ. ಸಂ." as supplied for Ṛ. Saṃ.])

1. I have drunk Soma. (From the joy of that drinking) I have made up my mind to give cows and horses (to those who praise me).

2. The Soma I have drunk excites me like a wind blowing violently. I have drunk Soma.

3. The Soma I have drunk stirs up my mind as the horses of a chariot carry the chariot swiftly. I have drunk Soma.

4. The hymn recited by those who worship me hastens me, as a cow hurries to her beloved calf. I have drunk Soma.

5. As a carpenter turns a piece of wood, I turn that hymn over in my mind (I meditate on it). I have drunk Soma.

6. The five peoples (the five tribes of men) appear to my eye as less than a blade of grass (a very small thing). I have drunk Soma.

7. The two worlds (earth and atmosphere) together could not equal even half of my (power). I have drunk Soma.

8. In greatness (in strength and capacity) I have surpassed heaven and this wide earth [text as supplied: "ಮಾರಿದ್ದೇನೆ"]. I have drunk Soma.

### Printed p. 609

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 609.*

9. Ha! This earth I shall set down here or elsewhere. I have drunk Soma.

10. Ha! I shall strike this earth swiftly from here to there. I have drunk Soma.

11. Half of my (body) is in the atmosphere; the other half I have lowered below. I have drunk Soma.

12. By my great glory I have been raised up to heaven. I have drunk Soma.

13. I (like Agni) am the priest in the sacrificial house and the bearer of the oblation to the gods. I have drunk Soma.

In many such Ṛks the description of Indra's drinking of Soma is given. Because it would become far too long, it is not possible to explain all of them here; the matters will be discussed only in the respective contexts.

#### Ṛk 12

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वे इं॒द्राप्य॑भूम॒ विप्रा॒ धियं॑ वनेम ऋत॒या सपं॑तः ।
> अ॒व॒स्यवो॑ धीमहि॒ प्रश॑स्तिं स॒द्यस्ते॑ रा॒यो दा॒वने॑ स्याम ॥१२॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> त्वे इति । इंद्र । अपि । अभूम । विप्राः । धियं । वनेम । ऋत्यया । सपंतः ।
> अवस्यवः । धीमहि । प्रशस्तिं । सद्यः । ते । रायः । दावने । स्याम ॥ १२ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे इंद्र विप्रा मेधाविनो वयं त्वे त्वयि त्वद्धृदयेऽभूम । अपि सानु-
> नयप्रश्ने वर्तते । ते तव हृदये किं वर्तामह इति । ऋतॆया । ऋतं कर्मफलं ।
> तत्कामनया सपंतस्त्वां परिचरणाख्येन कर्मणा स्पृशंतो वयं धियं त्वदुद्दे-
> शेन क्रियमाणं कर्म वनेम । संभजेमहि ॥ वन षण संभक्तौ! आशीर्लिज लिज्या-

> *he iṃdra viprā medhāvino vayaṃ tve tvayi tvaddhṛdaye 'bhūma | api sānu-*
> *nayapraśne vartate | te tava hṛdaye kiṃ vartāmaha iti | ṛtèyā | ṛtaṃ karmaphalaṃ |*
> *tatkāmanayā sapaṃtastvāṃ paricaraṇākhyena karmaṇā spṛśaṃto vayaṃ dhiyaṃ tvadudde-*
> *śena kriyamāṇaṃ karma vanema | saṃbhajemahi ‖ vana ṣaṇa saṃbhaktau! āśīrlija lijyā-*

*(The bhāṣya is cut off at the foot of the page, in the middle of the grammatical note on the root वन (*vana*) (van, "to share, to worship"); it continues on the next page.)*

*Note: in the supplied text Saṃhitā and Pada of this Ṛk were not in the machine reading (a picture); they were written into kn.md from the picture. The Pada reads ऋत्यया (*ṛtyayā*) where the Saṃhitā has ऋतया (*ṛtayā*), as printed.*

### Printed p. 610

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 610.*

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya** (continued from p. 609)

> शिष्यज्जत्यज्जत्ययः ॥ किंच अवस्यवस्त्वत्तो रक्षणमिच्छंतः प्रशस्तिं । शस्तिः शंसनं ।
> प्रकृष्टशंसनं स्तोत्रं धीमहि । त्वयि निदधीमहि । ततस्ते त्वदीयस्य रायो धनस्य
> दावने दानाय पात्रभूताः सद्यः स्याम । इदानीं भवेम ॥

*(Gist: the end of the grammatical note on the root vana, then: "desirous of protection, we offer you praise; thereby may we quickly become fit for the gift of your wealth.")*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Indra: O Indra. Viprāḥ: we, the wise. Tve: in your heart itself. Abhūma: may we be. Ṛtayā: with the wish for the fruit of the rite. Sapantaḥ: serving you. Dhiyam: the rite directed to you. Vanema: we worship. Avasyavaḥ: desiring your protection. Praśastim: your praise. Dhīmahi: I offer [as supplied; singular]. Te: your. Rāyaḥ: of wealth. Dāvane: for the gift. Sadyaḥ syāma: may we at once become fit.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, may we, the wise, be in your heart itself. Serving you with the wish for the fruit of the rite, we worship the rite directed to you. Desiring your protection, we offer you praise. May we at once become fit for the gift of your wealth.

**Printed English**

May we, your pious(worshippers), abide in you: approaching you with devotion, may we enjoy(the reward of) our adoration: desirous of protection, we meditate your glorification; may we ever be(thought worthy) of the gift of your treasures.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अपि (*api*) (api): "अपीति संसर्गं (*apīti saṃsargaṃ*)" (Nirukta 1-4). The word api ordinarily indicates the connection (saṃsarga) or relation between two words. Saṃsarga means joining one thing with another. Example: आहस्सु रात्रिष्टपि (*āhassu rātriṣṭapi*) [as supplied], that is, "in the day, and also in the night"; the word api indicates the relation, or the common quality, of night and day: the work that goes on in the day goes on in the night too. In the present Ṛk, as Sāyaṇa says, अपि सानुनयप्रश्ने वर्तते (*api sānunayapraśne vartate*) ("api is used in a courteous question"), it indicates a humble prayer.

विप्राः (*viprāḥ*) (viprāḥ): मेधाविनो वयं (*medhāvino vayaṃ*), "we, the wise". Although the word asmad is not used here, because the first-person verbs vanema and syāma occur in this Ṛk, the word वयं (*vayaṃ*) ("we") must be supplied.

### Printed p. 611

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 611.*

ऋतया (*ṛtayā*) (ṛtayā): the various meanings of the word ṛta we have explained at length in Ṛg-veda Saṃhitā part 10, pp. 143 and 673. Here the commentator takes ṛta to mean कर्मफलं (*karmaphalaṃ*) (karmaphalam, "fruit of the rite") and explains ṛtayā as "from the desire for that fruit of the rite".

धियं वनेम ऋतया सपंतः (*dhiyaṃ vanema ṛtayā sapaṃtaḥ*): O Indra, desiring the fruit of the rites we perform, serving you, or performing rites directed to you, we shall come near you (we shall reach you): this is the main sense of this sentence.

त्वे इंद्राप्यभूम (*tve iṃdrāpyabhūma*): वयं त्वद्धदयेऽभूम (*vayaṃ tvaddhadaye 'bhūma*) ("we were in your heart"). The meaning is: let us be those whose minds are fixed on you, or let us be dear to you. The sense of the second half is clear.

#### Ṛk 13

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स्याम॒ ते त॑ इंद्र॒ ये त॑ ऊ॒ती अ॑व॒स्यव॒ ऊर्जं॑ व॒र्धयं॑तः।
> शु॒ष्मिंत॑नुं॒ यं चा॒कना॑नु देवा॒स्मे र॒यिं रा॑सि वी॒रवं॑तं॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> स्याम । ते । ते । इंद्र । ये । ते । ऊती । अवस्यवः । उर्जं । वर्धयंतः ।
> शुष्मिन्तमं । यं । चाकनाम । देव । अस्मे इति । रयिं । रासि । वीरवंतं ॥ १३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे इंद्र ते तवोती ऊत्या प्रणयनेन ॥ अवतेः प्राप्त्यर्थस्य क्रीनि रूपं ॥
> ये ते तवावस्यवो रक्षाकामा ऊर्जं हविर्लक्षणमन्नं वर्धयंतो भवंति ते
> तव वयं ते स्याम । त्वदधीना भवेम । देव द्योतमान हे इंद्र शुष्मिंतमं
> दानभागायार्हतया बलवत्तमं यद्वा शत्रूणां शोषयितृतमं यं रयिं
> चाकनाम । कामायामहे ॥ कनतेः कांत्यर्थस्य यज्लुगंतस्य लोटि रूपं ॥
> वीरवंतं पुत्रपौत्रैः सहितं तादृशं रयिमस्मे अस्मभ्यं रासि । देहि ॥

*(Gist: those devoted to you who increase your strength with offerings, may we be of their company; grant us such wealth, with sons and grandsons.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Indra: O Indra. Te: your. Ūtī: by favour. Ye: which devotees. Te: your. Avasyavaḥ: desiring protection. Ūrjam: food in the form of the oblation.

### Printed p. 612

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 612.*

Vardhayantaḥ: who, offering abundantly, increase your strength. Te: those. Syāma: may we also become, joined to their company. Deva: O Indra, divine in nature. Śuṣmintamam: of strength to dry up enemies. Yam: which wealth. Cākanāma: we desire. Vīravantam: accompanied by heroic sons and others. Rayim: such wealth. Asme: to us. Rāsi: give.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, whichever devotees, by your favour, desiring your protection, offer you food in the form of the oblation in abundance and increase your strength, may we too join their company. O Indra, divine in nature, give us such wealth, accompanied by heroic sons and others, as has the strength to dry up enemies, which we desire.

**Printed English**

May we,(through) your(favour), Indra, be such as those are, who; desirous of your protection, augmeut(by their praises) your vigour: divine (Indra), you grant the wealth which we desire,(the source of) great power and of(numerous) progeny.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऊती (*ūtī*) (ūtī): ऊती ऊत्या प्रणयनेन । अवतेः प्राप्त्यर्थस्य क्तिनि रूपं (*ūtī ūtyā praṇayanena | avateḥ prāptyarthasya ktini rūpaṃ*) (the bhāṣya, as supplied: "ūtī is ūtyā, by leading; the form of the root avati in the sense of attainment with the suffix ktin"). Ordinarily the word ūti in the Ṛg-veda is formed from the root av, "to protect", and used in the sense of "protection"; but here the commentator says the word ūti is formed from the root avati having the sense of attainment (the meaning "to reach"), and that for ūtī, which stands in the nominative, the instrumental sense ūtyā must be understood; hence ūtī here means "by causing attainment, or by guidance".

अवस्यवः (*avasyavaḥ*) (avasyavaḥ): this word too is formed from the root av, "to protect", and in the sense रक्षाकामाः (*rakṣākāmāḥ*) ("desiring protection") is an adjective of the supplied word वयं (*vayaṃ*) ("we").

शुष्मिंतमं वीरवंतं रयिं चाकनाम (*śuṣmiṃtamaṃ vīravaṃtaṃ rayiṃ cākanāma*): in this sentence, the word शुष्मिंतमं (*śuṣmiṃtamaṃ*) is explained as दानभागायार्हतया बलवत्तमं (*dānabhāgāyārhatayā balavattamaṃ*): "very strong, because of the fitness for giving"; or शत्रूणां शोषयतृतमं (*śatrūṇāṃ śoṣayatṛtamaṃ*) [as supplied]: "most able to dry up or destroy enemies"; the rayim, that is, wealth. In the same way the word वीरवंतं (*vīravaṃtaṃ*) is explained as पुत्रपौत्रिः सहितं (*putrapautriḥ sahitaṃ*) [as supplied]: wealth accompanied by heroic sons, grandsons, servants and the like. Here

### Printed p. 613

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 613.*

(continuing the Special Topics of Ṛk 13) The two words शुष्मंतमं (*śuṣmaṃtamaṃ*) and वीरवंतं (*vīravaṃtaṃ*) are adjectives of the word रयिं (*rayiṃ*). Taken word by word the meaning is: wealth that is exceedingly strong and accompanied by sons and others who are heroes. In Sanskrit, and especially in the Ṛg-veda, when many things are to be indicated it is customary to name one principal thing and to describe the other things as accompanying that principal thing by means of adjectives. But the principal intention is this: that He should give us very great strength, that is, power and capacity; sons, grandsons, retainers and other followers who are valiant, that is, who fight in battle like heroic warriors; and wealth.

अस्मे (*asme*): the word अस्मे (*asme*) is used in all seven case-senses. In the present Ṛk it is said in the sense of the dative case: अस्मे (*asme*) is अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*), "to us".

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> रासि॒ क्षयं॒ रासि॑ मि॒त्रम॒स्मे रासि॒ शर्ध॑ इंद्र॒ मारु॑तं नः ।
> स॒जोष॑सो॒ ये च॑ मंदसा॒नाः प्र वा॒यवः॑ पां॒त्यग्र॑णीतिं ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> रासि । क्षयं । रासि । मित्रं । अस्मे इति । रासि । शर्धः । इंद्र । मारुतं । नः ।
> सजोषसः । ये । च । मंदसानाः । प्र । वायवः । पांति । अग्रनीतिं ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे इंद्र क्षयं । क्षियंति निवसंत्यत्रेति क्षयो गृहं । तदस्मभ्यं रासि । देहि
> तॆथा मित्रं सखायमस्मे अस्मभ्यं रासि॥ वाक्यभेदादनिघातः॥ मारुतं मरुतां देव-
> विशां संबंधि शर्धो बलं नोऽस्मभ्यं रासि । एतेन मरुतामप्यागमनं प्रार्थ्यते ।
> ये मरुतः सजोषसः सह प्रीयमाणा मदंतो मोदमाना वायवो यज्ञं
> प्रति गंतारः संतोऽग्रणीतिमग्रे नीयमानं सोमं प्रपांति । प्रकर्षण पिबंति
> तेषां शर्ध इति समन्वयः । यद्वा ये च वायवोऽग्रे नीयमानं सोमं
> पिबंति तेषां वायूनां बलं रासि ॥ वायव इति पूजायां बहुवचनं । वायु-
> र्हि सोमस्याग्रपातेति ॥

*(Gist: O Indra, give us a dwelling and a friend; give us the combined strength of the Maruts, who, united and exulting, going to the sacrifice, drink the Soma brought first.)*

### Printed p. 614

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 614.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Indra: O Indra. Kṣayam: a house fit to dwell in. Rāsi: give. Mitram: a friend. Asme: to us. Rāsi: give. Ye ca: which Maruts. Sajoṣasaḥ: united together. Mandasānāḥ: of joyful mind. Vāyavaḥ: going towards the sacrifice. Agraṇītim: the Soma brought in the foremost part. Pra pānti: drink fully; such. Mārutam: belonging to the Marut deities. Śardhaḥ: collective strength. Naḥ: to us. Rāsi: give.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, give us a house fit to dwell in and friends fit for companionship. And give us the collective strength belonging to those Maruts who, united together and going towards the sacrifice, there drink fully the Soma juice that is brought in the foremost part.

**Printed English**

Grant us a habitation; grant us friends; grant us, Indra, the strength of the Maruts: the winds, who, propitious and exulting, drink copiously of the first offering(of the Soma juice).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

क्षयं (*kṣayaṃ*) (kṣayam): क्षियंति निवसंत्यत्रेति क्षयो गृहं (*kṣiyaṃti nivasaṃtyatreti kṣayo gṛhaṃ*). The word क्षय (*kṣaya*) is formed from the root क्षि (*kṣi*), "to dwell". A place where one dwells, that is, a house, a home: this is the meaning.

मित्रं (*mitraṃ*) (mitram): सखायं (*sakhāyaṃ*), "a friend", or a well-wisher. The word मित्र (*mitra*) is formed from a root meaning "to be fond" (class 1, ātmanepada; the root as supplied is garbled in the print: इामिदा स्नेहने (*iāmidā snehane*) [?]). The chief mark that a friend or companion should have is to help one another in time of need. On this point the Ṛg-veda itself says:

न स सखा यो न ददाति सख्ये सचाभुवे सचमानाय पित्वः (*na sa sakhā yo na dadāti sakhye sacābhuve sacamānāya pitvaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-117-4, as printed "ಮ. ಸಂ"), that is: the man who gives nothing in time of need to his friend (does no kindness) is no friend at all; he is a hypocrite who pretends to be a friend. Besides this:

ददाति प्रतिगृह्णाति गुह्यमाख्याति पृच्छति । भुंक्ते च भोजयत्येव षड्विधं मित्रलक्षणं ॥ (*dadāti pratigṛhṇāti guhyamākhyāti pṛcchati | bhuṃkte ca bhojayatyeva ṣaḍvidhaṃ mitralakṣaṇaṃ ‖*)

### Printed p. 615

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 615.*

Thus a poet has described the qualities a friend should have: that is, a friend (if he is a true friend) gives his friend the things he needs; he too receives from him what he needs; he tells him his own secret, and he too listens with faith to the secrets (confidential matters) that the other tells; he feeds his friend, and he too eats in his house. These six kinds are said to be the marks that friends should have. Indra is prayed to give such a well-wishing friend.

मारुतं शर्धः (*mārutaṃ śardhaḥ*) (mārutaṃ śardhaḥ): the prayer is that He give the collective strength of the Marut deities. The reason why, although all the gods have abundant strength, the strength of the Maruts alone is prayed for here is this: the Maruts are forty-nine in number, and all of them are said to have the same form and the same strength. Therefore, if the strength of these forty-nine Maruts is added together, that combined strength becomes exceedingly great. Hence the prayer is that such strength be given. Whenever very great strength is to be spoken of, it is the custom to compare it to मारुतं शर्धः (*mārutaṃ śardhaḥ*), the collective (gathered together) strength of the Maruts. The commentators say that by the expression मारुतं शर्धः (*mārutaṃ śardhaḥ*) the coming of the Maruts is also indicated.

सजोषसः (*sajoṣasaḥ*) (sajoṣasaḥ): सह प्रीयमाणाः । समानप्रीतियुक्ताः ऐकमत्यभाजः (*saha prīyamāṇāḥ | samānaprītiyuktāḥ aikamatyabhājaḥ*): "those who love together; endowed with equal love; sharing unanimity". The meaning is that the gods, having a love for Indra equal to the love they have among themselves, remain in unanimity in order to conquer the Asuras, make Indra stand in front, that is, make him their leader, and obtain victory through Indra himself. The Nirukta (Nir. 8-8) explains the word सजोषसः (*sajoṣasaḥ*) as "being together". Yāska, having quoted the Ṛk:

आजुह्वान ईड्यो वंद्यश्चायाह्यग्ने वसुभिः सजोषाः त्वं देवनामसि यह्व होता स एनन्यक्षीषितो यजीयान् (*ājuhvāna īḍyo vaṃdyaścāyāhyagne vasubhiḥ sajoṣāḥ tvaṃ devanāmasi yahva hotā sa enanyakṣīṣito yajīyān*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-3)

explained the word सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) in it as सह जोषणः (*saha joṣaṇaḥ*), "one of equal love".

आ रुद्रास इंद्रवंतस्सजोषसो हिरण्यरथाः सुविताय गंतन । इयं वो अस्मत्पतिहर्यते मतिस्त्रृष्णजे न दिव उत्सा उदन्यवे ॥ (*ā rudrāsa iṃdravaṃtassajoṣaso hiraṇyarathāḥ suvitāya gaṃtana | iyaṃ vo asmatpatiharyate matistrṛṣṇaje na diva utsā udanyave ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-3-2-11)

In this Ṛk the meaning is given: इंद्रेण सह जोषणाः सजोषसः परमैश्वर्येण नित्यमासेविताः (*iṃdreṇa saha joṣaṇāḥ sajoṣasaḥ paramaiśvaryeṇa nityamāsevitāḥ*), that is, those served by Indra with supreme prosperity.

### Printed p. 616

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 616.*

वायवः (*vāyavaḥ*) (vāyavaḥ): this word, formed from the root वा गतिगंधनयोः (*vā gatigaṃdhanayoḥ*), has the sense "those who go", the meaning being the Marut deities who go towards the sacrificial ground. Or, as the saying वायुर्हि सोमस्याग्रपाता (*vāyurhi somasyāgrapātā*) has it, since the deity Vāyu drinks the Soma first, one may also interpret: "give him the greater strength that he obtains from it". Although वायवः (*vāyavaḥ*) is plural here, it is used in the sense of honour (to show respect), so the singular sense, "the strength of Vāyu", is what is indicated.

अग्रनीतिं (*agranītiṃ*) (agranītiṃ): अग्रे नीयमानं सोमं (*agre nīyamānaṃ somaṃ*), "the Soma being led in front"; the meaning is the Soma juice brought forward and placed before all, so that all can see it.

#### Ṛk 15

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> व्यंत्विन्नु येषु॑ मंदसा॒नस्तृ॒पत्सोमं॑ पाहि द्र॒ह्यदिं॑द्र ।
> अ॒स्मान्त्सु पृ॒त्स्व त॑रु॒त्राव॑र्धयो॒ द्यां बृ॒हद्भि॑र॒र्कैः ॥ १५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> व्यंतु । इत् । नु । येषु । मंदसानः । तृपत् । सोमं । पाहि । द्रह्यत् । इंद्र ।
> अस्मान् । सु । पृत्सु । आ । तरुत्र । अवर्धयः । द्यां । बृहत्भिः । अर्कैः ॥ १५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे इंद्र येषु तव सहायभूतेषु मरुत्सु मंदसानो माद्यन्भवसि ते
> मरुतो नु क्षिप्रं व्यंतु । सोमं भक्षयंतु । इत् एवार्थे । द्रह्यत् ॥ दृंहतेरिदं
> रूपं ॥ आत्मानं दृढीकुर्वन् त्वं तृपत् तर्पयंतमिमं सोमं पाहि । पिब ।
> तरुत्र शत्रूणां हिंसक हे इंद्र बृहद्भिर्बलवद्भिरर्कॆरर्चनीय्मैर्मरुद्धिः सहितस्त्वं।
> आ इति चार्थे । तेनास्मान् द्यां च सु सुष्टु पृत्सु पशुपुत्रादिभिरस्मान् अस्मत्पाल-
> नेन च दिवमवर्धयः । यद्वा येषु सोमेषु मंदसानो मोदमानो भवसि
> ते सोमा व्यंतु । त्वां गच्छंतु । शेषं पूर्ववत् ॥

*(Gist: O Indra, let the Maruts in whom you rejoice come and drink the Soma; you too drink it; with the Maruts, augment us and heaven, or, in the other reading, let the Somas come to you.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Indra: O Indra. Yeṣu: among which Maruts, who are your helpers. Mandasānaḥ: you who are joyful, those Maruts. Nu: quickly. Vyantu it: let them come and drink the Soma. Drahyat: you who are endowed with firmness. Tṛpat: that which brings satisfaction. Somam: the Soma juice. Pāhi: drink. Tarutra: O Indra, destroyer of enemies. Bṛhadbhiḥ: mighty. Arkaiḥ: and adorable, together with the Maruts. Asmān ā dyām: us and the people of the world of the gods. Su: in an excellent manner. Pṛtsu: so as to be endowed with cattle, sons and the like. Avardhayaḥ: cause to prosper.

### Printed p. 617

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 617.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, may those Maruts among whom, your helpers, you are joyful come quickly and drink the Soma. Do you, endowed with firmness, drink the Soma juice that brings satisfaction. O Indra, destroyer of enemies, together with the mighty and adorable Maruts, cause us and the people of the world of the gods to prosper, endowed with cattle, sons and the like, and in an excellent manner.

**Printed English**

Let those in whom you are delighted repair(to the libation), and do you, Indra, confiding, drink the satiating Soma: liberator(from evil), associated with the mighty and adorable(Maruts), augment our prosperity, and(that of) heaven.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the drinking of Soma by Indra together with the Maruts is indicated. On this subject:

⟦ಸಜೋಷಾ ಇಂದ್ರ ಸಗಣೋ ಮರುದ್ಭಿಃ ಸೋಮಂ ಪಿಬ ವೃತ್ರಹಾ ಶೂರ ವಿದ್ವಾನ್ ।
ಜಹಿ ಶತ್ರುರಪ ಮೃಧೋ ನುದಸ್ವಾಥಾಭಯಂ ಕೃಣುಹಿ ವಿಶ್ವತೋ ನಃ ॥⟧ (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-47-2)

अधा नियुत्वः सगणो मरुद्भिरस्मिन् यज्ञॆ बर्हिषि मादयस्व (*adhā niyutvaḥ sagaṇo marudbhirasmin yajñè barhiṣi mādayasva*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-101-9)

ये त्वा नूनमनुमदंति विप्राः पिबेंद्र सोमं सगणो मरुद्भिः । (*ye tvā nūnamanumadaṃti viprāḥ pibeṃdra somaṃ sagaṇo marudbhiḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-47-4)

अपूपमद्धि सगणो मरुद्भिः सोमं पिब वृत्रहा शूर विद्वान् । (*apūpamaddhi sagaṇo marudbhiḥ somaṃ piba vṛtrahā śūra vidvān |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-52-7)

सजोषा इंद्र वरुणेन सोमं सजोषाः पाहि गिर्वणो मरुद्भिः । (*sajoṣā iṃdra varuṇena somaṃ sajoṣāḥ pāhi girvaṇo marudbhiḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-34-7)

### Printed p. 618

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 618.*

In these and similar Ṛks Indra is invoked and prayed to drink Soma together with the Maruts.

तरुत्र (*tarutra*) (tarutra): शत्रूणां हिंसक (*śatrūṇāṃ hiṃsaka*) (the supplied text reads शत्रुवाणं (*śatruvāṇaṃ*)), "destroyer of enemies". Sāyaṇa explains the word तरुत्र (*tarutra*) as "one who injures enemies". Yāska, when giving the etymology for the Ṛk

त्यमूषु वाजिनं देवजूतं सहावानं तरुतारं रथानां । (*tyamūṣu vājinaṃ devajūtaṃ sahāvānaṃ tarutāraṃ rathānāṃ |*) (as printed "ಮ. ಸಂ.", no numbers)

explains the word तरुतारं (*tarutāraṃ*) as तारयितारं (*tārayitāraṃ*), "one who carries (people) across (dangers)".

नु, इत् (*nu, it*) (nu, it): नु (*nu*) means क्षिप्रं (*kṣipraṃ*), "quickly, soon"; इत् (*it*) is used in the sense एवार्थे (*evārthe*), that is, in the sense of "indeed".

अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*) (arkaiḥ): अर्चनीय्यैः (*arcanīyyaiḥ*) (as supplied), "worthy of worship"; it is used as an epithet of the Marut deities.

अस्मान् सु पृत्सु आ तरुत्र अवर्धयः द्यां बृहद्भिः अर्कैः (*asmān su pṛtsu ā tarutra avardhayaḥ dyāṃ bṛhadbhiḥ arkaiḥ*): the meaning that the commentator has given in one way to this sentence of the second half does not appear quite suitable here. The sentence can be interpreted in another way as well. The word पृत्सु (*pṛtsu*) is read among the names of battle (Nirukta 3-9). For the word द्यु (*dyu*) the etymology is द्युरित्यहो नामधेयं द्योतत इति सतः (*dyurityaho nāmadheyaṃ dyotata iti sataḥ*), "day" (Nirukta 1-7). The word अर्क (*arka*) also has the sense "food": अर्कमन्नं भवति (*arkamannaṃ bhavati*) (Nirukta 3-9). If, keeping these meanings, the above sentence is interpreted:

तरुत्र शत्रुहिंसकेंद्र । पृत्सु संग्रामेषु अस्मान् सु आ अवर्धयः शत्रुभिः रक्षित्वा यथा जयं लभेमहि तथा सुष्टु वृद्धिं कुरुष्ट । किंच द्यां अहस्सु दिवसेषु बृहद्भिरर्कॆः प्रभूतान्नैनार्नाविधैरन्नॆर्वा अस्थानवर्धयः त्वत्पसादद्वृद्धिं लभेमहि ॥ (*tarutra śatruhiṃsakeṃdra | pṛtsu saṃgrāmeṣu asmān su ā avardhayaḥ śatrubhiḥ rakṣitvā yathā jayaṃ labhemahi tathā suṣṭu vṛddhiṃ kuruṣṭa | kiṃca dyāṃ ahassu divaseṣu bṛhadbhirarkèḥ prabhūtānnainārnāvidhairannèrvā asthānavardhayaḥ tvatpasādadvṛddhiṃ labhemahi ‖*)

(the text is printed so; the words रक्षित्वा (*rakṣitvā*), कुरुष्ट (*kuruṣṭa*), प्रभूतान्नैनार्नाविधैरन्नॆर्वा अस्थानवर्धयः (*prabhūtānnainārnāvidhairannèrvā asthānavardhayaḥ*), त्वत्पसादद्वृद्धिं (*tvatpasādadvṛddhiṃ*) look garbled in the supplied text [?]). That is: "O Indra, destroyer of enemies, protect us from enemies in battles and make us prosper well; and cause us to prosper on every day with abundant food, so that we may gain increase by your grace" — another kind of meaning can also be given.

### Printed p. 619

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 619 (printed "119").*

#### Ṛk 16

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> बृ॒हंत॒ इन्नु ये ते॑ तरुत्रो॒क्षभि॑र्वा सु॒म्ममा॒विवा॑सान् ।
> स्त्र॒णा॒नासो॑ ब॒र्हिः प॒स्ता॑व॒त्तोता॒ इदिं॑द्र॒ वाज॑मग्मन् ॥ १६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> बृहंतः । इत् । नु । ये । ते । तरुत्र । उक्षेभिः । वा । सुम्मं । आऽविवासान् ।
> स्त्र्णानासः । बर्हिः । पस्यवत् । त्वाऊताः । इत् । इंद्र । वाजं । अग्मन् ॥ १६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> तरुत्र आपद्भ्यस्तारक हे इंद्र ये पुमांसः सुम्मं सुखकरं त्वामुक्थैः
> शस्त्रैराविवासान् । परिचरेयुः ॥ विवासतेर्लॆट्व्यडागमः ॥ त एव पुमांसो नु
> क्षिप्रमविलंबेन बृहंतो महांतो भवंतीति पूरणः । किंच बर्हिः स्तृणानासो
> वेद्यां बर्हिराच्छादयंतो ये त्वां परिचरंति ते त्वोता इत् त्वया रक्षिताः संत
> एव पस्तावत् गृहसहितं वाजमन्नमग्मन् । गच्छंति ॥

*(Gist: O Indra, rescuer from dangers, those who serve you with praises quickly become great; those who strew the barhis are protected by you and obtain a house and food.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Tarutra: rescuer from distress. Indra: O Indra. Ye: those men who. Summam: you, the giver of happiness, with praises. Āvivāsān: worship. Te: those devotees of yours. Nu it: quickly indeed. Bṛhantaḥ: become endowed with great qualities and a great station. Barhiḥ: the darbha seat. Stṛṇānāsaḥ: those who spread it (on the altar) for you and worship you. Tvotā it: being protected by you alone. Pastāvat: together with a house. Vājam: food. Agman: obtain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, rescuer from distress, those men who worship you with pleasing praises, those devotees of yours, quickly become endowed with great qualities and a great station. Those who spread the darbha seat for you on the altar and worship you, being protected by you alone, obtain food together with a house.

### Printed p. 620

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 620.*

**Printed English**

Liberator(from evil), they who worship you, the giver of happiness, with holy hymns, quickly become great: they who strew the sacred grass(in honour of you) are protected by you, and, together with their families, obtain (abundant) food.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Stṛṇānāso barhiḥ: the darbha grass called barhis, spread and prepared as a seat on which the gods come and sit, is called *barhistaraṇa* (spreading of the barhis). These darbha blades are cut to one even measure and laid in three layers so as to be soft. On this matter:

त्रिवृद्बर्हिर्भवति । माता पिता पुत्रः ।........त्रेधा सन्नद्धं भवति । त्रय इमे लोकाः । एष्टेव लोकेषु प्रतितिष्ठति ॥ (*trivṛdbarhirbhavati | mātā pitā putraḥ |........tredhā sannaddhaṃ bhavati | traya ime lokāḥ | eṣṭeva lokeṣu pratitiṣṭhati ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-6-3.1)

is said in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa. The barhis grass is to be spread in three layers, because these three layers are said to stand for mother, father and son, or for the three worlds: earth, atmosphere and heaven. Concerning this word Yāska explains:

बर्हिः परिबहर्णात् परिच्छेदनात् । (*barhiḥ paribaharṇāt paricchedanāt |*) (Si. [sic] 8-9)

that is, the name *barhis* is well known for darbha grass that is cut evenly and used as a seat in the sacrifice; and as an illustration he cites

⟦ಪ್ರಾಚೀನಂ ಬರ್ಹಿಃ ಪ್ರದಿಶಾ ಪೃಥಿವ್ಯಾ ವಸ್ತೋರಸ್ಯಾ ವೃಜ್ಯತೇ ಅಗ್ರೇ ಅಹ್ನಾಂ ।
ವ್ಯು ಪ್ರಥತೇ ವಿತರಂ ವರೀಯೋ ದೇವೇಭ್ಯೋ ಆದಿತಯೇ ಸ್ಯೋನಂ ॥⟧ (Pu. [sic] Saṃ. 10-10-4)

and gives its etymology:

प्राचीनं बर्हिः प्रदिशा पृथिव्या वसनायास्याः प्रवृज्यते अग्रे अह्नां बर्हिः पूर्वाह्ने तद्विप्रथते वितरं विकीर्णतरमिति वा विस्तीर्णतरमिति वा वरीयो वरतरमुरुतरं वा देवेभ्यश्चादितये च स्योनं । स्योनमिति सुखनाम स्यतेरवस्यंत्येतत् सेवितव्यं भवतीति वा ॥ (*prācīnaṃ barhiḥ pradiśā pṛthivyā vasanāyāsyāḥ pravṛjyate agre ahnāṃ barhiḥ pūrvāhne tadviprathate vitaraṃ vikīrṇataramiti vā vistīrṇataramiti vā varīyo varataramurutaraṃ vā devebhyaścāditaye ca syonaṃ | syonamiti sukhanāma syateravasyaṃtyetat sevitavyaṃ bhavatīti vā ‖*) (Si. [sic] 8-9)

That is, at the time of the sacrifice, to prepare a seat on which the gods may come and sit on the sacrificial altar, the darbha blades are cut evenly and, to be soft, are laid one upon another in three layers.

### Printed p. 621

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 621.*

The darbha blades so laid are spread out abundantly, widely and softly. Darbha spread in this way is called *barhis* or *barhirāstaraṇa* (spreading of the barhis). The matter of this spreading of the barhis is indicated on many occasions when the gods are invited and called.

Pastāvat vājam: since the word *pastya* is read among the names of the house (Nirukta 3-13), *pasty*a means house. The word *vāja* is read among the names of food (Nirukta 3-9). Here "food together with a house" means that they obtain both a house and food; this is the intention.

#### Ṛk 17

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒ग्रेष्णिन्नु शू॑र मदसा॒नस्त्रिक॑द्रुकेषु पाहि॒ सोम॑मिंद्र ।
> प्र॒दोधु॑व॒च्छश्रु॑षु प्रीणा॒नो या॒हि हरि॑भ्यां सु॒तस्य॑ पी॒तिं

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उग्रेषु । इत् । नु । शूर । मंदसानः । त्रिकद्रुकेषु । पाहि । सोमं । इंद्र ।
> प्रदोधुवत् । श्मश्रुषु । प्रीणानः । याहि । हरिभ्यां । सुतस्य । पीतिं ॥ १७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे शूर बलवन्निंद्र उग्रेषूद्गूर्णेषु बषुस्तोत्रशस्त्रवत्सु त्रिकद्रुकेषु
> ज्योतिर्गौरायुरित्येतन्नामकेष्वाभिप्लविकेष्टहःसु मंदसानो हृष्यन् सोमं
> पाहि । पिब । ततः प्रीणानः सोमपानेन प्रीतो भवन् श्मश्रुषु लिप्तं सोमं
> प्रदोधुवत् ॥ धूञ् कंपने । यज्लुगंतस्य शतरि रूपं॥ पुनःपुनर्धून्वन् सुत-
> स्याभिषुतस्य पीतिं पानमुद्दिश्य हरिभ्यामश्विभ्यां युक्तः सन् याहि । गच्छ ॥

*(Gist: O Indra, rejoicing in the Soma-days, drink the Soma; pleased, shaking the Soma from your beard, come with your two bay horses to the drinking of the pressed Soma.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Śūra: mighty. Indra: O Indra. Ugreṣu: in those carried out gravely with śastra-mantras. Trikadrukeṣu: on the days of Trikadruka. Mandasānaḥ: joyful. Somam: the Soma juice. Pāhi: drink (then). Prīṇānaḥ: pleased by it. Śmaśruṣu: the hairs of the face. Pradodhuvat: shaking repeatedly. Sutasya: of the pressed juice. Pītim: for the drinking. Haribhyām: with your horses. Yāhi: be pleased to come.

### Printed p. 622

*Running head: Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 11; Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, p. 622.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O mighty Indra, in the Trikadruka days, solemnly performed with śastra-mantras, drink the Soma juice in gladness; then, pleased by it, repeatedly wiping the hairs of your face, come (go) with your horses to the drinking of the pressed juice.

**English Translation (as printed)**

Indra, hero, exulting in the solemn Trikadruka rites, quaff the Soma juice, and repeatedly shaking it from your beard, repair, well pleased with your steeds, to the drinking of the effused libation.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Trikadrukeṣu: Sāyaṇa explains this word as "in the first three days, named Jyotiḥ, Gauḥ and Āyuḥ, of the sacrifice called Abhiplava". The word is also said to mean a wooden vessel in which the Soma juice is kept. It is always used in the plural. Since Soma-drinking takes place especially on these days, Indra is here prayed to come again and again for the Soma-drinking at that time.

#### Ṛk 18

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> धि॒ष्वा शवः॑ शूर॒ येन॑ वृ॒त्रम॒वाभि॑न॒द्दानु॑मौर्णवा॒भं ।
> अपा॑वृणो॒र्ज्योति॒रार्या॑य॒ नि स॑व्य॒तः सा॑दि॒ दस्यु॑रिंद्र ॥१८॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> धिष्ट्व । शवः । शूर । येन । वृत्रं । अव्यअभिनत् । दानुं । और्णवाभं ।
> अप । अवृणोः । ज्योतिः । आर्याय । नि । सव्यतः । सादि । दस्युः । इंद्र ॥ १८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> शूर हे इंद्र शवस्तादृशं बलं धिष्ट । धारय ॥ दधातेर्लोटि बहुलं
> छंदसीति शपो लुक् । छंदस्युभयथेति ना स आर्द्धधातुकत्वादिडागमः ॥ येन
> बलेन दानुं दानोः पुत्रं वृत्रमौर्णवाभं । ऊर्णनाभिः कीटविशेष और्णवाभ

> *śūra he iṃdra śavastādṛśaṃ balaṃ dhiṣṭa | dhāraya ‖ dadhāterloṭi bahulaṃ*
> *chaṃdasīti śapo luk | chaṃdasyubhayatheti nā sa ārddhadhātukatvādiḍāgamaḥ ‖ yena*
> *balena dānuṃ dānoḥ putraṃ vṛtramaurṇavābhaṃ | ūrṇanābhiḥ kīṭaviśeṣa aurṇavābha*

### Printed p. 623

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 623.*

> इत्युच्यते । तमिनावाभिनत् । बिभेदिथ । यद्वा और्ण ऊर्णनाभिः । तदाभं तत्सदृश-
> मित्यर्थः । ततस्तमोरूपस्य हननादार्याय कर्मणामनुष्ठात्रे जनाय कुत्साय
> राजर्षये वा ज्योतिः प्रकाशकमादित्यमपावृणोः । उद्घाटितवानसि । दस्युः कर्म-
> णामुपक्षपयिता स वृत्रः सव्यतस्तव सव्यपार्श्वे निषादि । त्वया नितरां सन्नो
> बाधितो वर्तते ॥

*(Gist: O Indra, keep the strength with which you struck Vṛtra, son of Danu, and opened the light for the Ārya; that Dasyu lies fallen on your left.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Śūra: valiant. Indra: O Indra. Yena: by which strength. Dānum: the son of Dānu. Vṛtram: Vṛtra the Asura. Aurṇavābham: (as one destroys) the enveloping spider. Avābhinat: you struck down. Āryasya [as printed; the Pada has *āryāya*]: for the royal sage who performs the rites. Jyotiḥ: the shining Āditya. Apāvṛṇoḥ: you uncovered and made to shine. Śavaḥ: strength (such as that). Dhiṣṭa: bear. Dasyuḥ: Vṛtra, the destroyer of rites. Savyataḥ: on your left side. Ni ṣādi: (struck by you) has fallen.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O valiant Indra, keep up that natural strength of yours by which you struck down Vṛtra, the son of Dānu, as one destroys an enveloping spider, and by which you freed the shining Āditya from his covering and made him shine for the royal sage who performs the rites. Vṛtra, the destroyer of rites, has fallen on your left side, struck by you.

**English Translation (as printed)**

Indra, hero, keep up the strength wherewith you have crushed Vritra, the spider-like son of Danu, and let open the light to the Arya: the Dasyu has been aset [sic] side on your left hand.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Dānum: Dānu is the mother of Vṛtra. Here the word *dānu* is used in the sense of Vṛtra, the son of Dānu.

Aurṇavābham: *ūrṇavābha* or *ūrṇanābhi* means a spider, which draws its thread from the navel region. Here *aurṇavābha* means that, just as a spider spins its web, covers a wide area and obstructs the movement of other insects such as mosquitoes and flies,

### Printed p. 624

*Running head: Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 11; Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, p. 624.*

so Vṛtra the Asura spreads through the atmosphere, bars the clouds and obstructs their movement and the rain; therefore Vṛtra is likened to a spider. The idea expressed in the second half of this Ṛk is that Indra struck down such a Vṛtra, made the sun (the sunlight), which had been covered by the veil of clouds that Vṛtra held back and so was not seen, visible to all the Āryas (all good people), and made the Dasyu Vṛtra fall on his left side.

> अ॒स्मभ्यं॒ तत्वा॒ष्टं वि॒श्वरू॑प॒मरं॑धयः सा॒ख्यस्य॑ त्रि॒ताय॑ ॥ १९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सनेम । ये । ते । ऊतिभिः । तरंतः । विश्वाः । स्पृधः । आर्येण । दस्यून् ।
> अस्मभ्यं । तत् । त्वाष्टं । विश्वरूपं । अरंधयः । साख्यस्य । त्रिताय ॥ १९ ॥

> स्टाष्ट्रमवधीदित्ययमर्थस्तॆत्तिरीयके विश्वरूपो वै त्वाष्ट्रः । तै- २-५-१ । इत्यत्र

> र्षये वशमानयः । यद्वा सनेम ते वयं प्रवृद्धा भवेम ये वयं तव रक्षाभिः
> स्पर्धमानाः प्रजा दस्यूंश्च तरंतोऽतिक्रामंतो भवेम ॥ सनेमेति वृद्ध्यर्थ
> आद्युदात्तः । यथा सनेम मित्रावरुणा सनंतः । ७-५२-१ । इति । सकर्मकश्चेत्
