### Printed p. 625

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 625.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Ye: we who. Te: your. Ūtibhiḥ: together with your protections. Viśvāḥ spṛdhaḥ: all the Asura-folk (the Dasyu people) who contend with us (who oppose us). Tarantaḥ: being those who destroy. Āryeṇa: with the Ārya spirit. Dasyūn: the destroying Asuras, harassing them on every side; such as we are. Sanema: may we obtain increase. Tat: that act of destroying those Asuras. Asmabhyam: for us (you have done). Sākhyasya: possessed of the state of friendship. Tritāya: for the great sage named Trita. Tvāṣṭram: the son of Tvaṣṭṛ. Viśvarūpam: the Asura named Viśvarūpa. Arandhayaḥ: you have slain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, may we, who together with your protections destroy all the Asura-folk that contend with us, and who, performing the rite with the Ārya spirit, drive far away the destroying Asuras, obtain increase. You did for us the act of destroying the Asuras. For the great sage named Trita, who possessed friendship, you slew the Asura named Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ.

**English Translation (as printed)**

Let us honour those men, who, through your protection, surpass all their rivals, as the Dasyus(are surpassed) by the Arya: this(have you wrought) for us: you have slain Vishvarupa, the son of Twashtri, through friendship for Trita.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the sage praises Indra, saying that for the royal sage named Trita he slew Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ. Who this Trita is, what is known of him, and many other matters concerning him, we have already explained at length in earlier places, namely in the Ṛg-veda-saṃhitā, Part 5, page 230, and Part 8, pages 443 and 473, and elsewhere. Although the word Trita occurs in many Ṛks, nothing more about him can be learned from them. Although the word Trita is also used in five or six places in the Atharva-veda, nothing is learned there either. Only his association with Indra is found in many places. What is known in all the places is only that, as is said, Trita is a deity dwelling very far away, and that sins or bad dreams are sent to him (Atharva-veda 1-131-1, 3; 19-56-4). In the Taittirīya-saṃhitā he is said to be the giver of long life

### Printed p. 626

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 1, Sū. 11; p. 626.*

(Taittirīya-saṃhitā 1-8-10-2); he is the preparer of the Soma juice that bestows immortality. Trita is one of the three deities named Ekata, Dvita and Trita; these three are sons of Agni; and Trita arose from water: so it is said in the Brāhmaṇas, in the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa 1-2-3-1, 2 and the Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa 3-2-8-10, 11 and elsewhere.

As stated in the Ṛg-veda, in three or four contexts Indra and Trita perform one and the same deed (the slaying of Rākṣasas). On one occasion Indra is the one who urges Trita on; in another place it is said that Indra himself is encouraged by Trita. Twice Indra has worked for Trita. As stated in the present Ṛk and as stated in the following Ṛk, Indra has slain for Trita the one named Viśvarūpa and also Arbuda. Trita seeks Agni; he kindles Agni in heaven; in the houses of men he dwells in place of Agni; his dwelling is hidden far away; Soma is there too. Indra drinks Soma, but Trita prepares the Soma. This is the difference between Indra and Trita.

From the points stated above, Trita may be called a lightning deity, that is, the atmospheric form of Agni; like the deities Agni, Vāyu, Indra and Sūrya he is a deity of the middle region. But since all the deeds of this deity have been done by Indra, he is a minor deity in the Ṛg-veda. It may be that the connexion between Trita and Soma is stated because, in the form of lightning, he brought Soma from heaven to earth. Since the evidence is not abundant, learned men have held one opinion each concerning him. To mention only a few: the scholar named Roth (Roth) has said that he is the deity of water and wind; the one named Hillbrandt (Hillbrandt) [as printed], that he is the deity of the kindled sky; the one named Perry (Perry), that he is the deity of the storm; another scholar, Pischel (Pischel), that he was first a deity of the sea and of water, afterwards a physician among men, and then obtained divinity. The one named Hardy (Hardy) has said that he is a moon deity.

In the present Ṛk, since Trita bears Indra's friendship, it is said that for his sake Indra slew the son of Tvaṣṭṛ named Viśvarūpa.

Āryeṇa dasyūn: the explanation of the meaning of the word *dasyu* and other matters concerning the Dasyus we have set out on pages 580 to 582 of this Part.

Viśvarūpam: the matter of Indra's slaying the one named Viśvarūpa is stated at the beginning of the fifth Prapāṭhaka of the second Kāṇḍa of the Taittirīya-saṃhitā. In the Ṛg-veda also, on this matter,

### Printed p. 627

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 627.*

⟦ಭೂರೀದಿಂದ್ರ ಉದಿನಕ್ಷಂತಮೋಜೋಽವಾಭಿನತ್ಸತ್ಪತಿರ್ಮನ್ಯಮಾನಂ ।
ತ್ವಾಷ್ಟ್ರಸ್ಯ ಚಿದ್ವಿಶ್ವರೂಪಸ್ಯ ಗೋನಾಮಾಚಕ್ರಾಣಸ್ತ್ರೀಣಿ ಶೀರ್ಷಾ ಪರಾ ವರ್ಕ್ ॥⟧ (Ṛg-veda-saṃhitā 10-8-9)

in this Ṛk it is said that Indra cut off the three heads of the one named Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ. That Indra did this deed for Trita, and that Trita helped Indra in this matter, is stated in the seventh Ṛk of the same sūkta:

⟦ಅಸ್ಯ ತ್ರಿತಃ ಕ್ರತುನಾ ವವ್ರೇ ಅಂತರಿಚ್ಛನ್ ಧೀತಿಂ ಪಿತುರೇವೈಃ ಪರಸ್ಯ ।
ಸಚಸ್ಯಮಾನಃ ಪಿತ್ರೋರುಪಸ್ಥೇ ಜಾಮಿ ಬ್ರುವಾಣ ಆಯುಧಾನಿ ವೇತಿ ।
ಸ ಪಿತ್ರಾಣ್ಯಾಯುಧಾನಿ ವಿದ್ವಾನಿಂದ್ರೆಷಿತ ಆಪ್ತ್ಯೋ ಅಭ್ಯಯುಧ್ಯತ್ ।
ತ್ರಿಶೀರ್ಷಾಣಂ ಸಪ್ತರಶ್ಮಿಂ ಜಘನ್ವಾನ್ ತ್ವಾಷ್ಟ್ರಸ್ಯ ಚಿನ್ನಿಃ ಸಸ್ಯಜೇ ತ್ರಿತೋ ಗಾಃ ॥⟧ (Ṛg-veda-saṃhitā 10-8-7, 8)

In the commentary on these Ṛks Sāyaṇa has told a story: Indra asked the help of Trita to kill Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, who had three heads. Trita, in order to increase his own strength, asked as a boon that a share of the oblation in the sacrifices be given to him. Indra agreed and gave him water for washing the hands and a share of the sacrifice. Then Trita, equipped with the weapons that belonged to his father and urged on by Indra, fought with Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, struck him hard with his weapons and made him swoon. Afterwards Indra cut off the three heads of that Viśvarūpa; so it is said in the Ṛks above.

This same matter is told at length in the Taittirīya-saṃhitā (Taittirīya-saṃhitā 2-5-1-1). That too is set out here with its meaning:

⟦ವಿಶ್ವರೂಪೋ ವೈ ತ್ವಾಷ್ಟ್ರಃ ಪುರೋಹಿತೋ ದೇವಾನಾಮಾಸೀತ್ ಸ್ವಸ್ರೀಯೋಽಸುರಾಣಾಂ ತಸ್ಯ
ತ್ರೀಣಿ ಶೀರ್ಷಾಣ್ಯಾಸನ್ತ್ಸೋಮಪಾನಗ್ಂ ಸುರಾಪಾನಮನ್ನಾದನಗ್ಂ ಸ ಪ್ರತ್ಯಕ್ಷಂ ದೇವೇ.
ಭ್ಯೋ ಭಾಗಮವದತ್ ಪರೋಕ್ಷಮಸುರೇಭ್ಯಃ ॥⟧

In former times Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, was the family priest of the gods. Since he was the son of the Asuras' sister, he had affection also for the Asuras. This Viśvarūpa had three heads (heads). With one of those three heads he drank Soma, with another he drank Surā (liquor), and with the third head he ate food and the like. While matters stood so, this Viśvarūpa used to say openly before all that he would procure for the gods their share of the oblation in the sacrifice; but within, unknown to anyone, he helped the Asuras.

### Printed p. 628

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 11; p. 628.*

सर्वस्मै वै प्रत्यक्षं भागं वदंति यस्मा एव परोक्षं वदंति तस्य भाग उदितस्तस्मादिंद्रोऽबिभेदीदृज् वै राष्ट्रं विपर्यावर्तयतीति तस्य वज्रपादाय शीर्षाण्यच्छिनत् ॥ (*sarvasmai vai pratyakṣaṃ bhāgaṃ vadaṃti yasmā eva parokṣaṃ vadaṃti tasya bhāga uditastasmādiṃdro 'bibhedīdṛj vai rāṣṭraṃ viparyāvartayatīti tasya vajrapādāya śīrṣāṇyacchinat ‖*)

People promise a share to everyone openly, face to face; but those who actually fulfil the promise are very rare. If a share is promised secretly (in private, so as to be trusted), that word is certainly kept. Therefore Indra, seeing Viśvarūpa's secret dealing, became afraid: fearing that he would betray him and hand the kingdom over to others (the Asuras), he took his thunderbolt and cut off all three heads of Viśvarūpa.

यत्सोमपानमासीत्स कपिंजलोऽभवद्यत्सुरापानग्ं स कलविंको यदन्नादनग्ं स तित्तिरिस्तस्यांजलिना ब्रह्महत्यामुपागृह्णात्ताग्ं संवत्सरमबिभस्तं भूतान्यभ्यक्रोशन् ब्रह्महन्निति ॥ (*yatsomapānamāsītsa kapiṃjalo 'bhavadyatsurāpānagṃ sa kalaviṃko yadannādanagṃ sa tittiristasyāṃjalinā brahmahatyāmupāgṛhṇāttāgṃ saṃvatsaramabibhastaṃ bhūtānyabhyakrośan brahmahanniti ‖*)

Of the three heads cut off by Indra, the one that used to drink Soma became the bird called Kapiñjala (hazecock); the second, which used to drink Surā, became the Kalaviṅka, that is the sparrow; the third, which used to eat food, became the bird called Tittiri (partridge). Since Indra had killed Viśvarūpa, a Brāhmaṇa, the fault of brahmin-slaying fell upon him, and Indra had to take it upon his own hands. So he bore the fault of brahmin-slaying for one year. All the creatures, seeing him (Indra), cried out: "O Indra, you are a slayer of a Brāhmaṇa."

स पृथिवीमुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्महत्यायै तृतीयं प्रतिगृहाणेति साब्रवीद्वरं वृणै खातात्पराभविष्यंती मन्ये ततो मा पराभूवमिति पुरा ते संवत्सरादपिरोहादित्यब्रवीत्तस्मात्पुरा संवत्सरात्पृथिव्यै खातमपिरोहति वारेवृतग्ग् ह्यस्यै तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णात्तत्त्वकृतमिरिणमभवत्तस्मादाहिताग्निः श्रद्धादेवः स्वकृत इरिणे नावस्येद्ब्रह्महत्यायै ह्येष वर्णः ॥ (*sa pṛthivīmupāsīdadasyai brahmahatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ pratigṛhāṇeti sābravīdvaraṃ vṛṇai khātātparābhaviṣyaṃtī manye tato mā parābhūvamiti purā te saṃvatsarādapirohādityabravīttasmātpurā saṃvatsarātpṛthivyai khātamapirohati vārevṛtagg hyasyai tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇāttattvakṛtamiriṇamabhavattasmādāhitāgniḥ śraddhādevaḥ svakṛta iriṇe nāvasyedbrahmahatyāyai hyeṣa varṇaḥ ‖*)

Seeing this, Indra (ashamed) went to Pṛthivī (the Earth) to be rid of the fault of brahmin-slaying and said: "O Earth, take one third of this brahmin-slaying that has come upon me." The Earth replied: "Then I shall ask a boon: people dig the earth and make pits, and that is a disgrace to me. Grant me a boon that this may not be so." Indra said: "So be it. Let a pit so dug, without any effort, within one year

### Printed p. 629

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 629.*

close up of itself, and let the earth become level as before." Thus he gave the boon. Therefore a pit dug in the earth closes up of itself before a year has passed. This is the boon the Earth asked for. Having received such a boon, the Earth took one third of Indra's fault of brahmin-slaying; thus it became natural to the Earth. Therefore a man who has kept the sacred fires, performed sacrifices and placed faith and devotion in the gods must not choose a dug pit in the earth as the sacrificial altar for a sacrifice, because such a dug pit is the sign (varṇa) of the brahmin-slaying that the Earth took upon herself.

स वनस्पतीनुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्महत्यायै तृतीयं प्रतिगृह्णीतेति तेऽब्रुवन् वरं वृणामहै वृक्षात्पराभविष्यंतो मन्यामहे ततो मा पराभूमेत्याव्रश्चनाद्यो भूयागांस उत्तिष्ठानित्यब्रवीत्तस्मादाव्रश्चनाद्वृक्षाणां भूयाग्ंस उत्तिष्ठंति वारेवृतग्स् हैषां तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णन्स् निर्यासोऽभवत्तस्मान्निर्यासस्य नाश्यं ब्रह्महत्यायै ह्येष वर्णोऽथो खलु य एव लोहितो यो वाव्रश्चनान्निर्येषति तस्य नाश्यं काममन्यस्य ॥ (*sa vanaspatīnupāsīdadasyai brahmahatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ pratigṛhṇīteti te 'bruvan varaṃ vṛṇāmahai vṛkṣātparābhaviṣyaṃto manyāmahe tato mā parābhūmetyāvraścanādyo bhūyāgāṃsa uttiṣṭhānityabravīttasmādāvraścanādvṛkṣāṇāṃ bhūyāgṃsa uttiṣṭhaṃti vārevṛtags haiṣāṃ tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇans niryāso 'bhavattasmānniryāsasya nāśyaṃ brahmahatyāyai hyeṣa varṇo 'tho khalu ya eva lohito yo vāvraścanānniryeṣati tasya nāśyaṃ kāmamanyasya ‖*)

Then Indra went near the trees and said: "O trees, take one third of my fault of brahmin-slaying." The trees replied: "Then we ask a boon: people cut the branches and so on of a tree, and that brings us harm. Grant that it may not be so." Indra said: "Very well; when a branch is cut, let more shoots sprout around the cut part." So he gave the boon. Therefore, as the trees asked, when branches are cut, more shoots sprout at that place. Then the trees, as they had said, accepted one third of Indra's brahmin-slaying. The sap that comes out when a tree is cut is the sign of this brahmin-slaying. Therefore no one should drink the sap that flows out of a cut tree, because it is the colour of brahmin-slaying. Or, if the sap flowing from the cutting of a tree is red, it should not be drunk; if it is not so, it may be drunk if one wishes.

स स्त्रीषग्ंसादमुपासीददस्यै ब्रह्महत्यायै तृतीयं प्रतिगृह्णीतेति ता अब्रुवन्वरं वृणामहा ऋत्वियात् प्रजां विंदामहै काममाविजनितोः संभवामेति तस्मादृत्वियात् स्त्रियः प्रजां विंदंते काममा विजनितोः संभवंति वारेवृतग्स् ह्यासां तृतीयं ब्रह्महत्यायै प्रत्यगृह्णन्ता मलवद्वासा अभवत् ॥ (*sa strīṣagṃsādamupāsīdadasyai brahmahatyāyai tṛtīyaṃ pratigṛhṇīteti tā abruvanvaraṃ vṛṇāmahā ṛtviyāt prajāṃ viṃdāmahai kāmamāvijanitoḥ saṃbhavāmeti tasmādṛtviyāt striyaḥ prajāṃ viṃdaṃte kāmamā vijanitoḥ saṃbhavaṃti vārevṛtags hyāsāṃ tṛtīyaṃ brahmahatyāyai pratyagṛhṇantā malavadvāsā abhavat ‖*)

### Printed p. 630

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 11; p. 630.*

Next Indra went to the company of women and said to them: "O women, take the remaining third of my fault of brahmin-slaying." The women replied: "Then we ask a boon: after our monthly course (after the ritual bath) we should bear children, and until delivery we should be able to find satisfaction of desire in union, if we wish." Indra said: "So be it." Therefore women obtain offspring after the course and, until delivery, have union at their wish. For this is the boon they asked. Then those women took one third of Indra's brahmin-slaying. The flow (rajas) that comes when women have their course is the sign of this brahmin-slaying. At the time of the flow (the course) women are impure (unclean).

तस्मान्मलवद्वाससा न संवदेत न सहासीत नास्या अन्नमद्याद्ब्रह्महत्यायै हैषा वर्णं प्रतिमुच्यास्तेऽथो खल्वाहुरभ्यंजनं वाव स्त्रिया अन्नमभ्यंजनमेव न प्रतिगृह्यं काममन्यदिति ॥ (*tasmānmalavadvāsasā na saṃvadeta na sahāsīta nāsyā annamadyādbrahmahatyāyai haiṣā varṇaṃ pratimucyāste 'tho khalvāhurabhyaṃjanaṃ vāva striyā annamabhyaṃjanameva na pratigṛhyaṃ kāmamanyaditi ‖*)

Therefore one should not speak with a woman having her course, nor sit with her, nor eat food given or touched by her; for she wears the sign of brahmin-slaying, the impure flow. During the course no oil-anointing and the like should be done, and no one should accept food and the like from her (touched by her).

यां मलवद्वाससग्ं संभवंति यस्ततो जायते सोऽभिशस्तो यामरण्ये तस्यै स्तेनो यां पराचीं तस्यै ह्रीतमुख्यपगल्भो या स्नाति तस्या अप्पु मारुको याभ्यंक्ते तस्यैदुश्चर्मा या प्रलिखते तस्यै खलतिरपमारी यांक्ते तस्यै काणो या दतो धावते तस्यै श्यावदन् या नखानि निकृंतते तस्यै कुनखी या कृणत्ति तस्यै क्लीबो या रज्जुग्ं सृजति तस्या उद्बंधुको या पर्णॆन पिबति तस्या उन्मादुको या खर्वॆण पिबति तस्यै खर्वस्तिस्रो रात्रीर्वतं चरेदंजलिना वा पिबेदखर्वण वा पात्रेण प्रजाय्कॆ गोपीथाय ॥ (*yāṃ malavadvāsasagṃ saṃbhavaṃti yastato jāyate so 'bhiśasto yāmaraṇye tasyai steno yāṃ parācīṃ tasyai hrītamukhyapagalbho yā snāti tasyā appu māruko yābhyaṃkte tasyaiduścarmā yā pralikhate tasyai khalatirapamārī yāṃkte tasyai kāṇo yā dato dhāvate tasyai śyāvadan yā nakhāni nikṛṃtate tasyai kunakhī yā kṛṇatti tasyai klībo yā rajjugṃ sṛjati tasyā udbaṃdhuko yā parṇèna pibati tasyā unmāduko yā kharvèṇa pibati tasyai kharvastisro rātrīrvataṃ caredaṃjalinā vā pibedakharvaṇa vā pātreṇa prajāykè gopīthāya ‖*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-1-1 to 7)

If one has union with a woman during her course, the son born of it becomes cursed; if a woman in her course has union in the forest, the son born to her becomes a thief; if the union is with the face covered or turned away, the son born becomes a bashful fellow who from shame cannot speak properly with anyone; to a woman who bathes during the course a son is born who will die drowned in water; (the text breaks off here, at the end of p. 630, in the middle of the account of the woman who anoints herself with oil)

### Printed p. 631

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 631.*

...a woman who plaits (her hair) at that time bears a son afflicted with a skin disease (leprosy, white patches); a woman who combs her hair bears a son who is bald, with no hair on his head, and weak. A woman who applies collyrium to her eyes bears a son who becomes blind; a woman who cleans her teeth at that time bears a son with foul teeth; a woman who cuts her nails bears a son whose nails are bad and ugly to look at; a woman who weaves (mats and the like) at that time bears a son who becomes impotent; a woman who twists rope bears a son who dies by suicide; a woman who drinks water from leaves (lotus leaves and the like) bears a son who becomes a drunkard; a woman who drinks water from a broken or cracked vessel bears a son who is deformed or has some defect of limb. Therefore women, while in their course, must observe the vow (rule) very strictly until three nights have passed. To obtain good offspring, women drinking water at that time should drink only from the hollow of the hands or from a good, unbroken vessel.

In the matters stated above, the slaying of Viśvarūpa by Indra, the fault of brahmin-slaying that came upon Indra from that slaying, the acts Indra performed to remove that fault, and, as a result, the rule to be observed by women during their course, have been stated. In the present context only the account of the slaying of Viśvarūpa is needed, but the other matters connected with it and found in the same anuvāka are also mentioned here. For in the Purāṇas and similar works it is said that the fault of brahmin-slaying came upon Indra from the slaying of Vṛtra and that Indra made efforts to remove it as described above; so this explanation became necessary to show the difference between the details of the Veda and those of the Purāṇas and the like.

#### Ṛk 20

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒स्य सु॑वा॒नस्य॑ मु॒ंदिन॑स्त्रि॒तस्य॒ न्यर्बु॑दं वावृधा॒नो अ॑स्तः ।
> अव॑र्तय॒त्सूर्यो॒ न च॒क्रं भि॒नद्व॒लमिंद्रो॒ अंगि॑रस्वान्

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अस्य । सुवानस्य । मंदिनः । त्रितस्य । नि । अर्बुदं । ववृधानः । अस्तरित्यस्तः ।
> अवर्तयत् । सूर्यः । न । चक्रं । भिनत् । वलं । इंद्रः । अंगिरस्वान् ॥ २० ॥

> *asya | suvānasya | maṃdinaḥ | tritasya | ni | arbudaṃ | vavṛdhānaḥ | astarityastaḥ |*
> *avartayat | sūryaḥ | na | cakraṃ | bhinat | valaṃ | iṃdraḥ | aṃgirasvān ‖ 20 ‖*

### Printed p. 632

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 11; p. 632.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> मंदिनोऽस्य मदकरमिमं सुवानस्य सुतवतस्तितस्य तीर्णनमस्कस्य महर्षे-
> रर्थ० वावृधानः सोमेन वर्धमान इंद्रोऽर्बुदं । अंबूनि ददातीत्यर्बुदो
> मेघः । अनुस्वारस्य रेफश्रांदसः । यद्वा नामैतत् । अर्बुदाख्यमसुरं न्यस्तः ॥
> स्त्र हिंसायामित्यस्य लजि तिपि बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् । गुणे कृते हल्वा-
> दिना तिलोपः ॥ नितरामवधीत् । महांतं चिदर्बुदं नि क्रमीः पदा । १-५१-६ ॥
> इत्यादिष्वर्बुदवधः स्पष्टमुक्तः । किंच अंगिरस्वान् अंगिरोभिः सहित इंद्रोS-
> वर्तयत् । असुरहननार्थ० वज्रमभ्रामयत् । सूर्यो न । यथा सूर्यश्चक्रं
> भ्रामयति तद्वत् । यद्वा यस्मादसुराद्भिया सूर्यः स्वकीयं रथचक्रं नावर्त-
> यत् । निषेधार्थियोऽत्र नकारः । वर्तयितुं नाशकत् । तं वलं पणीनां प्रभु-
> मसुरं भिनत् । वज्रेणाभिनत् । यद्वा द्विचक्रो हि पूर्व० सूर्यस्य रथः । तस्यैकं
> चक्रमिंद्रॆणापहृतं । सूर्य इति सुपां सुलुगिति षष्ठेकवचनस्य सुः । सूर्यस्य
> संबंध्यपहृतं तदेकं चक्रमवर्तयत् । रक्षोहननार्थमभ्रामयत् । तेन चक्रेण
> वलमभिनच्च । नश्चार्थे ॥

*Gist:* Indra, grown strong by the Soma of the pressing Trita, slew the Arbuda (cloud or asura); with the Aṅgirases he whirled his bolt as the sun whirls its wheel, and split Vala. The bhāṣya also offers a second sense, that Indra whirled the one wheel taken from the Sun's two-wheeled chariot, and gives its grammar.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

मंदिनः (*maṃdinaḥ*) (as printed in this section; the Pada reads another spelling)—intoxicating. अस्य (*asya*)—of this Soma juice. सुवानस्य (*suvānasya*)—that is pressed. श्रितस्य (*śritasya*) (as printed)—for the sage called Trita. वावृधानः (*vāvṛdhānaḥ*)—having grown (by drinking the Soma juice), you. अर्बुदं (*arbudaṃ*)—the cloud, or the asura called Arbuda. न्यस्तः (*nyastaḥ*)—destroyed completely. सूरो न चक्रं (*sūro na cakraṃ*) (as printed)—as the sun turns his wheel. अंगिरस्वान् (*aṃgirasvān*)—joined with the sages called Aṅgiras. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra. अवर्तयत् (*avartayat*)—whirled the bolt. वलं (*valaṃ*)—Vala, the asura who is lord of the Paṇis. भिनत् (*bhinat*)—split.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

You, who have grown by the Soma juice, drunk from the intoxicating Soma pressed for the sage Trita, slew the asura called Arbuda. As the sun turns his wheel, Indra, joined with the sages called Aṅgiras, whirled his bolt and split the asura called Vala, lord of the Paṇis.

**English Translation (as printed)**

Invigorated(by the libation) of the exulting Trita, offering you the Soma, you have annihilated Arbuda: Indra aided by the Angirasas, has whirled round his bolt, as the sun turns round his wheel, and slain Vala.

### Printed p. 633

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; p. 633.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अर्बुदं (*arbudaṃ*)—अर्बुदं । अंबूनि ददातीत्यर्बुदो मेघः । यद्वा नामैतत् । अर्बुदाख्यमसुरं । (*arbudaṃ | aṃbūni dadātītyarbudo meghaḥ | yadvā nāmaitat | arbudākhyamasuraṃ |*) Because it gives water (pours down water in the form of rain), the cloud is called *arbuda*; or it is the name of a certain rākṣasa called Arbuda: the bhāṣyakāra gives these two kinds of meaning. For the word *arbuda* Yāska gives this derivation:

अर्बुदो मेघो भवति । अरणमंबु तद्दोऽमृदः । अंबुमद्भातीति वा । अंबुमद्भवतीति वा । स यथा महान् बहुर्भवति वर्षंस्तदिवार्बुदं ॥ (*arbudo megho bhavati | araṇamaṃbu taddo 'mṛdaḥ | aṃbumadbhātīti vā | aṃbumadbhavatīti vā | sa yathā mahān bahurbhavati varṣaṃstadivārbudaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 3-10)

*Arbuda* means a cloud. *Araṇam* means "of a moving nature", and *ambu* is water, since it has a flowing nature; *araṇam* or *ambu* means water. *Tad-daḥ*: because it gives that (water), it is *ambuda* or *arbuda*, that is, a cloud. It is well known that when a cloud pours rain it pours a great deal of water. As that water is plentiful, so the cloud-word *arbuda* is also a word for a large number: this is the explanation of the word. As a number-word, *arbuda* means a hundred crores. In the present context this meaning is not needed.

For the word *arbuda* in the sense of an asura of this name:

महांतं चिदर्बुदं नि क्रमीः पदा सनादेव दस्युहत्याय बज्जिषे । (*mahāṃtaṃ cidarbudaṃ ni kramīḥ padā sanādeva dasyuhatyāya bajjiṣe |*) (Mā. Saṃ. 1-51-6, as printed)

यो अर्बुदमव नीचा बबाधे तमिंद्रं सोमस्य भृथे हिनोत । (*yo arbudamava nīcā babādhe tamiṃdraṃ somasya bhṛthe hinota |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-4)

अहन् वृत्रमृजीषम टीर्णवाभमहीशुवं । हिमेनाविध्यदर्बुदं । (*ahan vṛtramṛjīṣama ṭīrṇavābhamahīśuvaṃ | himenāvidhyadarbudaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-32-26)

निरबर्बुदस्य मृगयस्य मायिनो निः पर्वतस्य गा आजः । (*nirabarbudasya mṛgayasya māyino niḥ parvatasya gā ājaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-3-19)

न्यर्बुदस्य विष्टपं वर्षाणं बृहतस्तिर । कृषे तदिंद्र पौंस्यं ॥ (*nyarbudasya viṣṭapaṃ varṣāṇaṃ bṛhatastira | kṛṣe tadiṃdra pauṃsyaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-32-3)

इंद्रो मह्ना महतो अर्णवस्य वि मूर्धानमभिनदर्बुदस्य । (*iṃdro mahnā mahato arṇavasya vi mūrdhānamabhinadarbudasya |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-67-12)

### Printed p. 634

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 11; p. 634.*

In these Ṛks there are descriptions that Indra killed an asura called Arbuda. But since the name of Arbuda is mentioned in the Ṛgveda only in the Ṛks indicated above, there is no basis for learning more about him.

Besides the asura, the word *arbuda* also means a sage. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 26-1) it is said अर्बुदनामकः काद्रवेयः कश्चिदृषि (*arbudanāmakaḥ kādraveyaḥ kaścidṛṣi*)—that is, a certain sage born of the woman Kadrū, belonging to the serpent race. A story is told that this sage performed the office of the *grāvastut*, one of the Hotṛ group of priests (*grāva* is the stone that crushes the Soma plant; the priest who, with mantras, praises it at the time of the Soma pressing in the sacrifice is called *grāvastut*). Since that matter is not relevant here, it will be explained in its own context.

अवर्तयत् सूर्यः न चक्रं (*avartayat sūryaḥ na cakraṃ*)—here असुरहननार्थं वज्रमभ्रामयत् सूर्यो न यथा सूर्यश्चक्रं भ्रामयति तद्वत् (*asurahananārthaṃ vajramabhrāmayat sūryo na yathā sūryaścakraṃ bhrāmayati tadvat*); that is, Indra whirled his bolt for the killing of the asura, as the sun whirls his wheel, or whirled it and struck. The meaning stated is this. Here the word *na* is used in the sense of comparison. Or else (यद्वा यस्मादसुराद्भिया सूर्यः स्वकीयं रथचक्रं नावर्तयत् वर्तयितुं नाशकत् निषेधार्थियोऽत्र नकारः (*yadvā yasmādasurādbhiyā sūryaḥ svakīyaṃ rathacakraṃ nāvartayat vartayituṃ nāśakat niṣedhārthiyo 'tra nakāraḥ*)): the sun, out of fear of this asura, did not turn his own chariot wheel (was unable to turn it); this is another kind of meaning. Here the word *na* is used in the sense of negation. Or else (यद्वा द्विचक्रो हि पूर्वं सूर्यस्य रथः तस्यैकं चक्रमिंद्रॆणापहृतं सूर्यस्य संबंध्यपहृतं तदेकं चक्रमवर्तयत् रक्षोहननार्थमभ्रामयत् तेन चक्रेण वलमभिनच्च नश्चार्थे (*yadvā dvicakro hi pūrvaṃ sūryasya rathaḥ tasyaikaṃ cakramiṃdrèṇāpahṛtaṃ sūryasya saṃbaṃdhyapahṛtaṃ tadekaṃ cakramavartayat rakṣohananārthamabhrāmayat tena cakreṇa valamabhinacca naścārthe*)): the sun's chariot formerly had two wheels; Indra, for some other reason, carried off one of them, and with that stolen wheel he whirled it forcefully and struck, in order to destroy the rākṣasa called Vala; this is yet another kind of meaning. Here the word *na* is used in the sense of *ca*, "and". In this way Sāyaṇa has given three kinds of meaning for this sentence.

There is a somewhat obscure story. A race took place between the horse called Etaśa and the horses of the Sun called Haritas, and the Sun himself goes ahead. Then Indra obstructs the Sun. At this time one wheel of the Sun's chariot is lost. It may be stated here that Indra struck and killed Vala with that very wheel.

वलं (*valaṃ*)—concerning Vala, the bhāṣyakāra has told an itihāsa earlier. The asura called Vala had carried off the cows of the gods and hidden them in a cave. Then Indra, with the help of his army,

### Printed p. 635

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; p. 635 (printed 685 [sic]).*

surrounded that cave and freed the cows that were in it. This matter is described in the Yajurveda, Taittirīya Saṃhitā 2-1-1-1, and here and there in other mantras of the Ṛgveda. In the Ṛgveda the matter of this Vala is mentioned in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-62-4; 2-11-20; 2-14-3; 2-15-8; 2-12-3; 2-24-3; 3-30-10; 3-34-10; 40-50-5 (as printed); 6-18-5; 6-39-2; 8-14-7; 8-24-30; 10-62-2; 10-67-6; 10-68-6, 9, 10; 10-138-1.

It is customary to call the soldiers of this Vala the Paṇis. In many places it is said that they stole cows and hid them in caves; on this subject we have written at length at Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 2, page two hundred twenty-eight; and at the places given there as three-six-thirty, five-six-ninety-eight, six-fifteen, ten-one-seventy-five and thirteen-five-thirty-six (page references printed with Latin digits).

These Paṇis are great misers. Even when asked for the sake of sacrifices and the like, they do not give, and hide the cows. Saramā, the divine bitch who is Indra's messenger, finds the place where the cows are and tells them to give them back. The Paṇis mock her. These and similar matters are told at length in Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-108. In another place it is said that Indra, with the desire to obtain the cows, broke Vala's fortress, defeated the Paṇis and set the cows free (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-39-2). In yet another place (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-12-3; 3-30-10) it is said that Vala had held the cows shut up and Indra drove them out; here the name of the Paṇis does not occur at all. In many places—in the present Ṛk also—there are descriptions that the Aṅgirases were Indra's helpers in this defeat of Vala and in the act of releasing the cows.

अंगिरस्वान् (*aṃgirasvān*)—who the Aṅgirā sages are, what the course of their origin is, and similar matters, we have explained at length in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 3, pages five hundred twenty to five hundred twenty-two; and details concerning them we have written at Ṛ. Saṃ. Part six-three-sixty-five, eight-one-eighty-seven, nine-two-fifteen, ten-two-thirty-nine and ten-five-twenty-two (page references printed with Latin digits).

These Aṅgirases are sons of Agni (10-62-4); it is also thought that one Aṅgiras among them is the original ancestor. In many mantras (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-14-5, 6, etc.) the Aṅgirases are called Pitṛs (Fathers). In particular Yama and the Aṅgirases are regarded as the Pitṛs. Together with the Ādityas, Vasus and Maruts, Soma is offered to the Aṅgirases (7-44-4; 8-35-14), and they are praised like gods (3-53-7; 10 6-2 [as printed]). In the sacrifice they hold the place of the priest called Brahman (7-42-1); they discovered Agni, who was hidden in the forests (5-11-6); and they reflected on and determined the first rule relating to the sacrifice (10-67-2). Through the sacrifice itself they gained immortality and friendship with Indra (10-621 [as printed]); such descriptions occur. The matter of their performing a sacrifice is told in Tai. Brā. 2-1-1.

### Printed p. 636

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 11; p. 636.*

Indra and they have a very close relationship. It was for them that Indra showed the cows (8-52-3); for them Indra opened the doors of the cowsheds (1-51-3); accompanied by them Indra pierced and killed Vala (2-11-20) and drove the cows out (6-17-6); with the idea that he is their leader, Indra is called *aṅgirastama* on two occasions (1-100-4; 1-130-3); similarly Agni is called the leader of the Aṅgirases (1-31-1, 2); the Soma that gives Indra zeal is also said to have opened the doors of the cowsheds for the Aṅgirases (9-86-23); in the episode of the release of the cows the singing of the Aṅgirases is peculiar: praised by them, Indra pierced Vala (2-15-8), broke the doors of the cowsheds (4-16-18), and, after killing Vala, opened the doors of his fortress (6-18-5); the singing of the Maruts, who sing in many ways, is compared to the singing of the Aṅgirases (10-78-5); and it is said that the gods are invited to the sacrifice by the Sāman songs of the Aṅgirases (1-107-2). The praise-words uttered by ordinary priests are compared to the praise of the Aṅgirases (1-62-1, 2, etc.); keeping Indra with them, the Aṅgirases shut up the cow-stalls and horse-stalls (10-62-2) [as printed]; by the power of the rites they performed, they drove the cows out and pierced and killed Vala (10-62-2); by the power of the sacrificial rite they performed, they broke the rock and, together with the cows, shouted with joy and gladness (4-3-11); with their songs they broke the rock and saw the light (1-72-2); in the episode where Saramā searched for the cows carried off by the asura called Paṇi, for the sake of Indra, the Aṅgirases also have a part (10-108-8, 10); and it is also said that the Aṅgirases themselves discovered the cows and horses hidden by the Paṇis (1-83-4).

Bṛhaspati, too, is included in the release of the cows connected with the Paṇis (10-108-6, 10); when he breaks the mountain and brings the cows, he too has the epithet Aṅgirasa. On many occasions Agni is called by the word *aṅgira*. When, staying with Indra, he drove out the cows and made the waters flow, Bṛhaspati is called Aṅgirasa (2-23-18). Apart from this one occasion, all other uses of the word *aṅgirasa* in the singular refer to Agni. Agni is called the first among the Aṅgirā sages (1-31-1), the ancient Aṅgiras (the tenth Maṇḍala, Sūkta 92, Ṛk 15, and the first Maṇḍala, Sūkta 127, Ṛk 2; the print joins them with a colon, which the checker reads as one reference, 10-92-15-1-127-2), one with much inspiration (6-11-3), and the chief among the Aṅgirases (1-75-2), and so on.

The Aṅgirases are also included in the company of the Pitṛs (1-139-1); in the Anukramaṇikā it is said that the Aṅgirases belong to the family of the purohitas. For the seers of the ninth Maṇḍala, and in the phrase Atharva-Aṅgirasa too, they themselves

### Printed p. 637

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 637.*

are indicated. The Atharvaveda is also named Atharvāṅgirasa (A. Ve. 10-7-20). The same view is found in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (Śa. Brā. 11-5-6-7, etc.).

The name of the Virūpas, who have a connection with the Aṅgirases, is mentioned in the plural (*virūpāsaḥ*) in three places (3-53-7; 10-62-5, 6). It is said that the Aṅgirases and the Virūpas are sons of the sky-deity (*divas putrāsaḥ*) (3-53-7). It is also said there that the Virūpas and the Aṅgirases are sons of Agni. Sāyaṇa explains the word *virūpāsaḥ* as "having various forms" and construes it as an epithet of the Aṅgirases. It is not clear whether, as Sāyaṇa says, the *virūpāsaḥ* are the Aṅgirases themselves, having various forms, or other sages by the name Virūpa.

Besides the descriptions given so far, many other matters concerning the Aṅgirases are mentioned here and there. They will be discussed as occasion arises.

#### Ṛk 21

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> नू॒नं सा ते॒ प्रति॒ वरं॑ जरि॒त्रे दु॑ही॒यदिं॑द्र॒दक्षि॑णा म॒घोनी॑।
> शिक्षा॑ स्तो॒तृभ्यो॒ माति॑ द॒ग्भगो॑ नो बृ॒हद्व॑देम वि॒दथे॑ सु॒वीराः॑ ॥ २१ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> नूनं । सा । ते । प्रति । वरं । जरित्रे । दुहीयत् । इंद्र । दक्षिणा । मघोनी ।
> शिक्ष । स्तोतृभ्यः । मा । अति । धक् । भगः । नः । बृहत् । वदेम । विदथे । सुवीराः॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अहर्गणेषु माध्यंदिनसवनेषु तायमानरूपेष्वच्छावाकस्याहरहः शस्यसंज्ञक-
> मभितष्ट्वेवेति सूक्तं । नूनं सा त इत्येषा तस्यांते शंसनीया । सूत्रितं च । नूनं
> सा त अत्यंतमुत्तमं । आ. ७-४ । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र या दक्षिणा दक्षमत्साहनं करोतीति दक्षिणा स्तोतृभ्यो देया ते
> त्वत्संबंधिनी मघोनी धनवती जरित्रे स्तोत्रे वरं श्रेष्ठमभिमतमर्थं नूनं

> *ahargaṇeṣu mādhyaṃdinasavaneṣu tāyamānarūpeṣvacchāvākasyāharahaḥ śasyasaṃjñaka-*
> *mabhitaṣṭveveti sūktaṃ | nūnaṃ sā ta ityeṣā tasyāṃte śaṃsanīyā | sūtritaṃ ca | nūnaṃ*
> *sā ta atyaṃtamuttamaṃ | ā. 7-4 | iti ‖*
> *he iṃdra yā dakṣiṇā dakṣamatsāhanaṃ karotīti dakṣiṇā stotṛbhyo deyā te*
> *tvatsaṃbaṃdhinī maghonī dhanavatī jaritre stotre varaṃ śreṣṭhamabhimatamarthaṃ nūnaṃ*

### Printed p. 638

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 638.*

> प्रतिदुहीयत् । इदानीं प्रतिदोग्धि । संपादयतीत्यर्थः । तादृशीं दक्षिणां स्तोतृ-
> भ्योऽस्मभ्यं शिक्ष । प्रयच्छ । किंच भगो भजनीयस्त्वं माति धक् ॥ दहेर्दानार्थस्य
> लुज मंत्रे घसेत्यादिना चेर्लुक् ॥ नोऽस्मानतिक्रम्यान्येभ्यो दक्षिणां मा दाः ।
> प्रथममस्मभ्यं दत्वा पश्चादन्येभ्यो दीयतामित्यर्थः । यद्वा नोऽस्माकं कामान्
> मा धक् । मा धाक्षीः । अपेक्षितफलदानेन पूरयेत्यर्थः । सुवीराः शोभनपुत्र-
> पौत्राः संतो वयं विदथेऽस्मिन्यज्ञे बृहत् प्रभूतं स्तोत्रं वदेम । त्वामुद्दिश्य
> ब्रवाम ॥ अत्र सा ते प्रतिदुग्गामिति प्रकृत्य वीरो वीरयत्यमित्रान् वेतेर्वा
> स्याद्गतिकर्मणो वीरयतेर्वेत्यंतं निरुक्तमनुसंधेयं । नि- १-७ ॥

*Gist:* Indra is asked to give to us, his praisers, the same rich fee that fulfils the wishes of the sacrificer, not to pass us by, and to give it to us first; blessed with sons, we shall recite abundant praise at this sacrifice. The bhāṣya ends by referring to Yāska's Nirukta (Si. [sic] 1-7) on the word *vīra*.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra. ते (*te*)—given by you. मघोनी (*maghonī*)—possessing abundant wealth. सा दक्षिणा (*sā dakṣiṇā*)—that fee. जरित्रे (*jaritre*)—to the praising sacrificer. नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—certainly. वरं (*varaṃ*)—in the best way. प्रति दुहीयत् (*prati duhīyat*)—fulfils the desired object (such a fee). स्तोतृभ्यः (*stotṛbhyaḥ*)—also to us, the praisers. शिक्ष (*śikṣa*)—give. भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—you, the venerable. नः (*naḥ*)—us. माति धक् (*māti dhak*)—do not reject. सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*)—we, accompanied by excellent sons. विदथे (*vidathe*)—in this sacrifice. बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant praise. वदेम (*vadema*)—we recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, that fee, given by you and possessing abundant wealth, certainly fulfils in the best manner the desired object of the praising sacrificer. Give such a fee also to us, the praisers. Do not reject us, you who are venerable. Accompanied by excellent sons, we recite abundant praise at this sacrifice.

**Printed English**

That opulent donation, which proceeds, Indra, from you, assuredly bestows upon him who praises you the boon(which he desires): grant it to us, your praisers; do not you, who are the object of adoration, disregard(our prayer): so that, blessed with worthy descendants, we may glorify you at this sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*Mā ati dhak bhagaḥ naḥ:* अस्मानतिक्रम्यान्येभ्यो दक्षिणां मा दाः । प्रथममस्मभ्यं दत्वा पश्चादन्येभ्यो दीयतामित्यर्थः । यद्वा नोऽस्माकं कामान्’ मा (*asmānatikramyānyebhyo dakṣiṇāṃ mā dāḥ | prathamamasmabhyaṃ datvā paścādanyebhyo dīyatāmityarthaḥ | yadvā no 'smākaṃ kāmān’ mā*)

### Printed p. 639

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 6; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 639.*

धक् मा धाक्षीः । (*dhak mā dhākṣīḥ |*) This means: when giving such a fee, do not pass us over and give to others; or give to us first and afterwards to others; or do not reject us without giving our desired objects. Thus he has explained the meaning.

*Dakṣiṇā:* The word *dakṣiṇā* has many meanings. In sacrifices and in the performance of other good works, the goods, such as wealth, given as a reward for the work they have done to the officiating priests (*ṛtvij*) and purohitas, who take up the role for the sacrificer and carry out the tasks, are called *dakṣiṇā*. This fee may be in the form of wealth, or of useful things such as cows, or of jewels, a dwelling house, food and the like. The word *dakṣiṇā* also denotes a deity presiding over the fee; on this subject there is a whole sūkta in the Ṛgveda praising the fee, the sacrificer who gives it, the priests who receive it, and so on (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-107). Besides, here and there are Ṛks on this subject (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-18-5; 1-125-6). The daughter of Prajāpati also bears the name Dakṣiṇā. Yāska in his Nirukta has derived the meaning of the word *dakṣiṇā* thus:

दक्षिणा दक्षतेः समर्धयति कर्मणो व्युद्धं समर्थयतीति । अपि वा प्रदक्षिणागमनात् । दिशमभिप्रेत्य दिग्वस्तप्रकृतिः दक्षिणो हस्तो दक्षतेरुत्साहकर्मणः । दाशतेर्वा स्याद्दानकर्मणः ॥ (*dakṣiṇā dakṣateḥ samardhayati karmaṇo vyuddhaṃ samarthayatīti | api vā pradakṣiṇāgamanāt | diśamabhipretya digvastaprakṛtiḥ dakṣiṇo hasto dakṣaterutsāhakarmaṇaḥ | dāśatervā syāddānakarmaṇaḥ ‖*) (Si. [sic] 1-7)

and has given four kinds of meaning:

(1) दक्षिणा दक्षतेः समर्धयति कर्मणो व्युर्धं समर्धयतीति (*dakṣiṇā dakṣateḥ samardhayati karmaṇo vyurdhaṃ samardhayatīti*)—the word *dakṣiṇā* is formed from the root *dakṣ*. This root means "to increase (wealth)". Because it greatly increases (in the form of the fruit) the small wealth spent in the sacrifice, that is, given as fee, this word is called *dakṣiṇā*.

(2) अपि ना प्रदक्षिणागमनात् (*api nā pradakṣiṇāgamanāt*) (as printed; the quotation above reads अपि वा (*api vā*))—at the sacrificial altar one goes round the places called Gārhapatya, Sadas and Āgnīdhrīya in a clockwise circuit (*pradakṣiṇā*) and stands within the altar; the going round done in this way is called *dakṣiṇā*.

(3) दिशमभिप्रेत्य दिग्धस्तप्रकृतिः दक्षिणो हस्तो दक्षतेरुत्साहकर्मणः (*diśamabhipretya digdhastaprakṛtiḥ dakṣiṇo hasto dakṣaterutsāhakarmaṇaḥ*) (as printed; the quotation above reads दिग्वस्त (*digvasta*))—of the hands of Prajāpati, who faces east, the right hand is turned towards the south (the southern direction), and whatever work is to be done, the right hand does it with zeal; therefore it is customary to call the right hand by the word *dakṣiṇa*, as दक्षिणो हस्तः (*dakṣiṇo hastaḥ*).

(text of the supplied page ends here; the fourth meaning is not on this page)

### Printed p. 640

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 11; p. 640.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

(4) दाशतेर्वा स्याद्दानकर्मणः (*dāśatervā syāddānakarmaṇaḥ*)—the root *dāśati* means "to give a gift". Since in giving a gift the right hand (*dakṣiṇa-hasta*) is ordinarily used, the act of giving is called *dakṣiṇā*.

Of these four kinds of meaning, the last-mentioned one, the act of giving, is the meaning stated in this Ṛk.

The greatness of gift-giving in the form of *dakṣiṇā* is praised in many places in this way. In अयमात्मा दक्षिणान्नं वनुते यो न आत्मेति ह्यस्यृषिणाभ्युक्तं (*ayamātmā dakṣiṇānnaṃ vanute yo na ātmeti hyasyṛṣiṇābhyuktaṃ*) (Śa. Bhā. 10-12-10) it is said that the gift of *dakṣiṇā* secures and provides food. (Reference as printed: Śa. Bhā.)

Again:

य एष तपति तद्यॆत्तदादित्यो यशो यज्यो हैव तद्यशस्त्रद्यत्तदृत्वजो (*ya eṣa tapati tadyèttadādityo yaśo yajyo haiva tadyaśastradyattadṛtvajo*) यशो दक्षिणा हैव तद्यशस्त्रस्माद्यामस्मै दक्षिणामानयेयुर्न ता (*yaśo dakṣiṇā haiva tadyaśastrasmādyāmasmai dakṣiṇāmānayeyurna tā*) … द्योऽन्यस्मा अतिदिशेश् (*dyo 'nyasmā atidiśeś*) (Śa. Brā. 4-1.1-32; the word broken at the line-end "ಇತ್ಸ-ದ್ಯೋ" is as supplied). Since *dakṣiṇā* is itself a glory (*yaśas*), a blessing, it should not be rejected. And, as Sāyaṇa has cited,

ब्राह्मणा विविदिषंति यज्ञेन दानेन (*brāhmaṇā vividiṣaṃti yajñena dānena*) (Bṛ. U. 4-4-22)

—knowers of Brahman desire to become knowers (*jñānin*) by sacrifice and also by gift-giving; and

दानमिति सर्वाणि भूतानि प्रशग्ंसंति दानान्ना तिदुश्चरं तस्माद्दाने रमंते (*dānamiti sarvāṇi bhūtāni praśagṃsaṃti dānānnā tiduścaraṃ tasmāddāne ramaṃte*) (Tai. U. 4-100)

दानं यज्ञानां वरूथं दक्षिणा लोके दातारग्ं सर्वभूतान्युपजीवंति (*dānaṃ yajñānāṃ varūthaṃ dakṣiṇā loke dātāragṃ sarvabhūtānyupajīvaṃti*)

दानेनारातीरपानुदंत दानेन द्विषंतो मित्रा भवंति दाने सवस्ति (*dānenārātīrapānudaṃta dānena dviṣaṃto mitrā bhavaṃti dāne savasti*)

प्रतिष्ठितं तस्माद्दानं परमं वदंति (*pratiṣṭhitaṃ tasmāddānaṃ paramaṃ vadaṃti*) (Tai. U. 4-101)

That is: all people praise the act of giving. By giving gifts all undertakings are accomplished; nothing is impossible through it. Gift-giving is the means, like a chariot, by which a sacrifice proceeds well. In the world everyone lives depending on the giver. By gifts even enemies become friends. In gift-giving all the dharmas are established. Therefore they call gift-giving the highest of acts. Thus the praise of *dakṣiṇā*, or of gift-giving, is stated in many places. As said before, the eleven Ṛks of Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-107, beginning आविरभून्महि माघोनमेषां (*āvirabhūnmahi māghonameṣāṃ*), are wholly in praise of the *dakṣiṇā* and of those who give it; since writing all that out would be too lengthy, it will be explained on another occasion.

### Printed p. 641

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 641.*

*The twelfth sūkta.*

## Sūkta 12 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Introduction to the sūkta (Sanskrit, transcribed)**

> द्वितीयेऽनुवाक एकादश सूक्तानि । तत्र यो जात इति पंचदशर्चं प्रथमं
> सूक्तं गार्त्समदं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं ॥ संसवे निष्केवल्यॆ निविद्वानीयस्य पुरस्ताद्यो
> जात एवेति शंस्येत् । यदि पर्यायानिति खंडे सूत्रितं । यो जात एवेति निष्क-
> वल्यॆ । अ. ६-६ । इति ॥ अभिप्लविके तृतीयेऽहनि निष्केवल्यॆ यो जात एवेति निविद्दा-
> नीयं । सूत्रितं च । तृतीयस्य त्र्यर्यमा यो जात एवेति मध्यंदिनः । आ. ७-७ ।
> इति ॥ विश्वजिति माध्यंदिनसवने मैत्रावरुणः स्वशस्त्रे प्राकृतात्तूक्तूर्वं यो
> जात इति सामसूक्तं शंसेत् । विश्वजितोऽग्निं नर इति खंडे सूत्रितं । सत्रा मदासो
> यो जात एवाभूरेक इति सामसूक्तानि । अ. ८-७ । इति ॥ अग्निष्टुन्निष्केवल्यॆ निवि-
> द्धानमिदं । श्येनाजिराभ्यामिति खंडे सूत्रितं । तिष्ठा हरी इति यो जात एवेति
> मध्यंदिनः । आ. ९-७ । इति ॥ महाव्रते निष्केवल्यॆ यो जात एवेति सूक्तं । ऊरू
> इति खंडे सूत्रितं । वने न वा यो न्यधायि चाकन् यो जात एव प्रथमो मन-
> स्वान् । ऐ. आ. ५-३-१ इति ॥ अत्रेतिहासो बृहद्देवतायामुक्तः । संयुज्य तपसा-
> त्मानमैंद्रं बिभ्रह्नहद्वपुः । अदृश्यत मुहूर्तन दिवि च व्योम्मि चेह च ॥ तमिंद्र
> इति मत्वा तु दैत्यौ भीमपराक्रमौ । धुनिश्च चुमुरिश्चोभौ सायुधावभिपेततुः ॥
> विदित्वा स तयोर्भावमृषिः पापं चिकीर्षतोः । यो जात इति सूक्तेन कर्मा-
> ण्यॆंद्राण्यकीर्तयत् । अन्ये त्वन्यथा वर्णयंति । पुरा किलेंद्रादयो वैन्ययज्ञं
> समाजग्मुः । गृत्समदोऽपि तत्रागत्य सदस्यासीत् । दैत्याश्चेंद्रजिघांसया तत्र
> समागमन् । तान् दृष्ट्वा निर्जगामेंद्रो यज्ञादृत्समदाकृतिः । स च गृत्समदो
> वैन्यन पूजितो यज्ञवाटानिरगच्छत् । निर्गच्छंतं तमृषिं दृष्ट्वा अयमेवेंद्र
> इति मन्यमानास्तमसुराः परिववुः। नाहमिंद्रस्तुच्छः किंत्वेवंगुणोपेतः स इत्यनेन
> सूक्तेन तात्पृत्युवाच ॥ अपरे त्वेवं कथयंति । गृत्समदस्य यज्ञे प्रविष्टमेकाकिन-
> मिंद्रं ज्ञात्वासुराः परिववुः । स इंद्रो गृत्समदरूपेण यज्ञवाटान्निर्गत्य स्वर्गं
> जगाम । ततोऽसुरा इंद्रो विलंबित इत्यंतः प्रविश्य गृत्समदं दृष्ट्वा पूर्वमेव
> गृत्समदो गतः अयं त्विंद्रोऽस्मद्वयादृत्समदरूपेणास्त इति तं जगृहुः । स
> तान् नाहमिंद्रोयमित्यनेन सूक्तेन प्रत्युवाच । अयमेवार्थो महाभारते
> प्रपंचितः ॥

> *dvitīye 'nuvāka ekādaśa sūktāni | tatra yo jāta iti paṃcadaśarcaṃ prathamaṃ*
> *sūktaṃ gārtsamadaṃ traiṣṭubhamaiṃdraṃ ‖ saṃsave niṣkevalyè nividvānīyasya purastādyo*
> *jāta eveti śaṃsyet | yadi paryāyāniti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ | yo jāta eveti niṣka-*
> *valyè | a. 6-6 | iti ‖ abhiplavike tṛtīye 'hani niṣkevalyè yo jāta eveti nividdā-*
> *nīyaṃ | sūtritaṃ ca | tṛtīyasya tryaryamā yo jāta eveti madhyaṃdinaḥ | ā. 7-7 |*
> *iti ‖ viśvajiti mādhyaṃdinasavane maitrāvaruṇaḥ svaśastre prākṛtāttūktūrvaṃ yo*
> *jāta iti sāmasūktaṃ śaṃset | viśvajito 'gniṃ nara iti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ | satrā madāso*
> *yo jāta evābhūreka iti sāmasūktāni | a. 8-7 | iti ‖ agniṣṭunniṣkevalyè nivi-*
> *ddhānamidaṃ | śyenājirābhyāmiti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ | tiṣṭhā harī iti yo jāta eveti*
> *madhyaṃdinaḥ | ā. 9-7 | iti ‖ mahāvrate niṣkevalyè yo jāta eveti sūktaṃ | ūrū*
> *iti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ | vane na vā yo nyadhāyi cākan yo jāta eva prathamo mana-*
> *svān | ai. ā. 5-3-1 iti ‖ atretihāso bṛhaddevatāyāmuktaḥ | saṃyujya tapasā-*
> *tmānamaiṃdraṃ bibhrahnahadvapuḥ | adṛśyata muhūrtana divi ca vyommi ceha ca ‖ tamiṃdra*
> *iti matvā tu daityau bhīmaparākramau | dhuniśca cumuriścobhau sāyudhāvabhipetatuḥ ‖*
> *viditvā sa tayorbhāvamṛṣiḥ pāpaṃ cikīrṣatoḥ | yo jāta iti sūktena karmā-*
> *ṇyèṃdrāṇyakīrtayat | anye tvanyathā varṇayaṃti | purā kileṃdrādayo vainyayajñaṃ*
> *samājagmuḥ | gṛtsamado 'pi tatrāgatya sadasyāsīt | daityāśceṃdrajighāṃsayā tatra*
> *samāgaman | tān dṛṣṭvā nirjagāmeṃdro yajñādṛtsamadākṛtiḥ | sa ca gṛtsamado*
> *vainyana pūjito yajñavāṭāniragacchat | nirgacchaṃtaṃ tamṛṣiṃ dṛṣṭvā ayameveṃdra*
> *iti manyamānāstamasurāḥ parivavuḥ| nāhamiṃdrastucchaḥ kiṃtvevaṃguṇopetaḥ sa ityanena*
> *sūktena tātpṛtyuvāca ‖ apare tvevaṃ kathayaṃti | gṛtsamadasya yajñe praviṣṭamekākina-*
> *miṃdraṃ jñātvāsurāḥ parivavuḥ | sa iṃdro gṛtsamadarūpeṇa yajñavāṭānnirgatya svargaṃ*
> *jagāma | tato 'surā iṃdro vilaṃbita ityaṃtaḥ praviśya gṛtsamadaṃ dṛṣṭvā pūrvameva*
> *gṛtsamado gataḥ ayaṃ tviṃdro 'smadvayādṛtsamadarūpeṇāsta iti taṃ jagṛhuḥ | sa*
> *tān nāhamiṃdroyamityanena sūktena pratyuvāca | ayamevārtho mahābhārate*
> *prapaṃcitaḥ ‖*

*Gist:* The twelfth sūkta, यो जात (*yo jāta*), fifteen Ṛks, opens the second anuvāka (eleven sūktas); seer Gṛtsamada, deity Indra, Triṣṭubh. It is prescribed for the Nishkevalya-śastra of several rites, citing Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra passages (6-6, 7-7, 8-7, 9-7) and the Aitareya Āraṇyaka (5-3-1). Two versions of the story are told: Gṛtsamada, taking Indra's form, is mistaken for Indra by demons; he proclaims Indra's deeds, or replies "I am not Indra".

### Printed p. 642

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2 [as supplied], Sūkta 12; p. 642.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

Translation—With this sūkta the second anuvāka of this second Maṇḍala begins. In this anuvāka there are eleven sūktas. Of them, the sūkta beginning यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) is the first. It has fifteen Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭubh. At the *saṃsava* sacrifice, while the Nishkevalya-śastra mantras are recited, this sūkta यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) is to be recited before the Nivid-sūktas, as expounded by the sūtra यो जात एवेति निष्केवल्य (*yo jāta eveti niṣkevalya*) in the section यदि पर्यायान् (*yadi paryāyān*) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 6-6). And on the third day of the *abhiplava* sacrifice, at the time of reciting the Nishkevalya-śastra, this sūkta यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) is to be recited as the Nivid-sūkta, as expounded by the sūtra तृतीयस्य त्र्यर्यमा यो जात एवेति मध्यंदिनः (*tṛtīyasya tryaryamā yo jāta eveti madhyaṃdinaḥ*) of the same Sūtra (Ā. 7-7). And in the *viśvajit* sacrifice, at the midday pressing, when the Maitrāvaruṇa-śastra mantras are recited, the Sāma-sūkta यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) is to be recited before those mantras, as expounded in the section विश्वजितोग्निं नर (*viśvajitogniṃ nara*) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra सत्रा मदासो यो जात एवाभूरेक इति सामसूक्तानि (*satrā madāso yo jāta evābhūreka iti sāmasūktāni*) (Ā. 8-7). And when the Nishkevalya-śastra mantras relating to the praise of Agni are recited, this sūkta is to be recited as the Nivid-sūkta, as expounded in the section श्येनाजि.राभ्यां (*śyenāji.rābhyāṃ*) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra तिष्ठा हरी इति यो जात एवेति मध्यंदिनः (*tiṣṭhā harī iti yo jāta eveti madhyaṃdinaḥ*) (Ā. 9-7) (the supplied text has a stray full stop inside श्येनाजि.राभ्यां (*śyenāji.rābhyāṃ*)). And in the sacrifice called *mahāvrata* too, this sūkta यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) is to be recited for the Nishkevalya-śastra, as stated in the Aitareya Āraṇyaka in the section ऊरू (*ūrū*), in the sentence वने न वा यो न्यधायि चाकन् यो जात एव प्रथमो मनस्वान् (*vane na vā yo nyadhāyi cākan yo jāta eva prathamo manasvān*) (Ai. Ā. 5-3-1).

In the work called Bṛhaddevatā there is an account concerning this sūkta—

> संयुज्य तपसात्मानमैंद्रं बिभ्रन्महद्वपुः ।
> अदृश्यत मुहूर्तेन दिवि च व्योम्मि चेह च ॥
> तमिंद्र इति मत्वा तु दैत्या भीमपराक्रमौ ।
> धुनिश्च चुमुरिश्चोभौ सायुधावभिपेततुः ॥
> विदित्वा स तयोर्भावमृषिः पापं चिकीर्षतोः ।
> यो जात इति सूक्तेन कर्माण्यैंद्राण्यकीर्तयत् ॥

> *saṃyujya tapasātmānamaiṃdraṃ bibhranmahadvapuḥ |*
> *adṛśyata muhūrtena divi ca vyommi ceha ca ‖*
> *tamiṃdra iti matvā tu daityā bhīmaparākramau |*
> *dhuniśca cumuriścobhau sāyudhāvabhipetatuḥ ‖*
> *viditvā sa tayorbhāvamṛṣiḥ pāpaṃ cikīrṣatoḥ |*
> *yo jāta iti sūktena karmāṇyaiṃdrāṇyakīrtayat ‖*

Once upon a time, Gṛtsamada, the seer of this sūkta, performed austerity and, by its power, assumed the great form of Indra, and moved invisibly, unseen by the eye, in the regions where Indra dwells, such as the atmosphere and heaven, within a moment. Then two demons of great prowess, Dhuni and Cumuri, taking Gṛtsamada, who was moving in this way, to be Indra himself, and him— (the supplied text breaks off here, mid-sentence, at the end of printed p. 642: the Kannada ends with the word ಅವನನ್ನು, "him").

### Printed p. 643

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 643.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada)** (continued from p. 642)

[Having taken him to be Indra, the two demons] chased the seer, brandishing their weapons in order to kill him. Gṛtsamada, who was in Indra's form, understood the evil intention of these demons, and with this present sūkta beginning यो जात एव (*yo jāta eva*) he described and praised the heroic deeds of Indra: so it is said. Besides this—

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (introduction, continued; Sanskrit transcribed)**

> अन्ये त्वन्यथा वर्णयंति । पुरा किलेंद्रादयो वैन्ययज्ञं समाजग्मुः ।
> गृत्समदोऽपि तत्रागत्य सदस्यासीत् । दैत्याश्चेंद्रजिघांसया तत्र समा-
> गमन् । तान् दृष्ट्वा निर्जगामेंद्रो यज्ञाद्गृत्समदाकृतिः । स च गृत्स-
> मदो वैन्येन पूजितो यज्ञवाटान्निरगच्छत् । निर्गच्छंतं तमृषिं
> दृष्ट्वा अयमेवेंद्र इति मन्यमानास्तमसुरा परिवपुः । नाहमिंद्र-
> स्तुच्छः किंत्वेवंगुणोपेत स इत्यनेन सूक्तेन तान्प्रत्युवाच ॥

> *anye tvanyathā varṇayaṃti | purā kileṃdrādayo vainyayajñaṃ samājagmuḥ |*
> *gṛtsamado 'pi tatrāgatya sadasyāsīt | daityāśceṃdrajighāṃsayā tatra samā-*
> *gaman | tān dṛṣṭvā nirjagāmeṃdro yajñādgṛtsamadākṛtiḥ | sa ca gṛtsa-*
> *mado vainyena pūjito yajñavāṭānniragacchat | nirgacchaṃtaṃ tamṛṣiṃ*
> *dṛṣṭvā ayameveṃdra iti manyamānāstamasurā parivapuḥ | nāhamiṃdra-*
> *stucchaḥ kiṃtvevaṃguṇopeta sa ityanena sūktena tānpratyuvāca ‖*

Others, however, tell it in another way: in former times Indra and the other gods came to the sacrifice made by one named Vena. The seer Gṛtsamada also came there and was seated. Then the demons came there intending to kill Indra. Indra, knowing this trick, left the sacrificial ground in the guise of Gṛtsamada and went away. Gṛtsamada alone stayed there until Vena's sacrifice was completed, and, honoured by Vena, was setting out for his own hermitage. The demons saw that seer going to his hermitage, thought that Indra himself was going disguised as a seer, and surrounded him. Then Gṛtsamada said to those demons: "I am not that Indra; the qualities of that mean Indra are such as these"; so, describing in every Ṛk one quality of Indra, "O demon folk, he who has such qualities is that Indra, not I," he pacified them and went away to his hermitage.

> अपरे त्वेवं कथयंति । गृत्समदस्य यज्ञे प्रविष्टमेकाकिनमिंद्रं ज्ञात्वा-
> सुराः परिववुः । स इंद्रो गृत्समदरूपेण यज्ञवाटान्निर्गत्य स्वर्गं
> जगाम । ततोऽसुरा इंद्रो विलंबित इत्यंतः प्रविश्य गृत्समदं दृष्ट्वा
> पूर्वमेव गृत्समदो गतः अयं त्तिंद्रोऽस्मद्भयाद्गृत्समदरूपेणास्त
> इति तं जगृहुः । स तान् नाहमिंद्रोऽयमित्यनेन सूक्तेन प्रत्युवाच ।
> आयमेवार्थो महाभारते प्रपंचितः ॥

> *apare tvevaṃ kathayaṃti | gṛtsamadasya yajñe praviṣṭamekākinamiṃdraṃ jñātvā-*
> *surāḥ parivavuḥ | sa iṃdro gṛtsamadarūpeṇa yajñavāṭānnirgatya svargaṃ*
> *jagāma | tato 'surā iṃdro vilaṃbita ityaṃtaḥ praviśya gṛtsamadaṃ dṛṣṭvā*
> *pūrvameva gṛtsamado gataḥ ayaṃ ttiṃdro 'smadbhayādgṛtsamadarūpeṇāsta*
> *iti taṃ jagṛhuḥ | sa tān nāhamiṃdro 'yamityanena sūktena pratyuvāca |*
> *āyamevārtho mahābhārate prapaṃcitaḥ ‖*

Still others say as follows: the seer Gṛtsamada was once performing a sacrifice. The Asuras learned that Indra had come in there alone and surrounded the sacrificial house. Meanwhile Indra, knowing this, took the form of the seer Gṛtsamada, left the sacrificial house, and reached heaven, his dwelling-place. Then, after waiting a very long time and seeing that Indra did not come out, the Asuras entered the sacrificial house, saw Gṛtsamada there, and said, "What is this?

### Printed p. 644

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2 [as supplied], Sūkta 12; p. 644.*

Gṛtsamada has already gone away; how then has a Gṛtsamada come here? It is Indra himself who, afraid of us, sits here in the form of this Gṛtsamada," and they seized him (Gṛtsamada). Then Gṛtsamada said to those Asuras, "I am not Indra," and, describing the qualities of Indra with this sūkta, he pacified the Asuras and sent them away. This story is told in the Mahābhārata also.

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 12. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 12. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7, 8, 9. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 15. Seer: Gṛtsamada. Deity: Indra. Metre: ತ್ರಿಷ್ಟ್ಪ (as supplied; Triṣṭubh is meant, but the supplied spelling is kept).

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो जा॒त ए॒व प्र॑थ॒मो मन॑स्वान्दे॒वो दे॒वान्क्रतु॑ना प॒र्युभू॑षत् ।
> यस्य॒ शुष्मा॒द्रोद॑सी॒ अभ्य॑सेतां नृ॒म्णस्य॑ म॒ह्ना स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । जातः । एव । प्रथमः । मनस्वान् । देवः । देवान् । क्रतुना । परिअभूषत् ।
> यस्य । शुष्मात् । रोदसी इति । अभ्यसेतां । नृम्मस्य । मह्ना । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥

> *yaḥ | jātaḥ | eva | prathamaḥ | manasvān | devaḥ | devān | kratunā | pariabhūṣat |*
> *yasya | śuṣmāt | rodasī iti | abhyasetāṃ | nṛmmasya | mahnā | saḥ | janāsaḥ | iṃdraḥ ‖*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> गृत्समदो ब्रूते । जनासो जना हे असुराः यो जात एव जायमान
> एव सन् प्रथमो देवानां प्रधानभूतो मनस्वान् मनस्सिनामग्रगण्यो देवो

> बलात् रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावभ्यसेतां । अबिभीतां ॥ भ्यस भये । अनुदात्तेत् ।

> मंत्रांतरं । इमे चित्तव मन्यवे वेपेते भियसा मही । १-८०-११ । इति । नृम्मस्य
> सेनालक्षणस्य बलस्य मह्ना महत्वेन युक्तः स इंद्रो नाहमिति ॥ अत्र निरुक्तं ।

> त्पर्यगृह्णात्पर्यरक्षदत्यक्रामदिति वा यस्य बलवादा न्वापृथिव्यावप्यबिभीतां

> *gṛtsamado brūte | janāso janā he asurāḥ yo jāta eva jāyamāna*
> *eva san prathamo devānāṃ pradhānabhūto manasvān manassināmagragaṇyo devo*
> *dyotamānaḥ san kratunā vṛtravadhādilakṣaṇena svakīyena karmaṇā devān sarvānyāga-*
> *devānparyabhūṣat | rakṣakatvena paryagrahīt ‖ bhūṣa alaṃkāre bhūvādiḥ | laji*
> *rūpaṃ ‖ yadvā sarvānanyāndevānparyabhūṣat paryabhavat | atyakrāmat ‖ asminpakṣe*
> *bhavatervyatyayena ksaḥ | śrukaḥ kitītīṭpratiṣedhaḥ ‖ yasyeṃdrasya śuṣmāt śarīrāt*
> *balāt rodasī dyāvāpṛthivyāvabhyasetāṃ | abibhītāṃ ‖ bhyasa bhaye | anudāttet |*
> *bhyasa bhayavepanayoriti nairuktāḥ | ni- 3-21 ‖ abhyasetāmavepetāṃ vā | tathā ca*
> *maṃtrāṃtaraṃ | ime cittava manyave vepete bhiyasā mahī | 1-80-11 | iti | nṛmmasya*
> *senālakṣaṇasya balasya mahnā mahatvena yuktaḥ sa iṃdro nāhamiti ‖ atra niruktaṃ |*
> *yo jāyamāna eva prathamo manasvī devo devānkatunā karmaṇā paryabhava-*
> *tparyagṛhṇātparyarakṣadatyakrāmaditi vā yasya balavādā nvāpṛthivyāvapyabibhītāṃ*
> *nṛmmasya mahnā balasya mahatvena sa janāsa iṃdra ityṛṣerdṛṣṭārthasya prītirbhavatyā-*
> *khyānasaṃyuktā | ni- 10-10 | iti ‖*

*Gist:* Gṛtsamada speaks: O demons, he who, the moment he was born, became the foremost of the gods, wise and shining, and by his own deeds such as the slaying of Vṛtra guarded or surpassed all the gods, and at whose strength heaven and earth trembled, that is Indra, not I. The grammatical tail (the forms पर्यभूषत् (*paryabhūṣat*) and अभ्यसेतां (*abhyasetāṃ*)) and the Nirukta passage are not explained here.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Janāsaḥ: O people. Yaḥ: who. Jāta eva: immediately on being born. Prathamaḥ: the foremost, chief among the gods. Manasvān: firm in mind. Devaḥ: shining. Kratunā: by his deed in the form of the slaying of Vṛtra and the like. Devān: all the gods. Paryabhūṣat: honoured and protected; and yasya: whose; śuṣmāt: by the strength of the body; rodasī: both heaven and earth; abhyasetāṃ: were frightened; nṛmmasya: of the strength in the form of an army; mahnā: joined with greatness; saḥ: that very one; indraḥ: is Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who, immediately on being born, became the chief of the gods, firm of mind and shining, and who by his deed such as the slaying of Vṛtra honoured and protected all the gods, and by whose bodily strength heaven and earth were both frightened, he who is joined with the greatness of strength in the form of an army, he is Indra. [Kannada: ದ್ಯೋತಮಾನನಃ, as supplied; the full stop at the end is missing in the supplied text.]

**Printed English**

He who as soon as born, is the first(of the deities); who has done honour to the gods by his exploits; he, at whose might heaven and earth are alarmed, and(who is known) by the greatness of his strength, he, men, is Indra.

### Printed p. 646

*Running head: [646 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In every Ṛk of this sūkta one or two qualities or heroic deeds of Indra are described, with the idea that the one who did such a great and daring deed is Indra; others than Indra are unable to do such a deed; only Indra has the wonderful strength and capacity to do such work. Therefore at the end of each Ṛk of this sūkta there is, like a refrain, a description of his qualities: स जनास इंद्रः (*sa janāsa iṃdraḥ*) — "O people, (the one who did this wonderful deed) he is Indra." In Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-112 also, the sūkta of 25 Ṛks beginning ईळे द्यावापृथिवी (*īḷe dyāvāpṛthivī*), the sage Kutsa Aṅgirasa, describing in each Ṛk the heroic deeds of the Aśvin deities, prays to the Aśvins with याभिर्भरे कारमंशाय जिन्वथस्ताभिरूषु ऊतिभिरश्विना गतं (*yābhirbhare kāramaṃśāya jinvathastābhirūṣu ūtibhiraśvinā gataṃ*): "(with those protections of yours) by which ... — come to our sacrificial ground, together with such protections of yours, which comprise such heroic deeds." Sūktas of this kind occur in many places in the Ṛgveda.

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — जना हे असुराः । (*janā he asurāḥ |*) The sage addresses the asuras as "O asura people", describes Indra's qualities to them one by one so that they may understand, and says that the one who did such deeds is Indra, not I; thus the sage tries to remove the asuras' doubt. The account is this: once Indra was in Gṛtsamada's sacrificial ground, and knowing that the asuras were lying in wait to kill him, he escaped in the guise of the sage Gṛtsamada. The asuras waited a long time for Indra, but when he did not come out, they seized the real Gṛtsamada, thinking that Indra might be in that guise; and to remove the asuras' doubt he (saying "I am not Indra; Indra is one with such qualities", and describing Indra's qualities) recited this sūkta. We have explained this account at length in the introduction to this sūkta (p. 642).

यो जात एव प्रथमो मनस्वान् देवो देवान् क्रतुना पर्यभूषत् (*yo jāta eva prathamo manasvān devo devān kratunā paryabhūṣat*) — for this sentence in the first half of the Ṛk the meaning is: as soon as Indra was born he became the leader of all the gods, and by his valour became worthy of honour even to the other gods; or he increased the honour of the other gods; or he became an ornament to the other gods.

प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*) — Yāska (for the word प्रथम (*prathama*)): प्रथम इति मुख्यनाम प्रतमो भवति (*prathama iti mukhyanāma pratamo bhavati*) (Ni. 2-22) [the supplied text has प्रतमो (*pratamo*) here, as supplied]; that is, "prathama" is a name for "chief". Indra is the chief among the gods, the forerunner, the leader, the commander of the gods: this is the meaning.

जातः (*jātaḥ*) — Ordinarily men and gods alike become great by the heroic deeds they perform when grown up, or by their qualities. But in Indra's case it is not so. The sage has described that as soon as he was born he became chief and foremost among all the gods. In other places too there is support for this. In the sūktas Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-48 and 4-18 the birth of Indra is spoken of. Immediately on his birth he set the sun's wheel moving (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-130-9); as soon as he was born he became a warrior (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-51-8, 5-30-5, 8-45-4, 8-66-1, 10-113-4); from his birth he was unconquered (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-102-8, 10-133-2); such descriptions of Indra's birth exist. [The sentence beginning "But in Indra's case" runs across the page break in the print.]

### Printed p. 647

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 647.*

यस्य शुष्माद्रोदसी अभ्यसेतां नृम्मस्य मह्ना (*yasya śuṣmādrodasī abhyasetāṃ nṛmmasya mahnā*) — in this sentence it is said that heaven and earth trembled at the greatness of Indra's great strength. The word शुष्मं (*śuṣmaṃ*) is read among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9), the word रोदसी (*rodasī*) among the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-13), and the word नृम्म (*nṛmma*) among the names of strength (and wealth) (Ni. 3-9). Since the etymology is given: भ्यसते रेजत इति भयवेपनयोः । "यस्य शुष्माद्रोदसि अभ्यसेतां," "रेजते अग्ने पृथिवी मखेभ्यः" इत्यपि निगमौ भवतः (*bhyasate rejata iti bhayavepanayoḥ | "yasya śuṣmādrodasi abhyasetāṃ," "rejate agne pṛthivī makhebhyaḥ" ityapi nigamau bhavataḥ*) (Ni. 3-21), the word अभ्यसेतां (*abhyasetāṃ*) means अवेपेतां (*avepetāṃ*), अबिभीतां (*abibhītāṃ*): they staggered and trembled with fear. On the matter that heaven and earth trembled at Indra's immense strength:

तव त्विषो जनिमन् रेजत द्यौ रेजद्भूमिर्भियसा स्वस्य मन्योः । (*tava tviṣo janiman rejata dyau rejadbhūmirbhiyasā svasya manyoḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-17-2)

in this Ṛk it is said that at the time of Indra's birth heaven and earth trembled for fear of his wrath.

अस्येदु भिया गिरयश्च दृळ्हा द्यावा च भूमा जनुषस्तुजीते । (*asyedu bhiyā girayaśca dṛḷhā dyāvā ca bhūmā januṣastujīte |*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 1-61-14) [as supplied; "Ma." where one expects "Ṛ."]

in this Ṛk it is said that as soon as Indra was born, the immovable mountains, heaven and earth shook for fear of him.

इमे चित्तव मन्यवे वेपेते भियसा मही । (*ime cittava manyave vepete bhiyasā mahī |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-80-11)

in this Ṛk it is said that earth and heaven trembled in fear of your (Indra's) wrath.

अतश्चिदिंद्रादभयंत देवा विश्वा अपो अजयद्दासपत्नीः । (*ataścidiṃdrādabhayaṃta devā viśvā apo ajayaddāsapatnīḥ |*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 5-30-5) [as supplied; "Ma." where one expects "Ṛ."]

here it is said that all the gods were afraid of Indra. Thus, as soon as Indra was born, his strength and valour were so excessive that they made the gods and heaven and earth tremble with fear

### Printed p. 648

*Running head: [648 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2. Sū. 12.]*

— this is said in many places. The sage Gṛtsamada pacified the asuras who had come to kill him by saying that one of such immeasurable valour is Indra and not I. For this mantra [?] Yāska has given the etymology as follows:

यो जायमान एव प्रथमो मनस्वी देवो देवान् क्रतुना कर्मणा पर्यभवत् पर्यगृह्णात् पर्यरक्षदत्यक्रामदिति वा । यस्य बलाद्द्यावापृथिव्यावप्यबिभीतां नृम्णस्य मह्ना बलस्य महत्वेन स जनास इंद्र इति । यषेर्दृष्टार्थस्य प्रीतिर्भवत्ख्याख्यानसंयुक्ता ॥ (*yo jāyamāna eva prathamo manasvī devo devān kratunā karmaṇā paryabhavat paryagṛhṇāt paryarakṣadatyakrāmaditi vā | yasya balāddyāvāpṛthivyāvapyabibhītāṃ nṛmṇasya mahnā balasya mahatvena sa janāsa iṃdra iti | yaṣerdṛṣṭārthasya prītirbhavatkhyākhyānasaṃyuktā ‖*)
(Si- 10-10) [as supplied; probably the Nirukta, "Ni."]

Since the commentary written by Durgācārya on this etymology is very clear, we have given it here and explained Yāska's meaning.

यो जात एव जातमात्र एव प्रथमः मुख्यः सर्वभूतानां प्रतिमुख्यतां संपेदे । मनस्वी च मेधावी, अन्ये हि कालेन मुख्याः संपद्यंते मेधाविनश्च । देवो देवान् क्रतुना पर्यभूषत् । सत्यपि देवत्वाविशेषे इतरान् देवान् क्रतुना कर्मणा पर्यभूषत् पर्यगृह्णात्, परिगृहीतवान् स्वामित्वेन पर्यरक्षद्वा) मुख्यत्वात् अत्यक्रामद्वा प्रभावेण । यस्य च शुष्मात् शारीराद्बलात् रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावपि अभ्यसेतामबिभीतां। नृम्मस्य मह्ना बलस्य सेनालक्षणस्य महत्वेन अतिमहदसह्यमस्य बलमवश्यमयमावां सादयिष्यतीत्येवमतिमहत्यावेते द्यावापृथिव्यावबिभीतां यस्य शुष्मात् । जनासः हे असुरजनाः स इंद्रः नाहमिंद्रः ब्राह्मणोऽहं, तत्पसादादेवापत्तद्रूप इति । ऋषेर्दृष्टार्थस्य अनुभूतेंद्रमैत्रस्येंद्रवयस्यस्य गृत्समदस्य इंद्रं प्रति प्रीतिस्तुष्टिर्भवत्याख्यानसंयुक्ता आख्यानसंबंधात् । अतश्च दर्शयति मंत्राणामैतिहासिकोऽप्यर्थ उपेक्षितव्योऽसावपि तेषां विषय इति ॥ (*yo jāta eva jātamātra eva prathamaḥ mukhyaḥ sarvabhūtānāṃ pratimukhyatāṃ saṃpede | manasvī ca medhāvī, anye hi kālena mukhyāḥ saṃpadyaṃte medhāvinaśca | devo devān kratunā paryabhūṣat | satyapi devatvāviśeṣe itarān devān kratunā karmaṇā paryabhūṣat paryagṛhṇāt, parigṛhītavān svāmitvena paryarakṣadvā) mukhyatvāt atyakrāmadvā prabhāveṇa | yasya ca śuṣmāt śārīrādbalāt rodasī dyāvāpṛthivyāvapi abhyasetāmabibhītāṃ| nṛmmasya mahnā balasya senālakṣaṇasya mahatvena atimahadasahyamasya balamavaśyamayamāvāṃ sādayiṣyatītyevamatimahatyāvete dyāvāpṛthivyāvabibhītāṃ yasya śuṣmāt | janāsaḥ he asurajanāḥ sa iṃdraḥ nāhamiṃdraḥ brāhmaṇo 'haṃ, tatpasādādevāpattadrūpa iti | ṛṣerdṛṣṭārthasya anubhūteṃdramaitrasyeṃdravayasyasya gṛtsamadasya iṃdraṃ prati prītistuṣṭirbhavatyākhyānasaṃyuktā ākhyānasaṃbaṃdhāt | ataśca darśayati maṃtrāṇāmaitihāsiko 'pyartha upekṣitavyo 'sāvapi teṣāṃ viṣaya iti ‖*)
(Ni. 10-10, Durga's commentary)

Its main meaning is: as soon as Indra was born he attained the chief position among all beings (and gods), that is, he became the foremost leader of all. This Indra is very wise (intelligent); other intelligent persons, after being born, attain a high position after some time; but Indra gained the high position as soon as he was born. Though born as a god, by his heroic exertions he became acceptable as chief and lord of the other gods. Not only that, by his power he surpassed all the other gods in strength and daring.

### Printed p. 649

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 649.*

Heaven and earth (the heavens and the earth) trembled in fear of such great bodily strength of Indra. For they trembled in fear, thinking that Indra, by the greatness of his mighty valour together with his army, would destroy them (heaven and earth). Therefore, "O asura people, one with such greatness is Indra. I am not he; I am only a poor Brahmin. By the grace of that Indra I have taken on a form like his": this story shows the sage Gṛtsamada, the seer of the mantra, and his exceeding love for Indra. Since this narrative is historical, and it cannot be said that the mantras have this kind of meaning, some people disregard the matter contained in this narrative: such is the main idea.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः पृ॑थि॒वीं व्यथ॑माना॒मदृं॑ह॒द्यः पर्व॑ता॒न्प्रकु॑पिता॒ अर॑म्णात् ।
> यो अं॒तरि॑क्षं विम॒मे वरी॑यो॒ यो द्यामस्त॑भ्ना॒त्स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । पृथिवीं । व्यथमानां । अदृंहत् । यः । पर्वतान् । प्रकुपितान् । अरम्हात् ।
> यः । अंतरिक्षं । विममे । वरीयः । यः । द्यां । अस्तभ्नात् । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे जनाः य इंद्रो व्यथमानां चलंतीं पृथिवीमद्यंहत् । शर्करादिभि-
> र्दृढामकरोत् ॥ दृह दृहि वृदौ ॥ यश्च प्रकुपितान् इतस्ततश्चलितान्पक्षयुक्तान्पर्व-
> तानरम्मात् । नियमितवान् । स्वे स्वे स्थाने स्थापितवान् ॥ अरम्मात् रमु क्रीडायां।
> अंतर्भावितण्यर्थस्य व्यत्ययेन श्नाप्रत्ययः ॥ यश्च वरीय उरुतममंतरिक्षं
> विममे । निर्ममे । विस्तीर्णं चकारेत्यर्थः । यश्च द्यां दिवमस्तभ्नात् । स्तंभ-
> निरुद्धामकरोत् ॥ स्तंभु रोधन इति सौत्रो धातुः ॥ स एवेंद्रो नाहमिति ॥

> *he janāḥ ya iṃdro vyathamānāṃ calaṃtīṃ pṛthivīmadyaṃhat | śarkarādibhi-*
> *rdṛḍhāmakarot ‖ dṛha dṛhi vṛdau ‖ yaśca prakupitān itastataścalitānpakṣayuktānparva-*
> *tānarammāt | niyamitavān | sve sve sthāne sthāpitavān ‖ arammāt ramu krīḍāyāṃ|*
> *aṃtarbhāvitaṇyarthasya vyatyayena śnāpratyayaḥ ‖ yaśca varīya urutamamaṃtarikṣaṃ*
> *vimame | nirmame | vistīrṇaṃ cakāretyarthaḥ | yaśca dyāṃ divamastabhnāt | staṃbha-*
> *niruddhāmakarot ‖ staṃbhu rodhana iti sautro dhātuḥ ‖ sa eveṃdro nāhamiti ‖*

*Gist:* "O people, he who made firm the trembling earth, restrained the agitated mountains, measured out the wide atmosphere and propped up heaven: he is Indra, not I." The grammatical tail (roots दृह (*dṛha*), रमु (*ramu*), स्तंभु (*staṃbhu*)) is not explained here.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. व्यथमानां (*vyathamānāṃ*) — the shaking. पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*) — the earth. अदृंहत् (*adṛṃhat*) — made stand firm. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. प्रकुपितान् (*prakupitān*) —

### Printed p. 650

*Running head: [650 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

moving about everywhere with wings. पर्वतान् (*parvatān*) — the mountains. अरम्णात् (*aramṇāt*) — made bow to his control and remain steady. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. वरीयः (*varīyaḥ*) — wide. अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*) — the atmosphere. विममे (*vimame*) — fashioned. यः (*yaḥ*) who. द्यां (*dyāṃ*) — the world of heaven. अस्तभ्नात् (*astabhnāt*) — stopped and fixed. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who made the shaking earth stand firm; who made the mountains, which were moving about everywhere with wings, bow to his control and remain steady; who fashioned the wide atmosphere; who stopped and fixed the world of heaven: he is Indra.

**Printed English**

He, who fixed firm the moving earth; who tranquillized the incensed mountains; who spread the spacious firmament; who consolidated the heaven; he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk four heroic deeds of Indra are stated. Indra firmly established the earth so that it does not shake; he calmed the misdeeds of the mountains, which were full of anger; he fashioned the wide atmosphere; he established heaven so that it is solid (so that it does not fall down): descriptions of these four matters are given.

यः पृथिवीं व्यथमानां अद्यंहत् (*yaḥ pṛthivīṃ vyathamānāṃ adyaṃhat*) — Indra made the shaking earth immovable, that is, made it stand firm in one place without shaking. Although the earth stands firm always in one and the same place, without shaking this way and that, the sage describes that it is so by Indra's power.

यः पर्वतान् प्रकुपितान् अरम्नात् (*yaḥ parvatān prakupitān aramnāt*) — The commentator explains the meaning thus: the mountains, in anger, did not stay steady in one place but moved about from here to there with the help of their wings; Indra stopped this, cut off their wings, and subjected those mountains to restraint so that they stay settled steadily in one place. It is said in the Purāṇas and elsewhere that formerly mountains had wings, and as they flew about and settled on villages, towns and so on and destroyed them, Indra cut off the wings of those mountains with his thunderbolt. The commentator has explained just that here. But the matter of mountains having wings is not seen mentioned anywhere in the Ṛgveda.

### Printed p. 651

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 651.*

Therefore there is a difference of opinion about accepting this kind of meaning given by the commentator for this sentence. Since this sentence says प्रकुपितान् पर्वतान् (*prakupitān parvatān*) — "angry mountains", some think that fire-spewing fire-mountains (volcanoes) may be meant here. Or, since the word पर्वत (*parvata*) has the meaning "cloud" (Ni. 2-21), it is also the opinion of some that the sage's view may be that Indra struck and calmed the clouds roaring in anger (thundering) with his thunderbolt (lightning).

यः अंतरिक्षं विममे वरीयः (*yaḥ aṃtarikṣaṃ vimame varīyaḥ*) — Indra fashioned the wide atmosphere, or made the atmosphere very wide: such is the idea.

यः द्यां अस्तभ्नात् (*yaḥ dyāṃ astabhnāt*) — Indra propped up heaven (so that it does not fall down), held it in check. Since it is well known that heaven is very high in the atmosphere, it is described here that heaven, being so, stands without any support (without the support of pillars and the like), and that he checked it so that it does not fall down upon the earth. Ordinarily in the world, when houses and the like are built, it is well known that pillars are set up so that the roof above or an upper floor and such parts stay firm without falling down, and the weight rests on them. But here, in the atmosphere, without any support (pillars), heaven is made not to fall down, that is, to stand firm: the sage describes the greatness of Indra who has done this as worthy of praise.

In the first quarter of this Ṛk the use of the word यः (*yaḥ*) (the relative word) and in the last quarter the use of the word सः (*saḥ*) (the correlative word) emphasize the idea that the person who has performed such an extraordinary deed is Indra.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो ह॒त्वाहि॒मरि॑णात्स॒प्त सिंधू॒न्यो गा उ॒दाज॑दप॒था व॒लस्य॑।
> यो अश्म॑नोरं॒तर॒ग्निं ज॒जान॑ सं॒वृक्ष॒मत्तु॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । हत्वा । अहिं । अरिणात् । सप्त । सिंधून् । यः । गाः । उत्आजत् । अपधा ।
> वलस्य ।

> *yaḥ | hatvā | ahiṃ | ariṇāt | sapta | siṃdhūn | yaḥ | gāḥ | utājat | apadhā |*
> *valasya |*

### Printed p. 652

*Running head: [652 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

> यः । अश्मनोः । अंतः । अग्निं । जजान । संवृक् । समत्सु । सः । जनासः ।
> इंद्रः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> योऽहिं मेघं हत्वा मेघहननं कृत्वा सप्त सर्पणशीलाः सिंधून् स्यंदन-
> शीला अपोऽरिणात् । प्रैरयत् । यद्वा सप्त गंगायुमुनाद्या मुख्या नदीररि-
> णात् ॥ रीज् स्रवणे क्रादिः ॥ यश्च वलस्य वलनामकस्यासुरस्यापधा तत्कर्तृकान्नि-
> रोधान्निरुद्धा गा उदाजत् । निरगमयत् ॥ अपधा । अपपूर्वाद्भधातेरातश्चो-
> पसर्गे । पा- ३-३-१०६ । इति भार्वेऽज् प्रत्ययः । सुपां सुलुगिति पंचम्या आकारः ॥
> यश्चाश्मनोः । अश्नुते व्याप्त्यंतरिक्षमित्यश्मा मेघः ॥ अत्यंतमृदुरूपयो-
> र्मेघयोरंतर्मध्यॆ वैद्युतमग्निं जजान । उत्पादयामास । यश्च समत्तु ।
> संभक्षयंति योद्धरणामायूंषीति समदः संग्रामाः । तेषु संवृक् भवति ॥
> वृणक्तेर्हिंसार्थस्य क्विपि रूपं ॥ स इंद्रो नाहमिति ॥

> *yo 'hiṃ meghaṃ hatvā meghahananaṃ kṛtvā sapta sarpaṇaśīlāḥ siṃdhūn syaṃdana-*
> *śīlā apo 'riṇāt | prairayat | yadvā sapta gaṃgāyumunādyā mukhyā nadīrari-*
> *ṇāt ‖ rīj sravaṇe krādiḥ ‖ yaśca valasya valanāmakasyāsurasyāpadhā tatkartṛkānni-*
> *rodhānniruddhā gā udājat | niragamayat ‖ apadhā | apapūrvādbhadhāterātaśco-*
> *pasarge | pā- 3-3-106 | iti bhārve 'j pratyayaḥ | supāṃ sulugiti paṃcamyā ākāraḥ ‖*
> *yaścāśmanoḥ | aśnute vyāptyaṃtarikṣamityaśmā meghaḥ ‖ atyaṃtamṛdurūpayo-*
> *rmeghayoraṃtarmadhyè vaidyutamagniṃ jajāna | utpādayāmāsa | yaśca samattu |*
> *saṃbhakṣayaṃti yoddharaṇāmāyūṃṣīti samadaḥ saṃgrāmāḥ | teṣu saṃvṛk bhavati ‖*
> *vṛṇakterhiṃsārthasya kvipi rūpaṃ ‖ sa iṃdro nāhamiti ‖*

*Gist:* "He who, having slain the cloud, set the seven flowing waters (or the seven chief rivers such as the Gaṅgā and Yamunā) in motion; who drove out the cows held back by Vala; who produced lightning between two clouds; who is irresistible in battles: he is Indra, not I." The grammatical tail (roots रीज् (*rīj*), वृणक्तेः (*vṛṇakteḥ*) and the formation अपधा (*apadhā*)) is not explained here.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. अहिं (*ahiṃ*) — the cloud. हत्वा (*hatvā*) — having slain. सप्त (*sapta*) — seven in number, and सिंधून् (*siṃdhūn*) — flowing waters (the rivers Gaṅgā and the others). अरिणात् (*ariṇāt*) — released and made to flow. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. वलस्य (*valasya*) — of the asura named Vala. अपधा (*apadhā*) — caught in the obstruction. गाः (*gāḥ*) — the cows. उदाजत् (*udājat*) — released. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. अश्मनोः (*aśmanoḥ*) — of the clouds. अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*) — in the middle region. अग्निं (*agniṃ*) — the fire in the form of lightning. जजान (*jajāna*) — created (and). समत्सु (*samatsu*) — in battles. संवृक् (*saṃvṛk*) — remains unobstructed. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who slew the cloud and made the seven flowing rivers, the Gaṅgā and the others, run; who released the cows caught in the obstruction of the asura named Vala; who created the fire in the form of lightning in the middle region of the clouds; and who remains unobstructed in battles: he is Indra.

**Printed English**

He, who having destroyed Ahi, set free the seven rivers; who re-covered the cows detained by Vala; who generated fire in the clouds: who is invincible in battle: he, men, is Indra.

### Printed p. 653

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 653.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk too, four heroic deeds of Indra are described: Indra struck the demon Vṛtra, or the cloud, brought down rain and made the waters flow; he released the cows hidden by Vala; he produced (created) lightning-fire in the middle of the clouds in the atmosphere [the printed word reads "antaśikṣa", a misprint for the atmosphere; as printed]; and in battle he is unconquered by enemies.

यो हत्वा अहिं अरिणात् सप्त सिंधून् (*yo hatvā ahiṃ ariṇāt sapta siṃdhūn*) — the commentary's passage on this is the one given in the bhāṣya above. That is, Indra struck the cloud (with the thunderbolt), made the held-back rain fall, and made the fallen water flow in the form of a flood, or in the form of the seven rivers beginning with the Gaṅgā: such is the meaning.

अहिं (*ahiṃ*) — The word ahi is read among the names of the cloud (Ni. 2-21) and among the names of water (Ni. 2-24). Moreover, Yāska gives for the word ahi the explanation अहिरयनात् (*ahirayanāt*) — because it moves (in the atmosphere), the cloud is called ahi (Ni. 2-24).

(1) If the word ahi is derived from the root इण् (*iṇ*) "to go" (Adādi, Parasmaipada), the explanation एत्यंतरिक्षे (*etyaṃtarikṣe*), आयतेरेव (*āyatereva*) means: because it moves in the atmosphere, ahi means the cloud;

(2) or, if the word ahi is derived from a root meaning "to go" (Bhvādi, Ātmanepada), then too, because it travels in the atmosphere, the word ahi means the cloud;

(3) if it is derived from the root अह (*aha*) meaning "to pervade" (Svādi, Parasmaipada): अह्नोति (*ahnoti*) — it pervades the sky or the various quarters; so ahi means the cloud;
