(4) or: the root हन (*hana*) "to injure, to go", with the prefix आज् (*āj*): if derived thus, then in the sense of injury, since by contact with cold water it destroys the heat all around, the word ahi means the cloud. Or, if the root han is given the sense of "going", then because it has the nature of moving about in the atmosphere, ahi means the cloud;

(5) or another meaning is possible for the root han in the sense of injury. Namely: हिः हंता, न हंता अहंता (*hiḥ haṃtā, na haṃtā ahaṃtā*) — hiḥ is the one who injures, ahiḥ is the one who does not injure, that is, the non-injuring one, because it always gives rain to the world. Hiḥ means injuring; ahiḥ means not injuring: the cloud, because the cloud pours rain for the benefit of the people of the world and is therefore non-injuring. Hence ahi means the cloud.

### Printed p. 654

*Running head: [654 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

In support of this meaning, there is the passage of the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā of the Śukla Yajurveda: "He came to be united with Agni and Soma; he became all knowledge, all fame, all food, all splendour; because he became all this, he is Ahi." In the Ṛk at hand ahi means the cloud only.

सप्तसिंधून् (*saptasiṃdhūn*) — Sāyaṇa has given two kinds of meaning for these words. सप्त सर्पणशीलाः (*sapta sarpaṇaśīlāḥ*) — the word sapta is taken to mean "of a pushing, gliding nature", and सिंधून् (*siṃdhūn*) — the word sindhu to mean "of a flowing nature"; he impelled the rain-waters, that is, he impelled the cloud to pour rain: this is one kind of meaning. Or: सप्त गंगायमुनाद्या मुख्या नदीररिणात् (*sapta gaṃgāyamunādyā mukhyā nadīrariṇāt*) — having impelled the cloud to pour rain, he made those waters flow in the seven chief rivers, the Gaṅgā, Yamunā and the others: this is the other kind of meaning.

यो गा उदाजदपधा वलस्य (*yo gā udājadapadhā valasya*) — About the asura named Vala and the cows hidden by him, we have given a detailed explanation in this same volume on pages six hundred thirty-four to six hundred thirty-seven. Here too it is said that Indra released the cows of the gods which this asura Vala had held back and kept in a cave.

यः अश्मनोः अंतः अग्निं जजान (*yaḥ aśmanoḥ aṃtaḥ agniṃ jajāna*) — The word ashman is derived from the root अशू (*aśū*) "to pervade" (Svādi, Ātmanepada); for it the explanation is: because it pervades the atmosphere, ashman means the cloud. So अश्मनोः (*aśmanoḥ*) means "of the clouds" [dual in the text, "of two clouds"]. The word ashmā is also read among the cloud-names (the printed word reads "mena-names", a misprint for the cloud-names, as printed) (Ni. 2-21). Between two extremely soft clouds Indra produced the fire of lightning. It is very easy, and well known in the world, to produce fire by the friction of two very hard things such as stones. But to produce lightning between two clouds which are as soft as cotton is not possible for ordinary men or for the gods. The sage describes that it is possible only for the supremely glorious Indra, and so praises Indra's surpassing power. The use of the dual form here, अश्मनोः (*aśmanoḥ*), "between two clouds", is noteworthy; for, according to the opinion of modern scientists, electricity arises from the friction of two clouds charged with different kinds of electric current. (Even the modern scientists agree that clouds charged defferenty [sic] by positive and negetive [sic] electricity coming together cause lightning).

### Printed p. 655

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 655.*

संवृक् समत्सु (*saṃvṛk samatsu*) — in this sentence the word यः (*yaḥ*) and the verb भवति (*bhavati*) must be supplied. Done so: यः समत्सु संवृक् भवति (*yaḥ samatsu saṃvṛk bhavati*) — the Indra who injures, that is destroys, enemies in battles: Indra is he; such is the meaning of the sentence. संवृक् (*saṃvṛk*): वृणक्तेर्हिंसार्थस्य क्विपि रूपं (*vṛṇakterhiṃsārthasya kvipi rūpaṃ*) — the word संवृक् (*saṃvṛk*), formed from the root वृणक्ति (*vṛṇakti*) (which expresses injury) with the prefix सं (*saṃ*), means one who injures (enemies). And समत्सु (*samatsu*) — संभक्षयंति योद्धणामायूंषीति समदः संग्रामाः तेषु (*saṃbhakṣayaṃti yoddhaṇāmāyūṃṣīti samadaḥ saṃgrāmāḥ teṣu*) — because they devour (destroy) the lives of the warriors who fight, battles are called समदः (*samadaḥ*). The main purport of this sentence is that in such battles, where warriors die, Indra injures and destroys the enemies.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> येने॒मा विश्वा॒ च्यव॑ना कृ॒तानि॒ यो दासं॒ वर्ण॒मध॑रं॒ गुहाकः॑ ।
> श्व॒घ्नीव॒ यो जि॑गी॒वां ल॒क्षमाद॑द॒र्यः पु॒ष्पानि॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> येन । इमा । विश्वा । च्यवना । कृतानि । यः । दासं । वर्णं । अधरं । गुहा ।
> अकरित्यकः [?] ।
> श्वघ्नीऽइव । यः । जिगीवान् । लक्षं । आदत् । आर्यः । पुष्वानि । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥

> *yena | imā | viśvā | cyavanā | kṛtāni | yaḥ | dāsaṃ | varṇaṃ | adharaṃ | guhā |*
> *akarityakaḥ [?] |*
> *śvaghnī 'iva | yaḥ | jigīvān | lakṣaṃ | ādat | āryaḥ | puṣvāni | saḥ | janāsaḥ | iṃdraḥ ‖*

*Note: the word after गुहा in the Pada is garbled in the supplied text (अकरित्यकः (*akarityakaḥ*), left in doubt); the Pratipadārtha has अकः (*akaḥ*). The Saṃhitā and Pada print पुष्पानि (*puṣpāni*) / पुष्वानि (*puṣvāni*) while the bhāṣya and Pratipadārtha have पुष्टानि (*puṣṭāni*); kept as supplied.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> येनेंद्रेणेमेमानि विश्वा च्यवना नश्वराणि भुवनानि कृतानि । स्थिरीकृतानि ।
> यश्च दासं वर्ण० शूद्रादिकं । यद्वा दासमुपक्षपयितारमधरं निकृष्टमसुरं
> गुहा गुहायां गूढस्थाने नरके वाकः । अकार्षित् ॥ करोतेर्लुजु मंत्रे
> घसेत्यादिना ज्लेलुकि रूपं ॥ लक्षं लक्ष्यं जिगीवान् ॥ जि जये । क्वसौ सन्‌लिटो-
> र्जीरित्यभ्यासादुत्तरस्य कुत्वं । दीर्घश्चांदसः ॥ जितवान् योऽर्योऽरेः ॥ षष्ठ्ये-
> कवचने छांदसो यणादेशः ॥ शत्रोः संबंधीनि पुष्टानि समृद्धान्यादत् आदत्ते!
> तत्र दृष्टांतः । श्वघ्नव । श्वभिर्मृगान् हंतीति श्वघ्नी व्याधः । यथा व्याधो जिघृ-
> क्षंस्तं मृगं परिगृह्णाति तद्वत् ॥

*Gist:* "By Indra these perishable worlds were made firm; he put the base Dāsa tribe (or the asura) in a cave; he conquered the aimed-at enemy and took the enemy's rich possessions as a hunter seizes game." The grammatical tail (the form *akārṣīt*, root *ji*, the Vedic lengthening in *jigīvān*, the genitive in *aryaḥ*) is not explained here.

### Printed p. 656

*Running head: [656 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. येन (*yena*) — by which Indra. च्यवना (*cyavanā*) — perishable. इमा विश्वा (*imā viśvā*) — all these worlds. कृतानि (*kṛtāni*) — were made (or were set firm). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. दासं (*dāsaṃ*) — the destructive. वर्ण० (*varṇa0*) — the Śūdra and other classes. अधरं (*adharaṃ*) — the base asura. गुहा (*guhā*) — in the cave which is hell. अकः (*akaḥ*) — made to dwell (and). लक्षं (*lakṣaṃ*) — the enemies aimed at. जिगीवान् (*jigīvān*) — conquered. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. अरः (*araḥ*) — of the enemy. पुष्टानि (*puṣṭāni*) — the abundant riches. श्वघ्नव (*śvaghnava*) — as a hunter takes possession of game. आदत् (*ādat*) — took into his own possession. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, the Indra by whom all these perishable worlds were made as if firmly established; who made the destructive and base asura dwell in the cave that is hell; who conquered the enemy at whom he aimed; and who, as a hunter takes possession of game, made the abundant riches of the enemy his own: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, by whom all these perishable(regions) have been made; who consigned the base servile tribe to the cavern; who seizes triumphant the cherished(treasures) of the enemy, as a hunter(strikes) his prey; he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk too there is a description of four kinds of heroic deeds of Indra.

येन इमा विश्वा च्यवना कृतानि (*yena imā viśvā cyavanā kṛtāni*) — all these perishable worlds were made by Indra firm, that is, lasting. That is, because these worlds are perishable they did not exist before; Indra created them, or made them ever firm or lasting: such is the main purport.

यः दासं वर्ण० अधरं गुहा अकः (*yaḥ dāsaṃ varṇa0 adharaṃ guhā akaḥ*) — यश्च दासं वर्ण० शूद्रादिकं यद्वा दासमुपक्षपयितारमधरं निकृष्टमसुरं गुहा गुहायां गूढस्थाने नरके वाकः अकार्षित् (*yaśca dāsaṃ varṇa0 śūdrādikaṃ yadvā dāsamupakṣapayitāramadharaṃ nikṛṣṭamasuraṃ guhā guhāyāṃ gūḍhasthāne narake vākaḥ akārṣit*). Indra made the people of the Śūdra and other classes, who serve like slaves the Brāhmaṇa and other classes, or the extremely low Dasyu people who cause harm to the Āryas, dwell in the cave, that is,

### Printed p. 657

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 657.*

in the hidden place, hell and the like: such is the explanation of the commentator. A doubt arises here. That the commentator has explained the words दासं वर्ण० (*dāsaṃ varṇa0*) as शूद्रादिकं (*śūdrādikaṃ*), people of the Śūdra and other classes, does not appear very reasonable. For the Śūdras too are included among the people of the four classes beginning with the Brāhmaṇa; that is, among the people of the fourfold order Brāhmaṇa, Kṣatriya, Vaiśya, Śūdra, the Śūdras have a place (though the last). They too, subject to the rules of conduct of the fourfold order, can attain well-being. That being so, it does not seem just, either for the gods or for Indra, to put them in hell and other dreadful places merely because they are of the lowest class, even though no fault is found in them. Therefore, to say here that the words दासं वर्ण० (*dāsaṃ varṇa0*) mean शूद्रादिकं (*śūdrādikaṃ*) does not seem right. But if, as we have explained earlier the words dāsa and dasyu, we say that they mean the asuras who are not Āryas, who do not perform sacrifices and the like, who trouble the Āryas that praise the gods, or the dasyu people (wicked people), the original inhabitants of this land, the meaning can fit properly. And in this sentence the word गुहा (*guhā*) may be taken to mean inaccessible mountain caves rather than hell. Since war went on from time to time between the Āryas and the dasyu people, it is reasonable to say that Indra defeated in battle these dasyu people, enemies of the Āryas, slew many, and drove out the survivors, so that they fled in confusion and hid in the mountains and caves. Even now it is a matter known to all that the original inhabitants such as the Bhillas and Gonds live uncivilised in the hills and mountain regions like forest-folk. In many places there are plenty of descriptions that Indra destroyed the towns and forts of the asuras or dasyu people. In this connection Indra also has the apt name Purandara, destroyer of the towns of enemies. Considering all this, it is reasonable to take दासं वर्ण० (*dāsaṃ varṇa0*) to mean dasyu people or asuras, and गुहा (*guhā*) to mean inaccessible mountains.

श्वघ्नव यः जिगीवान् लक्षं आदत् अर्यः पुष्टानि (*śvaghnava yaḥ jigīvān lakṣaṃ ādat aryaḥ puṣṭāni*) — for the word श्वघ्नव (*śvaghnava*) in this sentence Sāyaṇa gives the explanation श्वघ्नव श्वभिर्मृगान् हंतीति श्वघ्नी व्याधः (*śvaghnava śvabhirmṛgān haṃtīti śvaghnī vyādhaḥ*): श्वघ्नि (*śvaghni*) means a hunter who kills animals; as such a hunter kills the animal at which he aims, so Indra conquered enemies and took their abundant wealth and other goods: so he has explained the meaning. But Yāska gives for the word श्वघ्नि (*śvaghni*) the etymology श्वघ्नी कितवो भवति स्वं हंति स्वं पुनराश्रितं भवति (*śvaghnī kitavo bhavati svaṃ haṃti svaṃ punarāśritaṃ bhavati*) (Ni. 5-22); that is, the word श्वघ्नि (*śvaghni*) means a gambler (kitava) [the printed word reads श्वघ्निशब्दक्क (*śvaghniśabdakka*), a slip for श्वघ्निशब्दक्कॆ (*śvaghniśabdakkè*); as printed]. The word kitava has the meanings cheat, deceiver, liar, gambler and so on. In the present sentence any of the meanings given above may be used. That is, as a gambler defeats his opponent

### Printed p. 658

*Running head: [658 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

so also the meaning may be given that Indra defeated his enemies, attacked them and plundered their wealth and other goods, as one robs another of the goods in his possession.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यं स्मा॑ पृ॒च्छंति॒ कुह॒ सेति॑ घो॒रमु॒तेमा॑हु॒र्नैषो अ॒स्तीत्ये॑नं ।
> सो अ॒र्यः पु॒ष्टीर्विज॑ इ॒वा मि॑नाति॒ श्रद॑स्मै धत्त॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यं । स्म । पृच्छंति । कुह । सः । इति । घोरं । उत । ईं । आहुः । न । एषः । अस्ति ।
> इति । एनं ।
> सः । अर्यः । पुष्टीः । विजःऽइव । आ । मिनाति । श्रत् । अस्मै । धत्त । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अपश्यंतो जना घोरं शत्रूणां घातकं यं पृच्छंति स्म कुह सेति । स
> इंद्रः कुत्र वर्तत इति ॥ सेति। सोऽचि लोपे चेत्पादपूरणमिति सोर्लोपे गुणः॥
> न क्वचिदसौ तिष्ठतीति मन्यमाना जना एनमिंद्रमाहुः एष इंद्रो नास्तीति । तथा च
> मंत्रैर्नेंद्रो अस्तीति नेम उ त्व आह । ९-१००-३ । इति । ईमिति पूरणः । स
> इंद्रो विज इव । इवशब्द एवार्थे । उद्वेजक एव सन् अर्योऽरेः संबंधीनि
> पुष्टिः पोषकाणि गवाश्वादीनि धनान्यामिनाति । सर्वतो हिनस्ति ॥ मीज् हिंसायां ।
> मीनातेर्निगम इति ह्रस्वः ॥ तस्मात् श्रदस्मा इंद्राय धत्त । स इंद्रोऽस्तीति विश्वास-
> मत्र कुरुत । यद्यप्यसौ विशेषतोऽस्माभिर्न दृश्यते तथापि अस्तीति विश्वासं कुरुत ।
> एवं निर्धारणमहिमोपेतः स इंद्रो नाहमिति ॥

*Gist:* People who cannot see Indra ask where the terrible destroyer of enemies is; some say he does not exist; he, as the one who agitates, destroys the enemy's cattle, horses and other riches; so have faith in him.

### Printed p. 659

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 659.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सः (*saḥ*) — as for that Indra. कुह स्म इति (*kuha sma iti*) — "where is he?" घोरं (*ghoraṃ*) — terrible to the enemy. यं (*yaṃ*) — whom (concerning whom). पृच्छंति (*pṛcchaṃti*) — they ask. एनं (*enaṃ*) — concerning such an Indra. विषः (*viṣaḥ*) [sic, as printed] — he. न अस्तीति (*na astīti*) — "he is not in one place only". आहुः (*āhuḥ*) — (the learned) say. सः (*saḥ*) — that same Indra. विजइव (*vijaiva*) — as one who agitates. अर्यः (*aryaḥ*) — belonging to the enemy. पुष्टिः (*puṣṭiḥ*) — nourishing cattle, horses and the like. आ मिनाति (*ā mināti*) — destroys on every side. जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. सः (*saḥ*) — he who has such great qualities. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra. अस्मै (*asmai*) — in this Indra. श्रद्धत्त (*śraddhatta*) [sic, as printed] — place faith.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Concerning that Indra, terrible to the enemy, about whom people ask "Where is he?", the learned say that he is not in one place only. That same Indra, as one who agitates, destroys on every side the cattle, horses and the like that belong to the enemy and nourish him. O people, place faith in such a great Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, whom, terrible, they ask for,(saying), Where is he? or, verily, they say of him, he is not(in any one place); but who, inflicting(chastisement), destroys the cherished(treasures) of the enemy: in him have faith: for he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

A special meaning is suggested in this Ṛk. Because Indra, unlike Agni and the other gods, is not seen by people's eyes, people ask: where is that terrible and mighty Indra? Though some say there is no Indra at all, the wonderful deeds Indra performs and the slaying of enemies are plain to our direct perception; therefore the Ṛṣi describes (the Ṛk as saying): have firm faith that the god called Indra certainly exists.

यं स्मृ पृच्छंति कुह सः इति घोरं (*yaṃ smṛ pṛcchaṃti kuha saḥ iti ghoraṃ*) — people ask where that Indra, the slayer of enemies, is. Agni, Sūrya and the other gods appear to us directly; there is no reason for doubt about their existence. But it is said that there is a god Indra of very great majesty. That Indra is not seen by our eyes; therefore many ask where he is. And,

### Printed p. 660

*Running head: [660 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

उत ईं आहुः न एषः अस्ति इति एनं (*uta īṃ āhuḥ na eṣaḥ asti iti enaṃ*) — besides, some say that this so-called Indra does not exist at all. If a thing can be seen, many say "it is" on seeing it; thus the Ṛṣi here describes what ordinary people say. No one can believe, without proper grounds, that a thing exists which cannot be seen. This is the way of the world and has a reason. For:

अनृतं वै वाचा वदति । अनृतं मनसा ध्यायति । चक्षुर्वै सत्यं। अद्रागित्याह। आदर्शमिति । तत्सत्यं ॥ (*anṛtaṃ vai vācā vadati | anṛtaṃ manasā dhyāyati | cakṣurvai satyaṃ| adrāgityāha| ādarśamiti | tatsatyaṃ ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-4-1, 2)

With the mouth one may speak untruth; with the mind too one may think untruth; but whatever is seen with the eye is true. When one says "I saw this thing," it is true that the thing seen exists. Since there is this Vedic statement, how can one believe that Indra, who is not seen by the eye, exists? Since he is not seen, people argue that there is no Indra at all. In this matter Sāyaṇa has cited the Ṛks of the same meaning:

नेंद्रो अस्तीति नेम उत्व आह क ईं ददर्श कमभि ष्टवाम (*neṃdro astīti nema utva āha ka īṃ dadarśa kamabhi ṣṭavāma*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-100-3)

अयमस्मि जरितः पश्य मेह विश्वा जातान्यभ्यस्मि मह्ना । (*ayamasmi jaritaḥ paśya meha viśvā jātānyabhyasmi mahnā |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-100-4)

In the first of these Ṛks Nema speaks thus: "There is no Indra. Who has seen him? Whom shall we praise?" When he asks so, Indra answers in the next Ṛk: "O praiser, behold, I am here itself; by my great might I overcome all beings." So he replied. In the present Ṛk too, to remove the doubt of those who argue that Indra does not exist, the Ṛṣi Gṛtsamada recites the second half of this Ṛk and removes the doubt.

सः आर्यः पुष्टीः विजइव आ मीनाति श्रत् अस्मै धत्त (*saḥ āryaḥ puṣṭīḥ vijaiva ā mīnāti śrat asmai dhatta*) — ईमिति पूरणः । स इंद्रो विजइव । इवशब्द एवार्थे । उद्वेजक एव सन् अर्योऽरेः संबंधीनि पुष्टीः पोषकाणि गवाश्वादीनि धनान्यामिनाति । सर्वतो हिनस्ति । तस्मात् श्रदस्मा इंद्राय धत्त । स इंद्रोऽस्तीति विश्वासमत्र कुरुत । यद्यप्यसौ विशेषतोऽस्माभिर्न द्व्यश्यते तथापि अस्तीति विश्वासं कुरुत ॥ (*īmiti pūraṇaḥ | sa iṃdro vijaiva | ivaśabda evārthe | udvejaka eva san aryo 'reḥ saṃbaṃdhīni puṣṭīḥ poṣakāṇi gavāśvādīni dhanānyāmināti | sarvato hinasti | tasmāt śradasmā iṃdrāya dhatta | sa iṃdro 'stīti viśvāsamatra kuruta | yadyapyasau viśeṣato 'smābhirna dvyaśyate tathāpi astīti viśvāsaṃ kuruta ‖*) In this sentence the word ईं (*īṃ*) is for filling out the pāda (the printed text breaks off here, in mid-sentence, at the end of this page).

### Printed p. 661

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 8.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 661.*

Since the word इव (*iva*) in विजइव (*vijaiva*) is also used in the sense of एव (*eva*) ("only, indeed"), the sentence means: Indra strikes down the enemies and seizes and plunders the riches and the cattle, horses and the like that are with them. Therefore, O men, place faith (trust) in Indra, that is, have confidence that Indra exists. For although Indra is not seen by our eyes, the daring deeds he performs are visible to our sight; hence the Ṛṣi, addressing the people, says: have confidence (trust) that he exists.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो र॒ध्रस्य॑ चोदि॒ता यः कृ॒शस्य॒ यो ब्र॒ह्मणो॒ नाध॑मानस्य॒
> की॒रेः ।
> यु॒क्तग्रा॑व्णो॒ योऽवि॒ता सु॑ति॒प्रः सु॒तसो॑मस्य॒ स ज॑नास॒
> इंद्रः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । रध्रस्य । चोदिता । यः । कृशस्य । यः । ब्रह्मणः । नाधमानस्य । कीरेः ।
> युक्तग्राव्णः । यः । अविता । सुशिप्रः । सुत्यसोमस्य । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो रध्रस्य ॥ रधहिंसासंराद्योः ॥ समृद्धस्य चोदिता धनानां प्रेरयिता
> भवति । यश्च कृशस्य च दरिद्रस्य च यश्च नाधमानस्य ॥ नाध्य णाध्य याज्योपता-
> पैश्वर्याशीषु ॥ याचमानस्य कीरेः ॥ करोतेः कीर्तयतेर्वा॥ स्तोतुब्रह्मणो
> ब्राह्मणस्य च धनानां प्रेरयिता । यश्च सुशिप्रः शोभनहनुः सुशीर्षको वा सन्
> युक्तग्राव्णोऽभिषवार्थमुद्यतग्राव्णः सुतसोमस्याभिषुतसोमस्य यजमानस्या-
> विता रक्षिता भवति स एवेंद्रो नाहमिति । ब्रह्मशब्दस्य त्वन्न परत्वे ह्याद्युदात्तता स्यात्
> यथा ब्रह्मवन्वानो अजरं सुवीरं । ३-८-२ । इति । आयं त्वंतोदात्तः पद्यत इति
> नान्नपरः ॥

> *yo radhrasya ‖ radhahiṃsāsaṃrādyoḥ ‖ samṛddhasya coditā dhanānāṃ prerayitā*
> *bhavati | yaśca kṛśasya ca daridrasya ca yaśca nādhamānasya ‖ nādhya ṇādhya yājyopatā-*
> *paiśvaryāśīṣu ‖ yācamānasya kīreḥ ‖ karoteḥ kīrtayatervā‖ stotubrahmaṇo*
> *brāhmaṇasya ca dhanānāṃ prerayitā | yaśca suśipraḥ śobhanahanuḥ suśīrṣako vā san*
> *yuktagrāvṇo 'bhiṣavārthamudyatagrāvṇaḥ sutasomasyābhiṣutasomasya yajamānasyā-*
> *vitā rakṣitā bhavati sa eveṃdro nāhamiti | brahmaśabdasya tvanna paratve hyādyudāttatā syāt*
> *yathā brahmavanvāno ajaraṃ suvīraṃ | 3-8-2 | iti | āyaṃ tvaṃtodāttaḥ padyata iti*
> *nānnaparaḥ ‖*

*Gist:* Indra is the one who sends wealth to the prosperous, the poor, the suppliant and the praising priest, and protects the sacrificer who has pressed Soma; he alone is Indra. A remark on the accent of the word ब्रह्म (*brahma*) follows.

### Printed p. 662

*Running head: [662 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. रध्रस्य (*radhrasya*) — to the rich man. चोदिता (*coditā*) — provides (wealth). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. कृशस्य (*kṛśasya*) — to the poor man (provides). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. नाधमानस्य (*nādhamānasya*) — to the one who begs. कीरेः (*kīreḥ*) — to the praising. ब्रह्मणः (*brahmaṇaḥ*) — to the priest (provides wealth). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. सुशिप्रः (*suśipraḥ*) — having excellent cheeks. युक्तग्राव्णः (*yuktagrāvṇaḥ*) — holding in the hand the stone for pressing Soma juice. सुतसोमस्य (*sutasomasya*) — of the sacrificer who has pressed the Soma juice. अविता (*avitā*) — is the protector. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who provides wealth alike to the rich, to the poor, to the one who begs of him and to the praising priest; he who, having excellent cheeks, is the protector of the sacrificer who holds in his hand the stone for pressing the juice and has pressed the Soma juice: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, who is the encourager of the rich, and of the poor, and of the priest, who recites his praise, and is a suppliant; who, of goodly features, is the protector of him, who, with ready stones, expresses the Soma juice; he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

यो रध्रस्य चोदिता (*yo radhrasya coditā*) — रथ हिंसासंराद्ध्योः । समृद्धस्य चोदिता । (*ratha hiṃsāsaṃrāddhyoḥ | samṛddhasya coditā |*) Since the root रथ (*ratha*), as printed in the Kannada) is used in the two senses of injuring and prospering, the commentator takes the sense "prospering" here and explains the word रध्रस्य (*radhrasya*) as "of a man who is prosperous with wealth and the like". Therefore रध्रस्य चोदिता (*radhrasya coditā*) here means one who impels wealth and other goods to a man who already has wealth and is well off, that is, one who gives wealth and the like.

यः कृशस्य यः ब्रह्मणः नाधमानस्य कीरेः ब्रह्मणः (*yaḥ kṛśasya yaḥ brahmaṇaḥ nādhamānasya kīreḥ brahmaṇaḥ*) — It is not that, as said above, Indra helps only the wealthy with wealth and the like and neglects the rest; this sentence says that he looks on all alike. Accordingly: कृशस्य (*kṛśasya*) — to the poor man who has no proper food and the like or who is not nourished for want of means; नाधमानस्य (*nādhamānasya*) — to one who makes a request, that is, to the helpless man who lives by begging; कीरेः ब्रह्मणः (*kīreḥ brahmaṇaḥ*) — to the Brāhmaṇa who recites praise, or to the priest called Brahman. To all these Indra gives wealth and the like impartially and equally, is the sense. (Page 662 ends here, the sentence running on to the next page.)

### Printed p. 663

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 8.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 663.*

(Continuation of the sentence of p. 662.) Yāska explains the word नाधमानाः (*nādhamānāḥ*) as याचमानाः (*yācamānāḥ*) ("those who ask") (Ni. 4-2), and has listed the word कीरि (*kīri*) among the names of the praiser (Ni. 3-19).

युक्तग्राव्यो योऽविता सुशिप्रः सुतसोमस्य (*yuktagrāvyo yo 'vitā suśipraḥ sutasomasya*) — यश्च सुशिप्रः शोभनहनुः सुशीर्षको वा सन् युक्तग्राव्योऽभिषवार्थमुद्यतग्राव्णः सुतसोमस्याभिषुतसोमस्य यजमानस्याविता रक्षिता भवति (*yaśca suśipraḥ śobhanahanuḥ suśīrṣako vā san yuktagrāvyo 'bhiṣavārthamudyatagrāvṇaḥ sutasomasyābhiṣutasomasya yajamānasyāvitā rakṣitā bhavati*) — Here ग्राव्णः (*grāvṇaḥ*) is the genitive singular of the word ग्रावन् (*grāvan*). Yāska explains the word ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*) as ग्रावाणो हंतेर्वा गृह्णातेर्वा (*grāvāṇo haṃtervā gṛhṇātervā*) (Ni. 9-8). That is: this (the stone) is taken in the hand (गृह्णातेर्वा (*gṛhṇātervā*)) for striking, that is, crushing, the Soma plant, and so it is called ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*). Or, from गृणातेर्वा (*gṛṇātervā*), सूयंतेर्वा (*sūyaṃtervā*) [as printed], because it is praised it is called ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*); such is the explanation of the meaning. Here युक्तग्राव्णः (*yuktagrāvṇaḥ*) means: standing ready, holding the stones called ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*) needed for the work, in order to crush the Soma plants and press out the juice.

सुशिप्रः (*suśipraḥ*) — one with excellent cheeks, or one with a (fine) head, that is, Indra of beautiful form.

सुतसोमस्य (*sutasomasya*) — of the sacrificer who waits, having pressed the Soma juice and kept it ready to offer to Indra.

The sense of the sentence: Indra protects the sacrificer who, holding in his hands the stones for crushing the Soma plants, the means of preparing the Soma juice, waits with the pressed Soma juice kept ready to offer to Indra.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यस्याश्वा॑सः प्र॒दिशि॒ यस्य॒ गावो॒ यस्य॒ ग्रामा॒ यस्य॒ विश्वे॒ रथा॑सः ।
> यः सूर्यं॒ य उ॒षसं॑ ज॒जान॒ यो अ॒पां ने॒ता स ज॑नास॒
> इंद्रः॑ ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यस्य । अश्वासः । प्रदिशि । यस्य । गावः । यस्य । ग्रामाः । यस्य । विश्वे । रथासः ।
> यः । सूर्यं । यः । उषसं । जजान । यः । अपां नेता । सः। जनासः। इंद्रः॥७॥

> *yasya | aśvāsaḥ | pradiśi | yasya | gāvaḥ | yasya | grāmāḥ | yasya | viśve | rathāsaḥ |*
> *yaḥ | sūryaṃ | yaḥ | uṣasaṃ | jajāna | yaḥ | apāṃ netā | saḥ| janāsaḥ| iṃdraḥ‖7‖*

### Printed p. 664

*Running head: [664 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यस्य सर्वांतर्यामितया वर्तमानस्य प्रदिशि प्रदेशनेऽनुशासनेऽश्वासोऽश्वा
> वर्तंते । यस्यानुशासने गावः । यस्यानुशासने ग्रामाः । ग्रसंतेऽत्रेति ग्रामा
> जनपदाः । यस्याज्ञायां विश्वे सर्वॆ रथासो रथा वर्तंते । यश्च वृत्रं हत्वा
> सूर्यं जजान । जनयामास । यश्चोषसं । तथा मंत्रः जजान सूर्यमुषसं
> सुदंसाः । ३-३२-८ । इति । यश्च मेघभेदनद्वारापां नेता प्रेरकः स इंद्र इत्यादि
> प्रसिद्धं ॥

*Gist:* Horses, cows, villages and all chariots abide under Indra's rule; he begot the sun and the dawn and, by splitting the cloud, sets the waters in motion; he alone is Indra.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यस्य (*yasya*) — of whom, the inner controller of all. प्रदिशि (*pradiśi*) — under (his) command. अश्वासः (*aśvāsaḥ*) — the horses are. यस्य (*yasya*) — in whose command. गावः (*gāvaḥ*) — the cows are. यस्य (*yasya*) — in whose control. ग्रामाः (*grāmāḥ*) — the villages are. यस्य (*yasya*) — in whose control. विश्वे रथासः (*viśve rathāsaḥ*) — all the chariots are (and). यः (*yaḥ*) — who (having slain Vṛtra). सूर‍्यं (*sūryaṃ*) — the sun. जजान (*jajāna*) — created (and). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. उषसं (*uṣasaṃ*) — created the dawn. यः (*yaḥ*) — who (having split the cloud). अपां (*apāṃ*) — of the waters. नेता (*netā*) — is the leader. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he under whose rule are horses, cows, villages and all chariots, he who, having slain Vṛtra, created the sun and the dawn, he who, having split the cloud, is the leader (impeller) of the waters: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, under whose control are horses and cattle, and villages, and all chariots: he who gave birth to the sun and to the dawn: and who is the leader of the waters: he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

प्रदिशि (*pradiśi*) — प्रदेशने अनुशासने (*pradeśane anuśāsane*): in control, under command (Under his control); the sense is "subject to Indra's command or authority".

ग्रामाः (*grāmāḥ*) — ग्रसंतेऽत्रेति ग्रामा जनपदाः (*grasaṃte 'treti grāmā janapadāḥ*): Sāyaṇa has explained the word *grāma* as *janapada* or communities, that is, the people of one nation; it may also be said to mean dwelling places such as villages and towns where people live.

### Printed p. 665

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 8.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 665.*

अश्वासः गावः ग्रामाः रथासः (*aśvāsaḥ gāvaḥ grāmāḥ rathāsaḥ*) — It should not be understood that only these four kinds, horses, cows, villages and chariots, are subject to Indra and not other things. Since it is not possible to name all the things of the world one by one, only a few are mentioned here; likewise all creatures and things in the world are to be understood as subject to Indra's command.

रथासः (*rathāsaḥ*) — For the word *ratha* Yāska in his Nirukta gives the etymology रथो रंहतेर्गतिकर्मणः । स्थिरतेर्वा स्याद्विपरीतस्य । रममाणोऽस्मिंस्तिष्ठतीति वा रपतेर्वा रसतेर्वा (*ratho raṃhatergatikarmaṇaḥ | sthiratervā syādviparītasya | ramamāṇo 'smiṃstiṣṭhatīti vā rapatervā rasatervā*) (Ni. 9-11). That is, the word *ratha* may be formed either from the root *raṃh*, which denotes motion, or from the root *sthirati* in the sense "to be firm" (here the first two syllables of the root *sthirati* must be transposed). As this vehicle is always going (moving about), it is called *ratha*. Or the warrior who sits in it is steady: just as the body shakes when one sits on horses and other animals, one can sit in a chariot firmly without shaking, so it is called *ratha*. Or, because one sits and roams about in it, it is called *ratha*. Thus the etymological meanings are explained.

यः सूर्यं यः उषसं जजान (*yaḥ sūryaṃ yaḥ uṣasaṃ jajāna*) — Indra produced the sun and the goddess Uṣas. On this point:

इंद्रस्य कर्म सुकृता पुरूणि व्रतानि देवा न मिनंति विश्वे । दाधार यः पृथिवीं द्यामुतेमां जजान सूर्यमुषसं सुदंसाः ॥ (*iṃdrasya karma sukṛtā purūṇi vratāni devā na minaṃti viśve | dādhāra yaḥ pṛthivīṃ dyāmutemāṃ jajāna sūryamuṣasaṃ sudaṃsāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-32-8)

In this Ṛk it is said that Indra, the doer of excellent deeds, upholds the earth and heaven and created the sun and the dawn (printed उपस्स (*upassa*) in the Kannada, a misprint for *Uṣas* [sic]).

यः अपां नेता (*yaḥ apāṃ netā*) — यश्च मेघभेदनद्वारापां नेता प्रेरकः (*yaśca meghabhedanadvārāpāṃ netā prerakaḥ*): Because Indra strikes the clouds in the atmosphere held back by Vṛtra and makes the water pour down, he is called the impeller of the waters; that is, it is by Indra's impulsion that the clouds shed rain, and by that rain the water comes to the earth. Such is the meaning.

(The line कंठनाथः (*kaṃṭhanāthaḥ*) stands here in the supplied text, before the next Ṛk; it is not explained: reported for the user.)

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यं क्रंद॑सी संय॒ती वि॒ह्वये॑ते॒ परेव॑र उ॒भया॑ अ॒मित्राः॑ ।
> स॒मा॒नं चि॒द्रथ॑मातस्थि॒वांसा॒ नाना॑ हवेते॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ ८ ॥

> *yaṃ kraṃdasī saṃyatī vihvayete parevara ubhayā amitrāḥ |*
> *samānaṃ cidrathamātasthivāṃsā nānā havete sa janāsa iṃdraḥ ‖ 8 ‖*

### Printed p. 666

*Running head: [666 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यं । क्रंदसी इति । संयती इति संयती । विह्वयेते इति विह्वयेते । परे ।
> अवरे । उभयाः । अमित्राः ।
> समानं । चित् । रथं । आतस्थिवांसा । नाना । हवेते इति । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यं क्रंदसी रोदसी शब्दं कुर्वाणे मानुषी देवी च द्वे सेने वा संयती
> परस्परं संगच्छंत्यायमिंद्रं विह्वयेते। स्वरक्षार्थं विविधमाह्वयतः। पर उत्कृष्टा
> अवरेऽधमाश्चोभया उभयविधा उभयमाह्वयंति । समानमिंद्ररथसदृशं
> रथमातस्थिवांसा आस्थितौ द्वौ रथिनौ तमेवेंद्रं नाना पृथक् पृथक् हवेते । आह्व-
> येते । यद्वा समानमेकरथमारूढाविंद्राग्नी हवेते । यज्ञार्थं यजमानैः
> पृथगाहूयेते । तयोरन्यतरः स इंद्रो नाहमिति ॥

*Gist:* Indra is the one whom the two hosts, high and low enemies, and two charioteers in one car each invoke separately for help; alternatively Indra and Agni in one chariot are invoked by sacrificers, and one of the two is Indra.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यं (*yaṃ*) — whom. क्रंदसी (*kraṃdasī*) — the two, heaven and earth, or the two hosts that make a noise, the divine host and the human host. संयती (*saṃyatī*) — coming together. विह्वयेते (*vihvayete*) — call for protection. परे (*pare*) — the superior ones. अवरे (*avare*) — and the inferior ones. उभयाः (*ubhayāḥ*) — of both these kinds. अमित्राः (*amitrāḥ*) — enemies also call. समानं चित् (*samānaṃ cit*) — like Indra's chariot. रथं (*rathaṃ*) — the chariot. आतस्थिवांसा (*ātasthivāṃsā*) — the two charioteers who have mounted and sit. नाना हवेते (*nānā havete*) — call separately. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he whom the divine host and the human host, making noise together, call for protection, and whom enemies also, the superior and the inferior, call; and whom the two charioteers who have mounted a chariot like Indra's call separately (for protection): he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

Whom(two hosts), calling and mutually encountering, call upon; whom both adversaries, high and low,(appeal to); whom two(charioteers), stand-ing in the same car, severally invoke; he, men, is Indra.

### Printed p. 667

*Running head: [Ā. 2 A. 6 V. 8. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 667]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The meaning of this Ṛk is somewhat difficult, or rather the meaning explained by Sāyaṇa is not so well suited. According to Sāyaṇa: O people, the divine host and the human host, making a noise together, call whom for protection, and the enemies also, the superior and the inferior, call whom, and the two charioteers who have mounted a chariot like Indra's call whom separately for protection: he is Indra. This is the principal meaning of the Ṛk. Another kind of meaning is also possible. Namely: when two armies have come together on the battlefield and are fighting, both armies call upon Indra himself for protection. That is, Indra is not partial to one person or one army. Of two hostile armies, one may be superior, or greater in number or strength; the other may be inferior, or small in strength. Yet, since the people of both armies call upon Indra to be their protector, it becomes clear that Indra is wanted by both and is partial to neither. Though the people of the two armies are enemies of each other, Indra is wanted by both; therefore both call him: this may be the seer's intention. And in the second half of the Ṛk also it is said that the two warriors who sit in a chariot and fight call upon Indra alone, who has equal affection for the two of them. Here the commentator has explained the words समानं चित् (*samānaṃ cit*) as इंद्रसदृशं रथमातस्थिवांसा (*iṃdrasadṛśaṃ rathamātasthivāṃsā*), "seated in a chariot equal to Indra's chariot"; but the words समानं चित् (*samānaṃ cit*) can be interpreted as "Indra himself, who has equal affection". It appears that doing so is right.

Some defects are seen in Sāyaṇa's explanation of the meaning of this Ṛk. For the words क्रंदसी संयती (*kraṃdasī saṃyatī*) he says रोदसी शब्दं कुर्वाणे (*rodasī śabdaṃ kurvāṇe*), that heaven and earth make a noise and call Indra for protection. If so, is there mutual enmity between heaven and earth? If so, did they fight with noise and seek Indra's help for protection? Such doubts arise. Therefore this kind of explanation does not fit. Or, according to the other meaning the commentator has given, मानुषी देवी च द्वे सेने वा (*mānuṣī devī ca dve sene vā*), that is, two armies, a human army and a divine army: in this meaning also, where does enmity between men and gods come from? If perhaps "men" is taken to mean Asuras, it is well known that the gods and Asuras are enemies of each other, and it is natural that they fight. But since the Asuras are enemies of Indra, they are not people who desire Indra's help or protection. Therefore this meaning also does not fit properly. Besides, in this Ṛk there is no need at all to supply the word रोदसी (*rodasī*) (heaven and earth), as Sāyaṇa does. For the Ṛk itself says plainly उभयाः अमित्राः (*ubhayāḥ amitrāḥ*), the two parties of enemies, mutually hostile,

### Printed p. 668

*Running head: [668 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2 Ā. 2 Sū. 12.]*

and the words परे अवरे (*pare avare*) can be taken to mean one big army and another small army. To give the word समानं (*samānaṃ*) the meaning "chariot" is also not right; it must be interpreted as "Indra" (accusative). If this is done, the meaning of the Ṛk becomes clear and no difficulty remains. Therefore the principal meaning of this Ṛk may be stated thus: O people, whom the two parties of enemies call for protection when they have come together for battle, and whom the two warriors, eager for battle, sitting in a chariot facing each other, call separately, Indra himself, for protection: he is Indra.

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यस्मा॒न्न ऋ॒ते वि॒जयं॑ते॒ जना॑सो॒ यं युध्य॑माना॒ अव॑से॒ हवं॑ते ।
> यो विश्व॑स्य प्रति॒मानं॑ ब॒भूव॒ यो अ॑च्युत॒च्युत्स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यस्मात् । न । ऋते । विजयंते । जनासः । यं । युध्यमानाः । अवसे । हवंते ।
> यः । विश्वस्य । प्रतिमानं । बभूव । यः । अच्युतच्युत् । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥९॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यस्मादृते जनासो जना न विजयंते । विजयं न प्राप्नुवंति । अतो
> युध्यमाना युद्धं कुर्वाणा जना अवसे स्वरक्षणाय यमिंद्रं हवंते । आह्व-
> यंति । यश्च विश्वस्य सर्वस्य जगतः प्रतिमानं प्रतिनिधिर्बभूव । यश्चाच्युतच्युत्
> अच्युतानां क्षयरहितानां पर्वतादीनां च्यावयिता स इंद्र इत्यादि प्रसिद्धं ॥

*Gist:* People do not win without Indra; hence fighters invoke him for protection; he is the counterpart of the whole world and the shaker of unshaken mountains and the like.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यस्मात् ऋते (*yasmāt ṛte*) — without whom. जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — men. न विजयंते (*na vijayaṃte*) — cannot win victory (and therefore). यं (*yaṃ*) — whom. युध्यमानाः (*yudhyamānāḥ*) — those engaged in battle. अवसे (*avase*) — for protection. हवंते (*havaṃte*) — call. यः (*yaḥ*) —

### Printed p. 669

*Running head: [A. 2 Ā. 6 V. 8. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 669]*

who. विश्वस्य (*viśvasya*) — of the whole world. प्रतिमानं (*pratimānaṃ*) — as the counterpart (representative). बभूव (*babhūva*) — has become (and). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. अच्युतच्युत् (*acyutacyut*) — made the unmoved mountains and the like move. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he without whom men cannot win victory, and therefore those engaged in battle call whom for protection, who is the representative of the whole world, and who made the unmoved mountains and the like move: he is Indra. (The supplied text prints इंदनु (*iṃdanu*) at the end, apparently a misprint for इंद्रनु (*iṃdranu*) [sic].)

**Printed English translation**

Without whom men do not conquer; whom, when engaged in conflict, they invoke for succour; he, who is the protctype [sic] of the universe and the caster down of the unyielding; he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अच्युतच्युत् (*acyutacyut*) — अच्युतानां क्षयरहितानां पर्वतादीनां च्यावयिता (*acyutānāṃ kṣayarahitānāṃ parvatādīnāṃ cyāvayitā*): the commentator's explanation is that he has the power to make mountains and the like, which are imperishable or do not move, move. It is not clear what the correct meaning of this word is. The seer's intention is to describe Indra as doing work that ordinary people cannot do. Therefore the main idea is that Indra is able, by his extraordinary power, to destroy even what is regarded as indestructible.

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः शश्व॑तो॒ मह्येनो॒ दधा॑ना॒नम॑न्यमाना॒ञ्र्वा॑ ज॒घान॑ ।
> यः शर्ध॑ते॒ नानु॒ददा॑ति श्र॒ध्यां यो दस्यो॑र्हं॒ता स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ १० ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । शश्वतः । महि । एनः । दधानान् । अमन्यमानान् । शर्वा । जघान ।

> *yaḥ | śaśvataḥ | mahi | enaḥ | dadhānān | amanyamānān | śarvā | jaghāna |*

### Printed p. 670

*Running head: [670 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṃ. 2 A. 2 Sū. 12]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । शर्धते । न । अनुददाति । श्रध्यां । यः । दस्योः । हंता । सः । जनासः ।
> इंद्रः ॥ १० ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो महि महदेनः पापं दधानान् शश्वतो बहूनमन्यमानान् आत्मान-
> मजानत इंद्रमपूजयतो वा जनान् शर्वा । शृणाति शत्रूननेनेति शरुर्वज्रः ।
> तेनायुधेन जघान ॥ हंतेर्लिटि रूपं ॥ यश्च शर्धत उत्साहं कुर्वतेऽनात्म-
> ज्ञाय जनाय शृध्यामुत्साहनीयं कर्म नानुददाति । न प्रयच्छति ॥ अनुपूर्वात्
> डुदाञ् दाने चौहोत्यादिकः । अभ्यस्तानामादिरिति तिजि चोदात्तावतीति गतेर्नि-
> घातः ॥ यश्च दस्योरुपक्षपयितुः शत्रोर्हंता घातकः स इंद्र इत्यादि पूर्ववत् ॥

*Gist:* Indra with his thunderbolt slew the many sinful, arrogant men who did not honour him; he gives no encouragement to the proud, and he is the slayer of the Dasyu.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. महि (*mahi*) — great. एनः (*enaḥ*) — sin. धधानान् (*dhadhānān*) [sic, as supplied] — those who bear (it), शश्वतः (*śaśvataḥ*) — numerous, अमन्यमानान् (*amanyamānān*) — and puffed up with arrogance: these men. शर्वा (*śarvā*) — with the thunderbolt. जघान (*jaghāna*) — slew. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. शर्धते (*śardhate*) — to the one who is not a knower of the Self. कृध्यां (*kṛdhyāṃ*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has श्रध्यां (*śradhyāṃ*)] — help that gives encouragement. न अनुददाति (*na anudadāti*) — does not give. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. दस्योः (*dasyoḥ*) — of the enemy who causes destruction. हंता (*haṃtā*) — is the destroyer. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who slew with the thunderbolt those who bear great sin, who are numerous and full of arrogance; who does not give (the supplied text has a misprint in this word [sic]) encouraging help to the one who is not a knower of the Self; who is the destroyer of the enemy that causes destruction: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, who with the thunderbolt has destroyed many committing great sin, and offering(him) no homage: who grants not success to the confident; who is the slayer of the Dasyu; he, men, is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

शश्वतः (*śaśvataḥ*) — Since the word शश्वत् (*śaśvat*) is read among the twelve words for "many" (उरु (*uru*), तुवि (*tuvi*) and the rest) (Ni. 3-13), शश्वतः (*śaśvataḥ*) means numerous, abundant.

### Printed p. 671

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 6 V. 9] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 671]*

एनः (*enaḥ*) — एन एतेः (*ena eteḥ*) (Ni. 11-24); तदस्येवमेवावश्यमेवैति (*tadasyevamevāvaśyamevaiti*). Because this (sin) follows the doer without fail, एनः (*enaḥ*) means sin.

अमन्यमानान् (*amanyamānān*) — The commentator explains: आत्मानमजानत इंद्रमपूजयतो वा जनान् (*ātmānamajānata iṃdramapūjayato vā janān*). मन्यमानान् (*manyamānān*) means those who know; अमन्यमानान् (*amanyamānān*) means those who do not know, that is, those who do not know the true state of things or the true reality, the Self (the Brahman), or those who, not knowing Indra's qualities and the like, do not worship him.

शर्वा (*śarvā*) — For the word शरु (*śaru*), formed from the root श्य हिंसायां (*śya hiṃsāyāṃ*), the meaning is that which injures, or a weapon such as the thunderbolt that is the means of injury.

यः शश्वतः महि एनः दधानान् अमन्यमानान् शर्वा जघान (*yaḥ śaśvataḥ mahi enaḥ dadhānān amanyamānān śarvā jaghāna*) — Indra struck with his thunderbolt the many people who had done great sin and, devoid of true knowledge, did not worship Indra; that is, Indra destroyed many wicked asuras who, devoted to sinful acts only, performed no sacrifices and did not worship Indra. This is the main idea.

यः शर्धते न अनुददाति श्रध्यां (*yaḥ śardhate na anudadāti śradhyāṃ*) — The word शर्धः (*śardhaḥ*) is read among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9). Here the commentator explains this sentence as शर्धत उत्साहं कुर्वतेनात्मज्ञाय जनाय श्रध्यामुत्साहनीयं कर्म नानुददाति न प्रयच्छति (*śardhata utsāhaṃ kurvatenātmajñāya janāya śradhyāmutsāhanīyaṃ karma nānudadāti na prayacchati*), that is, that Indra does not create enthusiasm in a man who is full of enthusiasm but is not a knower of the Self. The main idea is that Indra gives no help or encouragement of any kind to one who is proud of his strength. This meaning does not appear to be correct for the sentence. The seer's intention cannot be understood clearly.

यः दस्योः हंता (*yaḥ dasyoḥ haṃtā*) — He who destroys the Dasyu people, the enemies of the Āryas. Slaying the Dasyu is regarded as a special quality of Indra. It is the custom, when praising Indra, to describe him as the destroyer of the Dasyus.

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः शंब॑रं॒ पर्व॑तेषु क्षि॒यंतं॑ चत्वारुं॒श्यां श॒रद्य॒न्वविं॑दत् ।
> ओ॒जा॒यमा॑नं॒ यो अहिं॑ ज॒घान॒ दानुं॒ शया॑नं॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ ११ ॥

> *yaḥ śaṃbaraṃ parvateṣu kṣiyaṃtaṃ catvāruṃśyāṃ śaradyanvaviṃdat |*
> *ojāyamānaṃ yo ahiṃ jaghāna dānuṃ śayānaṃ sa janāsa iṃdraḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

### Printed p. 672

*Running head: [672 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṃ. 2 A. 2 Sū. 12]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । शंबरं । पर्वतेषु । क्षियंतं । चत्वारुंश्यां । शरदि । अनुअविंदत् ।
> ओजायमानं । यः । अहिं । जघान । दानुं शयानं । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः । ॥१०॥

> *yaḥ | śaṃbaraṃ | parvateṣu | kṣiyaṃtaṃ | catvāruṃśyāṃ | śaradi | anuaviṃdat |*
> *ojāyamānaṃ | yaḥ | ahiṃ | jaghāna | dānuṃ śayānaṃ | saḥ | janāsaḥ | iṃdraḥ | ‖10‖*

(The number printed at the end of the Pada is 10 [sic], as supplied; the Ṛk is the eleventh.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यः पर्वतेषु क्षियंतमिंद्रभिया बहून् संवत्सरान्प्रच्छन्नो भूत्वा
> पर्वतगुहासु निवसंतं शंबरमेतन्नामकं मायाविनमसुरं चत्वारिंश्यां शरदि
> चत्वारिंशे संवत्सरेऽनविंदत् । अन्विष्यालभत । लब्धा च य ओजायमानं ॥ कर्तुः
> क्यज् सलोपश्च ओजसोऽप्सरसो नित्यं । पा. ३-१-११-२ । इति सकारलोपः ॥ बलमा-
> चरंतमहिमाहंतारं दानुं दानवं शयानं शंबरमसुरं जघान हतवान् स
> इंद्रो नाहमिति ॥

> *yaḥ parvateṣu kṣiyaṃtamiṃdrabhiyā bahūn saṃvatsarānpracchanno bhūtvā*
> *parvataguhāsu nivasaṃtaṃ śaṃbarametannāmakaṃ māyāvinamasuraṃ catvāriṃśyāṃ śaradi*
> *catvāriṃśe saṃvatsare 'naviṃdat | anviṣyālabhata | labdhā ca ya ojāyamānaṃ ‖ kartuḥ*
> *kyaj salopaśca ojaso 'psaraso nityaṃ | pā. 3-1-11-2 | iti sakāralopaḥ ‖ balamā-*
> *caraṃtamahimāhaṃtāraṃ dānuṃ dānavaṃ śayānaṃ śaṃbaramasuraṃ jaghāna hatavān sa*
> *iṃdro nāhamiti ‖*

*Gist:* Hidden for forty years in the mountain caves for fear of Indra, the demon Śambara was found in the fortieth year and slain by Indra; the grammatical note explains the loss of स् (*s*) in ओजायमानं (*ojāyamānaṃ*) (Pā. 3-1-11-2, as printed).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. पर्वतेषु (*parvateṣu*) — in the mountains. क्षियंतं (*kṣiyaṃtaṃ*) — living in hiding. शंबरं (*śaṃbaraṃ*) — the asura named Śambara. चत्वारिंश्यां तरदि (*catvāriṃśyāṃ taradi*) [as supplied] — in the fortieth year. अन्वविंदत् (*anvaviṃdat*) — searched out and found. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. ओजायमानं (*ojāyamānaṃ*) — who relied on strength, अहिं (*ahiṃ*) — the slayer, दानुं (*dānuṃ*) — the son of Danu, शयानं (*śayānaṃ*) — who lay sleeping on the mountain: that Śambara. जघान (*jaghāna*) — slew. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who, after searching, at last found the asura named Śambara, who had lived hiding in the mountains for forty years, and who slew that demon who was strong and a slayer: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, who discovered Shambara dwelling in the mountains for forty years; who slew Ahi, growing in strength, and the sleeping son of Danu; he, mcn [sic], is Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

शंबरं (*śaṃbaraṃ*) — The word शंबर (*śaṃbara*) is read among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-21) and among the names of water (Ni. 2-24) (the supplied text prints a different, apparently misspelt, word for "is read" [sic]). In the present Ṛk, शंबर (*śaṃbara*) means the asura of that name.

### Printed p. 673

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 6 V. 9. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 673]*

This Śambara is an enemy of Indra. His name and his story occur in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-51-6, 1-54-4, 1-59-6, 1-101-2, 1-103-8, 1-112-14, 1-130-7, 2-12-11, 2-14-6, 2-19-6, 4-26-3, 4-30-14, 6-18-8, 6-26-5, 6-31-4, 6-31-1, 6-47-2, 6-47-21, 7-18-20, 7-99-5. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-26-5 he is called a Dāsa, the son of one Kulikāra. As a rule his name is recited together with Śuṣṇa, Pipru and Varcin. That he had 90, 99 or 100 forts (strongholds) is said in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-130-7, 2-19-6 and 2-14-6. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-24-2 the very word शंबराणि (*śaṃbarāṇi*) is used to denote his strongholds. That he was the chief enemy of Divodāsa Atithigva, and that Atithigva defeated him with the help of Indra, is said in Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-51-6, 1-130-7, 2-19-6 and 4-26-3.

Some Western scholars express a different opinion about this Śambara. Some hold that the doubt arises whether he was really a living person or one famous only by name. The scholar Hillebrandt takes him to be a real person, the chief enemy of one Divodāsa. Some hold that he was a well-known chieftain among the earlier inhabitants of this country, who lived in the hills and mountains and troubled the Āryas.

**Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu (continued from the previous page)**

ओजायमानं—बलमाचरंतं । (*ojāyamānaṃ—balamācaraṃtaṃ |*) Endowed with energy (ojas) or strength; the meaning is "very strong".

अहिं—आहंतारं । (*ahiṃ—āhaṃtāraṃ |*) For this word, formed from the root हन हिंसागत्योः (*hana hiṃsāgatyoḥ*), the meaning "slayer" is given.

दानुं—दानवं । दनोः पुत्रं । (*dānuṃ—dānavaṃ | danoḥ putraṃ |*) "the son of Danu, the mother of the rākṣasas". The commentator says in the bhāṣya of Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-32-9 that दानुर्दादनी वृत्रमाता (*dānurdādanī vṛtramātā*) (Dānu is Dā-danī, the mother of Vṛtra).

शरदि (*śaradi*) — in the autumn, that is, in the year. This word is formed from the root श्व हिंसायां (*śva hiṃsāyāṃ*) [as printed]; शीर्यंते अस्यां ओषधयः इति शरत् संवत्सरः (*śīryaṃte asyāṃ oṣadhayaḥ iti śarat saṃvatsaraḥ*): the plants, leaves and the like dry up in this season and fall down; therefore that season is called शरत् (*śarat*) or the autumn season. Since autumn comes only once in a year, it is customary in the Vedas to use the word शरत् (*śarat*) in the sense of "year".

तच्चक्षुर्देवहितं शुक्रमुच्चरत् । (*taccakṣurdevahitaṃ śukramuccarat |*)
पश्येम शरदः शतं जीवेम शरदः शतं ॥ (*paśyema śaradaḥ śataṃ jīvema śaradaḥ śataṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-66-16)

### Printed p. 674

*Running head: [674 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṃ. 2 A. 2 Sū. 12]*

दीर्घायुरस्या यः पतिर्जीनाति शरदः शतं । (*dīrghāyurasyā yaḥ patirjīnāti śaradaḥ śataṃ |*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 10-85-39)

In such Ṛks the word शरत् (*śarat*) is used in the sense of "year".

यः शंबरं पर्वतेषु क्षियंतं चॆत्वारुंश्यां शरदि अन्वविंदत् (*yaḥ śaṃbaraṃ parvateṣu kṣiyaṃtaṃ cètvāruṃśyāṃ śaradi anvaviṃdat*) — Indra searched out and slew the asura Śambara, who had hidden in the mountains for forty years so that no one could get at him. This describes Indra's valour.

#### Ṛk 12

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः स॒प्तर॑श्मिर्वृष॒भस्तु वि॑ष्मान॒वासृ॑ज॒त्सर्त॑वे स॒प्त सिंधू॑न् ।
> यो रौ॑हि॒णमस्सु॑र॒द्वज्र॑बाहु॒र्दार्मा मा॒रोहं॑तं॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑ ॥ १२ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । सप्तरश्मिः । वृषभः । तुविष्मान् । अव्यअसृजत् । सर्तवे । सप्त । सिंधून् ।
> यः । रौहिणं । अस्फुरत् । वज्रबाहुः । द्यां । आरोहंतं । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः ॥ १२ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यः सप्तरश्मिः सप्तसंख्याकाः पर्जन्या रश्मयो यस्य । ते च रश्मयो वरा-
> हवः स्वतपसो विद्युन्महसो धूपयः श्वापयो गृहमेधाश्चतीति ये चेमेS-
> शिमिविद्विषः पर्जन्याः सप्त पृथिवीमभिवर्षंति वृष्टिभिः । तै आ. १-९-४,५ । इति तैत्तिरी-
> यारण्यके ह्याम्नाताः । वृषभो वर्षकस्तुविष्मान् वृद्धिमान्बलवान्वा सप्त सर्पण-
> स्वभावान्सिंधूनपः सर्तवे सरणायानासृजत् । अवसृष्टवान् । यद्वा गंगाद्याः सप्त
> मुख्या नदीरस्पजत् । यश्च वज्रबाहुः सन् द्यां दिवमारोहंतं रौहिणमसुरम-
> स्फुरत् । जघान ॥ स्फुर स्पुरणे तुदादिः ॥

> *yaḥ saptaraśmiḥ saptasaṃkhyākāḥ parjanyā raśmayo yasya | te ca raśmayo varā-*
> *havaḥ svatapaso vidyunmahaso dhūpayaḥ śvāpayo gṛhamedhāścatīti ye cemeS-*
> *śimividviṣaḥ parjanyāḥ sapta pṛthivīmabhivarṣaṃti vṛṣṭibhiḥ | tai ā. 1-9-4,5 | iti taittirī-*
> *yāraṇyake hyāmnātāḥ | vṛṣabho varṣakastuviṣmān vṛddhimānbalavānvā sapta sarpaṇa-*
> *svabhāvānsiṃdhūnapaḥ sartave saraṇāyānāsṛjat | avasṛṣṭavān | yadvā gaṃgādyāḥ sapta*
> *mukhyā nadīraspajat | yaśca vajrabāhuḥ san dyāṃ divamārohaṃtaṃ rauhiṇamasurama-*
> *sphurat | jaghāna ‖ sphura spuraṇe tudādiḥ ‖*

*Gist:* The seven rays are seven Parjanyas (rain-clouds with their wind-hosts); the "showerer" lets the seven rivers flow, or the seven chief rivers beginning with the Gaṅgā; Indra slew the Asura Rauhiṇa as he climbed to heaven. The note on the root स्फुर (*sphura*) closes it.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. सप्तरश्मिः (*saptaraśmiḥ*) — he who has seven rays; वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*) — the showerer of rain; तुविष्मान् (*tuviṣmān*) — being powerful; सप्त सिंधून् (*sapta siṃdhūn*) — the seven rivers, or flowing waters;

### Printed p. 675

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 6 V. 9] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 675]*

सर्तवे (*sartave*) — for flowing. अवास्पजत् (*avāspajat*) [as supplied] — released (from Ahi). यः (*yaḥ*) — who. वज्रबाहुः (*vajrabāhuḥ*) — holding the thunderbolt in his hand. द्यां (*dyāṃ*) — the heavenly world. आरोहंतं (*ārohaṃtaṃ*) — climbing. रौहिणं (*rauhiṇaṃ*) — the asura named Rauhiṇa. अस्फुरत् (*asphurat*) — killed. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who, having seven rays, a showerer of rain and powerful, released the seven rivers beginning with the Gaṅgā from Ahi so that they might flow; who, holding the thunderbolt in his hand, killed the asura named Rauhiṇa as he climbed to the heavenly world: he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, the seven-rayed, the showerer, the powerful, who let loose the seven rivers to flow; who, armed with the thunderbolt, crushed Rauhina when scaling heaven; he, men, is Indra.

**Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu**

सप्तरश्मि:—सप्त संख्याकाः पर्जन्या रश्मयो यस्य । (*saptaraśmi:—sapta saṃkhyākāḥ parjanyā raśmayo yasya |*) The passage that follows in the text (Tai. Ā. 1-9-4 to 5) names the seven rays as वराहवः (*varāhavaḥ*), स्वतपसः (*svatapasaḥ*), विद्युन्महसो (*vidyunmahaso*), धूपयः (*dhūpayaḥ*), श्वापयो (*śvāpayo*) and गृहमेधाः (*gṛhamedhāḥ*), and says that the seven Parjanyas rain upon the earth with showers. When Indra pours down rain, that is, strikes the clouds and makes them rain, those clouds rain together with seven kinds of rays, or Parjanya deities; this is the meaning given, and the commentator cites the passage of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka in support. Explaining the words पर्जन्याः सप्त (*parjanyāḥ sapta*) in the quotation, it is said that the Parjanyas (clouds) accompanied by seven kinds of wind-hosts pour down rain. Which are those seven kinds of Parjanyas? —

(1) वराहवः (*varāhavaḥ*) — the commentary derives वराह (*varāha*) as that which calls forth excellent rain (वरं श्रेष्ठं वर्षमाह्वयतीति (*varaṃ śreṣṭhaṃ varṣamāhvayatīti*)); because it pours excellent rain, that wind-host is called वराह (*varāha*). The clouds accompanied by such a wind-host are called वराहवः (*varāhavaḥ*).

(2) स्वतपसः (*svatapasaḥ*) — those winds which burn of themselves, not depending on fire or the sun for heat. The clouds accompanied by a wind-host of the nature that burns by its own power, without needing fire or the sun for warmth, are called स्वतपसः (*svatapasaḥ*).

### Printed p. 676

*Running head: [675 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ[ ಮಂ. ೨. ಅ. ೨ ಸೂ ೧೨]*

(3) विद्युन्महसः (*vidyunmahasaḥ*) — विद्युत्समानं महस्तेजो येषां ते विद्युन्महसः (*vidyutsamānaṃ mahastejo yeṣāṃ te vidyunmahasaḥ*): those whose great radiance equals lightning. The clouds with their wind-hosts, shining with a great radiance equal to lightning (the flash), or with lightning itself, are called विद्युन्महसः (*vidyunmahasaḥ*).

(4) धूपयः (*dhūpayaḥ*) — they go about making all things fragrant by their own natural perfume alone, without depending on other substances such as guggulu or the ten-fold incense (दशांग (*daśāṃga*)). The clouds with their wind-hosts, endowed with such natural fragrance, are called धूपयः (*dhūpayaḥ*).

(5) श्वापयः (*śvāpayaḥ*) — the syllable शु (*śu*) is a synonym of आशु (*āśu*) (quick), hence "those that spread swiftly". The clouds with their wind-hosts, whose nature is to spread swiftly everywhere, are called श्वापयः (*śvāpayaḥ*).

(6) गृहमेधाः (*gṛhamedhāḥ*) — those worthy of worship in houses. The clouds accompanied by wind-deities, worshipped in houses and the like, are called गृहमेधाः (*gṛhamedhāḥ*).

(7) अशिमिविद्विषः (*aśimividviṣaḥ*) — the commentary explains शिमि (*śimi*) as work such as ploughing, and the winds that are favourable to ploughing and the like are meant. The clouds accompanied by wind-hosts that help to pour rain at the proper seasons, favourably to agriculture such as tilling, are called अशिमिविद्विषः (*aśimividviṣaḥ*).

Since it is said that Parjanya, or the clouds, are of the seven kinds stated above, सप्तरश्मि (*saptaraśmi*) here means that Indra pours rain by seven kinds of clouds.

यः अवासृजत् सर्तवे सप्त सिंधून् (*yaḥ avāsṛjat sartave sapta siṃdhūn*) — Indra, having poured rain with the seven kinds of clouds, after those waters fell on the earth, made the seven chief rivers, the Gaṅgā, Yamunā and the others, flow in streams. The expression सप्त सिंधून् (*sapta siṃdhūn*) here need not be understood as exactly seven rivers; they are only the chief rivers. The main sense is that he made the water fallen on the earth flow in the rivers and in other regions.

यः रौहिणं अस्फुरत् वज्रबाहुः द्यां आरोहंतं (*yaḥ rauhiṇaṃ asphurat vajrabāhuḥ dyāṃ ārohaṃtaṃ*) — it is said that Indra killed with the thunderbolt the asura named Rauhiṇa as he was climbing to heaven. The same matter is stated also in:

अहस्य हिमभिनदौ हिणं व्यहन्वंसं मघवा शचीभिः । (*ahasya himabhinadau hiṇaṃ vyahanvaṃsaṃ maghavā śacībhiḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-103-2)

### Printed p. 677

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೬. ವ. ೯] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 677]*

The Ṛk above says so too. In the Ṛgveda the name of the asura Rauhiṇa is mentioned only in these two places and nowhere else. Therefore nothing more can be said about this Rauhiṇa. Besides, since the word रौहिण (*rauhiṇa*) is also read among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-21), the meaning can also be given that Indra struck the cloud with the thunderbolt and produced rain.

In the same way, since the word शंबर (*śaṃbara*) too is read among the names of cloud, the slaying of Śambara may be understood, in the respective contexts, as the striking of the cloud: another kind of meaning.

#### Ṛk 13

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> द्यावा॑ चिदस्मै पृथि॒वी न॑मेते॒ शुष्मा॑च्चिदस्य॒ पर्व॑ता भयंते ।
> यः सो॑म॒पा नि॑चि॒तो वज्र॑बाहु॒र्यो वज्र॑हस्तः॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> द्यावा । चित् । अस्मै । पृथिवी इति । नमेते इति । शुष्मात् । चित् । अस्यॆ । पर्वताः ।
> भयंते ।
> यः । सोमपाः । निचितः । वज्रबाहुः । यः । वज्रहस्तः । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः
> ॥ १३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अस्मा इंद्राय द्यावा पृथिवी ॥ इतरेतरापेक्षया द्विवचनं प्र मित्रयो-
> र्वरुणयोः । ७_६६_१ । इतिवत् ॥ नमेते । स्वयमेव प्रह्वीभवतः ॥ णमु प्रह्वत्वे।
> कर्मकर्तरि न दुहस्सुनमां यक्चिणाविति यकः प्रतिषेधः ॥ चिदपि च अस्येंद्रस्य
> शुष्माद्बलात्पर्वता भयंते । बिभ्यति । यः सोमपाः सोमस्य पाता निचितः सर्वैः ।
> यद्वा अन्येभ्योऽपि देवेभ्यो दृढांगः । वज्रबाहुर्वज्रसदृशबाहुः । यश्च वज्र-
> हस्तो वज्रयुक्तः स इंद्र इत्यादि प्रसिद्धं ॥

*Gist:* Heaven and earth bow of themselves to Indra; mountains fear his might; he is the Soma-drinker, firm of body, with arms like the thunderbolt and the thunderbolt in his hand.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. अस्मै (*asmai*) — to this Indra. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — heaven and earth both. नमेते (*namete*) — bow in humility. चित् (*cit*) — and. अस्य (*asya*) — of this Indra. शुष्मात् चित् (*śuṣmāt cit*) — by (his) might alone. पर्वताः (*parvatāḥ*) — the mountains. भयंते (*bhayaṃte*) — are afraid. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. सोमपाः (*somapāḥ*) —

### Printed p. 678

*Running head: [678 ಸಾಯಣಭಾವ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ[ ಮಂ. ೨ ಅ. ೨. ಸೂ. ೧೨.]*

drinker of the Soma juice, निचितः (*nicitaḥ*) — firm of body, वज्रबाहुः (*vajrabāhuḥ*) — having arms equal to the thunderbolt. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. वज्रहस्तः (*vajrahastaḥ*) — holds the thunderbolt in his hand. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

To this Indra heaven and earth both bow in humility; and by this Indra's might alone the mountains are afraid. He who is a drinker of the Soma juice, firm of body, and has arms equal to the thunderbolt, he who holds the thunderbolt in his hand, he is Indra.

**Printed English translation**

He, to whom heaven and earth bow down; he, at whose might the mountains are appalled; he, who is the drinker of the Soma juice, the firm(of frame), the adamant-armed, the wielder of the thunderbolt; he, men, is Indra.

**Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu**

In this Ṛk Indra's great power and ability are stated. Heaven and earth bow to Indra; the mountains (or clouds) are afraid on seeing Indra's strength: thus the sage describes Indra's surpassing might.

पर्वताः (*parvatāḥ*) — mountains or clouds. Since the word पर्वत (*parvata*) is read among the names of cloud, two kinds of meaning, clouds and mountains, may be given here.

सोमपाः (*somapāḥ*) — we have already explained Indra's Soma-drinking at length earlier. It has been said much that among the Soma-drinking deities Indra is the foremost, and that Indra has great fondness for Soma-drinking. For the Soma-drinking Indra is invited more than all the other deities.

वज्रबाहुः वज्रहस्तः (*vajrabāhuḥ vajrahastaḥ*) — these two words have one meaning. Because they occur thus in one sentence, the commentators have explained वज्रबाहुः (*vajrabāhuḥ*) as one having arms like the thunderbolt, that is, hard as the thunderbolt (of exceeding arm-strength), and वज्रहस्तः (*vajrahastaḥ*) as one who bears the thunderbolt in his hand.

### Printed p. 679

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೬. ವ. ೯.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 679*

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः सु॒न्वंत॒मव॑ति॒ यः पचं॑तं॒ यः शंसं॑तं॒ यः श॑शमा॒नमू॒ती ।
> यस्य॒ ब्रह्म॒ वर्ध॑नं॒ यस्य॒ सोमो॒ यस्ये॒दं राधः॒ स ज॑नास॒ इंद्रः॑

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । सुन्वंतं । अवति । यः । पचंतं । यः । शंसंतं । यः । शशमानं । ऊती ।
> यस्य । ब्रह्म । वर्धनं । यस्य । सोमः । यस्य । इदं । राधः । सः । जनासः । इंद्रः
> ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यः सुन्वंतं सोमाभिषवं कुर्वंतं यजमानमवति । रक्षति । यश्च पुरो-
> डाशादीनि हवींषि पचंतं । यश्चोती ऊतये॥ सुपां सुलुगिति चतुर्थ्याः पूर्व-
> सवर्णदीर्घः ॥ स्वरक्षायै शस्त्राणि शंसंतं यश्च शशमानमवति । स्तोत्रं कुर्वाणं
> रक्षति । ब्रह्म परिवृढं स्तोत्रं यस्य वर्धनं वृद्धिकरं भवति । तथा यस्य सोमो
> वृद्धिहेतुर्भवति । यस्य चेदमस्मदीयं राधः पुरोडाशादिलक्षणमन्नं वृद्धिकरं
> भवति । स इंद्र इत्यादि प्रसिद्धं ॥

*Gist:* Indra protects the sacrificer who presses Soma, cooks the oblations, recites and praises; prayer, Soma and the food offered increase him.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जनासः (*janāsaḥ*) — O people. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. सुन्वंतं (*sunvaṃtaṃ*) — the sacrificer who presses the Soma juice. अवति (*avati*) — protects. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. पचंतं (*pacaṃtaṃ*) — protects the one who cooks the purodāśa and the like. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. ऊती (*ūtī*) — for protection. शंसंतं (*śaṃsaṃtaṃ*) — protects the one who prays. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. शशमानं (*śaśamānaṃ*) — protects the one who praises (and). यस्य (*yasya*) — to whom. ब्रह्म (*brahma*) — praise. वर्धनं (*vardhanaṃ*) — is an increaser. यस्य (*yasya*) — to whom. सोमः (*somaḥ*) — the drinking of the Soma juice is an increaser. यस्य (*yasya*) — to whom. इदं (*idaṃ*) — given by us. राधः (*rādhaḥ*) — the purodāśa and other food is an increaser. सः (*saḥ*) — he. इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*) — Indra.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O people, he who protects the sacrificer who presses the Soma juice, who protects the one who cooks the purodāśa and the like, who protects those who ask for protection, who protects the praiser, and for whom praise, Soma juice and the purodāśa and other foods are increasers: he is Indra.

### Printed p. 680

*Running head: [680 ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ[ಮಂ. ೨. ಅ. ೨ ಸೂ. ೧೨]*

**Printed English translation**

He, who protects(the worshipper) offering the libation, or preparing (the mixed curds and butter), repeating his praise and soliciting his assi- stance; he, of whom the sacred prayer, the(offered) Soma juice, the(presented sacrificial) food, augment(the vigour); he, men, is Indra.

**Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu**

This Ṛk states that Indra is pleased by the oblations and the like made and offered by the sacrificer and the priests.

यः सुन्वंतं अवति (*yaḥ sunvaṃtaṃ avati*) — सोमाभिषवं कुर्वंतं यजमानमवः (*somābhiṣavaṃ kurvaṃtaṃ yajamānamavaḥ*). Since Soma-drinking is pleasing to Indra, Indra protects the sacrificer who presses and prepares the Soma juice at the sacrifice to offer it to Indra.

यः पचंतं यः शंसंतं यः शशमान ऊती (*yaḥ pacaṃtaṃ yaḥ śaṃsaṃtaṃ yaḥ śaśamāna ūtī*) — पुरोडाशादिहवींषि पचंतं, ऊतये स्वरक्षायै शस्त्राणि शंसंतं स्तोत्रं कुर्वाणं रक्षति (*puroḍāśādihavīṃṣi pacaṃtaṃ, ūtaye svarakṣāyai śastrāṇi śaṃsaṃtaṃ stotraṃ kurvāṇaṃ rakṣati*). It must not be understood that Indra protects only the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice. The intended sense is that he also protects and looks after the priest who cooks (boils) the oblations and the like in the fire to offer them to Indra at the sacrifice; the priest called Hotṛ who recites the śastra mantras for Indra's pleasure; and the other priests and the like who pray to Indra for their own protection.

यस्य ब्रह्म वर्धनं यस्य सोमः यस्य इदं राधः (*yasya brahma vardhanaṃ yasya somaḥ yasya idaṃ rādhaḥ*) — in this sentence the meaning stated above is stated again in another way. It has been said that Indra protects the sacrificer and the priests. For the praise made to Indra by the priests or sacrificers is an increaser, that is, it is pleasing. In the same way the Soma juice and the oblations and the like that they offer are increasers for Indra. Therefore the sage explains that Indra protects the sacrificers and other people who praise him.

#### Ṛk 15

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यः सु॑न्व॒ते पच॑ते दु॒ध्र आ चि॒द्वाजं॒ दर्द॑षि॒ स किला॑सि स॒त्यः ।
> व॒यं त॑ इंद्र वि॒श्वह॑ प्रि॒यासः॑ सु॒वीरा॑सो वि॒दथ॒मा व॑देम ॥१५॥

> *yaḥ sunvate pacate dudhra ā cidvājaṃ dardaṣi sa kilāsi satyaḥ |*
> *vayaṃ ta iṃdra viśvaha priyāsaḥ suvīrāso vidathamā vadema ‖15‖*

### Printed p. 681

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೬. ವ. ೯.] ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ 681*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । सुन्वते । पचिते । दुध्रः । आ । चित् । वाजं । दर्दषि । सः । किल । असि । सत्यः ।
> वयं । ते । इंद्र । विश्वह । प्रियासः । सुवीरासः । विदथं । आ । वदेम ॥ १५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> इदानीमृषिः साक्षात्कृतमिंद्रं प्रति प्रब्रूते । हे इंद्र यो दुध्रो
> दुर्धरः सन् सुन्नते सोमाभिषवं कुर्वते पुरोडाशादिहवींषि पचिते यजमा-
> नाय वाजमन्नं बलं वादर्दर्षि । भृशं प्रापयसि स तादृशस्त्वं सत्यो यथार्थ-
> भूतोऽसि । न पुनर्नास्तीति बुद्धियोग्योऽसि । किलेति प्रसिद्दौ । ते तव प्रियासः
> सुवीरासः कल्याणपुत्रपौत्राः संतो वयं विश्वह सर्वेष्वहःसु विदथं स्तोत्रमा-
> वदेम । ब्रूयाम ॥

*Gist:* The sage addresses Indra: he who, unassailable, gives food and strength to the sacrificer who presses Soma and cooks the oblations is truly real; blessed with sons, we shall recite praise daily.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

इंद्र (*iṃdra*) — O Indra. यः (*yaḥ*) — who. दुद्रः (*dudraḥ*) — unassailable. सुन्वते (*sunvate*) — to the sacrificer who presses the Soma juice. पचते (*pacate*) — and who cooks the purodāśa and the like. वाजं (*vājaṃ*) — food and strength. आ दर्दर्षि (*ā dardarṣi*) — you give abundantly. सः (*saḥ*) — such a you. सत्यः (*satyaḥ*) — of the nature of truth. असि किल (*asi kila*) — you are, are you not. ते (*te*) — to you. प्रियासः (*priyāsaḥ*) — dear ones. सुवीरासः (*suvīrāsaḥ*) — (by your favour) endowed with auspicious sons, grandsons and so on. वयं (*vayaṃ*) — we. विश्वह (*viśvaha*) — on all days. विदथं (*vidathaṃ*) — praise. आ वदेम (*ā vadema*) — we recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra, you who, unassailable, give abundantly food and strength to the sacrificer who presses the Soma juice and cooks the purodāśa and the like, you are indeed of the nature of truth, are you not? We, who are dear to you and endowed by your favour with auspicious sons, grandsons and so on, recite praise on all days.

**Printed English translation**

Indra, you are difficult of approach; you, verily, are a true(bene-- factor), who bestow(abundant) food upon him who offers the libation, who prepares(the mixed curds and butter): may we, enjoying your favour, blessed with progeny, daily repeat(your praise) at the sacrifice.

### Printed p. 682

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 12; p. 682.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk, which concludes the sūkta, the seer addresses Indra directly and prays that he give the desired things, such as food and heroic sons and grandsons. Yāska has said that mantras are of many kinds according to the sense in them. It is necessary to state this briefly here—

तद्यानि नामानि प्राधान्यस्तु तीनां देवतानां तद्वैवतमित्याचक्षते । यत्काम (*tadyāni nāmāni prādhānyastu tīnāṃ devatānāṃ tadvaivatamityācakṣate | yatkāma*) ऋषिय स्यां देवतायामार्थपत्यमिच्छन्तुतिं प्रयुंक्ते तद्वैवतः स (*ṛṣiya syāṃ devatāyāmārthapatyamicchantutiṃ prayuṃkte tadvaivataḥ sa*) मंत्रो भवति । तास्त्रिविधा ऋचः परोक्षकृताः प्रत्यक्षकृता आध्यात्मिकाश्च । (*maṃtro bhavati | tāstrividhā ṛcaḥ parokṣakṛtāḥ pratyakṣakṛtā ādhyātmikāśca |*) तत्र परोक्षकृताः सर्वाभिर्नामविभक्तिभिर्युज्यंते प्रथमपुरुषैश्चाख्यातस्य । (*tatra parokṣakṛtāḥ sarvābhirnāmavibhaktibhiryujyaṃte prathamapuruṣaiścākhyātasya |*) अथ प्रत्यक्षकृता मध्यमपुरुषोपयोगास्त्वमिति चैतेन सर्वनाम्मा । (*atha pratyakṣakṛtā madhyamapuruṣopayogāstvamiti caitena sarvanāmmā |*) अथाध्यात्मिक्य उत्तमपुरुषयोगा अहमिशि चैतेन सर्वनाम्मा । परोक्ष- (*athādhyātmikya uttamapuruṣayogā ahamiśi caitena sarvanāmmā | parokṣa-*) कृताः प्रत्यक्षकृताश्च मंत्रा भूयिष्ठा अल्पश आध्यात्मिकाः ॥ (*kṛtāḥ pratyakṣakṛtāśca maṃtrā bhūyiṣṭhā alpaśa ādhyātmikāḥ ‖*) (Nirukta, Daivata-kāṇḍa 7-1 to 3)

That is, among mantras there are many kinds according to sense: the deity-mantra (दैवतमंत्र (*daivatamaṃtra*)), praise (स्तुति (*stuti*)), blessing (आशीः (*āśīḥ*)), oath (शपथ (*śapatha*)), curse (अभिशाप (*abhiśāpa*)), कश्चिद्भाव (*kaścidbhāva*), lament (परिदेवना (*paridevanā*)), censure (निंदा (*niṃdā*)) and eulogy (प्रशंसा (*praśaṃsā*)). A deity-mantra is one that describes the greatness of the deities; praise is a mantra containing hymns or prayers; आशीः (*āśīḥ*) is a benediction, a mantra wishing good; शपथ (*śapatha*) is a vow; अभिशाप (*abhiśāpa*) is an ill wish; कश्चिद्भाव (*kaścidbhāva*) is the explanation of some subject or principle; परिदेवना (*paridevanā*) is grief; निंदा (*niṃdā*) is blame, reviling; प्रशंसा (*praśaṃsā*) is praise, extolling; दानस्तु (*dānastu*) and so on [as printed].

Among the many kinds of mantra mentioned above, the Ṛks called deity-mantras are of three kinds: परोक्षकृत (*parokṣakṛta*), प्रत्यक्षकृत (*pratyakṣakṛta*) and आध्यात्मिक (*ādhyātmika*). परोक्षकृत (*parokṣakṛta*) means: since the deity praised is not present before the speaker, the mantras use, in speaking of that deity, third-person nominal words and verbal forms (Third person both in subject and predicate); प्रत्यक्षकृत (*pratyakṣakṛta*) means the Ṛks that praise the deity as if present before one's eyes, addressing it with second-person words such as "you" and "your" (Second person); आध्यात्मिक (*ādhyātmika*) means the mantras that praise using the first person (First person). Among these three kinds, the two kinds परोक्षकृत (*parokṣakṛta*) and प्रत्यक्षकृत (*pratyakṣakṛta*) are the more numerous; the आध्यात्मिक (*ādhyātmika*) mantras are only few. In the present sūkta the first fourteen Ṛks are परोक्षकृत (*parokṣakṛta*) mantras; the last, the fifteenth Ṛk, is प्रत्यक्षकृत (*pratyakṣakṛta*).

### Printed p. 683

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 10.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā, p. 683.*

*The thirteenth sūkta.*

## Sūkta 13 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Introduction to the sūkta (Sanskrit, transcribed)**

> ऋतुर्जनित्रीति त्रयोदशर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं गार्त्समदं । ऋतुः सप्तो-
> नांत्या त्रिष्टुबित्यनुक्रमणिका । अस्मभ्यं तद्वसो इति त्रयोदशी त्रिष्टुप् । शिष्टास्त्रि-
> ष्टु बंतपरिभाषया जगत्यः । इंद्रो देवता ॥ उक्थे तृतीयसवनेच्छावाकशस्त्रेऽस
> सूक्तस्य विनियोगः । सूत्रितं च । ऋतुर्जनित्री नू मर्तो भवा मित्रः । आ- ६-१ ।
> इति ॥ दशरात्रे षष्ठेऽहन्यपि तृतीयसवनेच्छावाकशस्त्र एतत्तूक्तं । ऋतुर्जनित्रीति
> नित्यान्यैकाहिकानि । आ. ८-४ । इति सूत्रितं ॥

> *ṛturjanitrīti trayodaśarcaṃ dvitīyaṃ sūktaṃ gārtsamadaṃ | ṛtuḥ sapto-*
> *nāṃtyā triṣṭubityanukramaṇikā | asmabhyaṃ tadvaso iti trayodaśī triṣṭup | śiṣṭāstri-*
> *ṣṭu baṃtaparibhāṣayā jagatyaḥ | iṃdro devatā ‖ ukthe tṛtīyasavanecchāvākaśastre 'sa*
> *sūktasya viniyogaḥ | sūtritaṃ ca | ṛturjanitrī nū marto bhavā mitraḥ | ā- 6-1 |*
> *iti ‖ daśarātre ṣaṣṭhe 'hanyapi tṛtīyasavanecchāvākaśastra etattūktaṃ | ṛturjanitrīti*
> *nityānyaikāhikāni | ā. 8-4 | iti sūtritaṃ ‖*

*Gist:* The thirteenth sūkta, ऋतुर्जनित्री (*ṛturjanitrī*), thirteen Ṛks, is the second sūkta of the second anuvāka; seer Gṛtsamada; the first twelve Ṛks are Jagatī, the last Triṣṭubh; deity Indra. It is used in the Acchāvāka's śastra at the third pressing of the Ukthya rite and on the sixth day of the Daśarātra, as prescribed in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (6-1; 8-4).

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

Translation—This sūkta beginning ऋतुर्जनित्री (*ṛturjanitrī*) is the second sūkta in the second anuvāka. It has thirteen Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada. Since the Anukramaṇikā says ऋतुः सप्तोनांत्या त्रिष्टुप् (*ṛtuḥ saptonāṃtyā triṣṭup*), the last Ṛk, the thirteenth, beginning अस्मभ्यं तद्वसो (*asmabhyaṃ tadvaso*), is in the Triṣṭubh metre; the remaining twelve Ṛks are in the Jagatī metre; the deity is Indra. This sūkta is applied to the śastra mantras to be recited by the priest called Acchāvāka at the third pressing in the Ukthya sacrifice, as set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra ऋतुर्जनित्री नू मर्तो भवा मित्रः (*ṛturjanitrī nū marto bhavā mitraḥ*) (6-1); and it is set out by the sūtra ऋतुर्जनित्री नित्यानैकाहिकानि (*ṛturjanitrī nityānaikāhikāni*) (8-4) that this sūkta is to be recited among the śastra mantras recited by the Acchāvāka priest at the third pressing on the sixth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra.

### Printed p. 684

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 13; p. 684.*

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 13. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 13. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 10, 11, 12. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 13. Seer: Gṛtsamada. Deity: Indra. Metre: 1-12 Jagatī; 13 Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ऋ॒तुर्जनि॑त्री॒ तस्या॑ अ॒पस्परि॑ म॒क्षू जा॒त आवि॑श॒द्यासु॒ वर्ध॑ते ।
> तदा॑ह॒ना अ॑भवत्प्॒युषी॒ पयों॒शोः पी॒यूषं॑ प्रथ॒मं तदु॒क्थ्यं॑

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋतुः । जनित्री । तस्याः । अपः । परि । मक्षु । जातः । आ । अविशत् । यासु । वर्धते ।
> तत् । अहनाः । अभवत् । पिप्युषी । पयः । अंशोः । पीयुषं । प्रथमं । तत् ।
> उक्तं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋतुर्वर्षाख्यः कालो जनित्री सोमस्य जनयित्री जननी भवति । तस्याः परि
> तस्या जनन्याः सकाशाज्ञातः सोमोऽप उदकानि मक्षु शीघ्रमाविशत् । यास्वप्सु
> सोमो वर्धते ता अविशदित्यन्वयः । तस्मादप्पु प्रविष्टत्वात् सोम आहना आहंत-
> व्योऽभिषोतव्यो भवति । आहना इति सकारांतमिदं पदं । तथा च मंत्रवर्णः ।
> ये ते मदा आहनसो विहायसः । ९-७५-५ । इति । या चांशुरूपा सोमलता
> पयः सारभूतं पिप्युषी वर्धयंत्यभवत् ॥ प्यायतेर्लिटि क्वसौ लड्यजोश्चेति
> पीभावः । वसोः संप्रसारणं । उगितश्चेति जिप् ॥ अंशोस्तस्याः सोमलतायाः
> पीयूषं रसभूतं पय उक्थं प्रशस्यं हविरिंद्रस्य भवति ॥

*Gist:* The season (the rainy season) is the mother of Soma; Soma, born of her, quickly entered the waters in which he grows; hence he is to be pressed. The plant's milk, the essence, is the offering for Indra.

### Printed p. 685

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 10] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 685.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋतुः (*ṛtuḥ*) — the rainy season. जनित्री (*janitrī*) — is the mother of Soma (the time that produces it). तस्याः परि (*tasyāḥ pari*) — from that mother. जातः (*jātaḥ*) — the Soma that has been born. यासु (*yāsu*) — in which waters. वर्धते (*vardhate*) — it grows. अपः (*apaḥ*) — such waters. मक्षु (*makṣu*) — quickly. आ विशत् (*ā viśat*) — enters, and. तत् (*tat*) — it. आहनाः (*āhanāḥ*) — becomes fit for pressing (that same Soma plant). पयः (*payaḥ*) — as the essence. पिप्युषी (*pipyuṣī*) — as the one that grows. अभवत् (*abhavat*) — becomes. अंशोः (*aṃśoḥ*) — of that same Soma plant. पीयूषं (*pīyūṣaṃ*) — the one that is the essence. प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*) — the one that is chief. तत् (*tat*) — that juice. उक्थ्यं (*ukthyaṃ*) — becomes worthy of praise.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The season called Varṣā (the rains) is the mother of Soma. The Soma born of that mother quickly enters the waters in which it grows. And, becoming the essence and the one that grows, it becomes fit for pressing. The juice of that same Soma plant, which is the essence and the chief, is worthy of praise.

**Printed English translation**

The season(of the rains) is the parent(of the Soma plant), which, as soon as born of her, enters into the waters in which it grows; thence it is fit for expression, as concentrating(the essence of the) water; and the juice of the Soma is especially to be praised(as the libation proper for Indra).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऋतुर्जनित्री (*ṛturjanitrī*) — the rainy season, that is, the monsoon, is the mother of Soma. That is, just as other plants grow in the monsoon, the Soma plant also grows well and flourishes with the rain water. Therefore the seer describes the rainy season as the mother of Soma. Although this Ṛk has no word denoting the Soma plant, yet because the words अहनाः (*ahanāḥ*) पयः (*payaḥ*) अंशोः (*aṃśoḥ*), which help the explanation concerning the Soma juice, are present, and because the Ṛk too speaks of the pressing of Soma, the word सोमस्य (*somasya*) must be supplied by adhyāhāra so as to complete the sentence ऋतुः सोमस्य जनित्री (*ṛtuḥ somasya janitrī*).

तस्याः अपः परि मक्षु जातः आ अविशत् यासु वर्धते (*tasyāḥ apaḥ pari makṣu jātaḥ ā aviśat yāsu vardhate*) — Since the Soma plant that grows in that rainy season grows only by the support of water, "it entered the water" means that the plant filled with juice (water) and grew sturdy. This is the sense. Any plant, with the help of water in the rainy season, not only grows well but becomes moist, green and juicy. The Soma plant too, growing well in the rains and being full of juice — this juiciness of the plant the seer describes in another manner, as "growing in water and entering the water".

तत् आहनाः तस्मादप्पु प्रविष्टत्वात् सोम आहना आहंतवोऽभिषोतव्यो भवति । आहना इति सकारांतमिदं पदं । तथा च मंत्रवर्णाः । ये ते मदा आहनसो विहायसः (*tat āhanāḥ tasmādappu praviṣṭatvāt soma āhanā āhaṃtavo 'bhiṣotavyo bhavati | āhanā iti sakārāṃtamidaṃ padaṃ | tathā ca maṃtravarṇāḥ | ye te madā āhanaso vihāyasaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-75-5) — Because the Soma plant is full of water (juice), it must be struck in order to bring that out, that is, it must be well beaten and crushed with stones. When it is so crushed, the juice comes out of the plant. This process is called आहनाः (*āhanāḥ*) or pressing. आहनाः (*āhanāḥ*) is a word ending in स (*sa*); the commentator says it is nominative singular. On this point he cites the Ṛk

ये ते मदा आहनसो विहायसस्तेभिरिंद्रं जोदय दातवे मघं (*ye te madā āhanaso vihāyasastebhiriṃdraṃ jodaya dātave maghaṃ*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-75-2)

and shows that the word आहनसः (*āhanasaḥ*) in it is a word ending in स (*sa*) and in the nominative plural.

अभवत् पिप्युषी पयः अंशोः पीयूषं प्रथमं तत् उक्ष्यं (*abhavat pipyuṣī payaḥ aṃśoḥ pīyūṣaṃ prathamaṃ tat ukṣyaṃ*) (as printed) — या चांशुरूपा सोमलता पयः सारभूतं पिप्युषी वर्धयंत्यभवत् । अंशोस्तस्याः सोमलतायाः पीयूषं रसभूतं पय उक्ष्यं प्रशस्यं हविरिंद्रस्य भवति (*yā cāṃśurūpā somalatā payaḥ sārabhūtaṃ pipyuṣī vardhayaṃtyabhavat | aṃśostasyāḥ somalatāyāḥ pīyūṣaṃ rasabhūtaṃ paya ukṣyaṃ praśasyaṃ haviriṃdrasya bhavati*) — On the word अंशु (*aṃśu*) Yāska says:

अंशुः शमष्टमात्रो भवति । अननाय शं भवतीति वा । (*aṃśuḥ śamaṣṭamātro bhavati | ananāya śaṃ bhavatīti vā |*)

(Si. 9-5, as supplied)
