> प्रथमे पर्याये होतुः शस्त्रयाज्याध्वर्यव इत्येषा । अध्वर्यवो भरतेंद्राय
> सोममिति याज्या । आ. ६-४ । इति सूत्रितं ॥
> अनुवादवु—अध्वर्यवो भरत ऎंब ई सूक्तवु ऎरडनॆय अनुवाकदल्लि मूरनॆय
> सूक्तवु. इदरल्लि हन्नॆरडु ऋक्कुगळिरुववु. ई सूक्तक्कॆ गृत्समदनु ऋषियु; इंद्रनु देव
> तॆयु; त्रिष्टुप् छंदस्सु अनुक्रमणिकॆयल्लि अध्वर्यवो द्वादश ऎंदु मुंतागि हेळिरुवुदु.
> ई सूक्तद विनियोगवु लैंगिकवु. अतिरात्रवॆंब यागदल्लि प्रथम पर्यायदल्लि होतृवु अध्व-
> र्यवो भरत ऎंब ई सूक्तद मॊदलनॆय ऋक्कन्नु शस्त्रयाज्यामंत्रवन्नागि पठिसबेकॆंदु
> आश्वलायन शौतसूत्रद—अध्वर्यवो भरतेंद्राय सोममिति याज्या ऎंब सूत्रदिंद
> विवृतवागिरुवुदु(आ. ६-४).

*Gist:* The fourteenth sūkta, *adhvaryavo bharata*, has twelve ṛks; the third sūkta of the second anuvāka; seer Gṛtsamada, deity Indra, metre Triṣṭubh; applied in the Atirātra, first paryāya, as the Hotṛ's śastra-yājyā, as prescribed in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 6-4).

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

Translation—This sūkta beginning अध्वर्यवो भरत (*adhvaryavo bharata*) (*adhvaryavo bharata*) is the third sūkta in the second anuvāka. It has twelve ṛks. Gṛtsamada is the seer of this sūkta, Indra the deity, Triṣṭubh the metre; the Anukramaṇikā says अध्वर्यवो द्वादश (*adhvaryavo dvādaśa*) (*adhvaryavo dvādaśa*) and so on. The application of this sūkta is the Liṅga (by the sense of the text). In the sacrifice called Atirātra, in the first paryāya, the Hotṛ priest is to recite the first Ṛk of this sūkta अध्वर्यवो भरत (*adhvaryavo bharata*) (*adhvaryavo bharata*) as the śastra-yājyā mantra; this is set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra by the sūtra अध्वर्यवो भरतेंद्राय सोममिति याज्या (*adhvaryavo bharateṃdrāya somamiti yājyā*) (*adhvaryavo bharatendrāya somam iti yājyā*) (Ā. 6-4).

### Printed p. 720

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 14; p. 720.*

## Sūkta 14 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 14. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 14. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13, 14. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 12. Seer: Gṛtsamada (printed गृत्समदॆः! (*gṛtsamadèḥ!*), a misprint or reading slip in the supplied text). Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭubh. (A stray line "१०६ राडाळि (*106 rāḍāḷi*)" follows in the supplied text; its meaning is unclear.)

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ भर॒तेंद्रा॑य॒ सोम॒माम॑त्रेभिः सिंचता॒ मद्य॒मंधः॑ ।
> का॒मी हि वी॒रः सद॑मस्य पी॒तिं जु॒होत॒ वृष्णे॒ तदिदे॒ष व॑ष्टि ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । भरत । इंद्राय । सोमं । आ । अमत्रेभिः । सिंचत । मद्यं । अंधः ।
> कामी । हि । वीरः । सदं । अस्य । पीतिं । जुहोत । वृष्णे । तत् । इत् । एषः । वष्टि ।
> ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> गृत्समदो ब्रूते । अध्वर्यवः अध्वरस्य नेतारः । अध्वरं यजमानाये-
> च्छंतः ॥ कव्यध्वरपृतनस्यर्चि लोप इत्यकारलोपः । अत्र यास्कः । अध्वर्युरध्वर-
> युरध्वरं युनक्त्यध्वरस्य नेताध्वरं कामयत इति । नि. १-८ ॥ हे अध्वर्यवः
> इंद्राय देवाय सोमं हविर्धानादुत्तरवेदिं प्रति भरत । हरत ॥ आमंत्रित-
> स्याविद्यमानत्वादनिघातः ॥ हृत्वामत्रेभिः । अमा सहादंत्यत्र होत्रादय इत्यम-
> त्राणि चमसाः । त्रैर्मद्यं मदकरमंधः सोमलक्षणमन्नमासिंचत । अग्नौ प्रक्षिपत ।
> अत्र यास्कः । आ सिंचतामत्रैर्मदनीयमंधोऽमत्रं पात्रममा अस्मिन्नदंति
> नि. ५-१ । इति । वीरः स इंद्रोऽस्य सोमस्य प्रीतिं पानं प्रति सदं सदैव कामी हि ।
> कामयमानो हि । तस्माद्वृष्टे वर्षित्र इंद्राय सोमं जुहोत । जुहुत ॥
> हु दानादनयोः । लोटि तप्तनप्तनथनाश्चति तबादेशः ॥ ऐष इंद्रस्तदिश् तदेव
> सोमद्रव्यं वष्टि । कामयते ॥

*Gist:* Gṛtsamada speaks: O Adhvaryus, carry Soma for Indra and pour the intoxicating Soma food into the fire; Indra, the hero, always desires the drinking of it, so offer it to him.

The grammatical remarks of the commentary (the elision of *a* in अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*) (*adhvaryavaḥ*), Yāska's explanation of *adhvaryu* (Ni. 1-8) and of the Soma vessels (Ni. 5-1), the non-accent of the vocative, the imperative of the root *hu*) are not explained here.

### Printed p. 721

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 721.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra. सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice. भरत (*bharata*)—bring. अमत्रेभिः (*amatrebhiḥ*)—with the ladle-vessels (camasa). मद्यं (*madyaṃ*)—intoxicating. अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*)—the food in the form of Soma. आ सिंचत (*ā siṃcata*)—offer (around the fire) in the manner of a sprinkling. वीरः (*vīraḥ*)—Indra, the valiant. अस्य (*asya*)—of this Soma juice. पीतिं (*pītiṃ*)—the drinking. सदं (*sadaṃ*)—always. कामी हि (*kāmī hi*)—he desires, does he not (therefore). वृष्टे (*vṛṣṭe*) [sic, as supplied]—to Indra, the giver of desired objects. जुहोत (*juhota*)—offer Soma and make the oblation. एषः (*eṣaḥ*)—Indra. तदित् (*tadit*)—that very Soma. वष्टि (*vaṣṭi*)—he desires.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, bring the Soma juice for Indra. With the camasa vessels offer the intoxicating food in the form of Soma around the fire, in the manner of a sprinkling. The valiant Indra always desires the drinking of this Soma juice. Therefore offer this Soma to Indra, the giver of desired objects, and make the oblation.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, bring the Soma libation for Indra; sprinkle with ladles the exhilarating beverage: he, the hero, is ever desirous of the draught: offer the libation to the showerer(of benefits), for, verily, he desires it.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

This sūkta begins with the sentence अध्वर्यवो भरतेंद्राय सोमं (*adhvaryavo bharateṃdrāya somaṃ*); the meaning of this sentence is: "O priests called Adhvaryus, bring the Soma juice for Indra." Every Ṛk of this sūkta (except the last) begins with the word आध्वर्यवः (*ādhvaryavaḥ*) [sic, as supplied]. In every Ṛk one or two heroic deeds of Indra and the matter of Indra's drinking of Soma are told, and besides this the sentence अध्वर्यवो भरतेंद्राय सोमं (*adhvaryavo bharateṃdrāya somaṃ*) mentioned above, or another sentence containing its meaning, is certainly present.

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—अध्वरस्य नेतारः । अध्वरं यजमानायेच्छंतः । (*adhvarasya netāraḥ | adhvaraṃ yajamānāyecchaṃtaḥ |*) अध्वर (*adhvara*) means sacrifice. The priest who takes the lead in it, that is, plays the chief part and carries out the various works of the sacrifice, is called Adhvaryu. Or: one who wishes to perform the sacrifice for the sacrificer, that is, who stands in as the sacrificer's representative and himself does the many procedures to be carried out in the sacrifice, is called Adhvaryu. Although there are many priests in a sacrifice, among them the Hotṛ plays the chief part in the recitation of mantras, and the Adhvaryu in all the other procedures.

### Printed p. 722

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 14; p. 722.*

**Special Topics (continued)**

Therefore the Adhvaryu must give special attention to all the procedures that take place in the sacrifice. For this very reason the priests called Adhvaryus are addressed here, to prepare and set down Soma juice for Indra's drinking of Soma. On the derivation of the word अध्वर्यु (*adhvaryu*) Yāska says (Ni. 1-8):

अध्वर्युरध्वरयुरध्वरं युनक्तध्वरस्य नेताध्वरं कामयत इति वा । अपि वाधीयाने युरुपबंधः । अध्वर इति यज्ञनाम । ध्वरतिर्हिंसाकर्मा तत्त्व,तिषेधः । (*adhvaryuradhvarayuradhvaraṃ yunaktadhvarasya netādhvaraṃ kāmayata iti vā | api vādhīyāne yurupabaṃdhaḥ | adhvara iti yajñanāma | dhvaratirhiṃsākarmā tattva,tiṣedhaḥ |*) (The supplied text reads तत्त्व,तिषेधः (*tattva,tiṣedhaḥ*) with a stray comma; the passage is left as supplied.)

The word अध्वर्यु (*adhvaryu*) means one who brings about the form of the sacrifice. How? In the word अध्वर्यु (*adhvaryu*) the letter *a* that stood after the *r* has undergone elision according to the Veda-grammar (the supplied text prints नेदव्याकरणसास्त्रानुसारवागि (*nedavyākaraṇasāstrānusāravāgi*), apparently a misreading). If it is restored the form is अध्वरयुः (*adhvarayuḥ*). For this Yāska gives the derivation अध्वरं युनक्तीति अध्वरयुः (*adhvaraṃ yunaktīti adhvarayuḥ*)—one who joins the sacrifice. अध्वरस्य नेता (*adhvarasya netā*) means one who conducts the sacrifice. Or अध्वरं कामयते कर्तुमित्यध्वर्युः (*adhvaraṃ kāmayate kartumityadhvaryuḥ*)—because he desires to perform the sacrifice (for the sacrificer), this priest is called Adhvaryu. Or अपि वाधीयाने युरुपबंधः (*api vādhīyāne yurupabaṃdhaḥ*)—if यु (*yu*) is taken as a prefix, the word means a Brāhmaṇa (priest) who has studied the अध्वर (*adhvara*) or sacrifice (the Yajurveda, which teaches the matters of the sacrifice). Here the root ध्वरति (*dhvarati*) has the meaning "to do harm", so ध्वन (*dhvana*) [sic, as supplied] is harm; अध्वर (*adhvara*) is explained as the rite or sacrifice that is without ध्वन (*dhvana*), that is, without harm. Hence the main sense is that the word अध्वरयु (*adhvarayu*) or अध्वर्यु (*adhvaryu*) means the priest who carries out the sacrificial rite (which is not a rite of harm).

अध्वर्यवः भरत इंद्राय सोमं (*adhvaryavaḥ bharata iṃdrāya somaṃ*)—हे अध्वर्यवः इंद्राय देवाय सोमं हविर्धानादुत्तरवेदिं प्रति भरत । (*he adhvaryavaḥ iṃdrāya devāya somaṃ havirdhānāduttaravediṃ prati bharata |*) "O priests called Adhvaryus, bring the Soma juice that is in the Havirdhāna place to the Uttaravedi, to offer it to Indra": thus the seer Gṛtsamada (or the sacrificer) has addressed the Adhvaryus.

अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*)—The word अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Moreover, Yāska has stated a derivation for this word:

अंध इत्यन्ननामाध्यानीयं भवति । तमोऽप्यंध उच्यते नास्मिन् ध्यानं भवति । न दर्शनमंधंतम इत्यभिभाषंते । अयमपीतरोऽन्न एतस्मादेव । पश्यदक्षण्वान्न विचेतदंधः इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*aṃdha ityannanāmādhyānīyaṃ bhavati | tamo 'pyaṃdha ucyate nāsmin dhyānaṃ bhavati | na darśanamaṃdhaṃtama ityabhibhāṣaṃte | ayamapītaro 'nna etasmādeva | paśyadakṣaṇvānna vicetadaṃdhaḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 5-1) (printed with the reference in the form of two figures joined by a full stop, as supplied)

### Printed p. 723

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 723.*

**Special Topics (continued)**

That is: अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is a name of food. Why? Because food is needed by all, it is prayed for (desired) by all; therefore अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is food. अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is also a name of darkness (tamas). Why? Because in darkness nothing is seen and so nothing can be done. Even in ordinary usage people say अंधंतम (*aṃdhaṃtama*) when darkness has fallen so that nothing can be seen by the eye. On this point there is also an example from the Veda: पश्यदक्षण्वान्न विचेतदंधः (*paśyadakṣaṇvānna vicetadaṃdhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-16).

आ अमत्रेभिः सिंचत मद्यं अंधः (*ā amatrebhiḥ siṃcata madyaṃ aṃdhaḥ*)—हृत्वामत्रेभिः । अमा सहादंत्यत्र होत्रादय इत्यमत्राणि चमसाः । तैर्मद्यं मदकरमंधः सोमलक्षणमन्नमासिंचत । अग्नौ प्रक्षिपत । (*hṛtvāmatrebhiḥ | amā sahādaṃtyatra hotrādaya ityamatrāṇi camasāḥ | tairmadyaṃ madakaramaṃdhaḥ somalakṣaṇamannamāsiṃcata | agnau prakṣipata |*) In the previous sentence it was directed to the Adhvaryus that the Soma should be brought and placed near the Uttaravedi, to be offered to Indra. The main sense of this sentence is: "Make an oblation in the fire to Indra, with camasas (wooden ladles), of the Soma so brought, or the food in the form of Soma." On the word अमत्र (*amatra*) the commentator says अमा सहादंत्यत्र होत्रादय इत्यमत्राणि चमसाः (*amā sahādaṃtyatra hotrādaya ityamatrāṇi camasāḥ*), that is, because in the Soma-drinking of the sacrifice the Hotṛ and the other priests together, all from one vessel, eat or drink, such a vessel is called camasa. The camasa is made of wood, has a handle, and is in the shape of a ladle. All the priests drink Soma from this camasa; each does not use a separate vessel; one vessel is for all. Yāska, taking this very Ṛk as the example for the word अमत्रेभिः (*amatrebhiḥ*), gives the derivation (Ni. 2-1-5, printed "2. 1-5" [as supplied; the intended value may be 2-1 or 5-1]):

आमत्रेभिः सिंचता मद्यमंधः । आसिंचतामत्रैर्मदनीयमंधः । अमत्रं पात्रममा अस्थिन्नदंत्यमा पुनरनिर्मितं भवति पात्रं पानात् । (*āmatrebhiḥ siṃcatā madyamaṃdhaḥ | āsiṃcatāmatrairmadanīyamaṃdhaḥ | amatraṃ pātramamā asthinnadaṃtyamā punaranirmitaṃ bhavati pātraṃ pānāt |*)

That is, अमत्र (*amatra*) means a vessel. Why? Because from it (food or Soma) is taken little by little and eaten, such a ladle-like vessel is called अमत्र (*amatra*). अमा (*amā*) means that which is without measure. When Soma juice is filled in abundance (without measure or gauge) in a large vessel, to drink it they fill from it little by little with the wooden ladle called अमत्र (*amatra*) or camasa and drink (Like drinking tea or coffee by means of spoons). Such a vessel is called अमत्र (*amatra*) or camasa. The sense of the sentence is: "Take, with such camasas, the intoxicating, that is, joy-producing, food in the form of Soma, and put it (into the fire, for Indra)." Since Indra is not a visible deity, all the oblations offered to him are, with reference to him, with mantras

### Printed p. 724

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 14; p. 724.*

**Special Topics (continued)**

(The passage continues from the previous page.) Since the oblation is to be made in the fire itself, the word आ सिंचत (*ā siṃcata*) conveys the sense "make the oblation in the fire itself, with the vaṣaṭ call". Moreover, in the second half of this Ṛk the expression जुहोत (*juhota*) ("make the oblation in the fire") actually occurs. Since both words मद्यं (*madyaṃ*) and अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) occur in this sentence, it may be understood either as the oblation in the form of intoxicating Soma juice (food), or as two kinds of substance, the intoxicating Soma juice and the food in the form of an oblation. But since in all the Ṛks of this sūkta only the matter of the Soma juice for Indra's drinking is mentioned, it is appropriate to take the words मद्यमंधः (*madyamaṃdhaḥ*) to mean the oblation in the form of Soma, that is, Soma.

कामिा हि वीरः सदं अस्य पीतिं (*kāmiā hi vīraḥ sadaṃ asya pītiṃ*)—the heroic Indra surely desires the drinking of this Soma always. The main sense is: "Is not the valiant Indra one who longs for the drinking of Soma?" Indra's attachment to Soma-drinking is greater than that of all the other deities. Therefore all the seers pray that Indra may come to the sacrifices they perform and drink the Soma they offer.

जुहोत वृष्णे तत् इत् एषः वष्टि (*juhota vṛṣṇe tat it eṣaḥ vaṣṭi*)—therefore offer Soma to the showerer Indra (वृष्णे (*vṛṣṇe*)). That very Soma substance this Indra desires. Therefore make the oblation of Soma juice in the fire for Indra, who gives rain or who gives the objects of desire; for it is this (the drinking of Soma) that this Indra desires. Although the matter stated in the second and the fourth pādas of this Ṛk is one, the seer has expressed the idea with different words and different sentences in order to explain the sense clearly.

The commentary written on this Ṛk by Durgācārya, the commentator on the Nirukta, contains some clear explanations, so it is given here:

गृत्समदस्येयमार्षं । रात्रिपर्याये शस्यते । होतुः शस्त्रे विनियुक्ता । (*gṛtsamadasyeyamārṣaṃ | rātriparyāye śasyate | hotuḥ śastre viniyuktā |*)

अस्यैव च शस्त्रस्य याज्येषा ॥ हे अध्वर्यवः भरत प्रापयतेमं सोमं । (*asyaiva ca śastrasya yājyeṣā ‖ he adhvaryavaḥ bharata prāpayatemaṃ somaṃ |*)

एतस्माद्धविर्धानादुत्तरवेदिं इंद्राय इंद्रार्थं । अथवा भरत धारयता- (*etasmāddhavirdhānāduttaravediṃ iṃdrāya iṃdrārthaṃ | athavā bharata dhārayatā-*)

ग्नेरुपरि । ततश्चैनं आमत्रेभिः अमत्रैः पात्रॆः एभिः सोमचमस्यॆः (*gnerupari | tataścainaṃ āmatrebhiḥ amatraiḥ pātrèḥ ebhiḥ somacamasyèḥ*)

वषट्कारे प्राप्ते आसिंचत तस्मिन्नग्नौ । मद्यं मदनीयं एतत्सोमाख्यं (*vaṣaṭkāre prāpte āsiṃcata tasminnagnau | madyaṃ madanīyaṃ etatsomākhyaṃ*)

अंधः । तस्मात् पुनरेवं ब्रवीमि । इतः यस्मात् कामि हि वीरः कामयत (*aṃdhaḥ | tasmāt punarevaṃ bravīmi | itaḥ yasmāt kāmi hi vīraḥ kāmayata*)

एव वीरः इंद्रः अस्य सोमस्य सदं सदैव नित्यकालमेव प्रीतिं पानमि- (*eva vīraḥ iṃdraḥ asya somasya sadaṃ sadaiva nityakālameva prītiṃ pānami-*)

त्यर्थः । तस्मात् कारणात् जुहोत तस्मै वृष्टे वर्षित्रे इंद्राय एतं (*tyarthaḥ | tasmāt kāraṇāt juhota tasmai vṛṣṭe varṣitre iṃdrāya etaṃ*)

सोमं । किमेतमेव केवलं सोममेष कामयते? नेत्युच्यते । (*somaṃ | kimetameva kevalaṃ somameṣa kāmayate? netyucyate |*)

### Printed p. 725

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 725.*

**Special Topics (continued)**

तदिदेष वष्टि तत्तत्सोमप्रदानमेष इंद्रो वष्टि कामयत इत्यर्थः । (*tadideṣa vaṣṭi tattatsomapradānameṣa iṃdro vaṣṭi kāmayata ityarthaḥ |*)

न केवलं रात्रिपर्यायेष्वित्यर्थः । तस्मादासिंचत इमं सोमं मा (*na kevalaṃ rātriparyāyeṣvityarthaḥ | tasmādāsiṃcata imaṃ somaṃ mā*)

विलंबध्वमित्यभिप्रायः । एवमत्र दानसंबंधात् अंधः शब्दोऽन्नार्थ (*vilaṃbadhvamityabhiprāyaḥ | evamatra dānasaṃbaṃdhāt aṃdhaḥ śabdo 'nnārtha*)

उपपद्यते । पठितमपि चान्ननामसु । अनेकार्थत्वात् तु संदिह्यते इत्येष (*upapadyate | paṭhitamapi cānnanāmasu | anekārthatvāt tu saṃdihyate ityeṣa*)

निगम उपात्तेः ॥ (*nigama upātteḥ ‖*) (Ni. 5-1. Du. Vyā.)

That is: this Ṛk was seen by the seer Gṛtsamada. In the Atirātra sacrifice, in the rātri-paryāya (night round), it is to be recited for the śastra mantras recited by the Hotṛ, and also as the yājyā mantra of that śastra; such is its application. O priests called Adhvaryus, take this Soma, for Indra's drinking, from the Havirdhāna place to the Uttaravedi. Then, with the camasa vessels, fill up this food in the form of intoxicating Soma juice and make the oblation in the fire. For this valiant Indra is at all times attached to Soma-drinking. Therefore make the oblation of Soma in the fire for Indra, the giver of all desired fruits. That is what Indra desires. Indra desires Soma-drinking not only at night but at all times. Therefore make the oblation of Soma without delay. Since the word अंध (*aṃdha*) also has the meaning "food", in order to show that in the present Ṛk the word अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) has just the meaning "food", the author of the Nirukta has cited the Vedic (nigama) sentence आमत्रेभिः सिंचता मद्यमंधः (*āmatrebhiḥ siṃcatā madyamaṃdhaḥ*) as an example. This is the main sense of the commentary above.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यो अ॒पो व॑व्रि॒वांसं॑ वृ॒त्रं ज॒घाना॒शन्ये॑व वृ॒क्षं ।
> तस्मा॑ ए॒तं भ॑रत तद्व॒शाय॑ ए॒ष इंद्रो॑ अर्हति प्री॒तिम॑स्य ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । अपः । वन्विवांसं । वृत्रं । जघान । अशन्या इव । वृक्षं ।
> तस्मै । एतं । भरत । तत्र्वशाय । एषः । इंद्रः । अर्हति । पीतिं । अस्य ॥ २ ॥

> *adhvaryavaḥ | yaḥ | apaḥ | vanvivāṃsaṃ | vṛtraṃ | jaghāna | aśanyā iva | vṛkṣaṃ |*
> *tasmai | etaṃ | bharata | tatrvaśāya | eṣaḥ | iṃdraḥ | arhati | pītiṃ | asya ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 726

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 14; p. 726.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः य इंद्रोऽप उदकानि वन्विवांसमावृत्य स्थितं वृत्रं । वृणो-
> श्याकाशमिति वृत्रो मेघः । तं मेघं वज्रेण जघान । हतवान् । अशन्येव । यथा-
> शन्या वैद्युताग्निना वृक्षं दहति तद्वत् । तस्मै तद्वशाय सोमकामाय तस्मा इंद्रा-
> य्यतं सोमं भरत । उत्तरवेदिं प्रति हरत । एष इंद्रोऽस्य सोमस्य प्रीतिं
> पानमर्हति ॥

> *he adhvaryavaḥ ya iṃdro 'pa udakāni vanvivāṃsamāvṛtya sthitaṃ vṛtraṃ | vṛṇo-*
> *śyākāśamiti vṛtro meghaḥ | taṃ meghaṃ vajreṇa jaghāna | hatavān | aśanyeva | yathā-*
> *śanyā vaidyutāgninā vṛkṣaṃ dahati tadvat | tasmai tadvaśāya somakāmāya tasmā iṃdrā-*
> *yyataṃ somaṃ bharata | uttaravediṃ prati harata | eṣa iṃdro 'sya somasya prītiṃ*
> *pānamarhati ‖*

Gist: Adhvaryus, bring the Soma to the Uttaravedi for Indra, who with his thunderbolt struck down Vṛtra, the cloud that enclosed the waters, as lightning burns a tree; he is worthy of drinking it.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वरवः (*adhvaravaḥ*) (as printed in the supplied text)—O Adhvaryus. यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who. अशन्या (*aśanyā*)—with the lightning fire. वृक्षं इव (*vṛkṣaṃ iva*)—as one splits and burns a tree. अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters. वन्विवांसं (*vanvivāṃsaṃ*)—who stood enclosing. वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—the demon Vṛtra, or the cloud. जघान (*jaghāna*)—split with the thunderbolt. तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to such. तद्वशाय (*tadvaśāya*)—to Indra, who desires Soma, in order to offer to him. एतं (*etaṃ*)—this Soma. भरत (*bharata*)—bring (to the Uttaravedi). एषः इंद्रः (*eṣaḥ iṃdraḥ*)—this Indra. अस्य (*asya*)—of this Soma juice. पीतिं (*pītiṃ*)—to obtain the drinking. अर्हति (*arhati*)—is worthy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, bring this Soma to the Uttaravedi, to offer it to Indra, who desires Soma and who, as the lightning fire splits and burns a tree, split with his thunderbolt Vṛtra, who stood enclosing the waters. This Indra is worthy of the drinking of the Soma juice.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, offer the libation to him who slew the rain-arresting Vritra, as(he strikes down) a tree with(his) thunderbolt; to him who desires it: and Indra is worthy of(the beverage).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

यः अपः वव्रिवांसं वृत्रं जघान अशन्येव वृक्षं (*yaḥ apaḥ vavrivāṃsaṃ vṛtraṃ jaghāna aśanyeva vṛkṣaṃ*)—offer Soma to the Indra who struck the demon Vṛtra, or the cloud, who in the atmosphere had enclosed the waters and held back the rain from falling, as the lightning (thunderbolt) burns a tree. This is the main sense of the sentence. Here that which held back the water from falling down, that is, upon the earth, may be the cloud or may be Vṛtra.

(Note on readings: the Saṃhitā has वव्रिवांसं (*vavrivāṃsaṃ*) while the Pada, the bhāṣya and the Pratipadārtha have वन्विवांसं (*vanvivāṃsaṃ*); the Pada has तत्र्वशाय (*tatrvaśāya*) where the Saṃhitā and the Pratipadārtha have तद्वशाय (*tadvaśāya*); the bhāṣya reads इंद्राय्यतं (*iṃdrāyyataṃ*); all as supplied. The Saṃhitā line of this Ṛk is printed in the supplied text without the Ṛk number, which the Pada carries.)

### Printed p. 727

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; p. 727.*

**Special Topics** (continued)

वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—The word वृत्र (*vṛtra*) is read among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-21). Explaining the word वृत्र (*vṛtra*), Yāska says:

तत्को वृत्रः । मेघ इति नैरुक्ताः । त्वाष्टोऽसुर इत्यैतिहासिकाः । अपां च ज्योतिषश्च मिश्रीभावकर्मणो वर्षकर्म जायते । तत्रोपमार्थेन युद्धवर्णा भवंति । (*tatko vṛtraḥ | megha iti nairuktāḥ | tvāṣṭo 'sura ityaitihāsikāḥ | apāṃ ca jyotiṣaśca miśrībhāvakarmaṇo varṣakarma jāyate | tatropamārthena yuddhavarṇā bhavaṃti |*) (Ni. 2-16)

This is the explanation he gives. That is: having asked "Who is Vṛtra?", he answers, and indicates the difference of opinion: the etymologists (Nairuktas) say that it is the cloud; the narrators of ancient history (Aitihāsikas) say that it is the demon Vṛtra, son of Tvaṣṭṛ Prajāpati. From the mixing of the sun's rays and the water of the ocean, that is, when the sun's rays fall upon the ocean, the act of raining arises as its effect. This very rain (a heavy downpour accompanied by lightning, flashes and thunder) is described by comparison with a battle. That is, when the clouds in the atmosphere, or the demon Vṛtra, hold back the rain from falling on the earth, Indra strikes them with his thunderbolt (lightning) and makes the rain fall. They have expressed their view that the battle between Indra and Vṛtra means just this description.

The commentator says: वृणोत्याकाशमिति वृत्रो मेघः । वृणोतेराच्छादनार्थात् (*vṛṇotyākāśamiti vṛtro meghaḥ | vṛṇoterācchādanārthāt*) (स्वादि परस्मैपदि (*svādi parasmaipadi*)). That is, because it remains covering the sky, Vṛtra means the cloud; this is the explanation of the meaning. Further, आच्छादयति ह्यसौ कृत्सं नभः (*ācchādayati hyasau kṛtsaṃ nabhaḥ*)—because it covers the whole region of the atmosphere and makes darkness spread, Vṛtra means the cloud; or गच्छत्यसौ कृत्सं नभः (*gacchatyasau kṛtsaṃ nabhaḥ*)—because it moves about in the sky, Vṛtra means the cloud; or वर्धतेर्वा वृद्ध्यर्थात् (*vardhatervā vṛddhyarthāt*) (भू. अ. (*bhū. a.*)) बाहुलकात् त्रन्, धकारस्य तकारो व्यत्ययेन, वर्धते हि वर्षासु मेघः (*bāhulakāt tran, dhakārasya takāro vyatyayena, vardhate hi varṣāsu meghaḥ*)—that is, because it grows in the rainy season, Vṛtra means the cloud. In this sense it should be understood that the word वृत्र (*vṛtra*) is formed from the root वृथ् (*vṛth*) [as printed], and that the त (*ta*) has arisen by variation in place of the ध (*dha*). How these meanings have support in the Vedas themselves:

(1) यदिमाल्लोकानवृणोत्तद्वत्रस्य वृत्रत्वं स इषुमात्रमिषुमात्रं विष्टज् अवर्धत (*yadimāllokānavṛṇottadvatrasya vṛtratvaṃ sa iṣumātramiṣumātraṃ viṣṭaj avardhata*) (Tai. Saṃ.) [as printed]

(2) अस्मा इदु प्र भरा तूतुजानो वृत्ताय वज्रमीशानः कियेधाः । (*asmā idu pra bharā tūtujāno vṛttāya vajramīśānaḥ kiyedhāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-61-12)

(3) श्रत्ते दधामि प्रथमाय मन्यवेऽहन्यद्वत्रं नर्यं विवेरपः । (*śratte dadhāmi prathamāya manyave 'hanyadvatraṃ naryaṃ viverapaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-147-1)

and so on; there are many examples.

### Printed p. 728

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā; Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 2, Sūkta 14; p. 728.*

अशन्येव वृक्षं (*aśanyeva vṛkṣaṃ*)—यथा अशन्या वैद्युताग्निना वृक्षं दहति तद्वत् । (*yathā aśanyā vaidyutāgninā vṛkṣaṃ dahati tadvat |*) While the rain is pouring, the lightning, called the electric fire, strikes a tall-grown tree and it is burnt up; the simile is made to the thunderbolt which Indra uses when he destroys Vṛtra.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यो दृभी॑कं ज॒घान॒ यो गा उ॒दाज॒दप॒ हि व॒लं वः ।
> तस्मा॑ ए॒तमं॒तरि॑क्षे॒ न वात॒मिंद्रं॒ सोम्मै॒रोर्णु॑त॒ जूर्न वस्त्रैः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । दृभीकं । जघान । यः । गाः । उत्ऽआजत् । अप । हि । वलाः [?]
> वरिति [?] वः ।
> तस्मै । एतं । अंतरिक्षे । न । वातं । इंद्रं । सोम्मैः । अ । ऊर्णुत । जूः । न ।
> वस्त्रैः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः य इंद्रो दृभीकं । सवान्विदारयति भियं करोतीति दृभी-
> को नामासुरः । तमसुरं जघान । यश्च बलासुरेण । निरुद्धा गा उदाजत् । निरग-
> मयत् । ततश्च तमेव बलमसुरं अपवः । अपावृणोत् । हिंसितवानित्यर्थः ॥
> वृणोतेर्लुङ् मंत्रे घसेत्यादिना ज्लेर्लुक् । हल्ब्यदिना तिपो लोपः ॥ तस्मा
> इंद्रायैतं सोममंतरिक्षे वातं न वात्मिव धाराभिर्वाप्तं कुरुत । तदेवाह
> सोमैरिंद्रमा ऊर्णुत । सर्वत अच्छाधयत ॥ ऊर्णुञ् आच्छादने । आदादिकः ।
> लोटि रूपं ॥ तत्र दृष्टांतः । जून्र जीर्णो यथा वस्त्रैरंगमाच्छादयति तद्वत्॥

> *he adhvaryavaḥ ya iṃdro dṛbhīkaṃ | savānvidārayati bhiyaṃ karotīti dṛbhī-*
> *ko nāmāsuraḥ | tamasuraṃ jaghāna | yaśca balāsureṇa | niruddhā gā udājat | niraga-*
> *mayat | tataśca tameva balamasuraṃ apavaḥ | apāvṛṇot | hiṃsitavānityarthaḥ ‖*
> *vṛṇoterluṅ maṃtre ghasetyādinā jlerluk | halbyadinā tipo lopaḥ ‖ tasmā*
> *iṃdrāyaitaṃ somamaṃtarikṣe vātaṃ na vātmiva dhārābhirvāptaṃ kuruta | tadevāha*
> *somairiṃdramā ūrṇuta | sarvata acchādhayata ‖ ūrṇuñ ācchādane | ādādikaḥ |*
> *loṭi rūpaṃ ‖ tatra dṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | jūnra jīrṇo yathā vastrairaṃgamācchādayati tadvat‖*

Gist: Adhvaryus, Indra slew Dṛbhīka, and destroyed Vala and freed the cows; cover him on all sides with Soma, as one covers the wind with streams of water in the sky, and as an old man with garments.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who. दृभीकं (*dṛbhīkaṃ*)—the demon named Dṛbhīka. जघान (*jaghāna*)—slew (and). वलं (*valaṃ*)—the demon named Vala. अप वः (*apa vaḥ*)—destroyed.

### Printed p. 729

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; p. 729.*

यः (*yaḥ*)—who. गाः (*gāḥ*)—the cows (held back by that demon Vala). उदाजत् (*udājat*)—released. तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to that Indra. एतं (*etaṃ*)—this Soma. अंतरिक्षे (*aṃtarikṣe*)—in the atmosphere. वातं न (*vātaṃ na*)—as one covers the wind with streams of water, fill (him). जूः (*jūḥ*)—an old man. वस्त्रैः न (*vastraiḥ na*)—as he becomes covered with garments. इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra. सोमैः (*somaiḥ*)—with Soma juices. आ ऊर्णुत (*ā ūrṇuta*)—cover completely (fill).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, Indra who slew the demon named Dṛbhīka, and who destroyed the demon named Vala and released the cows held by him: cover that Indra completely (fill him) with Soma juices, as one fills the wind in the atmosphere with streams of water, and as one makes an old man covered with garments.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, offer this libation, which, like the wind in the firmament,(is the cause of rain); to him who slew Drihhika, destroyed Vala, and liberated the cows; heap Indra with Soma juice, as an old man(is covered) with garments.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

यः दृभीकं जघान (*yaḥ dṛbhīkaṃ jaghāna*)—सर्वान् विदारयति भियं करोतीति दृभीको नामासुरः । तमसुरं जघान । (*sarvān vidārayati bhiyaṃ karotīti dṛbhīko nāmāsuraḥ | tamasuraṃ jaghāna |*) An asura named Dṛbhīka went about splitting and killing everyone and causing fear; therefore he got the name Dṛbhīka. It is said here that Indra destroyed that asura. The name of this Dṛbhīka occurs in the Ṛgveda only in this Ṛk, nowhere else. Therefore not much can be said about him.

यः गाः उदाजत् अप हि वलं वः (*yaḥ gāḥ udājat apa hi valaṃ vaḥ*)—It is described here that Indra injured the asura named Vala, who had stolen the cows of the gods and hidden them, and released those cows from bondage. We have explained the matter of Vala at length in places such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-465, 5-232, 692; 14-634.

तस्मै एतं अंतरिक्षे वातं न सोमैः ऊर्णुत जूर्न वस्त्रैः (*tasmai etaṃ aṃtarikṣe vātaṃ na somaiḥ ūrṇuta jūrna vastraiḥ*)—तस्मा इंद्रायैतं सोममंतरिक्षे वात्मिव धाराभिर्वाप्तं कुरुत । सर्वत आच्छादयत । तत्र दृष्टांतः । जूर्न जीर्णो यथा वस्त्रैरंगमाच्छादयति तद्वत् । (*tasmā iṃdrāyaitaṃ somamaṃtarikṣe vātmiva dhārābhirvāptaṃ kuruta | sarvata ācchādayata | tatra dṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | jūrna jīrṇo yathā vastrairaṃgamācchādayati tadvat |*) To that Indra, that is, to Indra possessed of the qualities described above, fill the Soma juice well, make it spread well; that is, let Indra drink the Soma juice in abundance. Here two illustrations are

### Printed p. 730

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 730.*

mentioned. The wind in the atmosphere makes the rain-water spread everywhere. In the same way, fill Indra abundantly with Soma juice, is the sense. And as an aged old man, unable to bear the cold, covers his body completely with garments, make Indra (or Indra's belly) be covered, that is, pervaded, by the Soma juices: thus the illustration has been stated.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ य उर॑णं ज॒घान॒ नव॑ च॒ख्यांसं॑ नव॒तिं च॑ बा॒हून् ।
> यो अर्बु॑द॒मव॑ नी॒चा ब॑बा॒धे तमिंद्रं॒ सोम॑स्य भृ॒थे हि॑नोत ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । उरणं । जघान । नव । चख्यांसं । नवतिं । च । बाहून् ।
> यः । अर्बुदं । अव । नीचा । बबाधे । तं । इंद्रं । सोमस्य । भृथे । हिनोत ॥४॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः य इंद्रो नव नवतिं च एकोनशतं बाहून् चख्यांसं ॥
> चष्टेरिदं रूपं । यद्वा खनतेः क्वसौ रूपं । निलोपः । छांदसं रूपं ॥ सर्वस्य
> दर्शयंतं । यद्वा आत्मनो देहे निखातवंतमुरणमेतन्नामकमसुरं जघान ।
> यश्चार्बुदमेतन्नामकमसुरं च नीचाधोमुखं कृत्वावबबाधे । तमेनमिंद्रं
> सोमस्य बृथे सोमं बिभ्रति पात्रे हिनोत । वर्धयत । यद्वा सोमस्य बृथे
> भरणे संपादने सति हिनोत । स्तोत्रैः प्रीणयत ॥ हि गतिवृद्ध्योः । स्वादिः ॥

> *he adhvaryavaḥ ya iṃdro nava navatiṃ ca ekonaśataṃ bāhūn cakhyāṃsaṃ ‖*
> *caṣṭeridaṃ rūpaṃ | yadvā khanateḥ kvasau rūpaṃ | nilopaḥ | chāṃdasaṃ rūpaṃ ‖ sarvasya*
> *darśayaṃtaṃ | yadvā ātmano dehe nikhātavaṃtamuraṇametannāmakamasuraṃ jaghāna |*
> *yaścārbudametannāmakamasuraṃ ca nīcādhomukhaṃ kṛtvāvababādhe | tamenamiṃdraṃ*
> *somasya bṛthe somaṃ bibhrati pātre hinota | vardhayata | yadvā somasya bṛthe*
> *bharaṇe saṃpādane sati hinota | stotraiḥ prīṇayata ‖ hi gativṛddhyoḥ | svādiḥ ‖*

Gist: Adhvaryus, Indra slew Uraṇa, who displayed ninety-nine arms (or had them fixed in his body), and threw Arbuda down headlong; nourish him in the Soma vessel.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*))—O Adhvaryus. यः (यः (*yaḥ*))—the Indra who. नव नवतिं च (नव नवतिं च (*nava navatiṃ ca*))—possessing the number ninety-nine. बाहून् (बाहून् (*bāhūn*))—arms. चख्वांसं (चख्वांसं (*cakhvāṃsaṃ*))—displaying (or having in the body).

### Printed p. 731

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 731.*

उरणं (उरणं (*uraṇaṃ*))—the asura named Uraṇa. जघान (जघान (*jaghāna*))—slew. यः (यः (*yaḥ*))—who. अर्बुदं (अर्बुदं (*arbudaṃ*))—the asura named Arbuda. नीचा (नीचा (*nīcā*))—having made head-downward. अव बबाधे (अव बबाधे (*ava babādhe*))—injured and destroyed. तं (तं (*taṃ*))—that. इंद्रं (इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*))—Indra. सोमस्य (सोमस्यॆ (*somasyè*))—of the Soma juice. भृथे (भृथे (*bhṛthe*))—in the vessel (offering Soma with a full vessel). हिनोत (हिनोत (*hinota*))—increase (him).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, Indra who slew the asura named Uraṇa, who was displaying all his ninety-nine arms, and who made the asura named Arbuda head-downward, injured and destroyed him: offer Soma with a full vessel to such an Indra and increase him.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, propitiate, by the offering of the Soma, that Indra, who slew Urana, displaying nine and ninety arms; and hurled Arbuda down headlong.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

य उरणं जघान नव चख्वांसं नवतिं च बाहून् (*ya uraṇaṃ jaghāna nava cakhvāṃsaṃ navatiṃ ca bāhūn*)—Sāyaṇa has explained this sentence in two ways. इंद्रो नव नवतिं च एकोनशतं बाहून् चख्वांसं सर्वस्य दर्शयंतं उरणमेतन्नामकसुरं जघान (*iṃdro nava navatiṃ ca ekonaśataṃ bāhūn cakhvāṃsaṃ sarvasya darśayaṃtaṃ uraṇametannāmakasuraṃ jaghāna*)—that is, Indra slew the asura named Uraṇa, displaying ninety-nine arms so as to be seen by all: this is one kind of meaning. यद्वा आत्मनो देहे चख्वांसं निखातवंतमुरणमेतन्नामकमसुरं जघान (*yadvā ātmano dehe cakhvāṃsaṃ nikhātavaṃtamuraṇametannāmakamasuraṃ jaghāna*)—or, Indra slew the asura named Uraṇa, who, with his ninety-nine arms, fell upon Indra and was wounding him: this is another kind of meaning. Here the ninety-nine arms may belong either to Indra or to the asura named Uraṇa. "Ninety-nine" need not be understood as exactly that number; it may be understood as "many". The sense is that Indra slew the asura named Uraṇa, who had many arms. Since the name Uraṇa occurs only in this Ṛk and nowhere else, nothing can be said about him.

यः अर्बुदमव नीचा बबाधे (*yaḥ arbudamava nīcā babādhe*)—It is said here that Indra made the asura named Arbuda head-downward, injured and killed him. We have explained the matter of the asura named Arbuda at length in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5, p. 175, and in pp. 633–634 of this same part.

तमिंद्रं सोमस्य भृथे हिनोत (*tamiṃdraṃ somasya bhṛthe hinota*)—तमेनमिंद्रं सोमस्य भृथे सोमं बिभ्रति पात्रॆ हिनोत वर्धयत (*tamenamiṃdraṃ somasya bhṛthe somaṃ bibhrati pātrè hinota vardhayata*). To such an Indra, offering the Soma filled completely in the vessel,

### Printed p. 732

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 732.*

increase him: this is one meaning; and यद्वा सोमस्य भृथे भरणे संपादने सति हिनोत स्तोत्रैः प्रीणयत (*yadvā somasya bhṛthe bharaṇe saṃpādane sati hinota stotraiḥ prīṇayata*)—or, after the vessel is filled with Soma juice, gratify Indra with hymns so that he may drink the Soma juice: this is another meaning; the commentator says both are possible.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यः स्वश्नं॑ ज॒घान॒ यः शुष्ण॑म॒शुषं॒ यो व्यं॑सं ।
> यः पिपुं॒ नमु॑चिं॒ यो रु॑धि॒क्रां तस्मा॒ इंद्रा॒यांध॑सो जुहोत ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । सु । अश्वं । जघान । यः । शुष्णं । अशुषं । यः । विअंसं ।
> यः । पिप्रुं । नमुचिं । यः । रुधिक्रां । तस्मै । इंद्राय । अंधसः । जुहोत ॥५॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः य इंद्रोऽश्नं । अश्नाति भक्षयति प्राणिजातमिति । यदा
> अश्नुते स्वतेजसा सर्वं व्याप्नोतीत्यश्चः कश्चिदसुरः । तमसुरं सु सुष्टु जघान
> अशुषं केनाप्यशोषणीयं शुष्णमसुरं यश्च तमसुरं व्यंसमंसहीनं कृत्वा
> जघान । यश्च पिप्रुं तेजसा जगद्व्याप्य वर्तमानमेतन्नामकमसुरं नमुचिमसुरं
> जघान यश्च । तैत्तिरीयके नमुचिमसुरं नालभतेति प्रकृत्य फेनेन शिर उदवर्त-
> यदित्यंतेन वाक्यसंदर्भेण नमुचिवधः प्रपंचितः । तथा मंत्रश्च । अपां फेनेन
> नमुचेः शिर इंद्रोदवर्तयः । ८-१४-१३ । इति । रुधिक्रामेतन्नामकमसुरं यश्च
> जघान । तस्मा इंद्रायांधसो हविर्लक्षणान्यन्नानि जुहोत । जुहुत ॥

> *he adhvaryavaḥ ya iṃdro 'śnaṃ | aśnāti bhakṣayati prāṇijātamiti | yadā*
> *aśnute svatejasā sarvaṃ vyāpnotītyaścaḥ kaścidasuraḥ | tamasuraṃ su suṣṭu jaghāna*
> *aśuṣaṃ kenāpyaśoṣaṇīyaṃ śuṣṇamasuraṃ yaśca tamasuraṃ vyaṃsamaṃsahīnaṃ kṛtvā*
> *jaghāna | yaśca pipruṃ tejasā jagadvyāpya vartamānametannāmakamasuraṃ namucimasuraṃ*
> *jaghāna yaśca | taittirīyake namucimasuraṃ nālabhateti prakṛtya phenena śira udavarta-*
> *yadityaṃtena vākyasaṃdarbheṇa namucivadhaḥ prapaṃcitaḥ | tathā maṃtraśca | apāṃ phenena*
> *namuceḥ śira iṃdrodavartayaḥ | 8-14-13 | iti | rudhikrāmetannāmakamasuraṃ yaśca*
> *jaghāna | tasmā iṃdrāyāṃdhaso havirlakṣaṇānyannāni juhota | juhuta ‖*

Gist: Adhvaryus, Indra slew Aśna, Śuṣṇa, Vyaṃsa, Pipru, Namuci and Rudhikrā; offer food-oblations to him. The bhāṣya cites the Taittirīya account of Namuci and a mantra on his beheading with the foam of waters.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*))—O Adhvaryus. यः (यः (*yaḥ*))—the Indra who. अश्नं (अश्नं (*aśnaṃ*))—the asura named Aśna. सु जघान (सु जघान (*su jaghāna*))—destroyed completely. यः (यः (*yaḥ*))—who. अशुषं (अशुषं (*aśuṣaṃ*))—(by others) impossible to defeat. शुष्णं (शुष्णं (*śuṣṇaṃ*))—the asura named Śuṣṇa. व्यंसं (व्यंसं (*vyaṃsaṃ*))—cutting off the arm (slew).

### Printed p. 733

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 733.*

यः (*yaḥ*)—who. पिपुं (*pipuṃ*)—the asura named Pipu. नमुचिं (*namuciṃ*)—Namuci, whom he slew. यः (*yaḥ*)—who. रुधिक्रां (*rudhikrāṃ*)—the asura named Rudhikra. जघान (*jaghāna*)—slew. तस्मै इंद्राय (*tasmai iṃdrāya*)—to that Indra. अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—the foods in the form of oblation. जुहोत (*juhota*)—offer and make the oblation.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, offer and make oblations of food, in the form of oblation, to that Indra who utterly destroyed the asura named Aśna; who cut off the arms of the asura named Śuṣṇa, who cannot be dried up by others, and then slew him; and who slew Pipu, Namuci and the asuras named Rudhikra.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, offer the Soma libation to that Indra, who slew Ashna, the, unabsorbable Sushna, and the mutilated(Vritra); who destroyed Pipru Namuchi and Rudhikra

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk it is said that Indra destroyed the asuras named Āśna, Śuṣṇa, Pipu, Namuci and Rudhikra.

यः सु अश्वं जघान (*yaḥ su aśvaṃ jaghāna*)—अश्नाति भक्षयति प्राणिजातमिति । यद्वा अश्नुते स्वतेजसा सर्वं व्याप्नोतीत्यश्नः । कश्चिदसुरः । तमसुरं सुष्टु इंद्रो जघान । (*aśnāti bhakṣayati prāṇijātamiti | yadvā aśnute svatejasā sarvaṃ vyāpnotītyaśnaḥ | kaścidasuraḥ | tamasuraṃ suṣṭu iṃdro jaghāna |*) Because he devours all living beings, this asura is named Ashma [sic, as printed]; or, because he pervades everything with his own radiance (power), he may also be called Ashma [sic, as printed]. Here it is said that Indra struck this asura named Aśna well and destroyed him. Since the name of that Aśna asura occurs nowhere else, little is known about him.

यः शुष्णं अशुषं यो व्यंसं (*yaḥ śuṣṇaṃ aśuṣaṃ yo vyaṃsaṃ*)—अशुषं केनाप्यशोषणीयं शुष्णमसुरं यश्च तमसुरं व्यंसमंसहीनं कृत्वा जघान (*aśuṣaṃ kenāpyaśoṣaṇīyaṃ śuṣṇamasuraṃ yaśca tamasuraṃ vyaṃsamaṃsahīnaṃ kṛtvā jaghāna*)—it is said that Indra first cut off the arms of the asura Śuṣṇa, who cannot be dried up, and afterwards slew him. Or, since the word यः (*yaḥ*) is read twice here, it may be taken that there are two separate sentences, यः शुष्णं आशुषं जघान (*yaḥ śuṣṇaṃ āśuṣaṃ jaghāna*) and यः व्यंसं जघान (*yaḥ vyaṃsaṃ jaghāna*). In that case the meaning can be given as: for the first sentence, Indra slew the asura Śuṣṇa; for the second, he cut the asura Vṛtra into pieces and slew him. The matter of Indra's slaying the asuras Śuṣṇa and Pipu mentioned in this Ṛk is

### Printed p. 734

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 14; p. 734.*

also stated in the Ṛk

यो व्यंसं जाहृषाणेन मन्युना यः शंबरं यो अहन्पिप्रमव्रतं । (*yo vyaṃsaṃ jāhṛṣāṇena manyunā yaḥ śaṃbaraṃ yo ahanpipramavrataṃ |*)
इंद्रो यः शुष्णमशुषं न्यावृणङ्मरुत्वंतं सख्याय हवामहे ॥ (*iṃdro yaḥ śuṣṇamaśuṣaṃ nyāvṛṇaṅmarutvaṃtaṃ sakhyāya havāmahe ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-101-2)

Here too it is said that Indra slew the asura Śuṣṇa, who is अशुषं (*aśuṣaṃ*) (not to be killed), that is, who cannot be killed by anyone other than Indra. This asura named Śuṣṇa drinks up all the water on the earth and causes drought. He is extremely powerful; apart from Vṛtra he is the most dangerous enemy. After he was slain by Indra the earth became rich in plants and full of fruit. The name of this Śuṣṇa is read about forty times in the Ṛgveda. He is chiefly the enemy of Kutsa. It is said that Indra destroys Śuṣṇa for Kutsa's sake or together with Kutsa (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-16-12, 5-29-9, etc.); since there are descriptions that he has horns (1-33-12), eggs (8-40-10, 11) and hisses (1-54-5), he may be called an asura of serpent form. He has strong forts ( -51-11 [sic, as printed, the first numeral missing]; 4-30-13), and these are moving forts (8-1-28). Indra destroys Śuṣṇa's forts and releases the waters (1-51-11). Having destroyed Śuṣṇa's line, he wins the heavenly waters (स्वर्वतीः (*svarvatīḥ*)) (8-40-10). The epithet कुयव (*kuyava*) (one who produces bad grain) is used four times of Śuṣṇa. In the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-103-8 and 1-104-3 the word कुयव (*kuyava*) itself is used in place of the name of Śuṣṇa. There are descriptions (8-85-17) and others that the result of the battle between Indra and Śuṣṇa is not only the release of the waters but may also be the gaining of the cows or of the sun. In the Kāṭhaka it is said that Śuṣṇa is a dānava who keeps nectar within himself. From the sentences mentioned above, Śuṣṇa may be called the cause of drought. We have dealt with him in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5-693, 6-72, 8-275, 10-2550, 13-380 and so on.

पिपुं (*pipuṃ*)—It is said in many Ṛks that the asura named Pipu was defeated by Indra many times. Just as Indra fought with Śambara for Divodāsa and Atithigva, so he fought with Pipu for Ṛjiśvan. The matter of Pipu is described in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-101-1, 2; 4-16-13, 5-29-11, 6-20-7, 8-49-10, 10-99-11, 10-138-3, 1-103-8, 6-18-8, and so on.

वि पिप्रोरहिमायस्य दृळ्हाः पुरो वज्रञ्भवसा न दर्दः । (*vi piprorahimāyasya dṛḷhāḥ puro vajrañbhavasā na dardaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-7)

### Printed p. 735

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 735.*

In this Ṛk it is said that Indra destroyed the firm ramparts (forts) of Pipu.

यः स्वबिंदमनशनिं पिपुं दासमहीशुवं । (*yaḥ svabiṃdamanaśaniṃ pipuṃ dāsamahīśuvaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-32-2)

In this Ṛk it is also said that Pipu belonged to the company of the Dāsa people. Some doubt whether Pipu was an asura or a man belonging to the company of the Dasyu people devoid of vows.

It is uncertain whether he was a demon, according to Roth’s view, which is favoured by the use of the word असुर (*asura*) or a human foe. as Ludwing, Oldenberg, Hillebrandt believe. The name may mean resister from the root प्रृ (*prṛ*). (Vedic Index of Names and Subjects p. 532)

This Pipu is the enemy of Ṛjiśvan, the dependent of Indra. This Ṛjiśvan, offering Soma juice to Indra, gets Indra's help when he fights against Pipru (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-29-11, 10-99-11). Indra, together with Ṛjiśvan or for him, defeated Pipu (1-101-1, 2; 10-138-3, 4-16-13, 6-20-7). Pipru, endowed with the māyās of Ahi, has many forts under his control. Indra breaks them (1-51-5, 6-20-7). Indra slew Pipru the Dāsa and some others and released the waters (8-32-2). Indra, being with Ṛjiśvan, demolished the strong forts of the asura Pipu, the possessor of māyā (10-138-3). Indra handed over to Ṛjiśvan Pipru, who was in the form of a wild beast; and he defeated fifty thousand black people and broke open the strongholds (4-16-3). Joining with Ṛjiśvan, Indra destroyed the very progeny of the black people (1-111-1). Many such descriptions about Pipru are stated here and there.

नमुचिं (*namuciṃ*)—इंद्रेण सह युद्धं न मुंचतीति नमुचिः तं । (*iṃdreṇa saha yuddhaṃ na muṃcatīti namuciḥ taṃ |*) Because he does not give up fighting with Indra, he is named Namuci. Namuci is an asura. Since he was very mighty, it became very difficult for Indra to fight and conquer him. We have written about this asura in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5-695, 105-50. But since the commentator indicates that Indra's slaying of Namuci is stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa, we have set it out here—

इंद्रो वृत्रेग्ं हत्वा । असुरान् पराभाव्य । नमुचिमासुरं नालभत । तग्ं (*iṃdro vṛtregṃ hatvā | asurān parābhāvya | namucimāsuraṃ nālabhata | tagṃ*)
शच्यागृह्णात् । तौ समलभेतां। सोऽस्मादभिशुनतरोऽभवत् । सोऽब्रवीत् । (*śacyāgṛhṇāt | tau samalabhetāṃ| so 'smādabhiśunataro 'bhavat | so 'bravīt |*)
संधाग्ं संदधावहै । अथ त्वावस्रक्ष्यामि । न मा शुष्केण नार्द्रेण हनः । न (*saṃdhāgṃ saṃdadhāvahai | atha tvāvasrakṣyāmi | na mā śuṣkeṇa nārdreṇa hanaḥ | na*)
दिवा न नक्तमिति । स एतमपां फेनमसिंचत् । न वा एष शुष्को नार्द्रो (*divā na naktamiti | sa etamapāṃ phenamasiṃcat | na vā eṣa śuṣko nārdro*)

(The passage continues on the next page.)

### Printed p. 736

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 736.*

व्युष्टासीत् । अनुदितः सूर्यः । न वा एतद्भिवा न नक्तं । तस्यैतस्मिन्लोके। अपां फेनेन शिर उदवर्तयत् । तदेनमन्ववर्ततॆ । मित्रद्रुगिति । स एतानपामार्गानजनयॆत् । तानजुहोत् । त्वर्वै स रक्षाग्‌स्यपाहत । यदपामार्गहोमो भवति । रक्षसामपहत्यै ॥ (*vyuṣṭāsīt | anuditaḥ sūryaḥ | na vā etadbhivā na naktaṃ | tasyaitasminloke| apāṃ phenena śira udavartayat | tadenamanvavartatè | mitradrugiti | sa etānapāmārgānajanayèt | tānajuhot | tvarvai sa rakṣāgsyapāhata | yadapāmārgahomo bhavati | rakṣasāmapahatyai ‖*)
(Śai. Brā. 1-7-15, 6, 7)

After Indra had slain the asura Vṛtra he also slew other rākṣasas. But he could not conquer the asura named Namuci. This one was extremely mighty and did not yield to weapons of any kind. Indra therefore wished to defeat him by his own bodily strength alone, and began a wrestling-fight with him. Yet the more they fought, the more Namuci's strength increased. Indra could not escape from his grip. Then Indra asked Namuci to release him, and the asura said to Indra: "O Indra, if I am to release you, you must do as I say. Let us now make a pact with each other. You may again wish to fight me by some stratagem and kill me. In such a case the weapon you use to kill me must be neither wet nor dry; you must not kill me by day, nor by night. If you agree to these conditions I shall release you." Indra agreed, saying "So be it", freed himself from his grip, and began to think how he might conquer him. After some time an expedient occurred to him. The foam of the sea lying before him fell within his sight. "This foam is neither wet nor dry; so I shall make it my weapon and slay Namuci." When the night has passed and the time of dawn has begun, before the sun rises, it is quite light. That time is not night, since it is not dark, and not day, since the sun has not risen. Having decided that if he fought at such a time and killed him with a weapon made of the water's foam he would not break the word he had given, he fought Namuci accordingly and cut off his head with that foam. Yet the asura did not die, but pursued Indra to kill him. "Hey, Indra, betrayer of a friend! Stop, stop! You gave your word that you would not kill me, and now you have done such a friend-betraying deed. I shall not let you go without killing you." Then Indra, frightened, created a special tree named apāmārga and made an offering in the fire with its sticks for the increase of his vigour. By that Indra's strength grew greater and he slew Namuci. Therefore it is said that those who wish to be rid of the affliction of rākṣasas should make an offering with apāmārga sticks. This matter of Indra's slaying this Namuci

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 13; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā; p. 737.*

is stated not only in this [?] but in the Ṛgveda [?]—

नम्मा यदिंद्र सख्या परावति निबर्हयो नमुचिं नाम मायिनं । (*nammā yadiṃdra sakhyā parāvati nibarhayo namuciṃ nāma māyinaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7)
अत्रा दासस्य नमुचेः शिरो यदवर्तयो मनवे गातुमिच्छन् । (*atrā dāsasya namuceḥ śiro yadavartayo manave gātumicchan |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7)
युजं हि मामकृथा आदिदिंद्र शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् । (*yujaṃ hi māmakṛthā ādidiṃdra śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8)
प्र श्येनो न मदिरमंशुमस्मै शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् । (*pra śyeno na madiramaṃśumasmai śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-6)
निवेशने शततमाविवेषीरहञ्च वृत्रं नमुचिमुताहन् । (*niveśane śatatamāviveṣīrahañca vṛtraṃ namucimutāhan |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5)
अपां फेनेन नमुचेः शिर इंद्रोदवर्तयः । (*apāṃ phenena namuceḥ śira iṃdrodavartayaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-13)
त्वं जघंथ नमुचिं मुखस्युं दासं कृष्णान ऋषये विमानं । (*tvaṃ jaghaṃtha namuciṃ mukhasyuṃ dāsaṃ kṛṣṇāna ṛṣaye vimānaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-73-7)
युवं सुराममश्विना नमुचावासुरे सचा । (*yuvaṃ surāmamaśvinā namucāvāsure sacā |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4)

It is stated in these Ṛks; nowhere else. [The supplied text then repeats a variant line: "It is stated in these words; nowhere else."]

यः रुधिक्रां तस्मै इंद्राय अंधसः जुहोत (*yaḥ rudhikrāṃ tasmai iṃdrāya aṃdhasaḥ juhota*)—रुधिक्रामेतन्नामकमसुरं यश्च जघान । तस्मा इंद्रायांधसो हविर्लक्षणान्यन्नानि जुहोत । जुहुत । (*rudhikrāmetannāmakamasuraṃ yaśca jaghāna | tasmā iṃdrāyāṃdhaso havirlakṣaṇānyannāni juhota | juhuta |*) O Adhvaryus, offer an oblation of food, in the form of oblation, into the fire to that Indra who slew the asura named Rudhikra. Since the name of the asura Rudhikra is not mentioned anywhere else in the Ṛgveda, nothing more can be said about him

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यः श॒तं शंब॑रस्य॒ पुरो॑ बि॒भेदाश्म॑नेव पू॒र्विः।
> यो व॒र्चिनः॑ श॒तमिंद्रः॑ स॒हस्र॑म॒पाव॑प॒द्भर॑ता॒ सोम॑मस्मै ॥

> *adhvaryavo yaḥ śataṃ śaṃbarasya puro bibhedāśmaneva pūrviḥ|*
> *yo varcinaḥ śatamiṃdraḥ sahasramapāvapadbharatā somamasmai ‖*

### Printed p. 738

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 738.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । शतं । शंबरस्य । पुरः । बिभेद । अश्मनाइव । पूर्विः ।
> यः । वर्चिनः । शतं । इंद्रः । सहस्रं । अपअवपत् । भरत । सोमं । अस्मै ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः य इंद्रः शंबरस्य मायाविनोऽसुरस्य पूर्वीः पुरातनीः
> शतं पुरः पुरीरश्मनेव अश्वसदृशेन वज्रेण बिभेद । यश्चेंद्रो वर्चिनः ॥ वर्च
> दीप्तौ । गमेरिनिः । उ. ४-६ । इति विधीयमान इनिर्बहुलवचनादस्माद्भवति ॥ यद्वा
> नामैतत् । तस्यासुरस्य शतं सहस्रमेतत्संख्याकानपरिमितान्वीरान् पुत्रानपावपत् ।
> युगपदेव भूम्यामपातयत् । तथा च मंत्रवर्णः । शतं वर्चिनः सहस्रं च साकं
> हथो अप्रत्यसुरस्य वीरान् । ७-९९_५ । इति । अस्मा इंद्राय सोमं भरत ॥

> *he adhvaryavaḥ ya iṃdraḥ śaṃbarasya māyāvino 'surasya pūrvīḥ purātanīḥ*
> *śataṃ puraḥ purīraśmaneva aśvasadṛśena vajreṇa bibheda | yaśceṃdro varcinaḥ ‖ varca*
> *dīptau | gameriniḥ | u. 4-6 | iti vidhīyamāna inirbahulavacanādasmādbhavati ‖ yadvā*
> *nāmaitat | tasyāsurasya śataṃ sahasrametatsaṃkhyākānaparimitānvīrān putrānapāvapat |*
> *yugapadeva bhūmyāmapātayat | tathā ca maṃtravarṇaḥ | śataṃ varcinaḥ sahasraṃ ca sākaṃ*
> *hatho apratyasurasya vīrān | 7-99_5 | iti | asmā iṃdrāya somaṃ bharata ‖*

Gist: Adhvaryus, Indra split Śambara's hundred ancient cities with the thunderbolt and felled Varcin's hundred thousand sons; offer Soma to him.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who. शंबरस्य (*śaṃbarasya*)—of the māyā-wielding asura Śambara. पूर्विः (*pūrviḥ*)—very ancient. शतं (*śataṃ*)—a hundred in number. पुरः (*puraḥ*)—cities. अश्मनेव (*aśmaneva*)—with the very hard thunderbolt. बिभेद (*bibheda*)—split asunder. यः इंद्रः (*yaḥ iṃdraḥ*)—the Indra who. वर्चिनः (*varcinaḥ*)—of the asura named Varci. शतं सहस्रं (*śataṃ sahasraṃ*)—innumerable heroic sons and others. अपावपत् (*apāvapat*)—felled all together and destroyed. अस्मै (*asmai*)—to such an Indra. सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma. भरत (*bharata*)—bring and offer.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, offer Soma, bringing it, to that Indra who split with the very hard thunderbolt the very ancient hundred cities (the supplied text reads "hundred-numbered" with a misprint in the Kannada word) of the māyā-wielding asura Śambara, and who felled all together and destroyed a hundred thousand, innumerable, heroic sons and others of the asura named Varci.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, present the Soma libation to him, who, with the adamantine (thunderbolt). demolished [sic, as printed] the hundred ancient cities of Shambara, and cast down the hundred thousand(descendants) [sic, as printed] of Varchin.

### Printed p. 739

*Running head: Ṛgvedasaṃhitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, A. 6, V. 13; p. 739.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk it is stated that Indra destroyed a hundred cities of the asura named Śambara, and that he killed eleven hundred heroes (a hundred and a thousand) of the asura Varcin.

यः शतं शंबरस्य पुरः बिभेद अश्मनेन पूर्विः (*yaḥ śataṃ śaṃbarasya puraḥ bibheda aśmanena pūrviḥ*)—यः इंद्रः शंबरस्य मायाविनोऽसुरस्य पूर्विः पुरातनीः शतं पुरः पुरीरश्मनेव अश्मसदृशेन वज्रेण बिभेद (*yaḥ iṃdraḥ śaṃbarasya māyāvino 'surasya pūrviḥ purātanīḥ śataṃ puraḥ purīraśmaneva aśmasadṛśena vajreṇa bibheda*). The meaning of this sentence is that Indra split (destroyed) with his thunderbolt, hard as stone, the hundred ancient cities of the asura named Śambara. Here the word शतं (*śataṃ*) can be taken to mean "many, numerous". Because—

इंद्राविष्णू द्यंहिताः शंबरस्य नव पुरो नवतिं च श्वथिष्टं (*iṃdrāviṣṇū dyaṃhitāḥ śaṃbarasya nava puro navatiṃ ca śvathiṣṭaṃ*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 7-99-5)

in this Ṛk it is said that Indra, together with Viṣṇu, destroyed ninety-nine cities of Śambara. Therefore here too the words नव नवतिं (*nava navatiṃ*) can be given the sense "many". Similarly, on the destruction of Śambara's cities by Indra—

भिनत्पुरो नवतिमिंद्र पूरवे दिवोदासाय महि दाशुषे नृतो वज्रेण दाशुषे नृतो (*bhinatpuro navatimiṃdra pūrave divodāsāya mahi dāśuṣe nṛto vajreṇa dāśuṣe nṛto*) ।
अतिथिग्वाय शंबरं गिरेरुग्रो अवाभरत् (*atithigvāya śaṃbaraṃ girerugro avābharat*) ।
(Ma. Saṃ. 1-130-7)

in this Ṛk it is said that Indra split ninety cities of Śambara.

दिवोदासाय नवतिं च ननेंद्रः पुरो वैरच्छंबरस्य (*divodāsāya navatiṃ ca naneṃdraḥ puro vairacchaṃbarasya*) ।
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-19-6)

here it is said that Indra destroyed ninety-nine cities of Śambara for Divodāsa.

This Śambara is one of Indra's chief enemies. His name and his story are mentioned in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-51-6, 1-54-4, 1-59-6, 1-101-2, 1-.03-8 [?], 1-112-14, 1-130-7, 2-12-11, 2-14-6, 2-19-6, 4-26-3, 4-30-14, 6-18-8, 6-26-5, 6-31-4, 6-31-1, 6-47-2, 6-47-21, 7-18-20, 7-99-5. His name is commonly read together with Śuṣṇa, Pipru and Varcin. His cities are said to have been 90, 99 and 100. He is said to have been the chief enemy of Divodāsa Atithigva, and that Atithigva defeated him with Indra's help, in the Ṛks 1-51-6, 1-130-7, 2-19-6, 4-26-3 and others. It can be understood that he was a chieftain among the early inhabitants of this country, who lived in the hills and hillocks and gave trouble to the Āryas

### Printed p. 740

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 740.*

(continued from p. 739:) He was a chieftain who caused trouble to the Āryas; so it can be understood. We have described his story in Ṛ. Saṃ. Bhāga 5-74, 5-694, 8-274, 10-550 and other places.

यः वर्चिनः शतं इंद्रः सहस्रं अपावपत् (*yaḥ varcinaḥ śataṃ iṃdraḥ sahasraṃ apāvapat*)—Indra felled to the ground and killed a hundred thousand heroes (together with the sons, grandsons and others of) the asura named Varcin. Here the commentator has given two kinds of meaning to the word वर्चिनः (*varcinaḥ*) (वर्च-दीप्तौ (*varca-dīptau*)): either "resplendent, that is, valiant heroes", or "of the asura named Varcin". Of these, taking the word Varcin as the asura of that name is the more fitting. Because—

शतं वर्चिनः सहस्रं च साकं हथो अप्रत्यसुरस्य वीरान् (*śataṃ varcinaḥ sahasraṃ ca sākaṃ hatho apratyasurasya vīrān*) ।
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-99-5)

उत दासस्य वर्चिनः सहस्राणि शतावधीः (*uta dāsasya varcinaḥ sahasrāṇi śatāvadhīḥ*) ।
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-30-5)

in these Ṛks the killing of a hundred thousand heroes of the asura Varcin is stated. Here the words शतं सहस्रं च (*śataṃ sahasraṃ ca*) can be understood in two ways, as "a hundred thousand", that is one lakh, or as "eleven hundred". The sense on the whole is "many heroes". And—

अहन् दासा वृषभो वस्ययंतोदव्रजे वर्चिनं शंबरं च (*ahan dāsā vṛṣabho vasyayaṃtodavraje varcinaṃ śaṃbaraṃ ca*) ।
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-21)

in this Ṛk the slaying of the asuras Varcin and Śambara is mentioned.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यः श॒तमा स॒हस्रं॒ भूम्या॑ उ॒पस्थेव॑पज्जघ॒न्वान् ।
> कुत्स॑स्या॒योर॑तिथि॒ग्वस्य॑ वी॒रान्न्यवृ॑ण॒ग्भर॑ता॒ सोम॑मस्मै ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यः । शतं । आ । सहस्रं । भूम्याः । उपस्थे । अवपत् । जघन्वान् ।
> कुत्सस्य । आयोः । अतिथिग्वस्य । वीरान् । नि । अवृणक् । भरत । सोमं । अस्मै ॥ ७ ॥

> *adhvaryavaḥ | yaḥ | śataṃ | ā | sahasraṃ | bhūmyāḥ | upasthe | avapat | jaghanvān |*
> *kutsasya | āyoḥ | atithigvasya | vīrān | ni | avṛṇak | bharata | somaṃ | asmai ‖ 7 ‖*

### Printed p. 741

*Running head: Ṛgvedasaṃhitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, A. 6, V. 14; p. 741.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवो जघन्वान् पूर्वं शत्रून् हतवान् य इंद्रः शतं सहस्रम-
> सुरान् भूम्या उपस्थ उत्संगेवपत् । एकैकेन प्रकारेणापातयत् । किंच यः
> कुत्सस्यॆतन्नामकस्य राजर्षेः । अयोः पौरूरवसस्य राजर्षेः । अतिथिग्वस्य दिवो-
> दासस्य । एतेषां त्रयाणां वीरानभिगंत्यान्प्रतिद्वंद्विनः शुष्णादीनसुरान् न्यवृणक् ।
> वृणक्तिर्हिंसाकर्मा । अवधीत् । तथा च मंत्रवर्णः । त्वं कुत्सं शुष्णहत्येश्वा-
> विधारंधयोऽतिथिग्वाय शंबरं । १.५१_६ । इति । तादृशायेंद्राय सोमं भरत ॥

*Gist:* Adhvaryus, offer Soma to that Indra who felled hundreds and thousands of asuras on the earth's lap and destroyed the foes of Kutsa, Ayu and Atithigva, such as Śuṣṇa.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who. शतं सहस्रं (*śataṃ sahasraṃ*)—thousands upon thousands (immeasurable in number) of asuras. जघन्वान् (*jaghanvān*)—having killed. भूम्याः (*bhūmyāḥ*)—of the earth. उपस्थे (*upasthe*)—upon the lap. आवपत् (*āvapat*)—felled one by one (and moreover). कुत्सस्य (*kutsasya*)—for the sage Kutsa. आयोः (*āyoḥ*)—for Āyu. अतिथिग्वस्य (*atithigvasya*)—for the sage named Atithigva. वीरान् (*vīrān*)—all the asuras, Śuṣṇa and others, who were hostile and tormenting. न्यवृणक् (*nyavṛṇak*)—destroyed. अस्मै (*asmai*)—to such an Indra. सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma. भरत (*bharata*)—bring and offer.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, offer Soma juice, bringing it, to that Indra who killed thousands of asuras and felled them one by one on the lap of the earth, and who, moreover, destroyed all the asuras, Śuṣṇa and others, who were hostile to the sages Kutsa, Āyu and Atithigva and tormented them.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, presented the Soma libation to him, who, slaying hundreds and thousands(of Asuras). cast them down upon the lap of the earth; who dest- royed [sic] the assailants of Kutsa, Ayu, aud [sic] Atithigwa.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

कुत्सस्य आयोः अतिथिग्वस्य (*kutsasya āyoḥ atithigvasya*)—It is said that Indra felled and killed many heroes who were enemies of these three persons, in order to help them. About them—

त्वमस्मै कुत्समतिथिग्वमायुं महे राज्ञे यूने आरंधनायः (*tvamasmai kutsamatithigvamāyuṃ mahe rājñe yūne āraṃdhanāyaḥ*) ।
(Ma. Saṃ. 1-53-10)

### Printed p. 742

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 742.*

त्वं कुत्सं शुष्णहत्येष्वाविधारंधयेऽतिथिग्वाय शंबरं । (*tvaṃ kutsaṃ śuṣṇahatyeṣvāvidhāraṃdhaye 'tithigvāya śaṃbaraṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-51-6)

and similar Ṛks say this. The matter of Kutsa and Āyu is stated in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 13-335; the matter of Kutsa in Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-678, 8-768, 821; 13-335, 380 (the pages named); and the matter of Atithigva in Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-174.

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवो॒ यन्न॑रः का॒मया॑ध्वे श्रु॒ष्टी वहं॑तो नशथा॒ तदिंद्रे॑ ।
> गभ॑स्तिपूतं भरत श्रु॒तायेंद्रा॑य॒ सोमं॑ यज्यवो जुहोत ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । यत् । नरः । कमयाध्वे । श्रुष्टी । वहंतः । न श थ । तत् । इंद्रे ।
> गभस्तिपूतं । भरत । श्रुताय । इंद्राय । सोमं । यज्यवः । जुहोत ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> नरः कर्मणां नेतारो हे अध्वर्यवः यदभिलषितमर्थं कामयाध्वे ॥
> कामयतेर्लेट्र्यडागमः ॥ श्रुष्टी क्षिप्रमिंद्रे सोमं वहंतः प्रापयंतो
> यूयं तदभिलषितं फलं नशथ । प्राप्नुत । श्रुताय लोके प्रसिद्धायेंद्राय
> गभस्तिपूतं हस्ताभ्यां मंजनदोहनादिभि शोधितं सोमं भरत । उत्तरवेदिं प्रति
> हरत । ततो यज्यवो यागं कुर्वाणा हे अध्वर्यव आह्ृतं सोममिंद्राय
> जुहोत । जुहुत । अग्नौ प्रक्षिपत ॥

*Gist:* Adhvaryus, you who lead the rites, bring the Soma quickly to Indra and offer it in the fire; thereby you will obtain what you desire.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नरः (*naraḥ*)—O leaders of the rite. अध्वरवः (*adhvaravaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. यत् (*yat*)—whatever desired objects. कामयाध्वै (*kāmayādhvai*)—you wish to be fulfilled. तत् (*tat*)—all that. इंद्रे (*iṃdre*)—for Indra. श्रुष्टि (*śruṣṭi*)—quickly. वहंतः (*vahaṃtaḥ*)—by bringing and carrying the Soma juice. नशथ (*naśatha*)—obtain.

### Printed p. 743

*Running head: A. 2, A. 6, V. 14; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā; p. 743.*

श्रुताय (*śrutāya*)—renowned in all the world. इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—to Indra. गभस्तिपूतं (*gabhastipūtaṃ*)—purified with both hands by pressing, milking and the like. सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma. भरत (*bharata*)—take and bring. यज्यवः (*yajyavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus, performers of the sacrifice. जुहोत (*juhota*)—offer (that juice) in the fire.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, leaders of the rite, whatever desired objects of yours you wish to be fulfilled, obtain all that by quickly offering the Soma juice to Indra. O Adhvaryus, performers of the sacrifice, bring to Indra, renowned in all the world, the Soma purified with both hands by pressing, milking and the like, and offer it.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, leaders(of the ceremony), may you, quickly bringing the offer- ing to Indra, receive that(recompense) which you may desire: solemnizing the sacrifice, offer the Soma libation, purified by expressing, to the renowned Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

नरः—कर्मणां नेतारः । (*naraḥ—karmaṇāṃ netāraḥ |*) Since they take the chief part in sacrifices and other rites and carry those rites through successfully, the word नरः (*naraḥ*) is used here as an epithet of the word अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*).

श्रुष्टी—श्रुष्टीति क्षिप्रनाम—आशु अष्टीति । (*śruṣṭī—śruṣṭīti kṣipranāma—āśu aṣṭīti |*) (Ni. 6-13) Since it indicates the sense "pervade swiftly", श्रुष्ठी (*śruṣṭhī*) means "quickly".

श्रुष्टी वहंतः नशथ तत् इंद्रे (*śruṣṭī vahaṃtaḥ naśatha tat iṃdre*)—Here the final letter of the word नशथ (*naśatha*) is long in the Saṃhitā text for the convenience of the metre. The chief sense is that you (the Adhvaryus), who carry the Soma juice for Indra, "in Indra", that is, for Indra, and offer it, will obtain the due reward of that rite.

गभस्तिपूतं भरत श्रुताय इंद्राय सोमं—लोके प्रसिद्धायेंद्राय गभस्तिपूतं हस्ताभ्यां मंजनदोहनादिभिः शोधितं सोमं भरत । उत्तरवेदिं प्रति हरत । (*gabhastipūtaṃ bharata śrutāya iṃdrāya somaṃ—loke prasiddhāyeṃdrāya gabhastipūtaṃ hastābhyāṃ maṃjanadohanādibhiḥ śodhitaṃ somaṃ bharata | uttaravediṃ prati harata |*) The sense is: bring to the Uttaravedi the Soma juice, purified with the hands and prepared for drinking, for Indra, who is renowned for having done many deeds of daring. The word गभस्ति (*gabhasti*) is read among the names of rays (Ni. 2-15) and among the names of arms (Ni. 3-8). Here the sense "arms" (hands) should be accepted.

### Printed p. 744

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 14; p. 744.*

When Soma plants are crushed, it is customary to place them on hides. This hide is called त्वक् (*tvak*). A cow-hide was used for it (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-94-9, 10-116-4). The vessels in which the Soma is stored have names such as कोश (*kośa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-4, 8-20-8), सधस्थ (*sadhastha*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-62-15, 9-1-2, 9-17-8), द्रु (*dru*) (Ṛ. Saṃ 9-1-2, 9-65-6, 9-98-2), वन (*vana*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-9, 9-66-9) and द्रोण (*droṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ 9-15-7, 9-33-2). स्रुव (*sruva*) means a ladle (Ṛ. Saṃ 1-1-6-24 [as printed]); the cloth or sieve that strains the Soma juice is called पवित्र (*pavitra*); in some cases the Soma was also strained through the gaps between the fingers.

मृजंति त्वा समग्रवोऽव्ये जीरावधि स्वणि । रेभो यदज्यसे वने । (*mṛjaṃti tvā samagravo 'vye jīrāvadhi svaṇi | rebho yadajyase vane |*)
(Pu. Saṃ 9-66-9)

In such Ṛks it is said that the juice obtained by soaking the Soma plant in water, crushing and squeezing it, is strained with the fingers of the hand; that same matter is explained here by the word गभस्तिपूतं (*gabhastipūtaṃ*).

The method of crushing the Soma plant and preparing the Soma juice is nowhere described at length in the Ṛgveda. It can be inferred from some sentences stated here and there. The stones for crushing the Soma plants have the names ग्रावा (*grāvā*) and अद्रि (*adri*). In some cases wooden boards known as अधिषवण (*adhiṣavaṇa*) are also used. If the plant is fresh, much juice comes; if not, that is, if some days have passed since it was cut and brought, they soak it in water and afterwards place it on the hide. Then they crush it with stones and take out the juice. Since the juice so obtained is mixed with husks, bits and the like of the plant, they strain it either through the gaps between the fingers, or through the sieve or cloth called पवित्र (*pavitra*), or through sheep's wool. The juice strained in this way is clear, without impurities. It has the names शुक्र (*śukra*) and शुचि (*śuci*). In appearance this juice is dull red or red mixed with yellow. It is customary to offer to Indra and Vāyu pure Soma juice, that is, without any admixture of आशिर (*āśira*) substances. For other deities milk, curds and grain flour are mixed in and used. The Soma juice mixed with these has the names गवाशिर (*gavāśira*), दध्याशिर (*dadhyāśira*) and यवाशिर (*yavāśira*) respectively.

### Printed p. 745

*Running head: A. 2, A. 6, V. 14; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā; p. 745.*

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवः॒ कर्त॑ना श्रु॒ष्टिम॑स्मै॒ वने॒ निपू॑तं॒ वन॒ उन्न॑यध्वं।
> जु॒षा॒णो हस्य॑म॒भि वा॑वशे व॒ इंद्रा॑य॒ सोमं॑ मदि॒रं जु॑होत ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । कर्तन । श्रुष्टिं । अस्मै । वने । निपूतं । वने । उत् । नयध्वं ।
> जुषाणः । हस्यं । अभि । वावशे । वः । इंद्राय । सोमं । मदिरं । जुहोत ॥९ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः अस्मा इंद्राय सोमं श्रुष्टिं सुखकरं कर्तन । कुरुत ॥
> करोतेर्लोटि बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् । तप्तनप्तनथनाश्चति तनबादेशः ।
> आमंत्रितस्याविद्यमानत्वान्न निघातः ॥ किंच वने संभजनीये वन उदके निपूतमा-
> प्यायनेन शोधितं सोममुन्नयध्वं । ऊर्ध्वं नयत । यद्वा वने तद्विकारे
> चमसे निपूतं दशापवित्रेण शोधितं सोमं वने चमस उन्नयध्वं । जुषाणः
> प्रीयमाणः स इंद्रो वो युष्माकं हस्तं हस्ताभ्यामभिषुतं सोममभिवावशे
> कामयते ॥ वश कांतौ । यज्-लुगंतस्य लटि व्यत्ययेनात्मनेपदं । लोपस्त
> आत्मनेपदेष्टिति तलोपः ॥ मदिरं मदकरमिमं सोममिंद्राय जुहोत ।
> इंद्रोद्देशेनागौ प्रक्षिपत ॥

*Gist:* Adhvaryus, prepare the Soma for Indra, lift the purified Soma up in the ladle, and offer it in the fire, for Indra desires it.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)—O Adhvaryus. अस्मै (*asmai*)—for this Indra (for Indra's sake). श्रुष्टिं (*śruṣṭiṃ*)—the pleasant (agreeable) Soma. कर्तन (*kartana*)—prepare. वने (*vane*)—pounded after being soaked in water. निपूतं (*nipūtaṃ*)—the purified Soma. वने (*vane*)—in the ladle vessel. उन्नयध्वं (*unnayadhvaṃ*)—lift up and set down for the offering. जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)—Indra, gladdened. वः (*vaḥ*)—your. हस्त्यं (*hastyaṃ*)—the Soma pressed out by the hands. अभि वावशे (*abhi vāvaśe*)—desires. मदिरं (*madiraṃ*)—exhilarating. सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma. इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra. जुहोत (*juhota*)—offer (in the fire).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Adhvaryus, prepare the pleasant Soma for this Indra, and lift up in the ladle vessel the Soma purified together with the water, for the offering. Indra, gladdened, desires the Soma pressed out by your hand-

### Printed p. 746

*Running head: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā; Maṃ. 2, A. 2, Sū. 1, 4 [as printed]; p. 746.*

s; offer the exhilarating Soma to Indra in the fire.

**Printed English translation**

Priests, offer to him the prompt(libation); lift it up, purified with water, in the ladle: well pleased, he desires it from your hand; present the exhilarating Soma juice to Indra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अध्वर्यवः कर्तन श्रुष्टिं अस्मै (*adhvaryavaḥ kartana śruṣṭiṃ asmai*)—O Adhvaryus, prepare for this Indra the pleasant Soma juice. Here the commentator has explained the word श्रुष्टिं (*śruṣṭiṃ*) as सुखकॆरं (*sukhakèraṃ*) [sic, as supplied; the bhāṣya reads सुखकरं (*sukhakaraṃ*)]. Yāska has given the word श्रुष्टिं (*śruṣṭiṃ*) the sense "quickly" (Ni. 6-13 [sic: no closing parenthesis in the source]).

वने निपूतं वन उत् नयध्वं (*vane nipūtaṃ vana ut nayadhvaṃ*)—वने संभजनीये वन उदके निपूतमाप्यायनेन शोधितं सोममुन्नयध्वं । ऊर्ध्वं नयत । यद्वा वने तद्विकारे चमसे निपूतं दशापवित्रेण शोधितं सोमं वने चमस उन्नयध्वं । (*vane saṃbhajanīye vana udake nipūtamāpyāyanena śodhitaṃ somamunnayadhvaṃ | ūrdhvaṃ nayata | yadvā vane tadvikāre camase nipūtaṃ daśāpavitreṇa śodhitaṃ somaṃ vane camasa unnayadhvaṃ |*) Here the word वने (*vane*) is read twice. Since the word वनं (*vanaṃ*) is read among the names of water (2-24), वने (*vane*) means "in water". Also, the large wooden vessel in which the prepared Soma juice is collected and kept is called वन (*vana*); or the ladle vessel (चमस (*camasa*)) made of wood (of the udumbara tree) is also called वन (*vana*). Therefore, if in this sentence the first वने (*vane*) is taken as "water" and the second वने (*vane*) as the ladle vessel made of wood, the chief sense of the sentence is suggested: take to the Uttaravedi, in order to offer it to Indra, the Soma juice that was soaked in water, cleansed, then pounded, pressed, strained and put into the wooden vessel called चमस (*camasa*).

जुषाणः हस्तं अभि वावशे वः (*juṣāṇaḥ hastaṃ abhi vāvaśe vaḥ*)—जुषाणः प्रीयमाणः स इंद्रो वो युष्माकं हस्तं हस्ताभ्यामभिषुतं सोममभिवावशे कामयते । (*juṣāṇaḥ prīyamāṇaḥ sa iṃdro vo yuṣmākaṃ hastaṃ hastābhyāmabhiṣutaṃ somamabhivāvaśe kāmayate |*) Here हस्तं (*hastaṃ*) means the Soma juice prepared by pounding the Soma plants with the hands, pressing and straining them. O Adhvaryus, Indra, being pleased, desires the Soma juice prepared and strained by your hands. Therefore the chief sense is: make an oblation in the fire to Indra of the exhilarating Soma juice.

### Printed p. 747

*Running head: A. 2, A. 6, V. 14; Ṛgvedasaṃhitā; p. 747.*

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अध्व॑र्यवः॒ पय॒सोध॒र्यथा॒ गोः सोमे॑भिरीं पृणता भो॒जमिंद्रं॑ ।
> वेदा॒हम॑स्य॒ निभृ॑तं म ए॒तद्दित्सं॑तं॒ भूयो॑ यज॒तश्चि॑केत ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्यवः । पयसा । ऊधः । यथा । गोः । सोमेभिः । ईं । पृणत । भोजं ।
> इंद्रं ।
> वेद । अहं । अस्य । निभृतं । मे । एतत् । दित्संतं । भूयः । यजतः । चिकेत ॥१०॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अध्वर्यवः यथा गोरूधः पयसा पूर्णं तद्वदीमेनं भोजं
> फलस्य दातारं रक्षितारं चेंद्रं सोमेभिः सोमृः पृणुत । पूरयत । मे मदी-
> यस्य सोमस्य निभृतं गूढं सुखसाधनस्वभावमहमेव वेद । जानामि । इति पूर्वं
> न परस्मै कथितं । तथापि युष्मभ्यं ब्रवीमीत्येवं । दित्संतं सोमं दातुमिच्छंतं
> यजमानं यजतो यष्टव्य इंद्रो भूयोऽतिशयेन चिकेत ॥ दित्संतं । डुदाञ्
> दाने । इच्छायां सन् । सनि मीमाघ्वित्याकारस्य इसादेशः । अत्र लोपोऽभ्यासस्ये-
> त्यभ्यासलोपः । सः स्यार्धधातुक इति सकारस्य तत्वं ॥

*Gist:* Fill Indra with Soma as a cow's udder is full of milk; I alone know the hidden nature of this Soma, and Indra knows the sacrificer who wishes to give.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अध्वरवः (*adhvaravaḥ*) [sic: as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada read अध्वर्यवः (*adhvaryavaḥ*)]—O Adhvaryus. यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner. गोः (*goḥ*)—of the cow. ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)—the udder. पयसा (*payasā*)—is full of milk, like that. भोजं (*bhojaṃ*)—the giver of fruit and the protector. ईं इंद्रं (*īṃ iṃdraṃ*)—this Indra. सोमेभिः (*somebhiḥ*)—with Soma juices. पृणत (*pṛṇata*)—fill. मे (*me*)—dear to me. अस्य (*asya*)—of this Soma. एतत् निभृतं (*etat nibhṛtaṃ*)—this hidden nature of its pleasantness. अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I (alone). वेद (*veda*)—know (saying so). यजतः (*yajataḥ*)—Indra, worthy of sacrifice. दित्संतं (*ditsaṃtaṃ*)—the sacrificer who offers the Soma. भूयः (*bhūyaḥ*)—exceedingly. चिकेत (*ciketa*)—knows (praises).
